

==========================================================================================
# WORKFLOW: launch-readiness-delta  (7 agents)
==========================================================================================

## AREA: provider-journeys (supply side: pandit/priest, astrologer, wellness instructor — onboarding, portals, earnings, payouts)   score=46

### [PJ-01] P0/money/M No provider can ever be paid — provider_kyc.verified has no writer anywhere in the codebase
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payout.service.ts:150-200 (getKycStatus + requestPayout gate), payout.service.ts:~440 (runWeeklySettlement skippedKyc), apps/api/src/routes/provider-kyc.ts:76-151
  - evidence: requestPayout throws 'Your KYC is pending verification. Payouts unlock once an admin verifies it.' unless kyc.verified is true; runWeeklySettlement does `if (!kyc.verified || !kyc.hasBank) { skippedKyc++; continue; }`. A repo-wide grep for provider_kyc across apps/ returns exactly three non-test files: routes/provider-kyc.ts (SELECT + an INSERT/UPSERT of documents only), lib/ensure-schema.ts (CREATE TABLE), and services/payout.service.ts (SELECT verified). There is no UPDATE provider_kyc SET verified anywhere in apps/api, apps/web, or apps/admin. provider-kyc.ts:29 even states 'Approval (verified=true) is admin-driven' — but the admin route it refers to does not exist.
  - customer_impact: Every pandit, astrologer, wellness instructor, store owner and temple manager who earns money is permanently told 'Your KYC is pending verification' and can never withdraw a rupee; the automatic Monday settlement silently skips all of them too, so nobody is ever paid by either path.
  - fix: Add an admin endpoint (e.g. PATCH /admin/provider-kyc/:userId/verify) that sets verified=true/verified_at=now(), plus a reject path writing rejection_reason, and surface it in the admin approvals queue. This is a code fix and unblocks the request path; the actual bank transfer remains owner-gated on RazorpayX/Cashfree.

### [PJ-02] P0/trust/S /become-priest promises 'Money in your bank account every Monday' — no payout rail exists to honour it
  - where: https://darshya.online/become-priest (live copy); apps/api/src/services/payout.service.ts:18-25
  - evidence: Live page copy, curl-verified: 'Earn 60-80% of every booking. Money in your bank account every Monday.' and 'Verified pandits only.' Against that, payout.service.ts:18-21 documents the truth — 'Admin processPayout: completed → mark Commissions PAID (the real money movement is a bank/UPI transfer, Razorpay Route, or manual — the reference is recorded)'. processPayout is a bookkeeping state flip; no RazorpayX/Cashfree/Route integration exists (grep for razorpayx/cashfree/fund_account/penny-drop across apps/api/src returns only provider-kyc.ts, payout.service.ts, ensure-schema.ts and a test). Combined with PJ-01 the transfer cannot even be requested. The same line's '60-80%' also contradicts the shipped uniform-80% doctrine and the GTM plan's 15-18% take rate.
  - customer_impact: A pandit recruited on this page performs pujas expecting a bank credit every Monday, receives nothing, and cannot even see why — the most expensive possible way to lose supply in a market where the GTM plan puts pandit CAC at Rs 2,500-4,500 and warns that one wronged pandit poisons an entire temple network.
  - fix: Rewrite the two claims to what is true today: state the actual provider share as a single number (80%), and replace the Monday bank promise with the real mechanism ('request a payout from your portal once your KYC is verified; transfers are processed manually while we finish bank integration'). Restore the weekly-bank-credit claim only after the RazorpayX/Cashfree rail is live.

### [PJ-03] P1/money/M Priest.commissionRate defaults to 0.70 and nothing can change it — pinned pandits get 70%, not the promised 80%, and their own profile says so
  - where: apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma:328 (`commissionRate Float @default(0.70)`), apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:478-479, apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:536-538 + 1034 + 1976, apps/web/app/priest/profile/page.tsx:64 and :447
  - evidence: Schema default is 0.70. booking.service.ts:478 does `if (priest && priest.commissionRate > 0 && priest.commissionRate <= 1) { priestShare = priest.commissionRate; }` whenever the offering has an assignedPriestId, and bookings.ts:536 resolvePriestShare does the same on reassign and from-offering. A repo-wide grep for commissionRate finds only readers — no create/update anywhere in apps/api, and apps/web/app/admin/settings/page.tsx holds commissionRates in local useState with no persistence — so the 0.70 default is unchangeable in practice. The pandit's own portal confirms it: priest/profile/page.tsx:447 renders `{(profile.commissionRate * 100).toFixed(0)}% of booking` over a fallback of 0.70.
  - customer_impact: On every temple-pinned or reassigned booking the pandit is paid 70% while the platform takes 30% — the pandit sees '70% of booking' in his own profile, directly contradicting the 80% the recruitment page, the payout docs and the take-rate doctrine all promise, and there is no admin control to correct it.
  - fix: Change the schema default to 0.80 and write a migration backfilling existing Priest rows to 0.80 (no live money has moved, so a straight backfill is safe); add an admin-only field to set a negotiated rate so the override path has an owner. Keep DEFAULT_PRIEST_SHARE=0.8 as the fallback so both branches agree.

### [PJ-04] P1/broken/L The tiered dispatch engine has no pandit-facing UI and its one-tap WhatsApp accept link is a dead URL
  - where: apps/api/src/services/dispatch.service.ts (acceptUrl construction, ~line 199); apps/web/app/priest/bookings/ (page.tsx + view/ only — no [id] segment); apps/api/src/services/priest.service.ts:158-161
  - evidence: A grep across all of apps/web for 'priest/offers', 'dispatchOffer' or 'offerExpiresAt' returns zero matches — no screen anywhere renders a dispatch offer or an accept/decline control, even though POST /priest/offers/:bookingId/accept and GET /priest/offers exist. The portal cannot substitute: priest.service.getAssignedBookings builds `const where = { priestId }`, so a booking that has been OFFERED but not yet assigned never appears in /priest/bookings. The WhatsApp fallback is also dead — dispatch.service builds `acceptUrl: `${acceptBase}/priest/bookings/${bookingId}?accept=1`` but apps/web/app/priest/bookings contains only page.tsx and view/ (no dynamic [id] route), and the list page reads only searchParams.get('status'), never 'accept'. Live-verified the query is lost regardless: GET /priest/bookings/<uuid>?accept=1 → 307 to /auth/login?redirect=%2Fpriest%2Fbookings%2F<uuid>, with ?accept=1 stripped from the redirect target.
  - customer_impact: A pandit toggles 'Turn on to receive instant puja requests near you (Uber-style dispatch)' on his dashboard, then has nowhere in the app to see or accept an offer, and the WhatsApp link lands on a 404 — so T1/T2/T3 dispatch is curl-only and every instant/1-hour booking times out to admin escalation.
  - fix: Add a /priest/offers screen (or an Offers tab on /priest/bookings) backed by the existing GET /priest/offers, with accept/decline calling the existing endpoints; add app/priest/bookings/[id]/page.tsx that honours ?accept=1; and preserve the full query string through the middleware login redirect so the intent survives sign-in.

### [PJ-05] P1/trust/S /settings/earnings serves a fabricated provider ledger on live production, including a fake bank-details form
  - where: https://darshya.online/settings/earnings (200 OK) → /_next/static/chunks/app/settings/earnings/page-539d695189a772dc.js
  - evidence: Live-verified. The URL returns 200 (not a redirect, not a 307 to login), and the DEPLOYED chunk contains the mock verbatim: 'Total Earnings', eight fabricated transaction ids TXN-20260408-008 through TXN-20260415-001, a 'Request Instant Payout' button and an 'Update Bank Details' button. The repaired version (a 37-line redirect('/payouts')) exists in the working tree but is not in the deployed bundle.
  - customer_impact: A provider opening this URL sees a plausible earnings ledger that is entirely invented, plus a bank-details form that silently discards what he types — so he can retype his account number, see no error, and every later payout would still target the old bank with no record of the attempt.
  - fix: Ship the redirect that is already written in apps/web/app/settings/earnings/page.tsx and redeploy the web service.

### [PJ-06] P1/trust/S /priest/profile renders another pandit's verified identity and fake stats whenever the API call fails
  - where: apps/web/app/priest/profile/page.tsx:112-140 (catch → setProfile(fallbackProfile)), fallback object at :50-72
  - evidence: The fetch of /priest/profile has `if (res.ok) {...} else { throw new Error('API unavailable'); }` and a bare `catch { setProfile(fallbackProfile); ... }`. fallbackProfile is a full fabricated persona: user.fullName 'Ramesh Sharma', phone '+91 98765 43210', email 'pandit.ramesh@email.com', aadhaarVerified: true, isVerified: true, verificationStatus: 'approved', ratingAvg 4.8 / ratingCount 156, commissionRate 0.70, stats 234 total / 218 completed bookings, and temple affiliations to Kashi Vishwanath and Trimbakeshwar. Because a 401 takes the else branch, an ordinary expired session triggers it.
  - customer_impact: A pandit whose session expires sees someone else's name, phone number and email on his own profile page, together with an 'approved / Aadhaar verified' badge and a 4.8-star history he has not earned — and if he edits and saves from that state he is writing another person's data into his own record.
  - fix: Delete fallbackProfile and render an explicit error state with a retry; on a 401 send the user through the normal re-auth path instead of substituting fixture data.

### [PJ-07] P2/broken/S GET /api/v1/priests/:id returns HTTP 500 with a raw Prisma error to unauthenticated callers on any non-UUID id
  - where: https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/priests/:id — live
  - evidence: Live-verified, no auth header required. GET /api/v1/priests/me → 500 {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2023","error":"Internal Server Error","message":"\nInvalid `prisma.priest.findFirst()` invocation:\n\n\nInconsistent column data: Error creating UUID, invalid character: expected an optional prefix of `urn:uuid:` followed by [0-9a-fA-F-], found `m` at 1"}. GET /api/v1/priests/foo returns the same shape. GET /api/v1/priests correctly returns 200 with 3 priests, so only the :id branch is affected.
  - customer_impact: Any mistyped or slug-shaped priest link returns a 500 instead of a 404, and the error body leaks the ORM in use, the model name and the exact query method to any unauthenticated caller — the same class A-008 closed on POST /auth/signup, still open here.
  - fix: Validate the :id param as a UUID before querying (zod .uuid()) and return 404 when it is not one; add a generic error handler on this route so Prisma error text never reaches the client in production.

### [PJ-08] P2/missing-feature/L The public supply funnel is a contact form — /leads/self-onboard creates no provider, so no pandit can self-serve onto the platform
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/leads-self-onboard.ts:121-209; live https://darshya.online/become-priest
  - evidence: POST /self-onboard does exactly one write: prisma.lead.upsert on (role, email) with status 'NEW'. There is no User creation, no UserRoleAssignment, no Priest/AstrologerProfile row — only a welcome email saying 'Our partnerships team will review your details and get back to you within 48 hours' and an ADMIN_EMAILS notification. The separate real path, onboardingService.upsertProviderProfile, is reachable only from /auth/signup and is never called by this form. Live corroboration: GET /api/v1/priests returns exactly 3 priests on production — 'Pandit Ramesh Shastri', 'Pandit Suresh Trivedi' and 'Demo Pandit' — i.e. seed data only, zero real supply.
  - customer_impact: Every pandit and astrologer who completes the live 'Become a pandit' form lands in an admin inbox with no account, no portal access and no way to progress; the promised 48-hour activation is entirely manual and nothing on the platform performs it.
  - fix: On lead approval, call onboardingService.upsertProviderProfile to mint the User, the UserRoleAssignment and the role profile, then send real portal credentials — or, until that exists, change the form copy to say a human will contact them rather than implying automated activation.

### [PJ-09] P2/ux/S Video proof is captured by a browser window.prompt() on the main pandit bookings list
  - where: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:144-165
  - evidence: The 'video' action does `videoUrl = window.prompt('Paste the video recording URL for this booking:')?.trim() ?? null;` then POSTs it to /priest/bookings/:id/video. The real component — components/provider/VideoProofUpload.tsx, a Supabase Storage upload with progress and a paste fallback — exists but is only wired into /priest/bookings/view.
  - customer_impact: On the screen a pandit actually uses to close out a booking, delivering the mandatory video proof requires him to have already hosted the video somewhere and to paste a URL into a raw browser dialog — on a phone, immediately after a puja, this is the step most likely to be abandoned, and video proof gates both the delivered transition and the payout.
  - fix: Replace the window.prompt branch with the existing VideoProofUpload component inline in the list row (or route the button to /priest/bookings/view?id=), so the same upload path serves both screens.

## AREA: devotee-funnel   score=60.5

### [DF-01] P0/broken/S Logged-out devotee loses the entire booking form AND the puja at the Pay button
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas/book?slug=… ; apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:770
  - evidence: page.tsx:770 reads `window.location.href = `/auth/login?redirect=${encodeURIComponent(window.location.pathname)}`` — pathname only, so `?slug=rudrabhishek&temple=…` is stripped. I confirmed the same code in the deployed chunk (app/pujas/book/page-8dcfaa3bcc1d90db.js): `if(...,!en){...window.location.href="/auth/login?redirect=".concat(encodeURIComponent(window.location.pathname));return}`. I also grepped the deployed chunk for sessionStorage/localStorage: ZERO hits, so no wizard state is persisted, and window.location.href is a full page navigation that discards all React state. I then loaded https://darshya.online/pujas/book with no slug and it renders "No puja selected — This booking page needs to know which puja you want."
  - customer_impact: A first-time devotee picks a temple, date and time slot, types every devotee's name, gotra and nakshatra, writes a sankalp, enters a prasad delivery address and accepts the terms — then taps Pay, is bounced to login, and after signing in lands on a bare "No puja selected" page with every field blank and the puja itself forgotten. They must restart from the catalogue. Since revenue is ₹0 and no devotee accounts exist, this is the path taken by 100% of paying customers.
  - fix: Change page.tsx:770 to `window.location.pathname + window.location.search` so the slug and temple survive the round trip. Ideally also persist the wizard payload to sessionStorage under a short-lived key before redirecting and rehydrate on mount, so the form itself is restored, not just the puja.

### [DF-02] P1/broken/M 81% of festival "Book with verified pandit" CTAs 404, including the homepage's next-festival hero
  - where: https://darshya.online/ (next-festival block), https://darshya.online/festival/* ; apps/web/lib/festivals.ts (pujasAvailable arrays, e.g. line 791)
  - evidence: The homepage hero block "In 4 days · Next festival · Hartalika Teej · Saturday, 15 August" renders `<a href="/pujas/parvati-puja">Book parvati puja</a>` — that URL returns 404. /festival/teej-hartalika renders the same dead link styled "Parvati Puja — Book with verified pandit →". I sampled 12 festival pages and extracted 16 unique puja slugs: 13 of 16 are 404 (parvati-puja, durga-puja, kali-puja, krishna-puja, saraswati-puja, satyanarayan-puja, sundarkand-path, maha-mrityunjaya, navagraha-shanti, karwa-chauth-puja, tulsi-puja, kubera-puja, chandi-homam, durga-saptashati-path, go-puja, surya-puja); only rudrabhishek, ganesh-puja and lakshmi-puja resolve. Parsing apps/web/lib/festivals.ts shows 61 festivals referencing 87 unique slugs, while the live catalogue is 10 pujas — so ~84 of 87 are dead. Note the near-misses are spelling drift: 'sundarkand-path' vs real 'sunderkand-path', 'satyanarayana-katha' vs 'satyanarayan-katha', 'maha-mrityunjaya' vs 'maha-mrityunjay-jaap', 'pitra-dosh-nivaran' vs 'pitru-dosh-nivaran-puja'.
  - customer_impact: The festival calendar is the highest-intent seasonal surface in the product and it dead-ends. A devotee arriving for Navaratri, Durga Puja, Karwa Chauth, Chhath or Hartalika Teej clicks the one orange Book button on the page and hits a 404 — exactly during the Sharad Navratri → Diwali → Chhath window the GTM plan names as the year's biggest revenue period. It also burns the homepage's single time-sensitive CTA.
  - fix: Two parts. (1) Immediate: filter pujasAvailable against the live catalogue at render time so only resolvable slugs become links (the Diwali page already renders just lakshmi-puja, so the filter exists somewhere — apply it on the homepage next-festival block and every festival detail page). (2) Then fix the four spelling-drift slugs to match the real slugs, and point the remaining festivals at the closest real puja or at /pujas with a deity filter rather than a dead slug.

### [DF-03] P1/trust/M Real astrologers with 0 consultations are shown a hardcoded 12,450-review wall and "Top Rated / 10K+ Consults" badges next to a paid CTA
  - where: https://darshya.online/astrology/view?id=1b0970ed-e956-4cb9-ae25-f93133c3d8e1 (and ?id=astrologers)
  - evidence: For a REAL provider the page calls GET /api/v1/astrology/astrologers/1b0970ed-… and then renders: "Acharya Vinod Sharma — 0 | 12,450 reviews | 0 consultations · Top Rated · 10K+ Consults · Offline · ₹25/min · First 5 min FREE" with buttons "Book Paid Consultation / Chat Now / Call", followed by a full review wall reading "Reviews 12,450 total" with a fabricated distribution (5★72%, 4★20%, 3★5%, 2★2%, 1★1%) and named reviewers ("Priya M. 2026-04-10"). The real rating (0) and real consultation count (0) are rendered inches from the invented 12,450. Separately /astrology/view?id=astrologers — where "astrologers" is a mis-routed path segment, not an id — renders a wholly fabricated "Pt. Raghunath Sharma, 4.9 | 12,450 reviews | 34,200 consultations, Banaras Hindu University, featured in leading spiritual magazines and TV channels, ₹50/min".
  - customer_impact: Every devotee evaluating an astrologer is shown invented social proof attached to a live paid-consultation button. Beyond the obvious trust damage when a "12,450-review, Top Rated" astrologer turns out to have never taken a call, fabricated reviews on a paid service are directly actionable in India under the CCPA fake-review framework (BIS IS 19000:2022) — this is the kind of thing that draws a notice, not just a bad review.
  - fix: Delete the hardcoded review/badge fallbacks from the astrologer detail component and render real values only. With 0 consultations show an honest "New on Darshya" state instead of "Top Rated / 10K+ Consults", and hide the review block entirely when reviewCount is 0. Also stop rendering Chat Now / Call as active when status is Offline.

### [DF-04] P1/broken/M Every "Book Now" on a temple page throws away the puja and the temple — ?temple= is silently ignored
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples/kashi-vishwanath → https://darshya.online/pujas?temple=kashi-vishwanath
  - evidence: The Kashi Vishwanath page lists specific bookable pujas at specific prices (₹1,100, ₹1,500, ₹2,100) with six separate CTAs — "Book Now" ×4, "Book a Puja", and the hero "Book Puja via Darshya" — and every one of them points at /pujas?temple=kashi-vishwanath. Loading that URL renders "Showing 10 of 10 pujas", the full unfiltered catalogue, with no mention of Kashi anywhere in the first 1500 characters of main. The param has no effect. (Contrast: /pujas/rudrabhishek/book?temple=kashi-vishwanath-varanasi DOES 307 correctly to /pujas/book?slug=rudrabhishek&temple=… and preselects the temple — so the working pattern already exists.)
  - customer_impact: A devotee who has read the temple page, chosen "Rudrabhishek ₹1,100 at Kashi" and pressed Book is dumped into a generic 10-item list with no temple context and no indication of what they clicked. They have to find the puja again and re-pick the temple. This is the second-most-travelled route into the funnel after the catalogue and it loses intent at the moment of highest commitment.
  - fix: Point each temple-page puja card at the already-working deep link /pujas/<pujaSlug>/book?temple=<apiTempleSlug> instead of /pujas?temple=…. Note the temple page uses the static slug 'kashi-vishwanath' while the API uses 'kashi-vishwanath-varanasi', so the mapping has to be resolved (see DF-11). Failing that, at minimum implement the ?temple= filter on /pujas so the list narrows and shows the temple name.

### [DF-05] P2/trust/S Homepage overstates inventory by roughly 10x — "200+ temples", "500+ vetted pandits", "1,500+ weddings"
  - where: https://darshya.online/ (hero trust strip, Live Darshan card, features grid, stats bar); https://darshya.online/pujas (wedding banner)
  - evidence: The homepage says "Watch live aarti from 200+ temples", "HD streams from 200+ temples" and "200+ verified temples" in three separate blocks, plus "500+ vetted pandits". Live reality: GET /api/v1/temples?limit=100 returns {"total":20}; /darshan links to 17 unique temples (19 links with jagannath-puri and tirumala-tirupati duplicated); the /temples page itself claims 22. There is no public providers/astrologers endpoint to back "500+ vetted pandits", and /api/v1/pujas/types/popular reports "bookingCount":0 on every puja. /pujas additionally advertises "1,500+ weddings" on the marriage banner and the homepage claims "5,000+ aartis, mantras, bhajans" and "15+ Astrologers"; /gift claims a catalogue of "Satyanarayan, Rudrabhishek, Ganesh Puja, Mahalakshmi, and 40+ others" when the catalogue is 10 pujas.
  - customer_impact: A cold visitor's first 30 seconds are built on numbers that are 10x the truth. The moment they click through to /temples (22) or /darshan (17) the gap is visible, and the "1,500+ weddings" claim on a platform with zero completed bookings is the kind of statement that destroys credibility with exactly the 40-65 demographic this product needs, and is unsafe under ASCI substantiation rules.
  - fix: Replace the invented counts with true ones or with non-numeric claims: "20 verified temples" / "Live darshan from 17 temples" / drop "500+ vetted pandits" and "1,500+ weddings" entirely until they are real. Keep the counts in one constants file sourced from the API so they cannot drift again.

### [DF-06] P2/money/S Homepage advertises cow adoption "from ₹500 a month" — no such plan exists; cheapest monthly is ₹3,000
  - where: https://darshya.online/ (Worship rail "Gaushala Seva" card) vs https://darshya.online/gaushala
  - evidence: The homepage carries two different prices for the same product in two different blocks: the quick-links rail says "Gau Seva — Adopt a cow from Rs 51/day" while the Worship rail says "Gaushala Seva — Adopt a cow from ₹500 a month · Sponsor seva". The live /gaushala page offers exactly four plans: ₹51/day, ₹3,000/month, ₹30,000/year, ₹2,25,000 one-time. There is no ₹500/month tier anywhere.
  - customer_impact: A devotee attracted by "₹500 a month" arrives to find the cheapest monthly commitment is ₹3,000 — six times what was advertised. That is a bait-and-switch on a donation product where trust is the entire proposition, and it is the sort of price discrepancy that is straightforwardly reportable as a misleading advertisement.
  - fix: Change the Worship-rail card to "from ₹51/day" (or "from ₹3,000/month") so both homepage blocks and the gaushala page state the same ladder. Source the entry price from the same plan constant the /gaushala page renders.

### [DF-07] P2/content/S Homepage still promises "INR or USD, GBP, AUD, CAD" months after Stripe was deleted and the platform went INR-only
  - where: https://darshya.online/ (Gift-a-puja block)
  - evidence: The homepage renders "Gift a puja from abroad — INR or USD, GBP, AUD, CAD / Pandit-verified, video proof delivered same day, 80G receipt to your PAN" with a "Send gift" CTA. The page it links to, /gift, is correct and honest: "NRI-friendly. Book from anywhere in the world. Billing is in INR; international cards are accepted" — I scanned it and found zero occurrences of USD/GBP/AUD/CAD. So the destination was cleaned up but the entry point was not.
  - customer_impact: An NRI is told on the front page they can pay in their own currency, clicks through, and discovers billing is INR-only. Beyond the wasted click it sets a false expectation about FX and card fees on the single highest-value customer segment. The corpus records the Stripe removal as including "a full legal/marketing content pass (~13 pages promising multi-currency)" — this instance survived that pass on the highest-traffic page in the product, so the pass should be re-run rather than assumed complete.
  - fix: Change the headline to "Gift a puja from abroad — billed in INR, international cards accepted" to match /gift. Then re-grep the whole web app for USD|GBP|AUD|CAD|multi-currency to catch any other survivors of the content pass.

### [DF-08] P2/broken/M On /gaushala the real cows 404 while the mock demo cows work
  - where: https://darshya.online/gaushala → /gaushala/<uuid>
  - evidence: The /gaushala page renders 12 "Adopt" links: eight to mock ids (/gaushala/cow_001, cow_003, cow_004, cow_005, cow_006, cow_008, cow_010, cow_011) and four to real database UUIDs. All four UUID pages 404: /gaushala/f0dfee69-aaf3-4b90-a589-8a7df95cda0b, /gaushala/2342e603-5af8-469a-acb3-da18d5a6b40b, /gaushala/74188f87-de0f-45f4-bc10-34ec4bb9469f, /gaushala/03edd2ff-4479-48f3-9ad1-6940e5558a4d — all 404. Both /gaushala/cow_001 and /gaushala/cow_005 return 200. So the fake inventory is browsable and the real inventory is not.
  - customer_impact: A third of the adoption CTAs on the gau-seva page are dead, and they are precisely the ones backed by a real cow the donor could actually adopt. The donor either hits a 404 or, worse, successfully adopts a cow that does not exist. It also means the page is presenting mock animals with names and ages as real adoptable inventory.
  - fix: Make the /gaushala/[id] route resolve real cow UUIDs against the API (the list page already has the data, so the detail route is the gap), and remove the cow_00X mock entries from the listing so only real adoptable cows are shown.

### [DF-09] P2/broken/M /panchang calendar is stuck on April 2026, shows the wrong lunar month, and prints raw ISO timestamps as festival names
  - where: https://darshya.online/panchang
  - evidence: Three defects on one page, all live. (1) The header correctly reads "Tuesday, August 11, 2026" and the today-panel is genuinely accurate (Krishna Chaturdashi, Punarvasu, Rahu Kaal 15:37-17:16, Abhijit 11:56-12:44 — matching the homepage's "Aaj Krishna Chaturdashi, Punarvasu"), but the month grid below renders "April 2026" with an April day layout. (2) The masa label reads "Chaitra-Vaishakha, Vikram Samvat 2083" — Chaitra-Vaishakha is the March-May window; in August it should be Shravana-Bhadrapada. (3) The "Upcoming Festivals & Vrats" list renders two bare ISO strings, "2026-08-14T00:00:00.000Z" and "2026-08-25T00:00:00.000Z", with no festival name and no date formatting. The page also makes zero API calls, so all of this is computed client-side/statically.
  - customer_impact: Panchang is the daily-habit surface that brings devotees back and feeds the muhurat-driven booking intent the whole GTM plan rests on. A user who opens it to find an auspicious date is shown a four-month-stale calendar, an incorrect lunar month, and two unlabelled machine timestamps where festival names should be. It reads as abandoned software and undermines the credibility of the (genuinely good) muhurat engine sitting behind it.
  - fix: Initialise the calendar grid to the current month rather than a hardcoded/build-time April date; derive the masa label from the same panchang computation that already produces the correct tithi and nakshatra; and format the upcoming-festival entries through the festival lookup so they render a name and a human date.

### [DF-10] P2/trust/M /darshan shows statically-cached LIVE/Completed statuses and fabricated viewer counts
  - where: https://darshya.online/darshan and https://darshya.online/darshan/kashi-vishwanath
  - evidence: I loaded /darshan at ~23:30 IST and again just after 00:05 IST and got byte-identical statuses both times: the 3:00 AM Kashi Mangala Aarti marked "Completed", the 4:00 AM Mahakaleshwar Bhasma Aarti, 4:30 AM ISKCON Vrindavan and 5:00 AM Tirupati Suprabhatam all marked "LIVE", and 5:30 AM onward "Upcoming". That status set is only self-consistent at roughly 5:00 AM IST, i.e. it is frozen from build time. Separately /darshan/kashi-vishwanath renders "LIVE · 12,450 watching", "PEAK VIEWERS 24,000" and "QUALITY HD 1080p" — none of which come from YouTube; the embed exposes no viewer API to the page. (The underlying stream is genuinely live, which makes the invented numbers unnecessary.)
  - customer_impact: For most of the day the schedule tells devotees an aarti is happening now when it finished hours ago, or that a completed one is still to come — and someone who tunes in for a 4 AM Bhasma Aarti at lunchtime sees nothing. The "12,450 watching" figure is the same fabricated-social-proof pattern as DF-03 and is the number most likely to be screenshotted and disproved, since anyone can open the same YouTube stream and see the real count.
  - fix: Compute the LIVE/Completed/Upcoming status client-side from the current IST time (the per-temple page already does this correctly — it showed "Next: Mangala Aarti in 2h 36m"), or mark the route dynamic so it is not frozen at build. Remove the hardcoded watching/peak-viewer numbers, or read the real concurrent-viewer count from the YouTube Data API.

### [DF-11] P3/ux/S /temples renders "Showing 33 of 22 temples" and ships duplicate pages for the same temple under two slugs
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples
  - evidence: The list header literally reads "Showing 33 of 22 temples" — showing more than the total. The cause is visible in the links: the page is a hardcoded list (it makes zero API calls) that contains the same temple twice under different slugs — /temples/kashi-vishwanath and /temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi, /temples/rameswaram and /temples/rameshwaram, /temples/tirupati-balaji and /temples/tirumala-tirupati, /temples/meenakshi-temple and /temples/meenakshi-madurai. All of them return 200, so each temple has two live URLs. The API's canonical set is 20 slugs (kashi-vishwanath-varanasi, ramanathaswamy-rameshwaram, tirumala-tirupati, meenakshi-madurai …), none of which match the short forms the list links to.
  - customer_impact: A visitor sees a self-contradicting count on the main browse page, and can land on either of two near-identical pages for the same temple depending on which card they clicked. For SEO it is straight duplicate content competing with itself on high-value queries like "Kashi Vishwanath". It is also the root cause of DF-04's slug mismatch, since the list links slugs the booking API does not recognise.
  - fix: De-duplicate the temple list to one entry per temple keyed on the API slug, fix the count to render filtered-length of total-length, and 301 the legacy short slugs (kashi-vishwanath, rameswaram, tirupati-balaji, meenakshi-temple) to their canonical API-slug URLs.

### [DF-12] P3/broken/S An invalid astrologer id spins on "Loading…" forever with no error and no 404
  - where: https://darshya.online/astrology/view?id=<anything-invalid>
  - evidence: I loaded /astrology/view?id=zzz-not-a-real-astrologer-999 and polled it twice: main.innerText stays "Loading…" indefinitely and performance shows zero requests to api.darshya.online — so it never even attempts a lookup, it just never resolves. There is no error state, no empty state and no 404. Related routing oddity: /astrology/astrologers 307-redirects to /astrology/view?id=astrologers, treating the path segment "astrologers" as an astrologer id (which is how I found the fabricated profile in DF-03), and /astrology/astrologers/live — the live directory the corpus says shipped in PR #235 — returns 404.
  - customer_impact: Any stale link, mistyped id or crawler-followed URL parks the devotee on a permanent spinner with no way to understand what happened or where to go. It also means the astrologer directory has no reachable canonical URL, so the "Talk to an Astrologer" journey has a broken entry point.
  - fix: Give the astrologer detail route a real not-found state (render a 404 with a link back to /astrology) and a fetch timeout so it can never hang. Fix the /astrology/astrologers route so it renders the directory rather than redirecting into the id-based view, and restore /astrology/astrologers/live.

### [DF-13] P3/perf/S The highest-volume free-tool page (/astrology/kundli) ships with no page metadata, and ~16 pages carry a doubled "| Darshya | Darshya" title
  - where: https://darshya.online/astrology/kundli, /pujas/book, /checkout/offering; and /pujas/<slug>, /panchang, /astrology/*
  - evidence: I pulled <title> across the funnel. Every astrology tool has a well-crafted keyword title — e.g. "Free Kundli Milan Online — Ashtakoot 36-Point Guna Match Calculator | Darshya | Darshya", "Sade Sati Calculator — Am I in Sade Sati? Free Saturn Transit Check | Darshya | Darshya" — except /astrology/kundli, which falls back to the generic "Darshya — India's Devotional Super App", as do /pujas/book and /checkout/offering. Separately, 16 of the pages I checked (all astrology tools plus /pujas/rudrabhishek and /panchang) end in a duplicated "| Darshya | Darshya". Also worth noting: /pujas/book renders an empty <main> server-side (all content is client-only), so the money page has no indexable content at all.
  - customer_impact: "Free kundli" is one of the highest-volume devotional search terms in India and it is the product's best zero-cost acquisition asset — but its landing page presents Google with a generic app title and no description, so it competes far below its sibling calculators that are correctly tagged. The doubled suffix wastes ~10 characters of the ~60-character SERP budget on every other tool page and reads as sloppy in results.
  - fix: Add a generateMetadata/metadata export to /astrology/kundli matching the pattern its siblings already use (e.g. "Free Kundli Online — Janam Kundli Birth Chart with Lahiri Ayanamsa"). Fix the title template so the site name is appended once rather than twice. Consider server-rendering the puja summary on /pujas/book so the booking page is indexable.

## AREA: commerce-temple-gaushala (store, temple manager, gaushala/donations/80G, yatra, wellness marketplace)   score=39

### [COM-01] P0/broken/M Store checkout dead-ends — not one of the 24 catalogue products can actually be ordered
  - where: https://darshya.online/store → /store/checkout · apps/web/app/store/view/page.tsx:52-67 · apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:196-206 · apps/api/src/routes/store-orders.ts (POST /orders product lookup, ~L168-190) · apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma:963
  - evidence: Live: GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/store/products returns 24 products with ids `prod_015`, `prod_004`, `prod_003` … `prod_024` (hardcoded seed array in store.service.ts, mirrored in apps/web/lib/products.ts). The PDP builds the cart item with `id: product.id` (store/view/page.tsx:57) so the cart carries `prod_015`. Checkout POSTs `items:[{productId:'prod_015',…}]` to /store/orders. That handler resolves products with `prisma.storeProduct.findMany({ where:{ id:{ in: productIds } } })` — and `StoreProduct.id` is `String @id @default(uuid()) @db.Uuid`. A non-UUID string against a uuid column either raises Prisma P2023 (→500) or matches nothing (→400 `Unknown product: prod_015`). Either way the handler returns before creating an order. Separately, GET /api/v1/store/products/live (the only endpoint that serves real StoreProduct rows) returns `{"products":[],"total":0}` — so there are zero orderable rows in the database anyway. I did not place an order on production; this is the code path, verified end to end against the live catalogue ids.
  - customer_impact: A devotee browses a polished storefront, adds a ₹1,299 brass thali to the cart, fills in their delivery address, taps Place Order — and gets "Order failed (500)" or "Unknown product". Every single product, every single time. The store cannot earn one rupee.
  - fix: Two-line unblock plus a real fix. Unblock: seed the 24 catalogue SKUs as real StoreProduct rows under a Darshya house StoreProfile and change apps/web/lib/products.ts ids (and store.service.ts) to those UUIDs — or add a `handle` column to StoreProduct and resolve by handle in POST /orders. Real fix: retire the hardcoded catalogue, make /store and /store/view read GET /store/products/live, and give StoreProduct a slug/handle so the PDP route and the order lookup share one keyspace. Add an integration test that POSTs the exact payload the checkout page sends for a catalogue handle and asserts 201.

### [COM-02] P0/trust/M Every 80G receipt Darshya issues is legally invalid — it asserts Section 80G eligibility with no registration number and no donee PAN
  - where: apps/api/src/lib/pdf-receipt.ts:137-157 · apps/api/src/routes/donations.ts:110-170 · live copy on https://darshya.online/donations and /gaushala
  - evidence: pdf-receipt.ts prints "Eligible for deduction under Section 80G of the Income Tax Act, 1961." UNCONDITIONALLY (line 156), while the 80G registration number and donee PAN are both conditional (`if (data.gaushala80GNumber)` L137, `if (data.gaushalaPan)` L141). Those values come from `gaushala_profiles.regn_80g` / `.pan`, columns created by an `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` at boot (donations.ts:72-75). A repo-wide grep shows `regn_80g` is READ in exactly one place (donations.ts:126) and WRITTEN NOWHERE — no admin form, no portal field, no seed, no migration default. So both are NULL for every gaushala, and for temple donations the tax-field query is never even executed (`if (gaushalaProfile && donation.gaushala_id)`, L124). Live marketing copy on /donations reads "80G tax-deductible donations with instant PDF receipts", /gaushala reads "Tax Receipt (80G) — Save on income tax", and the donate form (verified live at /donations?causeKind=temple) reads "Secure payment via Razorpay · 80G tax-deductible · Receipt emailed instantly". This directly violates PROJECT_CONTEXT.md immutable business rule 7: "80G receipt requires donee 80G regn + PAN."
  - customer_impact: A donor gives ₹5,100 believing it is tax-deductible, receives a PDF headed "80G Income Tax Exemption" that says "valid without signature", claims the deduction, and has it disallowed — there is no donee registration number, no donee PAN, and (post-AY 2022-23) no Form 10BD/10BE filing behind it. That is a tax misrepresentation to the donor and a consumer-protection exposure for a sole proprietorship.
  - fix: Make the 80G assertion conditional on the data existing: only print the Section 80G line when BOTH `regn_80g` and donee PAN are present; otherwise print "Donation receipt — not valid for Section 80G deduction." Then either (a) remove every 80G claim from /donations, /gaushala, the cow pages and the plan cards until a registered 80G donee is actually onboarded, or (b) build the admin/portal field to capture regn_80g + PAN per gaushala AND per temple, extend the temple branch to read them, and commit to the Form 10BD/10BE filing obligation. Option (a) today, (b) before marketing 80G again.

### [COM-03] P0/money/S Temple donations are recorded as gaushala donations — the receipt names "Darshya Gaushala Network" and the temple never sees the money
  - where: apps/web/components/DonationForm.tsx:230-235 · apps/api/src/routes/donations.ts:244-250, 300-326 · apps/api/src/routes/temple-portal.ts:268 · live https://darshya.online/temples/siddhivinayak-mumbai → /donations?causeKind=temple
  - evidence: The API was correctly fixed for polymorphic donees (PBO-2): createOrderSchema accepts `causeKind:'temple'` + `templeId` and inserts into `donations.temple_id`. The WEB form was never updated. DonationForm.tsx:230-235 sends `{ gaushalaId: causeId, amountInr }` and nothing else, with a stale comment: "Backend expects `gaushalaId` — we use it as the opaque cause id for both gaushalas and temples". `causeKind` is read from the URL and used only in the Razorpay `notes` blob (L275), never in the request body. So every temple donation takes the gaushala branch, writes `gaushala_id = <temple id or slug>` and leaves `temple_id` NULL. Downstream in finalizeDonationReceipt: `isTempleDonation = !!donation.temple_id` is false → the temple lookup is skipped → `gaushalaProfile` lookup by that id fails (caught to null) → `doneeName` falls back to the literal string 'Darshya Gaushala Network'. Verified live that the temple pages emit these links: /temples/siddhivinayak-mumbai contains `href="/donations?causeId=siddhivinayak&causeKind=temple&causeName=Shree%20Siddhivinayak%20Temple"`, and /donations lists 6 temple causes the same way. Meanwhile GET /temple-portal/donations filters on `Donation.templeId`, which is NULL for all of them.
  - customer_impact: A devotee donates ₹2,100 to Siddhivinayak Temple and is emailed an 80G receipt naming "Darshya Gaushala Network" as the donee. The temple manager's portal shows ₹0 donations forever. Every temple-donation figure in the product and in the books is wrong at the donee level.
  - fix: In DonationForm.tsx, branch the create-order body on the `causeKind` prop it already receives: send `{ causeKind:'temple', templeId: causeId, … }` for temples and `{ causeKind:'gaushala', gaushalaId: causeId, … }` otherwise. Also fix the cause ids — the temple detail pages emit a short slug (`siddhivinayak`) while the API's temple branch requires `templeId: z.string().uuid()`, so pass the temple UUID (the /donations page already has `t.id`). Add a test asserting the temple path sends templeId and the gaushala path sends gaushalaId.

### [COM-04] P1/trust/S Donor PAN is demanded on the form for ≥₹2,000 and then silently discarded — it never reaches the receipt
  - where: apps/web/components/DonationForm.tsx:197 (validation), :230-235 (request body) · apps/api/src/routes/donations.ts:241 (donorPan accepted), :168 (donorPan rendered)
  - evidence: Live on https://darshya.online/donations the form renders the field "PAN Number (required for 80G receipt on ≥ ₹2,000)" and DonationForm.tsx:197 enforces it above ₹2,000. But the create-order body at L230-235 is exactly `{ gaushalaId, amountInr }` — `donorPan` and `dedication` are not sent. The API-side fix is present and correct (donations.ts accepts `donorPan` with a PAN regex, persists it, and passes `donorPan: donation.donor_pan` into the receipt at L168), so the server half of PBO-14 is done and the client half is not. Result: `donations.donor_pan` is NULL for every real donation and the receipt's donor-PAN line never renders.
  - customer_impact: The donor is forced to type their PAN — a sensitive identifier — under an explicit promise that it is needed for their 80G receipt, and it is thrown away in the browser. The receipt they receive has no PAN, which is one of the two reasons it cannot support a deduction (see COM-02).
  - fix: Add `donorPan: donorPan.toUpperCase() || undefined` and `dedication: dedication.slice(0,200) || undefined` to the create-order body in DonationForm.tsx. One-line change; the API already validates and persists both.

### [COM-05] P1/money/M Yatra checkout takes the price from the client — a ₹38,500 Char Dham package can be paid for with ₹500, unauthenticated
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/yatra-inquiry.ts:64 · POST https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/yatra-inquiry/create-order
  - evidence: `totalInr: z.number().int().min(500).max(2_000_000), // sanity bound; trust server price next iteration` — the amount charged is whatever the caller sends, bounded only by a ₹500 floor. The handler has no `preHandler: [authenticateUser]`; I confirmed live with an intentionally invalid body that it answers `400 {"error":"invalid_body", …}` rather than 401, so there is no auth gate in front of it (I did not send a valid body — that would create a Razorpay order and a DB row on production). The same is true of POST /yatra-inquiry/verify-payment. Package prices on the live catalogue run ₹24,500–₹72,500 per person.
  - customer_impact: Today the blast radius is limited because the web UI never calls /create-order (it posts to the inquiry endpoint instead), but the endpoint is live, public and unauthenticated. The moment yatra checkout is switched on — or if anyone finds it now — a ₹72,500 Jyotirlinga booking can be paid at ₹500 and will pass HMAC verification and flip to `confirmed`, because the signature is checked against an order created at the attacker's own amount.
  - fix: Derive the price server-side from `getYatraBySlug(yatraSlug).packages[tier].price × travelersCount` plus server-known add-ons, ignore any client `totalInr`, and reject on mismatch. Add `authenticateUser` (or at minimum a strict rate limit + email verification) to /create-order. This is the same class the corpus already tracks under #85 yatra commerce legal hardening.

### [COM-06] P1/money/M Cart shows delivery + 5% GST that is never charged — displayed total ≠ amount sent to Razorpay
  - where: apps/web/components/CartContext.tsx:133-136 · apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:532-565 · apps/api/src/routes/store-orders.ts:204-207, 232 · apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:736
  - evidence: Client math: `deliveryFee = subtotal >= 499 ? 0 : 49`, `tax = Math.round(subtotal * 0.05)`, `total = subtotal + deliveryFee + tax`. The checkout summary renders "Subtotal / Delivery / GST (est.) / Total" and the button literally says "Place Order - ₹{total}". The server recomputes `serverTotal += unitPrice * it.qty` with NO delivery fee and NO tax, persists `totalAmountInr: serverTotal`, and payment.service.ts:736 charges `Math.round(Number(order.totalAmountInr) * 100)` paise. So a ₹199 camphor order displays ₹258 (₹199 + ₹49 + ₹10) and charges ₹199. Separately, the 5% figure is the code's own "5% GST estimate" — it is a single flat rate applied to books (nil-rated), brass idols and malas alike, and per the corpus Darshya has no GSTIN yet.
  - customer_impact: The number on the Place Order button is not the number the customer is charged. Direction favours the customer, so the loss is the founder's: the ₹49 shipping fee on every sub-₹499 order is never collected and neither is the tax line — roughly 23% of gross on a small basket, plus the real courier cost. And a checkout that shows a "GST" line item while the business has no GSTIN is a compliance problem the moment anyone screenshots it.
  - fix: Pick one source of truth. Move delivery-fee and tax computation into POST /store/orders so `serverTotal` includes them, and have the checkout page render the server's returned breakdown rather than its own. Until a GSTIN exists, remove the GST line entirely and quote tax-inclusive prices (the Indian retail norm), keeping only a real, collected shipping line.

### [COM-07] P1/broken/M Every real gaushala on the gau-seva page 404s — the only genuine supply is unreachable
  - where: https://darshya.online/gaushala → /gaushala/<uuid> · GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/gaushala/<uuid>
  - evidence: /gaushala carries a strip headed "Verified gaushadas on Darshya — Approved gaushalas you can support directly. Each one is reviewed before being listed." with four real DB profiles: Pathmeda Gauseva Sansthan (Sanchore), Shri Krishna Gaushala (Mathura), Hinganghat Goseva Samiti (Wardha), and one more. Every card links to /gaushala/<uuid>. Verified live: https://darshya.online/gaushala/03edd2ff-4479-48f3-9ad1-6940e5558a4d → 404, /gaushala/f0dfee69-aaf3-4b90-a589-8a7df95cda0b → 404, and the API behind them returns 404 `{"error":"Gaushala not found."}` because GET /gaushala/:id resolves only the hardcoded `gs_001/2/3` fixtures. Meanwhile the three mock gaushala cards above them link "Explore Gaushala" to /gaushala/cow_001 and /gaushala/cow_005 — a single cow's page, not a gaushala.
  - customer_impact: A donor who wants to support an actual verified gaushala clicks the card and hits a 404. The only clickable gaushalas on the page are three fictional ones. Four real onboarded partners have no reachable page and no way to receive a donation.
  - fix: Make GET /api/v1/gaushala/:id resolve a real GaushalaProfile by UUID (fall back to the fixture ids for the seed set), and add the matching /gaushala/[id] web page. Point "Explore Gaushala" at the gaushala page rather than a cow page. Until the real profiles resolve, do not render a strip that says "you can support directly".

### [COM-08] P1/content/S Yatra sells a lead form as "Razorpay secure checkout" — the indexed booking page never takes payment
  - where: apps/web/app/yatra/[slug]/book/page.tsx:23 (metadata) · apps/web/app/yatra/book/page.tsx:171-212 · live https://darshya.online/yatra/char-dham/book
  - evidence: The live meta description on /yatra/char-dham/book reads: "Book Char Dham Yatra: 11 days, all-inclusive package starting from ₹38,500. Verified pandits, hotel + transport handled, Razorpay secure checkout." That page 307-redirects to /yatra/book?slug=char-dham, whose only network call is `fetch(`${API_BASE}/api/v1/yatra-inquiry/`)` — the inquiry endpoint. The submit button reads "Request booking · ₹X". No Razorpay script is loaded anywhere in the yatra flow; /yatra-inquiry/create-order and /verify-payment exist on the API but have zero callers in apps/web. Also note the page still computes `totalUsd = Math.round((totalInr / 83) * 100) / 100` (line 173) and posts it — a leftover FX field after the Stripe/USD removal.
  - customer_impact: A pilgrim arrives on an SEO landing page promising secure checkout for a ₹38,500 trip, fills a multi-step form with traveller names and ages, and gets a callback promise instead. That is the highest-intent moment in the funnel spent on a lead capture, and the SERP snippet is a false claim.
  - fix: Either wire /yatra/book to /yatra-inquiry/create-order + Razorpay (after fixing COM-05's client-supplied price), or change the metadata and the page copy to say plainly "Request a booking — our yatra desk confirms availability within 24h" and drop "Razorpay secure checkout". Remove the dead `totalUsd` field.

### [COM-09] P1/ux/S Store buyers have nowhere to see their orders — /store/orders renders "this product does not exist"
  - where: https://darshya.online/store/orders · apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx (success screen "View My Orders" → /bookings) · apps/web/app/store/[handle]/page.tsx
  - evidence: GET /api/v1/store/orders is called from exactly two places in apps/web, both under /store-admin (the OWNER's view). No devotee-facing page fetches it. The checkout success screen's "View My Orders" CTA points at /bookings, and a grep of apps/web/app/bookings for storeOrder/orderNumber/ORD- returns no matches. /profile/orders → 404, /orders → 404, and https://darshya.online/store/orders 307-redirects into the catch-all product route (/store/view?handle=orders) which renders "The product you are looking for does not exist."
  - customer_impact: After paying, the buyer gets an order number on screen and then can never look it up again — no status, no tracking number, no invoice, no way to check whether the parcel shipped. The store-admin side has a full status machine and a tracking-number field whose output the customer cannot see.
  - fix: Add /profile/orders (or a Store Orders tab on /bookings) backed by the existing GET /store/orders, rendering orderNumber, items, total, status and trackingNumber. Point the success-screen CTA there. Also make /store/[handle] 404 properly instead of rendering a soft "product does not exist" body for arbitrary paths.

### [COM-10] P1/perf/M Product detail pages have no server-rendered content — zero product SEO for the entire store
  - where: https://darshya.online/store/view?handle=bhagavad-gita-deluxe-edition · apps/web/app/store/view/page.tsx · apps/web/app/store/[handle]/page.tsx
  - evidence: Every /store/<handle> URL 307-redirects to /store/view?handle=<handle> (verified: `location: /store/view?handle=bhagavad-gita-deluxe-edition`). Stripping scripts and tags from the delivered HTML of that page leaves only global nav, the word "Loading…", and the footer — no title, no price, no description, no availability. All product content is client-rendered. By contrast /store (the list) and /temples/<slug> ARE server-rendered with full content.
  - customer_impact: None of the 24 product pages can rank or produce a Product rich result — no price, no availability, no review markup in the HTML. Every product URL is also a redirect rather than a canonical 200, which dilutes it further. For a $0-budget business whose whole devotee-CAC thesis is organic reach, this silently zeroes out the store's share of it.
  - fix: Server-render the PDP: make /store/[handle] the real page (async server component reading the catalogue directly, with generateStaticParams over the 24 handles) instead of a redirect shim, and add Product JSON-LD with price/availability/aggregateRating. Keep /store/view only as a legacy alias.

### [COM-11] P1/missing-feature/L Store-owner products are invisible on the storefront — the supply side of the marketplace has no shelf
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/store.ts:12-44 (GET /products/live) · apps/api/src/routes/store-admin.ts:86 (POST /products) · apps/web (zero callers)
  - evidence: A store owner can create products (POST /store-admin/products, live and 401-gated). GET /api/v1/store/products/live exists to surface them and its own header comment says "store-owner products (store_products table) never surface publicly". A repo-wide grep for `products/live` across apps/web returns zero results — nothing in the web app ever calls it. Live it returns `{"products":[],"total":0}`, so there are also zero owner products to show. The public /store renders only the 24 hardcoded fixtures.
  - customer_impact: A store owner who onboards, does the KYC and lists inventory sees their products on no public page and can never receive an order. Combined with COM-01 this is the full picture: the products customers can see cannot be bought, and the products that can be bought cannot be seen.
  - fix: Merge the two catalogues behind one endpoint and one keyspace (see COM-01), have /store fetch it, and give StoreProduct a handle so its PDP route works. This is Phase-2 item 3 in the existing plan — COM-01 makes it urgent rather than nice-to-have.

### [COM-12] P2/ux/S 15 of 20 temples advertise "Book Puja via Darshya" with zero bookable offerings behind the CTA
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples/<slug> → /pujas?temple=<short-slug> · GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/temples/<slug>/pujas
  - evidence: I queried all 20 temple slugs. Exactly 5 return offerings (tirumala-tirupati, jagannath-puri, kashi-vishwanath-varanasi, somnath-gujarat, siddhivinayak-mumbai — 5 each); the other 15 return `{"pujas":[]}` — akshardham-delhi, badrinath-uttarakhand, dagdusheth-pune, meenakshi-madurai, shirdi-sai-baba, vaishno-devi-katra, dakshineswar-kolkata, dwarkadhish-dwarka, govind-dev-ji-jaipur, iskcon-bangalore, ramanathaswamy-rameshwaram, akshardham-gandhinagar, kapaleeshwarar-chennai, lingaraj-bhubaneswar, birla-mandir-hyderabad. Both /temples/siddhivinayak-mumbai and /temples/vaishno-devi-katra render an identical "Book Puja via Darshya — Verified priests, video proof, prasad delivery to your door" card. Also note the CTA href uses a truncated slug (`/pujas?temple=vaishno-devi` for temple `vaishno-devi-katra`), so even the filter key does not match the temple record.
  - customer_impact: Three quarters of temple pages make an explicit booking promise and then hand the devotee an empty or unfiltered list. Vaishno Devi and Badrinath are among the highest-intent temple queries in India — this is the traffic most likely to convert, landing on a promise with nothing behind it.
  - fix: Render the "Book Puja via Darshya" card only when GET /temples/<slug>/pujas is non-empty; show "Puja booking coming soon at this temple — notify me" otherwise. Fix the CTA to pass the full temple slug. Then seed offerings for the remaining 15 temples (the tirumala set is a usable template).

  - where: https://darshya.online/store · apps/web/app/store/page.tsx:221-225

Offer valid till April 30." with a "Shop Sale" button. Hardcoded string, no date logic. Today is 12 August 2026 — the offer expired 3.5 months ago and the festival it names is a spring festival.
  - customer_impact: The store's hero banner advertises a dead offer with a dead deadline. To a first-time visitor this reads as an abandoned site, which undercuts every trust badge sitting right next to it ("Temple Blessed", "Easy Returns", "Secure Checkout").
  - fix: Delete the hardcoded banner, or drive it from a festival/promo config with a start and end date so it self-retires. If a promo banner is wanted year-round, tie it to the existing festival calendar the panchang layer already computes.

### [COM-14] P2/content/S Wellness "Live Sessions — Upcoming this week" is fabricated, four months stale, and the Book button does not book
  - where: https://darshya.online/wellness · apps/web/app/wellness/page.tsx:65-70, 276-316 · GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/wellness/sessions
  - evidence: The live page renders "Live Sessions — Upcoming this week" with six cards dated "Apr 18 6:00 AM", "Apr 18 5:30 AM", "Apr 19", "Apr 20", "Apr 21" and spot counts "8 spots left", "3 spots left", "20 spots left". These are literals in page.tsx:65-70 (`date: 'Apr 18'`, `spotsLeft: 8`). The real endpoint GET /api/v1/wellness/sessions returns `{"data":[]}`. The "Book →" affordance is a `<span>` inside a `<Link href={`/wellness/${session.category}`}>` (L280-282, L315) — clicking a specific dated session navigates to the generic /wellness/yoga category page. Six instructors above it (Aarav Sharma 4.9/12yrs/₹599, Swami Dhyanananda 4.9/20yrs/₹799, Acharya Goenkaji 4.9/25yrs/₹999 …) are likewise fixtures.
  - customer_impact: A visitor sees six scarcity-framed live classes with real times and prices, taps Book on the one with "3 spots left", and lands on a marketing category page. The dates are from April. Nothing here is bookable and nothing here is real.
  - fix: Render the Live Sessions block from GET /wellness/sessions and hide it entirely when the list is empty (it is). Remove the hardcoded `upcomingSessions` array and the fabricated instructor fixtures, or label them unmistakably as examples. If the block stays, make each card link to that instructor's page rather than a category.

### [COM-15] P2/trust/S POST /wellness/book is unauthenticated, takes no payment, and mutates shared in-memory seat inventory
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/wellness.ts:463-506 · POST https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/wellness/book
  - evidence: `server.post('/book', async (request, reply) => {...})` — no `preHandler`. Verified live: POST with an empty body returns 400 (`sessionId is required`), not 401, confirming there is no auth gate. The handler then does `session.spotsLeft -= 1` on a module-level array, pushes to an in-memory `bookings` array, and returns `{ message: 'Session booked successfully.' }` with `userId: userId || 'guest'` — no payment, no persistence, no idempotency. I did not send a valid body: doing so would decrement production inventory. Confirmed by reading the handler, not by exercising it.
  - customer_impact: Anyone on the internet can drive every session's "spots left" to zero with a loop, at which point the public wellness page shows a fully booked schedule and the endpoint returns "Session is fully booked" to real users. It also returns a confirmed booking for a ₹599 class with no payment and no account. State resets whenever the Render dyno sleeps, which is its own inconsistency.
  - fix: Add `authenticateUser`, a rate limit, and a payment gate — or, since the wellness catalogue is fixtures and GET /wellness/sessions is empty, delete the endpoint until the real wellness-sessions path (which does persist and does charge) is the only one exposed.

### [COM-16] P2/trust/L "100% to cause" is printed on the donate form but no code ever moves the money to the cause
  - where: apps/web/components/DonationForm.tsx:584 · apps/api/src/routes/donations.ts (no commission/payout writer) · apps/api/src/routes/gaushala-portal.ts:19-108
  - evidence: The live donate form (verified at /donations?causeKind=temple) shows three badges: "Razorpay secured · 80G receipt · 100% to cause". Donation funds land in Darshya's own Razorpay account. Grepping donations.ts for commission/payout returns nothing — no Commission row, no TemplePayout, no PayoutRequest is written on any donation path, so donations never enter the unified payout rail that pays every other provider role. gaushala-portal/donations and temple-portal/donations only LIST donations; there is no disbursement code anywhere. Compounding it, gaushala donations are keyed on the fixture id `gs_001` while the portal query filters by the owner's User UUID, so even the listing will never match a real gaushala owner.
  - customer_impact: The platform makes an explicit financial promise — 100% of your donation reaches the cause — that no mechanism in the product can keep or evidence. Combined with the invalid 80G receipt (COM-02) and the misrouted donee (COM-03), the donation product asks for money on three claims it cannot substantiate. For a consumer-facing charitable collection this is the highest legal-exposure surface in the codebase.
  - fix: Remove the "100% to cause" badge until a settlement path exists. Then build donation settlement the same way the marketplace was built: write a payout record per donation keyed on the real donee (temple or gaushala User id), surface it in the respective portal, and disburse through the same PayoutRequest lifecycle. If the founder intends to keep a platform fee on donations, say so on the form instead.

### [COM-17] P2/ux/S Yatra tier selection is silently dropped on the redirect — the premium buyer lands on standard
  - where: apps/web/app/yatra/[slug]/book/page.tsx:31 · live https://darshya.online/yatra/char-dham/book?tier=premium
  - evidence: /yatra/char-dham renders three tier CTAs: /yatra/char-dham/book?tier=economy | standard | premium. Verified live: requesting ?tier=premium returns `307 → location: /yatra/book?slug=char-dham` — the query param is not forwarded (`redirect(`/yatra/book?slug=${slug}`)`). The destination page DOES read it (`searchParams.get('tier') … || 'standard'`, book/page.tsx:67), so the intent was there; the redirect just loses it and every visitor defaults to standard.
  - customer_impact: A pilgrim who deliberately clicks the premium tier on a ₹38,500 package silently lands on the standard tier with a lower price and different inclusions. Anyone who does not notice the tier chips further down submits the wrong package — and the highest-value tier is the one the funnel quietly discards.
  - fix: Forward the param: `redirect(`/yatra/book?slug=${slug}${tier ? `&tier=${tier}` : ''}`)`, reading tier from the page's own searchParams.

### [COM-18] P2/perf/S Store product images are full-resolution Wikimedia hotlinks — 5.7 MB downloaded for a 600px card
  - where: https://darshya.online/store · apps/api/src/services/store.service.ts (image urls) · apps/web/lib/products.ts
  - evidence: Measured live with a browser UA and a darshya.online referer: Rudraksha_mala.jpg = 5,728,036 B (200), Bhagavad_Gita_book_(small)_01.jpg = 5,038,680 B, Pure_Ghee-Homemade-Maharashtra.jpg = 4,044,816 B, Hindu_pooja_thali.jpg = 485,959 B. All resolve (no 404/429 today) but they are served at original resolution into 600×600 cards. The Unsplash URLs are correctly sized (~44–66 KB), so it is specifically the Wikimedia set. The /store HTML alone is 349 KB before images.
  - customer_impact: The store page pulls tens of megabytes on a 4G phone in India — the exact device and network the target buyer is on. It is also fragile: these are third-party hotlinks with no CDN contract, and Wikimedia can rate-limit or move a file at any time, at which point the product card goes blank.
  - fix: Use the Wikimedia thumbnail form (`/thumb/<path>/600px-<file>`) as an immediate one-line win, then self-host the 24 product images under /public and serve them through next/image. The self-host route is now viable — the public/ nesting bug that previously blocked it is fixed.

### [COM-19] P3/broken/S POST /gaushala/adopt lets any signed-in user mark a cow adopted for free, in shared process memory
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/gaushala.ts:64-84 · apps/api/src/services/gaushala.service.ts:578, 649-704
  - evidence: The handler calls `gaushalaService.adoptCow(userId, cowId, planId)`, which sets `cow.isAdopted = true` on a module-level `COWS` array and pushes to a `Map` keyed by userId — no payment, no DB write, no plan charge, and the mutation is visible to every other visitor because the fixture array is shared process state. It returns "Cow adopted successfully! You will receive monthly updates." Verified live that the endpoint exists and is auth-gated (401 without a header); I did not exercise it. The web app no longer calls it (the #230 fix routes adoption through /donations), so it is an orphaned endpoint.
  - customer_impact: A signed-in user can walk all eight cows to adopted for ₹0, after which the public /gaushala page shows "0 Available to Adopt" for everyone until the dyno restarts. Anyone who does hit it is told they have adopted a cow and will receive monthly updates, which nothing will ever send.
  - fix: Delete POST /gaushala/adopt and GET /my-adoptions now that the real adoption path is /donations with planId prefill — or gate adoption on a completed donation and persist it. Leaving a free, state-mutating "adopt" endpoint next to a paid donation flow is the risk.

### [PM-01] P0/money/S Every puja detail page shows the INR NRI price with a dollar sign — "$13,999" for a ₹1,501 puja
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas/<any-slug> (all 10 live); apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:336 and :536
  - evidence: Live PDP /pujas/satyanarayan-katha renders 'Starting from ₹1,501' next to 'NRI Price: $13,999'. Confirmed identically on /pujas/rudrabhishek (₹1,100 / $9,999), /pujas/ram-charit-manas-path (₹3,501 / $31,999), /pujas/hanuman-chalisa-path (₹451 / $3,999). The delivered RSC payload pairs a lucide-indian-rupee icon with the base price and a lucide-dollar-sign icon with nriPrice, so the currency contrast is deliberate in the markup. Source: `<span>NRI Price: ${Number(puja.nriPrice).toLocaleString('en-US')}</span>`. The API field is an INR figure (nriPrice 13999 = 9.33x the ₹1,501 base, matching the documented 9-10x rule). `git show origin/main` confirms both lines are committed; `git diff origin/main` on that file is empty, so live equals this code and it is not the in-flight fix pass.
  - customer_impact: A diaspora devotee — the highest-ARPU cohort in the category, where Sri Mandir earns ~₹7,000/yr per international user against ₹600-800 in India — is quoted $31,999 (≈₹27.5 lakh at ~₹86/USD) for a Ram Charit Manas Path the platform actually charges ₹3,501 for. Nobody converts; they conclude the site is broken or fraudulent and leave.
  - fix: Render nriPrice as INR with the rupee glyph, exactly like basePrice: replace the `$` prefix and lucide-dollar-sign at apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:336 and :536 with ₹ / lucide-indian-rupee. Then make a separate call on whether to show it at all — since /from-offering charges basePrice to everyone, an 'NRI Price ₹13,999' line advertises a premium that is never collected. Recommend hiding the line entirely until a server-quoted NRI amount exists, and deleting the now-dead `nriUsdPrice` memo at apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:604.

### [PM-02] P0/money/M The /pricing commission schedule is fiction — no code applies it, and it contradicts the API's own prices 2x
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing; apps/web/lib/pricing.ts:43-164; vs https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/subscriptions/plans
  - evidence: Live /pricing advertises Free=30% / Basic ₹499=20% / Pro ₹1,499=15% / Enterprise ₹4,999=10% commission, plus a per-role matrix (Astrologers 35/25/18/12, Store 25/15/12/8, Temples 15/10/8/5, Gaushalas 10/5/4/3). Live GET /api/v1/subscriptions/plans returns a completely different list for the same product: Pandit Basic ₹999, Pandit Pro ₹2,999, Astrologer Basic ₹799 / Pro ₹1,999, Store Basic ₹1,499 / Pro ₹3,999, Wellness Basic ₹699, Temple Pro ₹4,999 — and no Enterprise tier exists at all. apps/web/lib/pricing.ts is a static marketing table (dated Jun 15) with no API binding. Critically, `grep -rn 'commissionOverride|planCommission|lowerCommission|discountedCommission' apps/api/src` returns ZERO hits — no server code anywhere reduces commission based on subscription tier.
  - customer_impact: A pandit evaluating Darshya reads '₹499/month drops you to 20% commission', signs up, and finds the product asks ₹999 — then discovers that paying it changes their commission by nothing. For a supply-side audience the GTM plan describes as 40-65 and app-skeptical, being quoted two different prices for one plan on the founder's own site is a trust-ending first impression.
  - fix: Pick one source of truth. Delete the hardcoded PLANS/COMMISSION_RATES in apps/web/lib/pricing.ts and have /pricing, /settings/subscription and components/CommissionBanner.tsx fetch GET /api/v1/subscriptions/plans. Then either (a) implement the tier→commission override in the API so the table becomes true, or (b) remove every commission-rate column and the CommissionBanner 'upgrade to reduce commission' copy until it is. Do not ship the table again until a server test asserts the advertised rate is the rate charged.

### [PM-03] P0/money/M The live default platform take is 30%, not the 20% the whole business is built on
  - where: apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma:328 (on origin/main); apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:461-479, 71; apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:536-538
  - evidence: `git show origin/main:apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma` line 328: `commissionRate Float @default(0.70) @map("commission_rate")`. resolvePriestShare (bookings.ts:536-538) and booking.service.ts:478 both accept any stored value in (0,1] as authoritative and only fall back to DEFAULT_PRIEST_SHARE=0.8 outside that range — 0.70 is inside it, so the 0.8 constant never fires for a normally-onboarded pandit. No writer sets 0.80 and no migration changed the default. Net: a self-onboarded pandit is paid 70%, and the Commission row minted at confirm records percentage = 0.3000. The corpus records 'uniform 20% platform / 80% provider across EVERY marketplace role' as a settled decision; the shipped schema disagrees.
  - customer_impact: A pandit on a ₹10,000 booking receives ₹7,000, not the ₹8,000 the supply pitch and the take-rate doctrine promise — a ₹1,000 shortfall per booking, silently, on every pandit onboarded through the normal flow. At 30% the platform is also the most expensive option in the category for supply (Sri Mandir 20-25%, UC 20-25%, Utsav ~20%), which is the opposite of the stated 'defensible supply-winning floor' positioning.
  - fix: Owner decides the number first (see PM-08 — 30%, 20% and 15-18% are all currently 'the' rate). Then set it in one place: change the Prisma default to the chosen share and ship an additive `ALTER TABLE priests ALTER COLUMN commission_rate SET DEFAULT 0.8` in its own migration, with no backfill of existing rows unless the owner wants one. Do not implement the alternative of treating a stored 0.70 as 'unset' — that would silently override any pandit genuinely negotiated onto 70%.

### [PM-04] P1/money/M Any logged-in user can provision a paid subscription for ₹0 — no payment step exists
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/subscriptions.ts:37-57 → apps/api/src/services/subscription.service.ts:256-295
  - evidence: POST /subscriptions/subscribe validates the body, then calls subscriptionService.subscribe(userId, planId, role), which looks up the plan, cancels any prior ACTIVE sub, and does `prisma.marketplaceSubscription.create({ data: { ..., amount: plan.price, status: 'ACTIVE' } })`. There is no Razorpay order creation, no payment verification, no signature check and no paid-status gate anywhere between the request and the ACTIVE row — the price is recorded as revenue but never collected. The entitlement is real, not cosmetic: apps/api/src/lib/ai-usage.ts:79 reads marketplaceSubscription to grant AI Pandit quota, and apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:369/467 counts ACTIVE subscriptions into admin reporting. Auth wall verified live: unauthenticated POST → 401.
  - customer_impact: Any signed-in devotee can grant themselves 'Astro Monthly Unlimited' (₹499/mo, unlimited AI Pandit chat) or a provider can take 'Pandit Pro' (₹2,999/mo) for free, and admin revenue dashboards will report those amounts as earned. The subscription line — one of the few non-transactional revenue streams — cannot collect a rupee today.
  - fix: Gate subscribe() behind a verified payment: create a Razorpay order for plan.price, return it to the client, and only write the ACTIVE MarketplaceSubscription from the verified payment/webhook handler (the same pattern payments.ts already uses). Short of that, make POST /subscribe reject any plan with price > 0 so the free tiers still work and the paid ones fail loudly instead of granting silently.

### [PM-05] P1/content/M /uk quotes a fabricated rupee column and a 9-10x NRI premium the platform does not charge
  - where: https://darshya.online/uk (HTTP 200), 'Sample pujas in £' section
  - evidence: Live table: Rudrabhishek ₹2,500 / £240 (live catalog: ₹1,100), Lakshmi Puja ₹2,100 / £200 (live: ₹901), Satyanarayan ₹1,500 / £145 (live: ₹1,501), Mahamrityunjaya ₹5,500 / £525, Shraadh ₹3,100 / £295. Three of five rupee figures do not match the API catalog. Supporting copy: 'Final price at checkout reflects live FX and temple-specific fees' and 'NRI pricing is 9-10x domestic — covers temple operations + UK payment processing + USD/GBP infrastructure + a small dharmic donation.' None of that is true today: there is no FX at checkout (INR only), no temple-specific fee, no USD/GBP infrastructure (Stripe was deleted), and the documented decision is that the 9x multiplier is deliberately NOT applied server-side — /from-offering charges basePrice to everyone.
  - customer_impact: A London devotee is quoted £240 for a Rudrabhishek the platform will actually charge ₹1,100 (≈£10.50) for — a 23x overstatement. Either they bounce at the quoted price, or they proceed and the checkout contradicts the landing page. Both outcomes destroy the diaspora funnel, and the page is the dedicated acquisition surface for that segment.
  - fix: Regenerate the ₹ column from the live catalog rather than a hardcoded table, and set the £ column to a straight FX conversion of the amount actually charged (₹1,100 ≈ £10.50 at the stated £1≈₹105), not a 10x figure. Delete 'live FX and temple-specific fees' and the 'USD/GBP infrastructure' justification. If the 9-10x NRI premium is genuinely coming back, it must return as a server-quoted amount rendered before payment — not as marketing copy ahead of the code.

### [PM-06] P1/trust/S /pricing publicly promises weekly payouts landing in a bank account in 2-3 days; no payout rail exists
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing, FAQ 'When do I get paid?'; apps/web/app/pricing/page.tsx:44-46
  - evidence: Live copy: 'Payouts are processed every Monday for the previous week's completed orders. Funds typically reach your bank account within 2-3 business days after processing.' The underlying rail does not exist — the corpus records that Razorpay Route and RazorpayX both return 404 (not enabled on the account), admin 'Pay Now' is manual with no money movement, and penny-drop bank verification and PAN capture are missing. The page states this as current operating fact, with no 'coming soon' qualifier anywhere.
  - customer_impact: This is the single most load-bearing promise to a pandit deciding whether to join, and the platform cannot keep it on day one. The GTM plan's own risk register names under-liquid supply churn as the #1 category failure mode and notes that a wronged pandit poisons an entire temple network — a missed first payout after a written 2-3 day guarantee is exactly how that starts.
  - fix: Until the rail is live, change the answer to describe what actually happens: earnings accrue to a withdrawable balance, the provider raises a payout request, and the founder settles it manually — with an honest current turnaround. Restore the weekly-Monday/T+2 language on the day RazorpayX or Cashfree is approved and the settlement cron is running. The rail itself is a known-open owner gate; the public promise built on top of it is the part that needs changing today.

What is sellable is a ₹451-3,501 remote-puja catalog that promises included samagri it has no way to charge for, which inverts the margin on the low-priced SKUs.
  - fix: Add a tier concept to PujaType (or a PujaOffering variant table) with pandit-only / +kit / premium rows and per-tier price and payout, then seed Griha Pravesh and re-seed Satyanarayan onto the price book's numbers.
