  - evidence: No GST line appears on /pricing, in the static plan table, or in the booking total (which is exactly basePrice + ₹51 + optional ₹150). The subscription plans returned by the API are bare INR figures with no tax field. By contrast Sri Mandir's published partner economics net 18% GST off the top before computing the share (₹1,000 puja → ₹900 after discount → ₹763.56 net of GST → ₹381.78 to the partner). The corpus records GST registration as due within ~30 days of crossing the ₹20L services threshold, and TDS treatment (194-O 0.1% vs 194J 10%) as an unresolved CA question.
  - customer_impact: Not yet customer-visible, but it silently overstates every margin in the plan. On a ₹1,501 booking at a 20% take the commission is ₹300 and GST on it is ₹54 — 18% of gross margin that is nowhere in the model. If the puja service itself is held taxable rather than just the commission, either ₹1,501 becomes ₹1,771 at checkout or the margin absorbs it. Repricing after launch is precisely the 'teaser-then-raise' move the GTM plan forbids.
  - fix: Resolve the CA question (is the platform an aggregator collecting commission, or the supplier of the service?) before publishing any price as permanent. Then add an explicit tax field to the plan and puja price models and a GST line to the booking breakdown, so the number the founder sees as margin is post-tax. Decide TDS on provider payouts in the same conversation.

### [PM-10] P3/ux/S Astrologer per-minute rates render with no currency symbol at all
  - where: https://darshya.online/astrology, 'Verified astrologers on Darshya' strip

  - fix: Prefix the rate with ₹ in the astrologer card component, matching the rupee glyph already used on puja cards.

### [PM-11] P2/broken/S Both 'live directory' pages shipped in #235 are 404 on the web while their API returns 200

This is the same root-cause family as the #202 temple sitemap 404s.

## AREA: pricing-market   score=38

### [PM-01] P0/money/M Paid plans sell a commission discount that is implemented nowhere — ₹4,999/mo buys nothing
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing ; apps/web/lib/pricing.ts:31,51,73,95,117 ; apps/web/app/pricing/page.tsx:300
  - evidence: Live /pricing headline: 'Higher plans unlock lower commissions' plus a per-role rate table (Pandits 30%→20%→15%→10%). The number rendered is `plan.commissionOverride` from apps/web/lib/pricing.ts, a WEB-ONLY constant. Grep for `commissionOverride|commission_override` across apps/api returns NO MATCHES. No commission calculation anywhere reads `subscriptionTier` (it appears only in auth payloads, admin listings and test fixtures). The actual rates are fixed constants: storeProfile.commission 0.20, WELLNESS_PLATFORM_COMMISSION 0.20, Priest.commissionRate from the priest row.
  - customer_impact: A provider pays ₹1,499 or ₹4,999 every month for a lower commission rate and is charged exactly the same rate as a free user — a recurring charge for a benefit that does not exist, which is the definition of a deceptive trade practice under CPA-2019.
  - fix: Shortest honest fix: delete the commission column from the /pricing plan cards, the '% commission rate' line, the 'Higher plans unlock lower commissions' subhead and the per-plan commission features, leaving the flat published rate. Proper fix: resolve the effective rate through apps/api/src/lib/commission-math.ts from the provider's active subscription tier, and have every one of the five Commission writers call it. Do not ship the claim again until the second version exists.

### [PM-02] P0/money/S Advertised plan price is up to 2x lower than the price actually sold at checkout
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing vs GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/subscriptions/plans (200) ; CTA target apps/web/app/pricing/page.tsx:332 → /settings/subscription
  - evidence: /pricing publishes ONE universal ladder: Free · Basic ₹499/mo · Pro ₹1,499/mo · Enterprise ₹4,999/mo. The live API returns 16 role-specific plans at different prices: Pandit Basic ₹999 / Pandit Pro ₹2,999; Astrologer Basic ₹799 / Pro ₹1,999; Store Basic ₹1,499 / Store Pro ₹3,999; Instructor Basic ₹699. No ENTERPRISE plan exists for pandit, astrologer, store or wellness at all — the only ₹4,999 plan is 'Temple Pro'. The paid-plan CTA points at /settings/subscription (200), which renders the API plans via SubscriptionPanel.tsx.
  - customer_impact: A pandit reads 'Basic ₹499/month', clicks Start Free Trial, and is presented with 'Pandit Basic ₹999/month' — double. An Enterprise buyer is offered no such plan at all. Classic bait pricing; it will generate refund demands and destroy first-transaction trust with the exact supply-side users the business needs most.
  - fix: Make /pricing render from GET /api/v1/subscriptions/plans instead of the hardcoded apps/web/lib/pricing.ts PLANS array, so one source of truth drives both surfaces. Also remove 'Start Free Trial' — no trial exists in any plan object; use 'Subscribe'.

### [PM-03] P1/money/M Advertised commission rate differs from the charged rate for most roles — including one role over-charged
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing commission table ; apps/api/src/services/store-commission.service.ts:13,56-58 ; apps/api/src/routes/wellness-sessions.ts:81 ; apps/api/src/services/temple-payout.service.ts:31
  - evidence: Live /pricing free-tier column vs deployed code — Store Owners: 25% advertised, 0.20 charged. Wellness: 30% advertised, 0.20 charged. Astrologers: 35% advertised, 20% charged (rate cut in PR #227; consultation.ts:445 applies ASTROLOGER_PLATFORM_RATE — I could not read the literal before the grep timed out, so this one is corpus-plus-usage verified rather than literal-verified). Temples: 15% advertised, but the code takes a 20% platform cut PLUS a separate 5% TEMPLE_SHARE_RATE (DEFAULT_TEMPLE_SHARE_RATE = 0.05).
  - customer_impact: Three roles are under-charged, which is silent revenue leakage of 5–15 percentage points on every transaction. Temples are the dangerous direction: a temple manager signs up on a published 15% and is actually charged 20%+5% — a real over-charge against a published rate, and temples are precisely the partner class whose goodwill the whole content moat depends on.
  - fix: Pick the real number per role, put it in one server-side table, and render /pricing from it. Until then correct the six published free-tier rates to match code: pandit 30 (see PM-07), store 20, astrologer 20, wellness 20, temple 20 + 5% temple share stated separately, gaushala verified before publishing.

Every diaspora visitor — the highest-ARPU segment in this category, worth ~₹7,000/yr vs ₹600-800 domestic at Sri Mandir — bounces or reports the site as a scam. Pure demand destruction on the most valuable cohort.
  - fix: Delete both NRI price renders from apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx (lines 336 and 536). The nriPrice premium is not charged, so there is nothing to display; showing it in any currency is wrong. Revisit only when a server-QUOTED NRI amount exists per the recorded decision.

### [PM-05] P1/trust/S NRI Premium ₹5,000/year sells at least two benefits that do not exist
  - where: GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/subscriptions/plans (200) — plan 'NRI Premium', ENTERPRISE, DEVOTEE, INR 5000/year ; apps/api/src/services/subscription.service.ts:218-225
  - evidence: Live plan features: 'No ads anywhere on Darshya · No NRI surcharge on bookings · Priority booking 7 days early · Weekly 1:1 call with priest (Jitsi) · Free 80G receipt PDF for any donation · Unlimited AI astro chat'. There is no NRI surcharge to remove — booking.service.ts:414-454 documents that the isNri price swap was deliberately removed and charges basePrice to everyone. Darshya serves no advertising anywhere, so 'No ads' removes nothing either.
  - customer_impact: The single highest-value SKU in the product (₹5,000/yr, aimed at the highest-ARPU cohort) leads with two benefits that are worth zero. Any NRI who paid and then read the booking page would have a straightforward refund claim, and the SKU cannot be marketed honestly as written.
  - fix: Rewrite the NRI Premium feature list to only the four deliverable items, and verify each before publishing (especially 'Weekly 1:1 call with priest (Jitsi)' — confirm a Jitsi room and a scheduling path actually exist). If fewer than three real benefits survive, unpublish the plan rather than reprice it.

### [PM-06] P1/trust/S /pricing publishes a bank-payout SLA the platform has no rail to honour
  - where: https://darshya.online/pricing — FAQ 'When do I get paid?'
  - evidence: Live copy, verbatim: 'Payouts are processed every Monday for the previous week's completed orders. Funds typically reach your bank account within 2-3 business days after processing.' Both Razorpay Route and RazorpayX return 404 (not enabled on the account, per corpus), admin 'Pay Now' performs no money movement, and the weekly settlement cron is still an unbuilt Phase-2 item. The underlying rail gap is a KNOWN-OPEN owner item; what is new here is that a specific, dated, public SLA is printed on top of it.
  - customer_impact: This is the single most load-bearing promise made to supply. The first pandit who completes a puja on a Monday and does not see money by Thursday concludes the platform does not pay — and in a temple-networked, WhatsApp-connected community one such pandit poisons an entire recruitment channel. It is also the promise most likely to be screenshotted in a consumer complaint.
  - fix: Reword to what is true today: 'Payouts are reviewed weekly and released once your KYC and bank details are verified. We will confirm your payout schedule during onboarding.' Restore the specific Monday + 2-3 day SLA only after RazorpayX/Cashfree is live and the settlement cron runs.

### [PM-07] P2/money/M Deployed pandit take rate is 30%, not the 20% promised on every marketing and supply surface
  - where: origin/main apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma:328 `commissionRate Float @default(0.70)` ; apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:55-69,478
  - evidence: Live prod is origin/main 1715d4d6, where the schema default is 0.70 (provider share) = a 30% platform take. booking.service.ts:478 treats any stored value in (0,1] as authoritative, so 0.70 always wins and DEFAULT_PRIEST_SHARE=0.80 never fires — the in-file comment states this plainly: 'a pandit onboarded through the normal flow is paid 70%, not the 80% this constant and the supply pitch promise'. The fix (schema @default(0.80) + migration 20260812000000_priest_commission_rate_80 containing ALTER COLUMN SET DEFAULT 0.80 and UPDATE priests SET commission_rate=0.80 WHERE commission_rate=0.70) exists in the working tree only — uncommitted and unapplied, so not product state.
  - customer_impact: A pandit recruited on 'you keep 80%' is paid ₹7,000 instead of ₹8,000 on a ₹10,000 booking, and the Commission row records percentage=0.3000. Discovering a 10-point gap between the recruitment pitch and the first payout is the fastest way to lose a scarce, temple-networked supplier permanently.
  - fix: Commit and apply migration 20260812000000 in the documented order (ALTER TYPE migrations stay isolated), redeploy the API so Prisma regenerates, then verify a fresh Priest row lands at 0.80 and an existing one was updated. Confirm with the owner first whether the UPDATE should backfill existing pandits or only set the default.

The page renders `rates`, so `baseRate` is dead — but it is dead AND wrong, in the file a future developer is told to trust.

### [PM-10] P3/money/M Senior astrologers are priced 2-4x below the market ceiling, capping the highest-margin repeat revenue line
  - where: GET https://api.darshya.online/api/v1/astrology/astrologers/live (200)
  - evidence: Live rates: Acharya Vinod Sharma (22 yrs, Vedic/Kundali/Muhurat, verified) ₹25/min; Ritika Menon (9 yrs) ₹18/min. AstroTalk's published band is ₹5-₹200/min, its blended platform take is 50-57%, its astrologers average ₹7.82 lakh/yr and its top earner ₹1.87 Cr/yr; 80% of AstroTalk revenue is repeat. A 22-year Vedic astrologer at ₹25/min is priced at AstroTalk's junior end.
  - customer_impact: A 30-minute consult with the platform's most experienced astrologer bills ₹750, of which Darshya keeps ₹150. At AstroTalk-comparable senior pricing (₹60-90/min) the same consult bills ₹1,800-2,700. The platform is leaving roughly 2-3x on its stickiest, zero-fulfilment-cost revenue line while simultaneously under-paying the supply it is trying to poach from AstroTalk.
  - fix: Introduce experience-banded per-minute pricing — junior ₹15-25, mid ₹30-45, senior (15+ yrs, verified) ₹60-90 — and let astrologers set within band. Keep the 20% take: at ₹75/min the astrologer nets ₹60/min against AstroTalk's ~₹37/min after their 50% cut, which is the actual recruiting weapon. Pair with a free first 3 minutes, the category-standard acquisition hook.

Clicking "Sort: Popularity" twice leaves the label "Sort: Popularity" and the card order byte-identical. Source: apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx lines 474, 485, 496, 507 are <select className=…> with no value, no onChange, no name; line 517 is a <button className=…> with no onClick.

On /pujas/rudrabhishek the "Book This Puja" CTA is at absTop=1037, still below the 844px fold. The "Book Now" control is also only 34px tall, under the 44px minimum touch target.
  - customer_impact: A mobile visitor sees marketing copy, four dead filter dropdowns and two category cards before a single bookable puja. Combined with PF-02 (filters do nothing), the first two interactions a phone user attempts both fail to produce a result.
  - fix: Move the catalogue grid above the two category cards on small viewports, or add a sticky "Browse pujas" jump control; raise the Book Now hit area to at least 44px.

### [PF-08] P3/content/ Catalogue cards render the entire ~1,200-character scripture description
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas — every puja card
  - observed: The Rudrabhishek card's text content measures 1,261 characters, running from "Rudrabhishek is the ceremonial bathing of a Shiva linga…" through Taittiriya Samhita citations, the eight Panchamrit liquids, Shiva Purana references and Pradosham timing, before finally reaching "1h 30m | planetary ₹1,100 onwards Book Now".
  - customer_impact: The listing cannot be scanned. Price, duration and the Book button are buried under a wall of Sanskrit-citation prose on every card, so comparing ten pujas requires reading roughly 12,000 characters. It also pushes the first CTA far down the page (see PF-07).
  - fix: Truncate the card description to ~140 characters with a line-clamp and keep the full text on the detail page, which already renders it well.

### [PF-09] P3/ux/ Booking date picker offers a year dropdown spanning 1900–2031
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas/book?slug=hanuman-chalisa-path — "Select Date" calendar
  - observed: Opening the date control reveals a year <select> whose options run 2031, 2030, 2029 … down to 1900, on a field whose purpose is choosing a future puja date. (Day cells are correctly handled: 1–12 are disabled and 13–31 enabled for the current month.)
  - customer_impact: A 132-entry year list, 126 of which are in the past, on a control the user must touch to change the date. On mobile this is a long scroll wheel where every sensible option is at the very top, and picking a past year is offered as a first-class action for a service that cannot be performed in the past.
  - fix: Restrict the year range in the shared DateInput component (apps/web/components/ui/DateInput.tsx) to the current year through current+2 when the field is a forward-looking booking date.

### [PF-10] P3/error/ /pujas/home-puja/book returns 200 with a "Puja not found" dead end
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas/home-puja/book
  - observed: HTTP 200, page body renders h1 "Puja not found" with a "Browse home pujas" link. Source: apps/web/app/pujas/home-puja/book/page.tsx:86 renders this whenever ?slug= does not match a homePujaCategories entry.
  - customer_impact: Low direct impact today because nothing links here — but this is the working wizard that PF-01's 404ing CTAs should be routed to, and it will start receiving no-slug traffic the moment that redirect is added. Serving 200 for a not-found state also lets search engines index a dead page.
  - fix: Once the PF-01 shim always supplies a slug, make the bare route redirect to /pujas/home-puja instead of rendering a dead end, and return a proper notFound() for unknown slugs.

### [PF-11] P3/content/ Wedding wizard shows an unquantified "+ GST" during the decision steps
  - where: https://darshya.online/pujas/marriage/book — sidebar "Wedding summary"
  - observed: On step 1 the persistent sidebar reads "Tier Traditional / Duration 4-hour ceremony / Amount ₹25,000 + GST". The rupee value of the GST is not shown at this point. (Source apps/web/app/pujas/marriage/book/page.tsx:574-580 does render "GST (18%) ₹4,500" and "Estimated total ₹29,500" — but only on the final review step.)
  - customer_impact: Through the four steps where the customer is actually deciding, the number on screen is ₹25,000 when the real commitment is ₹29,500. An unquantified "+ GST" on a five-figure purchase reads as evasive, and the ₹4,500 gap only surfaces after the couple has entered names, dates, pandit choice and venue.
  - fix: Render the computed GST line and estimated total in the sticky sidebar from step 1 onward, reusing the same tierAmount*0.18 calculation already present at line 575.

## AREA: home-nav — https://darshya.online/ plus every link in the global header and footer (Worship / Library / Astrology / Yatra / Shop dropdowns, search, language switcher, skip-to-content), crawled on Chromium desktop 1280x720 (+1600x900 check) and mobile 390x844   score=

### [HN-01] P0/perf/ Homepage ships 15.2 MB, 14.9 MB of it full-resolution images hotlinked from Wikimedia
  - where: https://darshya.online/ — measured via performance.getEntriesByType('resource') after a full-page scroll
  - observed: Total transfer 15,551 KB; 16 image files = 14,942 KB. By host: upload.wikimedia.org 15 files / 14,914 KB, images.unsplash.com 1 file / 29 KB. Heaviest single file: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9c/Rudraksha_mala.jpg at 5,594 KB. Next: Marble_Dagadusheth_Halwai_Ganapati_Statue at 2,971 KB, then six 1920px temple JPGs at 633-1,111 KB each. Cold first visit on desktop: domcontentloaded 8,737 ms, load event 13,582 ms, networkidle 32,897 ms. Mobile 390x844 cold: 21,521 ms to networkidle. A warm cached repeat is fast (dom 716 ms, TTFB 29 ms) — the cost lands entirely on first-time visitors.
  - customer_impact: Every first-time visitor on an Indian mobile data connection downloads ~15 MB to see one homepage. At a typical 4G rate that is 30-60 seconds and a meaningful chunk of a daily data pack, on the single most important page in the product. This is the largest customer-felt defect found in this route set and it hits 100% of new traffic — precisely the traffic that has not yet decided to trust the brand.
  - fix: Stop hotlinking upload.wikimedia.org at source resolution. Self-host the 15 homepage images through next/image (or an image CDN), serve AVIF/WebP at the rendered display size with width/quality params, and set long-lived immutable cache headers. Rudraksha_mala.jpg alone (5.5 MB) should be roughly 40-60 KB at its rendered size. Target under 500 KB total image weight for the homepage.

### [HN-02] P1/ux/ Cookie consent banner covers 41% of the mobile fold and hides all three primary entry cards
  - where: https://darshya.online/ at 390x844 (and the same banner at 1280x720)
  - observed: The fixed consent dialog occupies y=497 to y=844 — height 347 px of an 844 px viewport = 41% of the first screen. The three primary entry cards sit underneath it: 'Live Darshan / Watch live aarti fr…' at top=488, 'Book a Puja / Schedule a ritual at…' at top=642, 'Ask a Pandit / AI guidance on dhar…' at top=797. The bottom tab bar (Home/Book/Library/Profile) at top≈772-788 is also overlapped. Banner text begins 'We use cookies for darshan, devotion, and DPDP/UK-GDPR compliance' with Reject all / Manage preferences / Accept all. Confirmed in the mobile screenshot; the same banner covers the identical three cards at desktop 1280x720.
  - customer_impact: On a first visit — the only visit where the banner shows — a mobile user sees the hero and then a wall of consent copy. The three cards that route to the three revenue paths (darshan, puja booking, ask-a-pandit) are invisible until they interact with the banner. First-impression conversion is being spent on a compliance dialog.
  - fix: Shrink to a single-line bar pinned to the very bottom (≈72-96 px) with Accept / Reject inline and 'Manage preferences' as a text link opening a sheet. Keep the full-height card for the preferences view only. Also offset the bottom tab bar by the banner height while it is visible so the two do not overlap.

### [HN-03] P1/content/ /search returns zero results for 'aarti' and 'hanuman' while the site has full pages for both
  - where: https://darshya.online/search?q=aarti and https://darshya.online/search?q=hanuman
  - observed: Literal on-page text at /search?q=hanuman: '0 results for “hanuman” No matches. Try a different term.' Same for ?q=aarti: '0 results'. Control queries prove the index is alive: ?q=gita → '3 results', ?q=shiva → '2 results', ?q=a → '50 results'. Meanwhile the site serves /aarti (12,262 chars, 101 links, h1 'Hindu Aarti Lyrics') and /chalisa (15,480 chars, 55 links), and the header typeahead for the same word 'hanuman' returns 4 correct hits: /pujas/hanuman-chalisa-path, /pujas/sunderkand-path, /content/hanuman-chalisa, /content/bajrang-baan.
  - customer_impact: A devotee searching for the two most common devotional terms in the product is told the site has nothing. Hanuman is arguably the single most-searched deity term in this market, and 'aarti' is a core product noun that appears in the site's own nav and marketing copy. It reads as an empty, unfinished product.
  - fix: Two search systems currently disagree: the header typeahead (temples/pujas/content) is healthy, the /search knowledge index is not. Either point /search at the same index the typeahead uses, or expand the knowledge index to cover the aarti/chalisa/content corpus. At minimum, make the zero-result state fall back to typeahead results instead of a dead end.

### [HN-04] P1/content/ All five non-English homepages are 23%-size stubs that still declare lang="en" and keep an English header
  - where: https://darshya.online/hi, /ta, /te, /bn, /mr — all six languages are advertised in the global footer
  - observed: /hi: HTTP 200, title 'दर्शया — भारत का आध्यात्मिक सुपर-ऐप', h1 'भारत का आध्यात्मिक सुपर-ऐप', body copy genuinely Hindi. But document.documentElement.lang === 'en'. docHeight 1902 px vs 8159 px for the English homepage (23%), and 0 images vs 15. The entire header renders in English on the Hindi page: 'Worship, Library, Astrology, Yatra, Shop, Ask Pandit, List your business, Today, Search puja/temple/kundli/saint…, Cart (0 items), Sign In'. Only the two hero buttons are translated ('पूजा बुक करें', 'लाइव दर्शन देखें'). Same pattern on /ta (docH 2054), /te (1983), /bn (1983), /mr (1902) — all with html lang='en'. Canonical and the six hreflang alternates are correctly emitted.
  - customer_impact: A Hindi-speaking user who taps 'हिन्दी' in the footer lands on a page missing roughly three-quarters of the homepage, with navigation still in English — so they cannot actually browse in Hindi. lang="en" also makes screen readers pronounce Devanagari/Tamil/Telugu/Bengali with English phonetics, and tells Google the Hindi page is English despite the hreflang tags saying otherwise.
  - fix: Set html lang per locale (hi/ta/te/bn/mr). Route the header/nav strings through the same i18n dictionary the hero already uses. Then either bring the locale homepages to parity with the English sections or stop advertising six languages in the footer until they are ready.

### [HN-05] P1/content/ Broken temple image on /temples — a Wikimedia hotlink failing in production today
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples — the Mallikarjuna Jyotirlinga (Srisailam) card
  - observed: Of 33 images on /temples, 1 renders with img.complete === true and img.naturalWidth === 0: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/MKJNTemple.jpg/960px-MKJNTemple.jpg, alt='Mallikarjuna Jyotirlinga (Srisailam) - Srisailam'. No 4xx/5xx response was recorded for it, so the fetch is being dropped rather than answered — the signature of upstream hotlink throttling.
  - customer_impact: A blank tile sits in the temple grid on the page that is supposed to establish that these temples are real and verified. It is also the leading indicator for HN-01: the entire image strategy depends on a third party that is under no obligation to serve this traffic, so today's one broken tile can become the whole grid without any code change.
  - fix: Self-host the temple imagery (same change as HN-01). Wikimedia's own guidance discourages hotlinking their originals at scale. Add an onError fallback to a local placeholder so a dropped upstream fetch never renders as a blank tile.

### [HN-06] P2/ux/ "List your business" header CTA is invisible at 1280px — the whole supply-side entry point only appears at ≥1600px
  - where: https://darshya.online/ global header, 'List your business' button
  - observed: At viewport 1280x720 the header button 'List your business' has a zero-height bounding box (visible=false); 'Visit store' is absent entirely. At 1600x900 both appear: the header enumerates BUTTON 'List your business' visible=true and A 'Visit store' href=/store visible=true. The only other route to the supply pages is the footer, 91 links deep.
  - customer_impact: 1280px is a standard laptop width. Every temple trust, pandit, astrologer and gaushala browsing on a normal laptop sees no header path to list their business — they have to scroll past the entire homepage to the footer. This is the acquisition funnel for the supply side of a marketplace, hidden from the majority of desktop users.
  - fix: Confirm the breakpoint driving this (it hides somewhere between 1280 and 1600). Either drop the reveal to the standard lg breakpoint (1024px) by shortening the label to 'List your business' → 'For partners', or move it into the overflow/user menu so it is reachable at every width rather than absent.

### [HN-07] P2/dead-cta/ /content?tab= query parameter is ignored — two different footer links land on the same undifferentiated page
  - where: Footer links 'Mantra' → /content?tab=mantra and 'Bhajan' → /content?tab=bhajan
  - observed: Four distinct URLs — /content, /content?tab=mantra, /content?tab=bhajan, /content?tab=aarti — all render identically: 1,376 chars, 17 content cards, and byte-identical first 220 characters ('Devotional Content Library … English All22 Aarti5 Chalisa6 Mantra6 Articles5 Today's Featured'). Programmatic diff across the four: 1 distinct rendering. The URL in the address bar keeps the tab param; the page never reads it.
  - customer_impact: A user who clicks 'Bhajan' in the footer expecting bhajans gets an unfiltered mixed library and has to re-filter by hand. Two separate footer promises resolve to the same place, which reads as broken navigation. It also means these URLs cannot be shared or bookmarked as filtered views.
  - fix: Read the tab search param on /content and initialise the filter state from it (and write it back on filter change so the view is linkable). Note the filter chips advertise 'Bhajan' but the visible counts are All22 / Aarti5 / Chalisa6 / Mantra6 / Articles5 — confirm a bhajan category actually exists before linking to it.

### [HN-08] P2/a11y/ Nav dropdown triggers never update aria-expanded — screen readers are told the menu is closed while it is open
  - where: https://darshya.online/ header — the Worship, Library, Astrology, Yatra and Shop buttons
  - observed: Hovering 'Worship' opens a panel containing 9 links (/today, /darshan, /pujas, /gift, /temples, /gaushala, /donations, /panchang); 'Library' opens 44 links (/vedas, /upanishads, /gita, …); 'Shop' opens 7. While those panels are visibly open, button.getAttribute('aria-expanded') still reads "false" on every one of them — measured before and after both click and keyboard activation, it never becomes "true". Verified reachable: hovering, traversing the mouse into the panel and clicking '/darshan' navigated correctly to https://darshya.online/darshan.
  - customer_impact: The entire site taxonomy — 60+ destinations — hangs off these five buttons. A screen-reader user is told 'collapsed' even when the menu has opened, so the announced state contradicts what is actually on screen and there is no reliable way to know the submenu is available.
  - fix: Bind aria-expanded to the panel's open state (it is already wired to a state variable — the mobile drawer's toggle sets aria-expanded="true" correctly, so mirror that). Also add aria-controls pointing at the panel id and aria-haspopup="true".

### [HN-09] P2/a11y/ Skip-to-content link updates the hash but never moves focus, so keyboard users are dumped back into the header
  - where: https://darshya.online/ — first tab stop, 'Skip to main content' → #main
  - observed: The link is correctly the first tab stop and becomes visible on focus (top=8px). Pressing Enter gives: location.hash === '#main', but document.activeElement is BODY, and window.scrollY is still 0. The target element <main id="main"> has no tabindex attribute (getAttribute('tabindex') === null), which is why the browser refuses to move focus to it.
  - customer_impact: The one control that exists specifically to let keyboard and screen-reader users bypass a 14-item header does nothing. The next Tab press returns them to the top of the navigation, so they must tab through the entire header on every page load — the exact burden the link is advertised to remove.
  - fix: Add tabindex="-1" to <main id="main"> so it can receive programmatic focus. If a smooth-scroll or scroll-restoration handler is intercepting the hash navigation, call main.focus() explicitly in the link's click handler after preventDefault.

### [HN-10] P3/ux/ Clicking a nav dropdown trigger closes the panel that hovering just opened
  - where: https://darshya.online/ header — Worship, Library, Astrology, Yatra, Shop
  - observed: The panels open on hover (Worship +9 links, Library +44, Shop +7) and stay open while the mouse traverses down into them. But because a real mouse click is preceded by hover, the click toggles the already-open panel shut: after btn.click() the newly-visible link count returns to 0 and aria-expanded reads false, on all five menus. Keyboard activation works correctly (focus + Enter opens the panel: +9 / +44 / +7). Confirmed reachable by hover — traversing into the Worship panel and clicking a link navigated to /darshan successfully.
  - customer_impact: These are rendered as <button> elements, which invites a click. A user who clicks 'Library' sees the panel flash open and immediately vanish, which reads as a broken menu even though hovering works. Minor friction, but it sits on the primary navigation.
  - fix: Make the trigger a genuine toggle whose click handler checks current open state rather than blindly toggling after hover has already opened it — or drop the hover-open behaviour and open on click only, which is also the more predictable and touch-consistent pattern.

### [HN-11] P3/content/ /blog is an empty shell linked from the global footer
  - where: https://darshya.online/blog, reached from footer link 'Blog'
  - observed: HTTP 200, h1 'Stories, Insights & Updates', but only 571 characters of main content and 2 links in main (docHeight 1717 px). For comparison, sibling footer pages render 2,000-40,000 chars: /about 6,034, /careers 1,961, /vrat 40,709.
  - customer_impact: A prospective customer checking whether the company is alive clicks Blog and finds a heading with nothing under it. For a pre-revenue devotional marketplace asking users to trust it with puja money and 80G donations, an empty blog is a small but real credibility leak.
  - fix: Either publish the first 3-5 posts (the repo already contains substantial GTM and festival content that could seed them) or remove 'Blog' from the footer until there is something to read.

### [HN-12] P3/mobile/ Mobile header controls are 28-36px — below the 44px minimum touch target
  - where: https://darshya.online/ at 390x844 — header and in-page links
  - observed: Measured bounding boxes: brand link 'Darshya home' 28x28, 'Search' 36x36, 'Cart (0 items)' 36x36, 'Open menu' 36x36 — every header control is under 40px. In-page: 'All streams' 71x15 and 'Browse Pujas' 84x15 are 15 px tall. 'Explore Temples' 155x38 and 'Book a Puja' 107x38 are also under 40px high. The mobile header also drops the 'Darshya' wordmark entirely, leaving only a 28px house icon.
  - customer_impact: Mis-taps on the hamburger, cart and search on the primary mobile surface — and 15px-tall text links are effectively un-tappable for anyone with less than perfect dexterity. WCAG 2.1 AA (2.5.5) and both platform HIGs specify 44px.
  - fix: Pad the four header controls to a 44x44 hit area (visual icon can stay 20-24px — use padding, not icon size). Give the 15px text links a block padding so their hit box reaches 44px. Consider restoring the wordmark on mobile for brand recall.

### [HN-13] P3/ux/ /gurukul-portal is publicly linked in the footer but renders a bare 44-character auth wall with no way to sign in
  - where: https://darshya.online/gurukul-portal, reached from footer link 'Gurukul portal'
  - observed: HTTP 200, docHeight 962 px, no h1, and 44 characters of main content: 'Please sign in to access the gurukul portal.' Only 1 link in main (the skip link). By contrast the sibling /become-gurukul page handles the same state properly — it shows 'Please sign in to register.' alongside an actual <a href="/auth/login">sign in</a>.
  - customer_impact: An anonymous visitor clicking a public footer link hits a sentence with no sign-in button, no explanation of what a gurukul portal is, and no way forward except the browser back button. It is a dead end reachable from every page on the site.
  - fix: Copy the pattern already used on /become-gurukul: add a real 'Sign in' call-to-action linking to /auth/login with a returnTo back to the portal, plus a one-line description and a link to /become-gurukul for people who do not yet have a gurukul listed.

## AREA: temples-darshan   score=

### [TD-01] P1/perf/ /temples takes ~37s to settle — the flagship browse page is effectively unusable on a first visit
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples (also /darshan 16.9s, /calendar 13.6s, /live 12.3s)
  - observed: Runner line: "### [ROUTE] https://darshya.online/temples  status=200 finalUrl=https://darshya.online/temples loadMs=37514". loadMs = page.goto(waitUntil:'domcontentloaded') plus a networkidle settle hard-capped at 12,000ms, so DOMContentLoaded alone was roughly 25s. Same measure on the rest: /darshan 16942, /calendar 13602, /live 12319, /kundli 9800, /panchang 9726, /festival 8476. This was the first navigation of a cold browser session so Render free-tier cold start is a likely contributor, but a second spec run that re-loaded /temples and then issued 33 plain HTTP GETs still took 5.9 minutes end to end. The page ships 148 anchors and 33 images.
  - customer_impact: A devotee tapping "Temples" in the nav stares at a blank or half-painted screen for 20-35 seconds. On Indian 4G they bounce long before the grid paints — and this is the top-of-funnel page feeding every puja booking and donation downstream.
  - fix: Split the number with Server-Timing / navigation timing to separate Render cold start from payload. If cold start: keep-warm ping the web and API services (both Render free tier, both spin down). If payload: next/image with priority only on the first row and lazy below the fold, and stop server-rendering the 148-anchor mega-footer on every route.

### [TD-02] P1/dead-cta/ The hero CTA on /darshan links back to /darshan — "Watch Mahakaleshwar Bhasma Aarti now" is a no-op
  - where: https://darshya.online/darshan — anchor labelled "Watch Mahakaleshwar Bhasma Aarti now"
  - observed: Link enumeration on /darshan returned: "Watch Mahakaleshwar Bhasma Aarti now -> /darshan". The href is the page the user is already on. Every other live card on the same page points at a real detail route, and /darshan/mahakaleshwar is in that very list. Verify in 30s: open https://darshya.online/darshan and inspect that anchor's href.
  - customer_impact: The single highest-intent action on the live-darshan page — a named aarti happening "now" — does nothing. The user clicks, the page appears to reload, and they conclude the live stream is broken. This is exactly the CTA that turns a one-time visitor into a daily user.
  - fix: Point the href at /darshan/mahakaleshwar (already a live card on the same page) or at the specific stream route. Add a guard test asserting no anchor's resolved href equals its own page URL.

### [TD-03] P2/trust/ /temples shows 33 cards for only 29 distinct temples — Ramanathaswamy is listed three times, and the two Meenakshi cards disagree about LIVE
  - where: https://darshya.online/temples
  - observed: All 33 temple deep links from the grid return 200, but they cover 29 temples. Ramanathaswamy x3: /temples/rameswaram "Rameswaram Temple (Ramanathaswamy)", /temples/rameshwaram "Ramanathaswamy Temple,", /temples/ramanathaswamy-rameshwaram "Ramanathaswamy Temple Rameshwaram, Tamil Nadu". Meenakshi Madurai x2: /temples/meenakshi-temple "Arulmigu Meenakshi Sundareswarar Temple Madurai" WITH a LIVE badge, and /temples/meenakshi-madurai "Meenakshi Amman Temple Madurai" WITHOUT one. Tirupati x2: /temples/tirupati-balaji "Tirumala Tirupati Venkateswara Temple" and /temples/tirumala-tirupati "Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanam Tirupati".
  - customer_impact: The duplicates contradict each other — pick the wrong Meenakshi card and you are told there is no live darshan at a temple that is streaming. It reads as an unmaintained directory, which is fatal for a product whose entire pitch is "verified" temples, and it splits SEO authority across three URLs for Rameswaram.
  - fix: Deduplicate on a canonical identity (deity + city, or a curated canonical slug), keep one card, and 301 the losing slugs to the winner so inbound links and the sitemap survive. TASKS.md claims a "temples dup-merge count" fix already shipped — it is not covering these three clusters.

### [TD-04] P2/error/ /live returns HTTP 404 and serves the homepage <title> on the error page
  - where: https://darshya.online/live
  - observed: status=404, finalUrl unchanged, h1="Page not found", body text "The page you're looking for may have moved or doesn't exist on Darshya.", one console error: "Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 404 ()". The <title> served on that 404 is "Darshya — India's Devotional Super App" — the normal homepage title. Confirmed in the repo: `ls apps/web/app/live` -> "cannot access 'live': No such file or directory", while darshan/, temples/, festival/, panchang/ and calendar/ all exist.
  - customer_impact: /live is the URL a user types or a partner links for live darshan — the site brands these streams "LIVE" on every card. They hit a dead end. Because the 404 carries a normal-looking homepage title, search engines and WhatsApp link previews render it as a legitimate Darshya page, so the dead end also gets shared.
  - fix: Add a permanent redirect /live -> /darshan in next.config, the same pattern already used for /kundli -> /astrology/kundli and /festivals -> /festival. Separately give the 404 page its own <title> such as "Page not found | Darshya".

### [TD-05] P2/content/ The free Kundli page — a top acquisition keyword — has no page-specific <title>
  - where: https://darshya.online/kundli, which redirects to https://darshya.online/astrology/kundli
  - observed: status=200, finalUrl=https://darshya.online/astrology/kundli, h1="Free Kundli Generator", but title="Darshya — India's Devotional Super App" — the site-wide default. Every other page in the route set carries a bespoke title ("Sacred Temples of India | Darshya", "Live Darshan | Darshya", "Hindu festival calendar 2026 — pujas, rituals, dates | Darshya").
  - customer_impact: "free kundli" is one of the highest-volume devotional searches in India and this is the page built to capture it. With no keyword in the title tag it cannot rank, and anyone who bookmarks or shares it gets an unidentifiable link. Free tools are the cheapest top-of-funnel Darshya has and this one is invisible.
  - fix: Export metadata (or generateMetadata) from apps/web/app/astrology/kundli/page.tsx with something like "Free Kundli — Janam Kundli & Birth Chart | Darshya" plus a description, matching what /temples and /festival already do.

### [TD-06] P3/content/ Two page titles end in a doubled " | Darshya | Darshya"
  - where: https://darshya.online/panchang and https://darshya.online/calendar
  - observed: Literal titles read from the live DOM: "Panchang Today — Tithi, Nakshatra, Rahu Kaal | Darshya | Darshya" and "Hindu Calendar 2026 — Complete Festival & Vrat Calendar | Darshya | Darshya".
  - customer_impact: Visible in the browser tab, in Google results, and in every WhatsApp link preview — on the two pages devotees are most likely to share daily. Small, but it is the sort of detail that makes a paying user wonder whether anyone is minding the store.
  - fix: Page-level metadata already appends "| Darshya" while the root layout's title.template appends it again. Drop the suffix from those two page-level title strings, or switch them to title: { absolute: ... }.

### [TD-07] P2/ux/ Three floating overlays stack inside the first viewport at once — cookie bar, chat launcher, and aarti reminder
  - where: https://darshya.online/festival, /panchang, /calendar, /kundli and /live at 1280x720
  - observed: On each of those pages the visible-button enumeration returned, all with top offsets inside the 720px fold: "Reject all"@y627, "Manage preferences"@y627, "Accept all"@y627, "Need help? Chat with us"@y644, "Dismiss aarti reminder for this session"@y666, alongside a link "Sandhya Aarti starts in 08:32:22". A cookie consent bar, a support chat launcher and a countdown reminder occupy the bottom ~95px of the first screen simultaneously.
  - customer_impact: On a 720px laptop the bottom eighth of the very first screen is three competing interruptions before the devotee has read a single festival date; on a phone it will be proportionally worse and probably covers content. It also makes the cookie choice harder to act on, which is a consent-quality issue and not just clutter.
  - fix: Sequence them: hold the chat launcher and the aarti reminder until the cookie choice is resolved, then show at most one. At minimum give them distinct anchors — cookie bar full-width bottom, chat bottom-right, reminder as an in-flow banner — so they cannot share a band.

### [TD-08] P3/a11y/ Dozens of visible links expose no accessible name, including the panchang previous/next-day arrows
  - where: https://darshya.online/panchang (and the shared mega-footer on /calendar, /festival, /darshan, /temples)
  - observed: Enumeration reads innerText and falls back to aria-label; both were empty for 44 visible anchors on /panchang, logged as "(no text)". Examples: "(no text) -> /panchang?offset=-1" and "(no text) -> /panchang?offset=1" (the day stepper), plus footer entries "(no text) -> /gita", "/ramayana", "/mahabharata", "/vedas", "/nakshatra", "/rashi", "/muhurat", "/jyotirlinga". /panchang reports imgs=0, so none of these can be deriving a name from image alt text. Two buttons at y=412 are likewise nameless. Caveat: I measured innerText + aria-label only — a title attribute or a visually-hidden span would still supply a name, so confirm with axe before sizing the fix.
  - customer_impact: A screen-reader user on the daily panchang hears "link" for both date arrows and cannot tell forward from back, which is the page's only interaction. Elderly and low-vision devotees are a core segment here, and this is a WCAG 2.1 2.4.4 / 4.1.2 exposure for an India-facing consumer service.
  - fix: Run axe-core against /panchang and the shared footer. Add aria-label="Previous day" / "Next day" to the offset links and make each footer chip render its label text (or an aria-label) instead of relying on a decorative child.

### [TD-09] P3/trust/ Temple photography is hotlinked from upload.wikimedia.org rather than self-hosted
  - where: https://darshya.online/darshan and https://darshya.online/temples (network requests)
  - observed: Requests captured during the /temples -> /darshan navigation include https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Grishneshwar_temple_in_Aurangabad_district.jpg/960px-..., .../Ramanathaswamy_temple7.JPG/960px-..., .../Vaishno_devi.jpg/960px-..., .../Shree_Siddhivinayak_Temple_Mumbai.jpg/960px-.... Important qualifier: on every page I rendered the broken-image count was 0 (/temples 33 images 0 broken, /darshan 12 images 0 broken), so unlike the earlier homepage report nothing in this route set was actually failing at test time.
  - customer_impact: Not breaking today, but the visual identity of every temple card depends on a third party that discourages hotlinking and can rate-limit or referrer-block at any moment. If Wikimedia throttles, the temple directory becomes a wall of empty frames with no deploy on Darshya's side. It also puts image latency outside your control on the page that is already the slowest (TD-01).
  - fix: Mirror the images into Supabase storage or an image CDN, serve via next/image with a local loader, and keep the Wikimedia URL as attribution metadata only — a /credits page already exists.

# WORKFLOW: authenticated-deep  (12 agents)
==========================================================================================

## AREA: pandit-fulfilment   score=14

### [PF-01] P0/broken-feature/S Every booking action button in the pandit portal returns 400 — pandit cannot accept, start or complete anything
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings (buttons "Accept", "Decline", "Start Puja", "Mark Complete") and http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings/view?id=<id> (buttons "Accept Booking", "Start Puja", "Mark Puja Complete"). Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/view/page.tsx:131-137 and apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:169-176
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com / demo123.
2. Go to /priest/bookings/view?id=<any booking in 'Accepted' status>.
3. Click "Start Puja".
4. Watch the Network tab and the red banner at the top of the page.
5. Repeat on /priest/bookings — click "Start Puja" on any Accepted card; nothing happens at all.
  - observed: Network: `400 PATCH http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/priest/bookings/7c18cd90-4c50-4eea-9bfc-8b7523609b49/start`. On-screen banner: `Action failed: API 400: {"statusCode":400,"code":"FST_ERR_CTP_EMPTY_JSON_BODY","error":"Bad Request","message":"Body cannot be empty when content-type is set to application/json"}`. Booking stayed at "Accepted". On the list page the same request also failed — `400 PATCH http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/priest/bookings/87489563-24c9-4d8b-ae99-314f8ebce594/start` — and the row still showed "Start Puja" with no message of any kind (handleAction has `// Silent fail for MVP`). The identical PATCH sent WITHOUT a Content-Type header returns 200, so the endpoints are fine — the client request is malformed.
  - expected: Clicking Start Puja moves the booking to In Progress. A bodyless PATCH must not declare a JSON content-type.
  - customer_impact: Total supply-side outage. No pandit can accept, start or complete a single booking in the product. Every paid puja stalls at 'Accepted', the devotee never gets their video, and nobody gets paid. This is the first click a new pandit makes.
  - fix: Remove the `'Content-Type': 'application/json'` header from the bodyless PATCH calls (or send `body: JSON.stringify({})`) in apps/web/app/priest/bookings/view/page.tsx:131-137 and apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:169-176. While there, replace the list page's silent `if (res.ok)` with an error toast.

### [PF-02] P0/wrong-data/M Uploading video proof never creates the pandit's payout — earnings stay ₹0 forever
  - where: POST /api/v1/priest/bookings/:id/video -> apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:1339-1376 (uploadVideoProof) vs the payout hook at apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:771-775. Surfaces on http://localhost:3000/priest (EarningsCard) and http://localhost:3000/priest/payouts
  - steps: 1. As pandit@demo.com, take a booking to `completed`.
2. Upload video proof (POST /priest/bookings/:id/video with a videoUrl) — returns 200 "Video proof uploaded successfully", status becomes video_uploaded.
3. Open /priest/payouts and /priest (dashboard).
4. Compare with GET /api/v1/priest/payouts.
  - observed: Before proof: payouts summary `{totalEarned:"770", pending:"770"}`, 1 row. After a successful proof upload on a ₹1,100 booking (priestPayout "770"): summary IDENTICAL, `matching: []` — no payout row for that booking. Dashboard: `earnings: {total: 0, thisMonth: 0}` with `bookings.completed: 11`. At one point 6 bookings sat at `video_uploaded` with proof attached and the pandit's Total Earned still read ₹0. Root cause: `uploadVideoProof` does a direct `prisma.pujaBooking.update`, so it never reaches `updateBookingStatus`, which is the only place `paymentService.createPriestPayout` is called. Proof: running `PATCH /bookings/:id {"status":"delivered"}` (which does go through updateBookingStatus) immediately produced `{amount:"770", status:"pending", payoutMethod:"bank_transfer", booking:{bookingNumber:"BS-20260812-WPU0N"}}`.
  - expected: Uploading proof — the pandit-facing step the UI calls "mandatory before payout is processed" — should register the earning.
  - customer_impact: Pandits do the work, file the proof, and see ₹0. This is the single fastest way to lose supply: the first payout cycle produces nothing and every pandit churns. It also means the platform's own books have no record of what it owes.
  - fix: Call `paymentService.createPriestPayout(bookingId)` at the end of `booking.service.uploadVideoProof` (it is already idempotent and Aadhaar-gated), or route the proof write through `updateBookingStatus('video_uploaded', {...})` so the existing hook fires.

### [PF-03] P0/broken-feature/S Video proof upload can silently fail while the UI shows a green "Video Proof Uploaded"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings/view?id=<id>, the "Or paste video URL" submit button. Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/view/page.tsx:327-339
  - steps: 1. As pandit@demo.com open a booking in `completed` on /priest/bookings/view?id=<id>.
2. Have the proof POST fail — in real use this happens whenever the booking left `completed` since the page loaded (uploaded from the phone first, admin refunded it, the pandit released it). The API answers 400 in all those cases.
3. Paste a URL and press the submit button.
4. Read the Actions panel, then check the booking in the DB/API.
  - observed: With the endpoint returning the API's own literal error `{"error":"Video proof can only be uploaded for bookings in \"completed\" status. Current: \"video_uploaded\"."}` (400), the Actions panel rendered: `Video Proof Uploaded / View uploaded video / Video preview`. `showsAnyError: false`. Meanwhile the API still reported `{status: "completed", videoProofUrl: null}` — nothing was saved. The handler is `await fetch(...); setVideoSubmitted(true);` with no `res.ok` check and no try/catch.
  - expected: A failed upload must show the error and leave the upload control available for a retry.
  - customer_impact: The pandit believes the proof is filed and walks away. The devotee never receives the video they paid for, the payout never fires, and the dispute surfaces days later with no evidence and a pandit who insists he uploaded it.
  - fix: In the `onUploaded` callback, check `res.ok`, surface `await res.text()` in the existing `actionError` banner, and only set `videoSubmitted` on success.

### [PF-04] P1/broken-feature/M No UI anywhere can mark a booking delivered — the final fulfilment step is curl-only
  - where: apps/web (priest + devotee portals), apps/admin, apps/mobile — no control for PujaBooking status `delivered` in any of them. API: PATCH /api/v1/bookings/:id {"status":"delivered"}
  - steps: 1. Take a booking to `video_uploaded` with valid proof.
2. Search the pandit portal, the devotee booking page, the admin console and the mobile app for any control that confirms delivery.
3. Only a curl PATCH moves it.
  - observed: Grepping all four apps for a delivered transition returns nothing for puja bookings (the only hits are store-order statuses in apps/mobile/app/(store-admin)/orders.tsx). The state machine (booking.service.ts:291) has `video_uploaded: ['delivered','refunded']` and the route explicitly authorises both the booking owner and the assigned pandit to drive it (routes/bookings.ts:285-292) — but no client ever calls it. `curl -X PATCH .../bookings/<id> -d '{"status":"delivered"}'` returns 200.
  - expected: Either the devotee confirms receipt or the pandit closes the job from the portal.
  - customer_impact: Bookings pile up in `video_uploaded` forever. Since that is also the only transition that mints the payout (see PF-02), the whole revenue-recognition chain depends on a step no human can perform in the product.
  - fix: Add a "Confirm delivery" button on the devotee booking page (owner is already authorised) and/or a "Mark delivered" action in the pandit portal after proof upload.

### [PF-05] P1/broken-feature/S Video-proof direct-upload endpoint 400s with an internal TypeError instead of the designed 503
  - where: POST /api/v1/priest/bookings/:id/video-proof/signed-url. Code: apps/api/src/routes/priest.ts:299-305 (the r2.enabled guard), apps/api/src/lib/r2.ts:41-43, apps/api/src/lib/supabase.ts:10-34
  - steps: 1. As pandit@demo.com take a booking to `completed`.
2. POST /api/v1/priest/bookings/<id>/video-proof/signed-url with {"contentType":"video/mp4"}.
  - observed: `400 {"error":"supabaseAdmin.storage.from is not a function"}`. The endpoint has a guard that is supposed to answer `503 {"error":"Storage backend not configured.", "message":"Set R2_ACCOUNT_ID/... or SUPABASE_URL/SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY"}`, but it never fires: `r2.enabled` is `r2Enabled || supabaseEnabled` and `supabaseEnabled` is true whenever SUPABASE_URL + service key are set, while `lib/supabase.ts` separately treats `https://demo.supabase.co` as demo mode and returns a Proxy whose `storage` property is a bare function. Two different definitions of "storage is configured".
  - expected: Either a working presigned URL, or the intended actionable 503.
  - customer_impact: The one-tap in-app video upload the pandit is meant to use is dead, and the error tells them nothing. On prod a partial storage config produces the same crash instead of a diagnosable 503.
  - fix: Make r2.ts's `supabaseEnabled` reuse `isDemoMode` from lib/supabase.ts (or export a single `storageReady`), and wrap the presign call so a thrown TypeError becomes a 503 rather than a 400.

### [PF-06] P1/broken-feature/S A blank or junk proof URL is accepted, then permanently blocks delivery with no way to fix it in the UI
  - where: POST /api/v1/priest/bookings/:id/video (apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:1339-1376) + the "Or paste video URL" field on /priest/bookings/view (apps/web/components/provider/VideoProofUpload.tsx:77-89, no URL validation)
  - steps: 1. As pandit@demo.com take a booking to `completed`.
2. Paste two spaces (or any non-URL text) into "Or paste video URL" and submit.
3. Try to upload the real link.
4. Try to move the booking to delivered.
  - observed: Step 2: `200 {"message":"Video proof uploaded successfully.","booking":{"status":"video_uploaded","videoProofUrl":"  "}}`. Also accepted verbatim: `"not-a-url-at-all"`. Step 3: `400 {"error":"Video proof can only be uploaded for bookings in \"completed\" status. Current: \"video_uploaded\"."}`. Step 4: `409 {"error":"Video proof required","message":"Video proof is mandatory before a booking can move to \"delivered\"."}` — because the gate trims the stored value and a whitespace URL trims to empty. The booking is stuck: cannot re-upload, cannot deliver, so per PF-02 it can never be paid. The only repair found was `PATCH /bookings/:id {"status":"delivered","data":{"videoProofUrl":"..."}}` via curl, which worked (200).
  - expected: Reject a non-URL at the boundary, and let the assigned pandit replace a proof URL while the booking is still video_uploaded.
  - customer_impact: One fat-fingered paste permanently bricks a paid booking — no video for the devotee, no payout for the pandit, and support has to hand-run SQL/curl. The list page validates the URL with `new URL()`; the detail page (the one the UI actually routes you to) does not.
  - fix: Validate/trim `videoUrl` with a Zod `z.string().trim().url()` in the route, mirror the `new URL()` check into VideoProofUpload.handleManualSubmit, and allow re-upload while status is `video_uploaded`.

### [PF-07] P1/wrong-data/M Pandit booking list silently shows only the first 20 of 34 bookings, with wrong tab counts and no pagination
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings — tab strip and card list. Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:109 (fetch has no page/limit) + apps/api/src/services/priest.service.ts:159 (limit defaults to 20)
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com with more than 20 assigned bookings.
2. Open /priest/bookings and count the cards and the tab badges.
3. Compare with GET /api/v1/priest/bookings?limit=100.
  - observed: Server: `total: 30` (`priest_assigned: 21, video_uploaded: 6, delivered: 2, completed: 1`). Page rendered 20 cards and a tab strip reading `All 20 / Pending 0 / Accepted 15 / In Progress 0 / Completed 5` — so the pandit is told he has 15 accepted jobs when he has 21, and 5 completed when he has 9. A later run measured `total: 34, defaultPageReturns: 20, hiddenCount: 14`. There is no Next/Load-more control; the API already returns `total/page/limit/totalPages` and the page ignores all four.
  - expected: Either all bookings load, or a pagination control plus server-side counts.
  - customer_impact: A busy pandit stops seeing his oldest bookings — including unaccepted ones that then breach SLA — and the counts he plans his day around are wrong.
  - fix: Consume the API's `total/totalPages` and add pagination (or infinite scroll) in apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx; derive tab counts from server-side status counts rather than the current page.

### [PF-08] P1/broken-feature/S Booking detail page cannot open any booking past row 20
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings/view?id=<id>. Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/view/page.tsx:94-105
  - steps: 1. As pandit@demo.com, get a booking id that is not in the first 20 of GET /api/v1/priest/bookings (e.g. `b1330da2-c830-41ff-93da-ded81963b163`, BS-20260812-T6GL8, status video_uploaded).
2. Open /priest/bookings/view?id=<that id>.
  - observed: Page rendered only the shell — sidebar (Dashboard/Messages/Bookings/…/Sign Out) then straight to the site footer, no booking, no "Back to Bookings", no error text. The page has no by-id endpoint: it fetches `${API}/priest/bookings` (default 20 rows) and does `.find(b => b.id === bookingId)`, so for row 21+ it can only ever fall into `throw new Error('Booking not found')`.
  - expected: A booking that belongs to this pandit opens regardless of its position in the list.
  - customer_impact: The only screen with the video-proof control becomes unreachable for older bookings, so those can never be completed. It also downloads the whole booking list on every detail view.
  - fix: Add `GET /priest/bookings/:id` on the API and fetch that single record, instead of scanning a truncated list client-side.

### [PF-09] P1/wrong-data/S Every date in the pandit portal renders "Invalid Date"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings (each card) and http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings/view (Schedule & Location) and http://localhost:3000/priest (Upcoming Bookings). Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:55-58, view/page.tsx and priest/page.tsx:76-79 — all do `new Date(dateStr + 'T00:00:00')`
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com.
2. Open /priest/bookings and look at the date on any card.
3. Open any booking's detail page and look under Schedule & Location.
  - observed: List card text: `Kashi Vishwanath Temple, Varanasi / Invalid Date / 6:00 AM / 1 devotee`. Detail page: `Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh` then `Invalid Date` then `6:00 AM`. The API sends `scheduledDate: "2026-08-19T00:00:00.000Z"`, and the helper concatenates `'T00:00:00'` onto it producing `2026-08-19T00:00:00.000ZT00:00:00`, which parses to Invalid Date.
  - expected: "19 Aug 2026".
  - customer_impact: The pandit cannot tell which day any puja is on — from the booking list, the detail page, or the dashboard. He has to open the devotee chat to find out. For a scheduling product this is the most important field on the screen.
  - fix: Parse the ISO string directly (`new Date(dateStr)`), or slice to `YYYY-MM-DD` before appending the time. Same fix in all three files.

### [PF-10] P1/wrong-data/S Booking detail Price Breakdown shows "Platform Commission -₹NaN"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings/view?id=<id>, "Price Breakdown" card. Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/view/page.tsx:401 reads `booking.platformCommission`; apps/api/src/services/priest.service.ts:182-206 never selects it
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com.
2. Open any booking at /priest/bookings/view?id=<id>.
3. Read the Price Breakdown card.
  - observed: `Booking Amount ₹1,100 / Platform Commission -₹NaN / Your Payout ₹770`. GET /api/v1/priest/bookings returns fields `[id, bookingNumber, status, scheduledDate, scheduledTimeSlot, priceAmount, currency, priestPayout, devoteeNames, sankalpDetails, specialInstructions, videoProofUrl, videoUploadedAt, createdAt, temple, pujaType, user]` — no `platformCommission`, so `formatCurrency(undefined)` yields "₹NaN".
  - expected: "-₹330" (₹1,100 − ₹770).
  - customer_impact: The one screen where a pandit checks what the platform is taking shows a broken number. For a marketplace whose pitch is a transparent 20% take rate, "₹NaN" on the commission line destroys the trust the pitch is built on.
  - fix: Add `platformCommission: true` to the select in `priestService.getAssignedBookings`, or derive it client-side as priceAmount − priestPayout.

### [PF-11] P1/wrong-data/M Devotee reviews never reach the pandit — Reviews page shows 0 while the dashboard shows 43
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/reviews (GET /api/v1/priest/reviews, apps/api/src/routes/priest.ts:429-452) vs the Rating card on http://localhost:3000/priest. Writer: apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:1382-1463
  - steps: 1. As devotee@demo.com, submit a review on a completed booking: POST /api/v1/bookings/:id/review {"rating":5,"reviewText":"Excellent puja, very devoted pandit ji."} -> 200.
2. As pandit@demo.com open /priest (Rating card) and then /priest/reviews.
  - observed: Dashboard rating moved from `{ratingAvg: 4.6, ratingCount: 42}` to `{ratingAvg: 4.61, ratingCount: 43}`. GET /api/v1/priest/reviews returned `{reviews: [], total: 0}` both before and after — `priestReviewsEndpointRows: 0`. `submitReview` writes `PujaBooking.rating`/`reviewText` and bumps the `priests` aggregate, but never inserts a `Review` row, and /priest/reviews reads only the `Review` table (`providerId = userId, providerType = 'PANDIT'`).
  - expected: The 5-star review with its text appears on /priest/reviews and the two screens agree.
  - customer_impact: A pandit with 43 ratings sees an empty Reviews page and "0.0 average" — the two screens contradict each other. He can never read a word of devotee feedback, which is the main reason a provider opens that tab.
  - fix: Insert a `Review` row inside `submitReview` (providerId = priest's User id, providerType 'PANDIT'), or change GET /priest/reviews to read PujaBooking rating/reviewText for this priest. Backfill existing booking ratings either way.

### [PF-12] P2/wrong-data/S "Total Earned" on the Payouts page counts unpaid money and contradicts the dashboard's "Total Paid"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/payouts ("Total Earned" card) vs http://localhost:3000/priest (EarningsCard). Code: apps/api/src/services/priest.service.ts:453-457 (aggregate with no status filter) vs :73-77 (`status: 'completed'`)
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com.
2. Open /priest/payouts and read "Total Earned" and "Pending Payout".
3. Open /priest and read the earnings card.
  - observed: /priest/payouts: `Total Earned ₹2,310`, `Pending Payout ₹2,310`, `Last Payout N/A`, 3 rows all in `Pending`. /priest dashboard for the same account: `earnings: {total: 0, thisMonth: 0, pending: "2310"}`. The payouts summary sums every PriestPayout regardless of status; the dashboard sums only `completed` ones.
  - expected: One definition. "Total Earned" should either be lifetime paid (₹0 here) or be relabelled, and both screens should agree.
  - customer_impact: A pandit reads "Total Earned ₹2,310" on one screen and ₹0 on another in the same portal. Whichever is wrong, he will chase support about the missing money.
  - fix: Make the payouts summary use the same `status: 'completed'` filter as the dashboard and add a separate explicit "Pending" figure, or relabel the card to "Total Recorded".

### [PF-13] P2/security/S Invalid ?status leaks a raw Prisma error with absolute server paths and source lines
  - where: GET /api/v1/priest/bookings?status=<anything> and GET /api/v1/priest/payouts?status=<anything>. Code: apps/api/src/routes/priest.ts:40-46 (`status: z.string().optional()` — no enum)
  - steps: 1. Sign in as any pandit and get a bearer token.
2. curl -H 'Authorization: Bearer <tok>' 'http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/priest/bookings?status=not_a_status'
3. Same with /api/v1/priest/payouts?status=zzz
  - observed: `400 {"error":"\nInvalid `prisma.pujaBooking.count()` invocation in\nE:\\NewClaude\\bhaktisetu\\apps\\api\\src\\services\\priest.service.ts:211:26\n\n  208   skip,\n  209   take: limit,\n  210 }),\n→ 211 prisma.pujaBooking.count({...` — the response body contains the server's absolute filesystem path, the source file, the line number and surrounding source code. Identical leak from the payouts route at priest.service.ts:452:27.
  - expected: `400 {"error":"Invalid query"}` from the Zod layer, with the enum enforced.
  - customer_impact: Any authenticated pandit can map the server's directory layout and read fragments of source. It is also a 400 where the query should simply have been rejected cleanly.
  - fix: Change `status` to `z.enum([...BOOKING_STATUSES])` / `z.enum([...PAYOUT_STATUSES])` in bookingsQuerySchema and the payouts query, and add a global error serialiser that never returns `err.message` verbatim for Prisma errors.

### [PF-14] P2/dead-control/S On-duty toggle never reflects the saved state — pandit cannot tell if they are on duty, and needs two clicks to go off
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest, the "Go on duty for instant bookings" toggle (aria-label "Toggle on-duty"). Code: apps/web/app/priest/page.tsx:111 (`useState(false)`, never hydrated) + GET /api/v1/priest/dashboard which does not return isOnDuty
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com and open /priest.
2. Turn the on-duty toggle on (PATCH /priest/availability returns 200 and persists isOnDuty).
3. Reload the page.
  - observed: After reload: `aria-pressed="false"`, banner reads `Go on duty for instant bookings`, `showsOnDuty: false` — even though the server row is on duty. The component initialises `onDuty` to `false` unconditionally and the dashboard payload (`{priest, earnings, bookings, upcomingBookings}`) carries no isOnDuty field.
  - expected: The toggle reflects Priest.isOnDuty on load.
  - customer_impact: The pandit can never see whether he is in the instant-dispatch pool. To go OFF duty after a reload he must first click ON (sending isOnDuty:true) and then click again — so a pandit trying to stop receiving 1-minute-SLA offers actually re-arms himself first.
  - fix: Return `isOnDuty` (and `lastSeenAt`) from GET /priest/dashboard and seed the toggle state from it.

### [PF-15] P2/ux/S When the bookings API fails the pandit is told "You have no assigned bookings yet"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/priest/bookings (empty state) and /priest/payouts. Code: apps/web/app/priest/bookings/page.tsx:112-122 and payouts/page.tsx:99-111 — a non-OK response is thrown and caught into an empty array
  - steps: 1. Sign in as pandit@demo.com.

Reproduced identically when the API was unreachable: tab strip `All 0 / Pending 0 / Accepted 0 / In Progress 0 / Completed 0` for an account holding 25 bookings. Same swallow on /priest/payouts (falls back to `{totalEarned: 0, pending: 0}`).

  - customer_impact: During any API hiccup every pandit is told they have no jobs today. They stop checking, bookings breach SLA, and the failure is invisible to the founder because the UI reports a normal empty day.
