## AREA: devotee-booking   score=62

### [DB-01] P0/wrong-data/S My Bookings shows only 20 of the devotee's 33 bookings — no pagination, and the tab counts lie
  - where: http://localhost:3000/bookings — apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:191 (fetch) and :220-225 (tab counts)
  - steps: 1. Log in as devotee@demo.com.
2. Open http://localhost:3000/bookings and wait for hydration.
3. Read the tab row: it says "All 20".
4. In a terminal, call the API the page itself calls, but ask for everything: GET /api/v1/bookings/my?limit=100 with the devotee's Bearer token.
  - observed: The page renders exactly 20 booking cards and the tabs read "All 20 · Upcoming 12 · Completed 7 · Cancelled 1". The API says otherwise: GET /api/v1/bookings/my returns {"total":33,"page":1,"limit":20,"totalPages":2}; GET /api/v1/bookings/my?limit=100 returns 33 rows. The page fetches `${API_BASE_URL}/bookings/my` with no page or limit param (apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:191), so the server's default limit of 20 (apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:716 `limit: Math.min(Number(limit) || 20, 100)`) silently truncates. There is no "Load more", no page control, and no row anywhere on the page mentioning 33. The tab counts are computed from the already-truncated array (`count: bookings.length`), so "All 20" is presented as the total. Concretely: booking BS-20260812-QXUWN — the one I created by clicking "Pay ₹1,151 with Razorpay" in the UI — is in the 33 but not in the first 20, so it never appears.
  - expected: All 33 bookings reachable, either by paginating or by requesting a higher limit, and the "All" tab count should equal the API's `total` (33), not the length of the truncated page.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who has paid for a puja cannot find it. The most recent booking is not guaranteed to be in the visible 20, so the very order someone just paid for can be missing from My Bookings — which reads as "you took my money and lost my booking". It also breaks the support loop: the customer cannot quote a booking number they cannot see. Any devotee past their 20th booking (exactly the repeat customers worth the most) is permanently affected.
  - fix: In apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:191 request the full set or paginate: fetch `${API_BASE_URL}/bookings/my?limit=100` (or thread `page` through and render a Load-more using the `totalPages`/`total` the API already returns). Then derive the "All" tab count from the API's `total` field rather than `bookings.length`.

### [DB-02] P1/ux/S Raw database enum leaks as the status badge right after payment — a paid booking reads "confirmed", unpaid ones read "pending_payment"
  - where: http://localhost:3000/bookings — apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:92-103 `getStatusConfig`, rendered at :338
  - steps: 1. Log in as devotee@demo.com.
2. Open http://localhost:3000/bookings and wait for the rows to render.
3. Look at the status pill on row BS-20260812-3GTL7 (a paid booking) and on any BS-…-S5VSQ / IMJRW / M9OBX row.
  - observed: Literal on-screen text captured from the rendered page: "BS-20260812-3GTL7 confirmed Rudrabhishek Kashi Vishwanath Temple, Varanasi 12 Sept 2026 7:00 AM ₹1,100 View Details Cancel" and "BS-20260812-S5VSQ pending_payment Ganesh Puja Tirumala Tirupati Devasthanam, Tirupati 26 Sept 2026 6:00 AM ₹802 View Details Cancel". Eight rows showed the literal string `pending_payment` (underscore and all) and one showed `confirmed`, both in the default grey fallback style. Every other status rendered correctly in the same list: "Cancelled", "Completed", "Priest Assigned", "Video Ready", "Delivered", "In Progress". Root cause: the Prisma enum has 9 values (apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma:93-103 — pending_payment, confirmed, priest_assigned, in_progress, completed, video_uploaded, delivered, cancelled, refunded) but `getStatusConfig`'s map has only 7 keys and is missing `pending_payment`, `confirmed` and `refunded`; it also carries a `booked` key that is not a valid enum value at all. The fallback is `{ label: status }`, i.e. print the raw enum. `confirmed` is the status a booking gets the instant payment verifies (apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:660 `data: { status: 'confirmed' }`) and again after a reschedule.
  - expected: A human-readable, styled pill — e.g. "Confirmed" (green), "Payment Pending" (amber), "Refunded". The sibling detail page already does this correctly (apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:232 maps pending_payment → "Payment Pending").
  - customer_impact: This is the first screen a customer sees after paying — the success screen's "View Booking" CTA links straight to /bookings. Their brand-new ₹1,151 order greets them with a grey lowercase `confirmed` and, if they abandoned an earlier attempt, several `pending_payment` rows. It reads as a broken/unfinished product at the single highest-trust moment in the funnel, on a page where the customer is deciding whether their money is safe.
  - fix: Add the three missing keys to the map in apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:92-103 (`pending_payment` → "Payment Pending"/amber, `confirmed` → "Confirmed"/green, `refunded` → "Refunded"/red), drop the dead `booked` key, and type the map against the real enum so the compiler catches the next addition. Reuse the labels already in apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:232.

### [DB-03] P1/broken-feature/M Clicking Pay creates and persists a real booking before the gateway opens; if checkout fails the devotee is told nothing happened but a slot-holding booking exists with no way to pay it
  - where: http://localhost:3000/pujas/book?slug=rudrabhishek step 4 → apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:800 (create-order) then :863-873 (abort); apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1704-1721
  - steps: 1. Log in as devotee@demo.com and go to http://localhost:3000/pujas/book?slug=rudrabhishek.
2. Pick Kashi Vishwanath, keep the default date, pick "Morning (7:00 AM)", enter a devotee name, tick the terms box, reach the Payment step.
3. Click "Pay ₹1,151 with Razorpay".
4. Read the red banner, then check GET /api/v1/bookings/my?limit=100 with your Bearer token.
  - observed: The UI shows: "Payment Failed — Payments are temporarily unavailable — the payment gateway is not configured, so we did not open checkout. You have NOT been charged. Please try again in a few minutes, or email support@darshya.online and we will complete this booking for you." But the network trace shows the booking was already committed before that: POST /api/v1/bookings/create-order → 201 {"orderId":"648307ee-8548-4bb7-afd2-5d0ef345f742","bookingId":"648307ee-8548-4bb7-afd2-5d0ef345f742","bookingNumber":"BS-20260812-QXUWN","razorpayOrderId":"order_demo_1786520479019","amount":115100,"currency":"INR"}, and only afterwards GET /api/v1/payments/config → 200 {"keyId":null,"isDemo":true} which is what triggered the abort. The API confirms BS-20260812-QXUWN persists with status pending_payment and priceAmount 1151. The same devotee already carries 8 other pending_payment rows from earlier aborted attempts. On /bookings those rows appear under the "Upcoming" tab (isUpcoming at apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:127-136 does not exclude pending_payment) with only a "Cancel" button — there is no "Complete payment" / "Pay now" control anywhere, and the detail page's pending_payment banner (apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:620-630) is text-only with no CTA either. Each row also holds one of the offering's 5 slot seats for UNPAID_HOLD_MINUTES = 30 (apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:150).
  - expected: Either mint the booking only once the gateway session is actually opening, or — if the booking must exist first — give every pending_payment booking a working "Complete payment" action, and say so in the failure copy instead of implying nothing was created.
  - customer_impact: Two costs. For the customer: their My Bookings quietly fills with unpaid ghost bookings they were told did not happen, they cannot pay for any of them, and their only option is to cancel. For the founder: every abandoned or failed checkout is silently unrecoverable revenue — there is no resume-payment path at all, and five abandoned attempts on one popular slot lock that slot out for 30 minutes for everyone else.
  - fix: Add a "Complete payment" action on both /bookings rows and /bookings/view for status pending_payment that re-enters the create-order/checkout flow (the server already reuses the existing initiated payment idempotently). Exclude pending_payment from the Upcoming tab. Correct the failure copy to state that a reservation was held and how to complete or release it. Longer term, hold the slot without a full booking row until the gateway session opens.

### [DB-04] P1/dead-control/M All four filter dropdowns and the Sort button on /pujas are dead — they are static markup in a server component with no handler
  - where: http://localhost:3000/pujas — apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx:472-521 (four <select> at :474, :485, :496, :507; sort <button> at :517)
  - steps: 1. Open http://localhost:3000/pujas (no login needed).
2. Note the 10 puja cards and their order.
3. Change the Category dropdown to "Abhishekam". Then Deity to "Lord Hanuman". Then Price to "Under ₹500". Then click the "Sort: Popularity" button.
4. Compare the card list after each action.
  - observed: I drove every option of every dropdown programmatically (9 category options, 11 deity, 10 occasion, 6 price = 36 selections) and the rendered card list was byte-identical after each one — always ["Rudrabhishek","Ganesh Puja","Satyanarayan Katha","Maha Mrityunjay Jaap","Lakshmi Puja","Navgraha Shanti Puja","Sunderkand Path","Hanuman Chalisa Path",...] and "Showing 10 of 10 pujas". Selecting "Under ₹500" still shows the ₹3,501 Ram Charit Manas Path. In the source, PujasPage is `export default async function` (a server component) and each <select> has no `name`, no `value`, no `defaultValue`, no `onChange`, and sits outside any <form>; the "Sort: Popularity" <button> at :517 has no onClick and no href. No network request and no console error fires on any of them — they cannot do anything by construction.
  - expected: Each control filters/sorts the catalogue, or is removed until it does. The search box directly above them was already converted to a working GET form (?q=) and works correctly, which is the pattern to copy.
  - customer_impact: Five controls that look like the primary way to navigate the catalogue do literally nothing. A devotee looking for a Hanuman puja under ₹500 sets both dropdowns, sees an unchanged list including ₹3,501 items, and reasonably concludes the site is broken or has nothing for them — then leaves. It is the most-clicked surface on the browse page in the paid funnel, and it silently fails.
  - fix: Follow the same pattern the search box already uses (apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx:359-375): put the four selects inside the same GET <form action="/pujas">, give them `name` (category/deity/occasion/price) and `defaultValue` from searchParams, add a submit, and extend the server-side filter beside `matchesQuery` at :300-306. Make the Sort button a Link that sets ?sort=. If any facet is not going to be implemented now, delete it rather than shipping it inert.

### [DB-05] P1/broken-feature/S POST /bookings/create-order charges the ₹150 prasad delivery fee with no delivery address — booking is created with prasadDeliveryAddressId null
  - where: POST http://localhost:4000/api/v1/bookings/create-order — schema at apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1635-1646, address write at :1683-1701
  - steps: 1. Get a devotee Bearer token: POST /api/v1/auth/login {"email":"devotee@demo.com","password":"demo123"}.
2. POST /api/v1/bookings/create-order with a valid pujaSlug/templeOfferingId/future date/slot/one devotee and `"prasadDelivery": true` and NO `deliveryAddress` key at all.
3. Read the response amount, then GET /api/v1/bookings/<bookingId>.
  - observed: HTTP 201: {"orderId":"f98660c8-...","bookingNumber":"BS-20260812-PXP93","razorpayOrderId":"order_demo_1786513663296","amount":130100,"currency":"INR"} — i.e. ₹1,301 = ₹1,100 base + ₹51 platform + ₹150 prasad delivery. Fetching the booking back gives: priceAmount = 1301, prasadDeliveryRequested = True, prasadDeliveryAddressId = None. The row is also visible on My Bookings as "BS-20260812-PXP93 pending_payment Rudrabhishek Kashi Vishwanath Temple, Varanasi 1 Sept 2026 6:00 AM ₹1,301". The Zod schema declares `deliveryAddress` as `.optional()` with no cross-field refinement against `prasadDelivery`, and the route only writes an address `if (body.prasadDelivery && body.deliveryAddress)`, so the ₹150 is charged unconditionally while the address is silently dropped. The web wizard does validate the address client-side (6 "is required" errors, confirmed live), so this is reachable via any non-browser client — the mobile app, a retried request, or anything hitting the API directly.
  - expected: 400 Validation Error when prasadDelivery is true and deliveryAddress is absent — the same way a bad pincode or phone is already rejected — so a prasad fee is never taken without somewhere to ship it.
  - customer_impact: A devotee is charged ₹150 for prasad delivery that physically cannot be fulfilled: ops has a booking flagged prasadDeliveryRequested with a null address. That becomes a refund, a support ticket, and a broken promise on the most emotionally loaded part of the order. The client-side guard means it will surface as a rare, hard-to-reproduce complaint rather than an obvious bug.
  - fix: Add a `.superRefine` (or `.refine`) to createOrderBodySchema at apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1635-1646 requiring `deliveryAddress` whenever `prasadDelivery === true`, and make the fee conditional on a persisted address id rather than on the boolean alone. The sibling POST /from-offering path (:1817+) has the same shape and needs the same guard.

### [DB-06] P1/legal/S Checkout and the puja page promise "100% Money-Back" / "100% refund if not satisfied" while the shipped policy is a 100/75/50/25 ladder that pays 100% only 48h+ out
  - where: apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:2265 (payment step trust badge) and apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:382 (detail page badge); actual policy at apps/api/src/services/cancellation.service.ts:47-58
  - steps: 1. Open http://localhost:3000/pujas/rudrabhishek and read the trust badges — one says "Money-Back Guarantee — 100% refund if not satisfied".
2. Book it through to the Payment step (temple → date → slot → name → terms) and read the badge row just above the Pay button.
3. Compare with the checkbox text on the previous step and with DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY in the API.
  - observed: On the live Payment screen, immediately above "Pay ₹1,151 with Razorpay", the badges read: "Secure Payment · Video Proof Guaranteed · 100% Money-Back". The detail page badge reads "Money-Back Guarantee / 100% refund if not satisfied". The terms checkbox one step earlier says something different: "I understand that cancellation is allowed up to 24 hours before the scheduled puja time." And the shipped policy is different again — DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY tiers: 48h+ → 1.0, 24-48h → 0.75, 4-24h → 0.5, <4h → 0.25; on top of that, apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:850-856 hard-refuses any cancellation inside 24h ("Cancellation is not allowed within 24 hours of the scheduled puja time."). So a devotee who cancels 30 hours out gets 75%, not 100%, and one who is simply "not satisfied" after the puja has no refund path in the code at all — no dissatisfaction-based refund exists anywhere.
  - expected: The badge should state the real policy (e.g. "Free cancellation 48h+ before") or link to the Refund Policy, and the three surfaces should agree.
  - customer_impact: An unconditional "100% refund if not satisfied" shown at the moment of payment, on a product where no such refund exists, is a straightforward misrepresentation — the kind of unfair-trade-practice claim that Indian consumer forums and payment-gateway chargeback teams act on. Practically it also guarantees angry disputes: the customer will quote the badge back at support, and support will have to refuse.
  - fix: Replace both strings with the actual guarantee. On the payment step (apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:2265) use the top tier verbatim, e.g. "Free cancellation 48h+ before", and on the detail badge (apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:382) drop "if not satisfied". Ideally render the ladder from DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY so copy and code cannot drift again. Separately note that the 24h hard block makes the 50% and 25% tiers unreachable — pick one behaviour.

### [DB-07] P2/broken-feature/S A single devotee can consume an entire slot's capacity by abandoning checkout five times, and the API accepts bookings years into the future
  - where: POST http://localhost:4000/api/v1/bookings/create-order — capacity check via apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts (UNPAID_HOLD_MINUTES=150); no upper date bound in the schema at apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1610-1621
  - steps: 1. Get a devotee Bearer token.
2. GET /api/v1/bookings/slots?templeId=<ganesh offering id>&date=<45 days out> and note remaining:5.
3. POST /bookings/create-order six times with identical pujaSlug/offering/date/slot and any devotee name.
4. Re-read /bookings/slots for the same date.
5. Separately, POST create-order with scheduledDate "2031-01-01".
  - observed: Attempts 0-4 each returned 201 with a distinct booking (BS-20260812-IMJRW, M9OBX, KXVGX, B6R1Z, S5VSQ), all for the same offering+date+slot and all owned by the same user; attempt 5 returned 409 {"error":"The selected time slot is no longer available.","code":"SLOT_UNAVAILABLE"}. GET /slots then reported the 06:00 slot as {"remaining":0,"total":5} while the other six slots stayed at 5. So the capacity guard works, but nothing stops one unpaid user from taking all five seats — the holds only expire after UNPAID_HOLD_MINUTES = 30. Separately, `scheduledDate: "2031-01-01"` returned 201 and the resulting booking BS-20260812-0UEIV renders on My Bookings as "1 Jan 2031"; the schema at :1610-1621 only refines against past dates, never an upper bound, while the web picker caps at getMaxDate(). Also minor: a slot string that was never offered ("99:99") answers 409 "no longer available" rather than "not a valid slot".
  - expected: A per-user cap on concurrent unpaid holds for the same offering+date+slot (one is enough), and a server-side maximum booking horizon mirroring the web picker's max.
  - customer_impact: A popular slot at a marquee temple can be blanked out for 30 minutes by one person clicking Pay five times — no malice required, since every failed gateway attempt mints another hold (see DB-03). Real devotees see "0 spots left" for a slot nobody paid for. The unbounded date lets a mis-typed year create a booking five years out that no pandit will ever be assigned to and that sits in Upcoming forever.
  - fix: Before creating the hold, reject when the same userId already has a pending_payment booking on the same templePujaOfferingId + scheduledDate + scheduledTimeSlot (reuse it instead — the payment service is already idempotent per booking). Add a max-date refinement to createOrderBodySchema matching the client's getMaxDate() window. Return a distinct 400 for a slot time that is not in the offering's availableSlots, as POST /from-offering already does at :1992.

### [DB-08] P2/broken-feature/S A completed booking can never be reviewed — the review endpoint rejects the "completed" status it is meant to serve
  - where: POST http://localhost:4000/api/v1/bookings/<id>/review; called from apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:367
  - steps: 1. Get a devotee Bearer token.
2. Find a booking with status "completed" via GET /api/v1/bookings/my?limit=100 (e.g. BS-20260812-JMHM4).
3. POST /api/v1/bookings/<that id>/review with {"rating":5,"reviewText":"QA review"}.
  - observed: HTTP 409: {"error":"Reviews can only be submitted for bookings in \"delivered\" or \"video_uploaded\" status. Current: \"completed\"."} The devotee's list currently holds 3 bookings in `completed` (BS-20260812-9HWMA, JMHM4, A6UON), all rendered on /bookings with the friendly pill "Completed" — and none of them can be rated. Rating bounds are handled correctly (rating 9 → 400 "Number must be less than or equal to 5").
  - expected: `completed` should be reviewable — it is the terminal success state a customer recognises. At minimum the allowed set should include it alongside delivered/video_uploaded.
  - customer_impact: Every devotee whose booking lands in `completed` loses the ability to rate their pandit, and the product loses the review that drives the next devotee's trust — reviews are the whole social-proof engine for a marketplace. If the UI surfaces a Rate control for these bookings the customer gets a bare 409 for their trouble.
  - fix: Add 'completed' to the allowed-status list in the review handler in apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts (search the string "Reviews can only be submitted for bookings in"), or converge the lifecycle so bookings do not rest in `completed`. Add a regression test covering all three terminal statuses.

### [DB-09] P2/wrong-data/S Booking detail shows a "Video Ready" / "Watch Video" affordance for records whose videoProofUrl is an empty string or not a URL
  - where: http://localhost:3000/bookings and /bookings/view?id=… — video block at apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:745-770; list CTA gated on `booking.videoUrl` at apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:384
  - steps: 1. Get a devotee Bearer token and GET /api/v1/bookings/my?limit=100.
2. Pick the rows with status video_uploaded / delivered and GET /api/v1/bookings/<id> for each; read videoProofUrl.
3. Open /bookings and look at the same rows.
  - observed: Four such rows, and the stored URLs are: BS-20260812-QX6DC status=video_uploaded videoProofUrl="" (empty string) uploadedAt=2026-08-12T06:34:20.968Z; BS-20260812-IACI5 status=delivered videoProofUrl="not a url at all"; BS-20260812-683BK status=delivered videoProofUrl="https://drive.google.com/CORRECT-LINK"; BS-20260812-STNEO status=video_uploaded videoProofUrl="https://cdn.example.com/c1.mp4". On /bookings, BS-20260812-QX6DC renders the pill "Video Ready" with no Watch Video button (empty string is falsy), while BS-20260812-683BK renders "Delivered ... Watch Video" pointing at a link that cannot play. Nothing validates videoProofUrl on write, and nothing reconciles it with the status. Caveat I want to be straight about: some of these values look like they were written by another test run against this shared local DB, so treat the specific strings as illustrative — the reportable part is that the API accepts and the UI trusts an unvalidated, possibly empty videoProofUrl while advertising the video as ready.
  - expected: videoProofUrl should be validated as an http(s) URL on write, and a booking should not be able to sit in video_uploaded/delivered with an empty or malformed one; the UI should show a clear "video pending" state rather than a broken promise.
  - customer_impact: Video proof is the headline guarantee of the product — the payment screen literally badges "Video Proof Guaranteed". A devotee told their video is ready, who then finds no player or a dead link, has been promised the one thing they paid extra trust for and not given it. It is also unrecoverable from the customer's side: there is no "report missing video" path on the page.
  - fix: Validate with `z.string().url()` on whichever endpoint sets videoProofUrl (priest/admin upload), reject the transition to video_uploaded when the URL is empty, and in apps/web/app/bookings/view/page.tsx:745-770 render an explicit "Video is being processed — contact support if it does not appear" state when the status says ready but the URL is missing or unparseable.

### [DB-10] P3/ux/S Typing a past or out-of-range date into the booking date field silently snaps back with no message
  - where: http://localhost:3000/pujas/book?slug=rudrabhishek step 1, "Select Date" — apps/web/components/ui/DateInput.tsx:139-147 (handleTextBlur)
  - steps: 1. Open http://localhost:3000/pujas/book?slug=rudrabhishek.
2. The date field is a text input showing "13/08/2026" with a DD/MM/YYYY placeholder (no native date picker).
3. Clear it, type 01/01/2020, and click elsewhere.
  - observed: The field reverts to "13/08/2026" with no error text, no red border, and no explanation. In the source, handleTextBlur only calls onChange when `isInRange(iso, min, max)`; otherwise it does `setDisplay(value ? formatForDisplay(value) : '')` — a silent revert. The same silent behaviour applies to a date beyond the booking window. The guard itself is correct (the past date never reaches the server, and the API independently rejects it with 400 "Cannot book a past date."), and the existing `errors.date` slot right above the input at page.tsx:1474-1478 is never populated for this case.
  - expected: An inline message such as "Bookings open from 13 Aug 2026" (or whatever min/max are), consistent with the clear messages the rest of the wizard gives ("Name is required", "Please agree to the terms & conditions").
  - customer_impact: Low but real friction at the top of the funnel: a devotee typing a date the system will not accept gets no explanation for why their input keeps vanishing, and may try two or three times before giving up or assuming the field is broken. Everything else in this wizard explains itself, so the silence is conspicuous.
  - fix: In apps/web/components/ui/DateInput.tsx:139-147, surface the rejection — either an internal error string rendered under the input, or an onInvalid callback the booking page can route into its existing `errors.date` slot at apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:1474.

## AREA: admin-ops   score=24

### [AO-01] P0/broken-feature/S Block and Delete on /admin/users report success but persist nothing
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/users - Ban (Block) and Trash (Delete) icon buttons on every row; apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:162 handleToggleBlock and :195 handleDelete; apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:192 updateUser
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/users.
2. Click the Ban icon on any row - the Status badge flips to 'blocked'.
3. Click the Trash icon on another row, then Delete in the popover - the row disappears.
4. Reload the page. Both users are back and both show Status 'active'.
5. Confirm at the API: curl -X PATCH http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/users/47542478-977f-4d9d-bab0-4b1212e400c8 -H 'Authorization: Bearer <admin>' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"blocked":true}' then GET the same user.
  - observed: PATCH returns 200 {"message":"User updated","userId":"47542478-977f-4d9d-bab0-4b1212e400c8"} and the subsequent GET /admin/users/<id> returns {"blocked":false,"blockedAt":null} - unchanged. adminService.updateUser accepts `blocked` in its signature and in userUpdateSchema but never writes it; only `roles` and `approved` are applied. The User model does have blocked / blockedReason / blockedAt columns (prisma/schema.prisma:187-189), so the data is simply never written. The UI mutates local state optimistically, so it always looks like it worked.
  - expected: PATCH {blocked:true} sets User.blocked = true and stamps blockedAt, the change survives a reload, and the blocked user can no longer sign in. If the field is not implemented, the endpoint must return an error and the buttons must not be shown.
  - customer_impact: This is the platform's only moderation control. An operator who blocks a fraudulent pandit or an abusive devotee gets a green confirmation and moves on; the account stays fully live and can keep taking bookings and money. The Delete button is worse - the row vanishes from the admin's view, so they have positive evidence the account is gone while it is untouched. There is no way for the operator to discover the failure short of reloading and re-checking every action.
  - fix: In adminService.updateUser, add a prisma.user.update writing { blocked, blockedAt: blocked ? new Date() : null, blockedReason } when updates.blocked !== undefined, and include blocked/blockedAt in the getUsers and getUserById projections so the table's Status column reflects reality.

### [AO-02] P0/dead-control/M "Pay Now" on /admin/revenue is a dead button - no handler, no request, no feedback
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/revenue - Pending Payouts table, Action column; apps/web/app/admin/revenue/page.tsx:339
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/revenue.
2. Scroll to the 'Pending Payouts' card - it lists provider rows with a green 'Pay Now' button each.
3. Click 'Pay Now'.
4. Watch the network tab and re-read the page.
  - observed: 3 'Pay Now' buttons rendered. On click: instrumented result 'any non-GET request fired? false | new netfails: 0', and GET /admin/revenue afterwards still returns pendingPayouts: 3200 (unchanged). No toast, no confirmation dialog, no error, no spinner - nothing at all happens. Source confirms it: the <button> at revenue/page.tsx:339 has className only, no onClick.
  - expected: Either the button opens a payout confirmation (provider, amount, reference) and calls POST /admin/bookings/payout-requests/:id/process, or it is removed until the payout rail is wired.
  - customer_impact: This is the single button in the entire admin UI that looks like it moves money to a provider, sitting in a table headed 'Rs. 3,200 due'. An operator clicks it, sees nothing break, and reasonably concludes the payout went through. Providers do not get paid, and the operator has no signal that anything failed. Combined with AO-03 there is no working payout path at all.
  - fix: Wire an onClick that opens a confirm modal and calls the existing POST /api/v1/admin/bookings/payout-requests/:id/process (action: 'complete', reference), then refetch. Until then, delete the button rather than ship a control that no-ops on a money screen.

### [AO-03] P0/parity/L No admin page exists for payouts, payout-requests, refunds or provider KYC - the APIs are unreachable
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/payouts, /admin/payout-requests, /admin/refunds, /admin/bookings, /admin/kyc; sidebar in apps/web/app/admin/layout.tsx:30-46
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com.
2. Read the admin sidebar - Dashboard, Users, Approvals, Leads, Outreach, Waitlist, Support, Ratings, Commissions, Subscriptions, Revenue, Reports, Metrics, Agents, Settings. No payouts, no refunds, no bookings, no KYC.
3. Navigate directly to /admin/payouts, /admin/payout-requests, /admin/refunds, /admin/bookings, /admin/kyc.
  - observed: All five return HTTP 404. Meanwhile the backend endpoints are live and answer 200 for an admin bearer: GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/payouts/pending -> {"ok":true,"groups":[{"providerName":"Demo Pandit","role":"PRIEST","bookingsCount":12,"totalPayout":9900},{"providerName":"Demo Wellness","totalPayout":2800},{"providerName":"Acharya Vinod Sharma","totalPayout":400}]}; GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/payout-requests -> {"requests":[]}; POST /api/v1/admin/bookings/payout-requests/:id/process (actions complete|reject|fail); GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/refunds/pending -> 200; GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/provider-kyc -> 200.
  - expected: Sidebar entries and pages for Payouts (with the pending-payout groups and a process action), Refunds, and Provider KYC, so an operator can settle providers and refund devotees without curl.
  - customer_impact: Day one, a founder cannot pay a single provider or refund a single devotee from the admin panel. The system already knows Rs 13,100 is owed across three providers and exposes an endpoint to settle it - there is simply no screen. Every payout and every refund has to be run by hand against the API, which for a solo founder means they will not happen on schedule, which is the fastest way to lose supply.
  - fix: Add /admin/payouts (GET /admin/bookings/payouts/pending + GET /admin/bookings/payout-requests with the process action) and /admin/refunds (GET /admin/bookings/refunds/pending), plus sidebar links. The API contract already exists, so this is UI-only.

### [AO-04] P0/wrong-data/M Headline platform revenue is structurally always Rs. 0 - two admin screens say Rs. 0 while Reports shows Rs. 58.7K
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin ('Revenue This Month' tile) and /admin/revenue ('Total Revenue' card); apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:375 (mtdRevenueAgg over prisma.transaction)
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com, open /admin - read the third stat tile.
2. Open /admin/revenue - read the first overview card.
3. Open /admin/reports - read the Popular Pujas card.
  - observed: Dashboard tile reads 'Rs. 0 / Revenue This Month'. Revenue card reads 'Rs. 0 / Total Revenue'. /admin/reports on the adjacent screen reads 'Rudrabhishek - 49 bookings - Rs. 54.7K' and 'Ganesh Puja - 5 bookings - Rs. 4.0K'. API confirms: /admin/dashboard totalRevenue: 0, recentTransactions: []; /admin/reports popularPujas revenueInr 54662 and 4010. Root cause: both Rs. 0 figures sum the Transaction table, and grep shows prisma.transaction.create is called from exactly one file - services/subscription.service.ts (lines 298, 426). No puja booking, store order, donation, astrology consultation or wellness enrollment ever writes a Transaction row, so these tiles can only ever show marketplace-subscription revenue.
  - expected: 'Revenue This Month' should reflect captured marketplace GMV (Payment rows with status 'captured', or the commission ledger), and must not disagree with the revenue shown on /admin/reports.
  - customer_impact: The founder's single most important number is wrong by construction and is wrong in the direction that hides success - it will read Rs. 0 on the day the platform takes its first Rs. 100,000. Two admin screens flatly contradict a third. Any decision made off this tile (spend on acquisition, runway, whether the marketplace is working) is made on a fabricated zero.
  - fix: Point getDashboard.totalRevenue and getRevenue's Total Revenue at the same source the /admin/metrics page already uses - prisma.payment.aggregate({where:{status:'captured', createdAt:{gte:monthStart}}}) - or derive from the Commission ledger. Whichever is chosen, /admin, /admin/revenue, /admin/reports and /admin/metrics must all read the same one.

  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/users - user name links and the Eye 'View' button; apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:298 and :327 href={`/admin/users/${user.id}`}
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/users.
2. Click any user's name, or the Eye icon in the Actions column.

Console: 'Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 404 (Not Found)'. Network: '404 GET http://localhost:3000/admin/users/47542478-977f-4d9d-bab0-4b1212e400c8'. The app/admin/users directory contains only page.tsx and view/page.tsx - there is no [id] segment.
  - expected: Clicking a user opens that user's record.
  - customer_impact: There are 24 dead links on the first page of the user table alone (12 rows x name + eye). The user list is the entry point for every support and moderation task, and it leads nowhere - an operator handling 'this pandit is a fraud' or 'this devotee says they were charged twice' cannot open the account.

  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/users/view?id=<uuid>; apps/web/app/admin/users/view/page.tsx:29 comment '// Mock user data keyed by ID'
  - steps: 1. Get a real user id: curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/users?limit=5' -H 'Authorization: Bearer <admin>' -> 47542478-977f-4d9d-bab0-4b1212e400c8 (Hinganghat Goseva Samiti, gaushala.hinganghat@darshya.online).

The real name and email are absent (instrumented: 'shows real user name? false  shows real email? false'). The page makes no API call - it looks the id up in a hardcoded UserProfile map.
  - expected: The page fetches GET /api/v1/admin/users/:id (which exists and returns the full user with profile) and renders it.

### [AO-07] P1/wrong-data/M User list silently truncates at 20 of 23 - the count shown, the filters, the pagination and the CSV export are all wrong
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/users footer 'Showing 1-10 of 20 users'; apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:123 getUsers(token) called with no limit; apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:94 limit default 20
  - steps: 1. curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/users?limit=500' -H 'Authorization: Bearer <admin>' and note total.
2. Open /admin/users and read the footer.
3. Click through both numbered pages and count rows.
4. Set the Role filter to 'Pandit'.
  - observed: API reports total 23 (23 users returned). The page footer reads 'Showing 1-10 of 20 users'; paginating both pages yields exactly 20 distinct rows, 0 duplicates, 3 users never reachable. Role filter Pandit -> 'Showing 1-2 of 2 users' while the API role census says PANDIT: 3. Role filter Devotee -> 2 while the API says DEVOTEE: 3. The page fetches one unparameterised page of 20 and then filters/paginates client-side, so every derived number inherits the truncation.
  - expected: The footer count matches the API total, all 23 users are reachable, and the Pandit filter returns 3.
  - customer_impact: The admin cannot see part of their own user base and is not told. It is worse than an empty list because the numbers look authoritative - '2 pandits' when there are 3. Today it hides 3 users; at 500 users it hides 480. Every filter, the pagination, and the Export Users CSV (AO-13) are all downstream of the same truncated array.
  - fix: Move filtering and pagination server-side (getUsers already accepts role, status, search, page, limit and returns total), and render the API's `total` in the footer rather than filtered.length.

### [AO-08] P1/dead-control/S Status filter on /admin/users is inert and the per-row Approve button can never appear
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/users - Status dropdown (All/Active/Pending/Blocked) and the per-row UserCheck Approve button; apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:73 mapStatus, :333 {user.status === 'pending' && ...}
  - steps: 1. Open /admin/users.
2. Set the Status dropdown to 'Pending'. Then to 'Blocked'. Then back to 'Active'.
3. Look for any Approve (person-with-tick) icon in the Actions column.
  - observed: Status=pending -> 'Showing 0-0 of 0 users'. Status=blocked -> 'Showing 0-0 of 0 users'. Status=active -> 'Showing 1-10 of 20 users' (i.e. all of them). The Approve icon never renders on any row. Cause: GET /admin/users returns only {id, fullName, email, phone, roles, subscriptionTier, createdAt} - there is no status or blocked field - so mapStatus() falls through to 'active' for every user, permanently.
  - expected: Status reflects User.blocked / approval state; 'Blocked' returns blocked users; the Approve action is available on users awaiting approval.
  - customer_impact: Two of the three filters on the main user screen are decorative, and a per-row action the design provides is unreachable. An operator who blocks someone (which itself no-ops, AO-01) can never filter back to find them. The dropdown actively misleads: 'Blocked: 0 users' is read as 'nobody is blocked'.
  - fix: Return blocked/blockedAt and an approval flag from adminService.getUsers, map them in mapStatus, and pass the status filter through to the API (the query schema already accepts it).

The subscriptions endpoint literally returns subscriptions: [], total: 0, active: 0, activeMrr: 0 with an unrendered note field: 'Marketplace subscriptions schema not yet shipped...' - yet MarketplaceSubscription exists and the other two endpoints query it successfully.

A founder checking whether the subscription business exists sees a hard zero on the Subscriptions page. When paid plans launch, this page will report Rs. 0 MRR against real revenue.

### [AO-10] P1/broken-feature/M /admin/settings 'Save Changes' is a 700ms timer - it shows "Saved", calls no API, and the value reverts on reload
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/settings - Commission Rates by Category + Save Changes; apps/web/app/admin/settings/page.tsx:111 handleSave = `await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 700))`

After clicking Save: the button state shows 'Saved' (instrumented: 'shows Saved? true'), and the number of non-GET API requests made during the save was 0. After reload the field reads 25 again. handleSave contains no fetch of any kind. The Admins tab behaves the same way - handleAddAdmin only pushes to local state.
  - expected: Save persists the rates through an API and they survive a reload; or the tab is marked read-only.

If a founder discounts a category to onboard supply, they will believe the discount is live while the ledger keeps charging the old rate. The displayed rates are themselves wrong (AO-12 sibling): Settings claims 'Astrology Consultations 30%' while the live commission row for Acharya Vinod Sharma is gross Rs 500 / platform cut Rs 100 = 20%.
  - fix: Either implement GET/PUT /admin/settings/commissions backed by a config table and have the ledger read from it, or replace the editable inputs with read-only values sourced from the actual rate constants and remove the Save button.

### [AO-11] P1/wrong-data/M Revenue 'Pending Payouts' shows Rs. 3,200 while the payout engine says Rs. 13,100 is owed
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/revenue 'Pending Payouts' card and table vs GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/payouts/pending; apps/web/app/admin/revenue/page.tsx:136 pendingPayoutRows derived from revenue.commissions
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/revenue; read the fourth overview card and the 'Rs. X due' label on the Pending Payouts table.
2. curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/bookings/payouts/pending' -H 'Authorization: Bearer <admin>'.
  - observed: UI: 'Rs. 3,200 / Pending Payouts' and 'Rs. 3,200 due'. Payout engine: groups = [{providerName:'Demo Pandit', role:'PRIEST', bookingsCount:12, totalPayout:9900}, {providerName:'Demo Wellness', bookingsCount:2, totalPayout:2800}, {providerName:'Acharya Vinod Sharma', bookingsCount:1, totalPayout:400}] = Rs. 13,100. The Revenue page only sums PENDING Commission rows, so it is blind to the entire legacy priest rail (12 completed bookings, Rs. 9,900). Secondary defect in the same table: pendingPayoutRows is filtered from revenue.commissions, which the service caps at `take: 50` - past 50 commissions the payout queue silently under-lists.
  - expected: One number for 'what we owe providers', consistent with the payout endpoint, covering both the Commission ledger and the priest rail.
  - customer_impact: The operator's picture of platform liabilities is understated 4x. They settle Rs 3,200, believe the queue is clear, and 12 completed pujas worth Rs 9,900 to a pandit are never paid - the exact failure that loses supply. And the 50-row cap means the discrepancy grows silently with volume.
  - fix: Have /admin/revenue read pendingPayouts and the payout table from GET /admin/bookings/payouts/pending (already reconciles both rails and reports a `truncated` flag) instead of re-deriving from the 50 most recent commissions.

  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/revenue - 'Pending Payouts' PROVIDER/BOOKING columns and 'Recent Commissions' BOOKING column; apps/web/app/admin/revenue/page.tsx:330-331 render p.providerId and p.bookingId directly
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com, open /admin/revenue.

Recent Commissions: first row's BOOKING cell is blank, second is a bare UUID. /admin/commissions shows the identical rows as 'Demo Wellness / Wellness Instructor' and 'Acharya Vinod Sharma / Astrologer' with item name, gross, platform cut and 20% rate. The commissions endpoint already returns providerName, providerRole, bookingNumber and itemName; the revenue endpoint's serializer at admin.service.ts:507 drops them.

It is also jarringly inconsistent: the neighbouring Commissions screen shows the same rows perfectly.

### [AO-13] P2/wrong-data/S Export Users CSV silently drops 3 of 23 users
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/reports - 'Export Users' and 'Export All' buttons; apps/web/app/admin/reports/page.tsx:96 and :136 both call getUsers(token) with no limit
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com, open /admin/reports.
2. Click 'Export Users' and open darshya-users-2026-08-12.csv.
3. Count data rows and compare with curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/users?limit=500' total.
  - observed: CSV contains 21 lines = 1 header + 20 data rows. API reports total 23. 'Export All' (darshya-platform-2026-08-12.csv, 38 lines) is capped the same way. No warning, no row count shown, filename implies a complete export.
  - expected: The export contains all 23 users, or the button states the cap.
  - customer_impact: An export is the artefact that leaves the product - it goes into a spreadsheet, an accountant's inbox, a mail-merge. It is silently incomplete, and the shortfall scales with the user base (at 1,000 users it would export 20). Nobody re-counts a CSV against the DB, so this loss is invisible indefinitely.
  - fix: Pass an explicit high limit (or paginate to exhaustion) in the export paths, and render the exported row count next to the button.

### [AO-14] P2/wrong-data/S /admin/metrics reports '0 signups today' while /admin lists 10 signups dated today
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/metrics 'SIGNUPS' tile vs http://localhost:3000/admin 'Recent Signups' table; apps/api/src/routes/admin-metrics.ts:254 startOfDayUtc + :263 todaySignups
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com, open /admin/metrics and read the TODAY row.
2. Open /admin and read the DATE column of the Recent Signups table.
  - observed: /admin/metrics: 'TODAY (2026-08-12) ... SIGNUPS 0'. /admin: Recent Signups lists 10 users all dated '12 Aug' (Hinganghat Goseva Samiti, Pathmeda Gauseva Sansthan, Neha Srinivasan, ...). Their raw createdAt is 2026-08-11T19:19:10Z - i.e. 12 Aug 00:49 IST but 11 Aug in UTC. Metrics computes 'today' with startOfDayUtc while the dashboard formats dates with toLocaleDateString('en-IN').
  - expected: Both screens use the same day boundary. For an India-first product that should be Asia/Kolkata.
  - customer_impact: For the first 5.5 hours of every IST day, the founder's 'real-numbers dashboard' reports zero signups, zero bookings and zero GMV for activity the dashboard on the next tab is already showing. The metrics page is explicitly framed as the 'Founder + investor view', so this is the number most likely to be screenshotted and quoted.
  - fix: Compute the day window in Asia/Kolkata (or expose the timezone on the tile) and use the same boundary helper for the metrics endpoint and any UI that formats dates in en-IN.

### [AO-15] P2/wrong-data/S On /admin/reports the Popular Pujas rows sum to more bookings than the 'Total Bookings' tile directly above them
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/reports - 'Total Bookings' stat tile vs 'Popular Pujas' card; apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:561 statusGroups vs :606 pujaAgg (no status filter)
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/reports.
2. Read the 'Total Bookings' tile.
3. Add up the booking counts in the Popular Pujas list.
  - observed: 'Total Bookings 53' and 'Cancelled 1'. Popular Pujas: 'Rudrabhishek 49 bookings' + 'Ganesh Puja 5 bookings' = 54. The tile's status whitelist excludes cancelled/refunded; the pujaAgg groupBy applies no status filter at all, so cancelled bookings are counted as popular ones. The same groupBy sums priceAmount across pending_payment and cancelled rows and labels it 'Rs. 54.7K' revenue.
  - expected: Both figures use the same booking population, and 'revenue' counts only captured payments.
  - customer_impact: Two numbers on one screen that cannot both be true, and the discrepancy grows with cancellations. Worse, the 'Rs. 54.7K' next to each puja is not revenue - it includes bookings that were never paid and bookings that were cancelled, so the per-puja profitability read an operator makes off this card is inflated.
  - fix: Apply the same status filter to pujaAgg that statusGroups uses, and either sum only captured Payment amounts for revenueInr or relabel the column 'Booked value'.

### [AO-16] P2/broken-feature/M Five admin CRUD endpoints are hardcoded stubs that return empty lists and fake success on writes
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/admin.ts:283 GET /temples, :337 PATCH /temples/:id, :344 DELETE /temples/:id, :349 GET /priests, :377 GET /pujas, :400 GET /content, :432 GET /payments, :449 POST /users, :455 DELETE /users/:id
  - steps: 1. Get an admin bearer token.
2. curl each of: GET /api/v1/admin/temples, /admin/priests, /admin/pujas, /admin/payments, /admin/content.
3. curl -X PATCH /api/v1/admin/temples/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000 -d '{"name":"X-DELETE-ME"}' and DELETE the same id.
4. curl -X POST /api/v1/admin/users -d '{"fullName":"Ghost","email":"ghost@x.com","role":"DEVOTEE"}' and DELETE /api/v1/admin/users/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000.
  - observed: GET /admin/temples -> 200 {"temples":[],"total":0} despite 20 seeded temples. /admin/priests -> 200 total 0 (3 priests exist). /admin/pujas, /admin/content, /admin/payments all -> 200 with empty arrays. PATCH /admin/temples/<nonexistent uuid> -> 200 {"message":"Temple updated","templeId":"000...","updates":{"name":"X-DELETE-ME"}}. DELETE /admin/temples/<nonexistent> -> 200 {"message":"Temple deleted"}. POST /admin/users -> 200 {"message":"User created"} (no user created). DELETE /admin/users/<nonexistent> -> 200 {"message":"User deleted"}. Only POST /admin/temples is real.
  - expected: Real queries and mutations, or 501 Not Implemented. A write against a non-existent id must 404, never 200 'deleted'.
  - customer_impact: No web admin page consumes these today, which is the only reason this is not P0 - but they are live, admin-authenticated, and they lie. Any client wired to them (the mobile admin screen, an agent, a future page) will show empty temple/priest/puja/payment lists and will report successful deletes and updates that never happened. A stub that answers 'deleted' is more dangerous than one that 404s.
  - fix: Implement the reads against Prisma (the models all exist) and make the writes real; until then return 501 with a clear message rather than a fabricated success payload.

### [AO-17] P3/ux/S /admin/consultations shows a raw user UUID in the USER column and repeats the astrologer's UUID under their name
  - where: http://localhost:3000/admin/consultations - USER and ASTROLOGER columns
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com and open /admin/consultations.
2. Read the single row in the table.
  - observed: ASTROLOGER cell shows 'Acharya Vinod Sharma' followed by '71e54617-6963-418e-845f-920f4350505b'; USER cell shows only '2b5bd615…' (a truncated UUID, no name or email); SCHEDULED shows '—'. Ref AC-20260812-0266CC, status Confirmed, amount 500.
  - expected: USER shows the devotee's name/email; the astrologer's UUID is not surfaced next to their name.
  - customer_impact: An operator driving the consultation lifecycle (the page's own note says only admin can) cannot tell which devotee a Rs 500 consultation belongs to without a separate DB lookup. Same UUID-leak class as AO-12 but on a lower-traffic screen.
  - fix: Join the User row and render fullName/email, and drop the astrologer id from the cell.

### [AO-18] P3/ux/S GET /admin/bookings accepts an invalid status and returns 200 with an empty list instead of 400
  - where: GET http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/bookings?status=NOT_A_STATUS; apps/api/src/routes/admin-bookings.ts:89
  - steps: 1. curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/bookings?status=completed' -H 'Authorization: Bearer <admin>' -> rows returned.
2. curl 'http://localhost:4000/api/v1/admin/bookings?status=NOT_A_STATUS' with the same header.
  - observed: 200 {"bookings":[],"page":1,"limit":50,"total":0}. By contrast the sibling endpoints do validate: /admin/commissions?status=processing -> 400 'Invalid enum value. Expected PENDING | PAID | REVERSED', and /admin/bookings/payout-requests?status=PENDING -> 400 'Expected pending | processing | completed | failed | rejected'.
  - expected: 400 with the list of valid statuses, consistent with the neighbouring admin endpoints.
  - customer_impact: A typo'd or case-wrong status silently reads as 'no bookings in that state' rather than an error - the same class of bug the CommissionsPanel comment records having already shipped once (lowercase chips matching zero rows). Note the inconsistency this exposes: payout-requests wants lowercase statuses while commissions wants uppercase, which is a trap for whoever builds the payouts UI in AO-03.
  - fix: Add a zod enum for the status query param on /admin/bookings, and align status casing across the admin endpoints.

## AREA: astro-wellness (astrologer portal, wellness portal, consumer /astrology + /wellness, consultation & class booking, 80/20 earnings math)   score=14

### [AW-01] P0/broken-feature/M Astrologer can never see or join a consultation they were paid for — dashboard panel is a hardcoded empty state and the Consultations page reads a different, always-empty data source
  - where: http://localhost:3000/astrologer-portal ("Upcoming Sessions" panel) and http://localhost:3000/astrologer-portal/consultations — apps/web/app/astrologer-portal/page.tsx:200-218 and apps/web/app/astrologer-portal/consultations/page.tsx:78
  - steps: 1. Log in as a real onboarded astrologer (astro.vedic@darshya.online, AstrologerProfile 71e54617-6963-418e-845f-920f4350505b).
2. As devotee@demo.com, book and pay for a consultation with that astrologer (POST /api/v1/consultation/book then /verify-payment, or the UI at /astrology/71e54617-.../book).
3. Open /astrologer-portal. Read the stat cards, then read the Upcoming Sessions panel.
4. Click "View All" -> /astrologer-portal/consultations.
  - observed: Dashboard stat cards read "1 Today's Sessions ... 1 Active Clients", yet the panel directly below reads "Upcoming Sessions  View All  No upcoming sessions  Bookings will appear here once devotees consult you." The Consultations page reads "Upcoming 0  Past 0  No upcoming consultations". Meanwhile GET /api/v1/astrologer-portal/dashboard returns upcomingSessions:[{"reference":"AC-20260812-628085","amount_inr":"375","status":"confirmed","room_name":"darshya-astrologer-acharya-vinod-sharma-..."}]. grep of page.tsx shows `upcomingSessions` occurs exactly once in the whole file — on line 45, inside the TypeScript interface. It is fetched and never rendered. The Consultations page calls GET /astrology/consultations?role=astrologer, served by astrology.service.ts:859-863 which filters an in-memory `this.sessions` Map, not the astrologer_consultations table; it returned {"consultations":[]} for an astrologer holding two confirmed rows.
  - expected: The paid consultation, its reference, scheduled time and a working "Join room" link appear in the astrologer's Upcoming Sessions panel and on the Consultations page.
  - customer_impact: The devotee pays, gets a Jitsi room and the message "Your astrologer has been notified. Join the room when ready." The astrologer has no way to find that room. Every paid consultation is a guaranteed no-show and a guaranteed refund request. This alone makes the astrology marketplace unsellable to supply.
  - fix: Render `api.upcomingSessions` in the dashboard panel (replace the static block at page.tsx:211-217). Repoint apps/web/app/astrologer-portal/consultations/page.tsx:78 off /astrology/consultations onto a DB-backed endpoint, and fix that endpoint per AW-11 so it matches on both AstrologerProfile.id and User.id the way /astrologer-portal/dashboard already does (astrologer-portal.ts:63-65).

### [AW-02] P0/broken-feature/M The main consumer astrology funnel sells consultations with 15 fictional astrologers — money is captured, no Commission row is written, nobody can deliver
  - where: http://localhost:3000/astrology ("Talk to Astrologers" grid) -> /astrology/astro_001/book — apps/web/app/astrology/AstrologyClient.tsx:269 fetches /astrology/astrologers?limit=100
  - steps: 1. Open /astrology as any devotee. The grid lists "Pt. Raghunath Sharma ₹50/min", "Acharya Bhaskar Rao ₹90/min", etc., with "9 astrologers online now".
2. Click any card -> Book -> "Pay ₹750 & Start Consultation".
3. As admin@demo.com, GET /api/v1/consultation/admin/all and GET /api/v1/admin/commissions?page=1&pageSize=20.
  - observed: Booking succeeded: consultation 05723e03-d227-436d-8c53-6b9993724daa, reference AC-20260812-3F1C15, astrologer_id "astro_001", amountInr 750, status "confirmed", room minted. The success screen says "Your astrologer has been notified." The full admin commission ledger has total=3 rows and NONE of them references booking 05723e03 — the ledger contains only the two wellness rows and the one real-astrologer row. consultation.ts:433 `if (!profile?.userId) return; // mock astrologer — no real provider to credit` silently skips the write. The 3 genuinely onboarded astrologers (Acharya Vinod Sharma ₹25/min, Ritika Menon ₹18/min, Dr. Mahesh Iyer ₹20/min) appear nowhere on /astrology.
  - expected: The consumer directory lists real, approved AstrologerProfile rows. A paid booking always credits a real provider, or the booking is refused.
  - customer_impact: ₹750 taken from a devotee for a consultation with a person who does not exist. No astrologer is notified, no room will ever be joined, no payout ledger entry exists, and the refund has to be handled manually. Simultaneously, every genuinely onboarded astrologer is invisible in the funnel and can never earn.
  - fix: Point AstrologyClient.tsx:269 at /astrology/astrologers/live (real approved profiles) or merge real profiles ahead of the fixture. Until then, make POST /consultation/book refuse a non-UUID astrologerId rather than accepting payment for a fixture.

### [AW-03] P0/broken-feature/M /astrology/astrologers/live — the only page that lists real onboarded astrologers — is a hard 404
  - where: http://localhost:3000/astrology/astrologers/live — no route exists under apps/web/app/astrology/ (directory contains [id], view, kundli, horoscope, ... but no 'astrologers' folder)

Network log: 404 GET http://localhost:3000/astrology/astrologers/live. The backing API is healthy and returns the 3 real astrologers: GET /api/v1/astrology/astrologers/live -> {"total":3,"source":"live"}.

### [AW-04] P1/wrong-data/S Astrologer dashboard shows GROSS revenue under the label "Paid out" — ₹375 claimed paid when ₹0 is paid and the true earning is ₹300
  - where: http://localhost:3000/astrologer-portal (EarningsCard) — apps/web/app/astrologer-portal/page.tsx:178-182 + apps/api/src/routes/astrologer-portal.ts:80-105
  - steps: 1. As a real astrologer with one confirmed ₹375 consultation, open /astrologer-portal and read the three money cards.
2. Click "Earnings" in the left nav and read the three money cards there.
3. GET /api/v1/payouts/balance with the same token.
  - observed: Dashboard: "Paid out ₹375 · Pending payout — · This month ₹375". Earnings page at the same moment: "Total earnings ₹700 · Pending payout ₹700 · Paid out ₹0". /payouts/balance: {"availableInr":700,"pendingInr":0,"paidOutInr":0}. The dashboard number is SUM(amount_inr) over confirmed rows — gross, before the 20% cut — and it is piped into EarningsCard's `totalPaid` prop, which renders under the label "Paid out". `pending` is passed literally as `null`, so "Pending payout" renders as an em dash.
  - expected: Dashboard "Paid out" = ₹0 (nothing settled), "Pending payout" = the real pending figure, "This month" = the 80% share (₹300 for a ₹375 consultation), and it should agree with the Earnings page.
  - customer_impact: An astrologer reads "Paid out ₹375", checks their bank, sees nothing, and opens a support ticket. The figure also overstates their actual take by 25% because it is gross, not the 80% share — the exact earnings-inversion class this page has a history of. The wellness dashboard was fixed to show the instructor share; the astrologer dashboard was not.
  - fix: astrologer-portal.ts: return the 80% share (mirror wellness-portal.ts:97-102) and add a real paid/pending split. page.tsx:178: pass totalPaid=paid, pending=pending, thisMonth=net-month.

### [AW-05] P1/broken-feature/S Wellness Earnings page is dead — renders "Invalid query" and "No commission records yet." while ₹800 is pending; client always sends pageSize=200 against an API that caps at 100
  - where: http://localhost:3000/wellness-portal/earnings — apps/web/lib/commission-api.ts:120 (COMMISSION_PAGE_SIZE=200) vs apps/api/src/routes/payout-requests.ts:103 (pageSize max 100)
  - steps: 1. Log in as wellness@demo.com.
2. Have at least one PAID enrolment so a Commission row exists (create a class, enrol a devotee, verify payment).
3. Open /wellness-portal/earnings.
  - observed: Page renders the amber error banner "Invalid query" and the empty state "No commission records yet." Network: 400 GET /api/v1/payouts/commissions?page=1&pageSize=200 with body {"error":"Invalid query","details":{"fieldErrors":{"pageSize":["Number must be less than or equal to 100"]}}}. The same request with pageSize=100 returns 200 with the real row: amount "800", grossAmount "1000", platformCut "200", ratePercent 20. The instructor's dashboard on the adjacent page shows ₹800, so the two screens disagree.
  - expected: The Earnings page lists the ₹800 PENDING commission with gross ₹1,000 and platform cut ₹200.
  - customer_impact: Every wellness instructor's earnings page is permanently blank with an inscrutable "Invalid query". They cannot reconcile a single rupee. This is the same defect class that killed this page before (the old admin-only route) — it was moved to a new endpoint but the page size was never reconciled. Astrologers hit the same 400 on /payouts/commissions?pageSize=200.
  - fix: Set COMMISSION_PAGE_SIZE to 100 in apps/web/lib/commission-api.ts, or raise the max in payout-requests.ts:103 to 200. Add a contract test that the client's default page size is accepted by both routes.

### [AW-06] P1/wrong-data/M Cancelling a refund-eligible consultation leaves the astrologer's commission PENDING and withdrawable — platform refunds the devotee and still pays the provider
  - where: POST /api/v1/consultation/:id/cancel — apps/api/src/routes/consultation.ts:546-564 (flips status only, never touches Commission)
  - steps: 1. As devotee@demo.com book + pay a ₹500 consultation with real astrologer profile 71e54617 (writes Commission amount=400, status=PENDING).
2. GET /api/v1/payouts/balance as that astrologer -> note availableInr.
3. POST /api/v1/consultation/<id>/cancel as the devotee.
4. GET /api/v1/payouts/balance again.
  - observed: Cancel returns 200 {"cancelled":true,"refundEligible":true,"note":"Cancellation accepted. Refund will be processed within 5-7 business days."}. Astrologer payout balance BEFORE: {"availableInr":400}. AFTER: {"availableInr":400} — unchanged. /astrologer-portal/earnings still shows the row as status PENDING, earningsInr 400, and summary pendingInr 400. Only the dashboard changed (it filters status='confirmed'), dropping to earningsTodayInr 0 — so the dashboard says ₹0 while the withdrawable balance says ₹400.
  - expected: On a refund-eligible cancellation the Commission row moves to REVERSED and drops out of the payout balance. The Commission model already has a REVERSED status and the UI already styles it.
  - customer_impact: Real cash leakage: the platform refunds the devotee the full gross and the weekly settlement / manual payout still hands the astrologer their 80%. Every cancelled consultation is a 100% loss plus the 80% payout. An astrologer could also book-and-cancel against themselves through a second account.
  - fix: In the cancel handler, when refundEligible, update the Commission for that bookingId to status REVERSED inside the same transaction as the status flip (mirror whatever the puja refund ladder does). Add a regression test asserting payout balance returns to 0 after cancellation.

### [AW-08] P1/broken-feature/S astro@demo.com is unusable — every astrologer-portal route returns HTTP 500 with a raw Prisma stack trace exposing absolute server paths and SQL
  - where: POST /api/v1/auth/login (astro@demo.com) then any astrologer route — apps/api/src/routes/auth.ts:78-90 and apps/api/src/middlewares/auth.ts:145-170
  - steps: 1. curl -X POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/auth/login -d '{"email":"astro@demo.com","password":"demo123"}'
2. curl http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/astrologer-portal/dashboard -H "Authorization: Bearer demo-demo-astro-token"
3. Repeat step 2 for /astrologer-portal/earnings, /consultation/mine, /users/me.
4. For contrast, repeat with demo-demo-wellness-token.

Every DB-backed route then 500s. /astrologer-portal/dashboard: {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2023","message":"Invalid `prisma.astrologerProfile.findUnique()` invocation in E:\\NewClaude\\bhaktisetu\\apps\\api\\src\\routes\\astrologer-portal.ts:53:52 ... Error creating UUID, invalid character ... found `m` at 3"}. /consultation/mine: {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2010","message":"Raw query failed. Code: `22P02`. Message: `ERROR: invalid input syntax for type uuid: \"demo-astro\"`"}. In the browser, /astrologer-portal renders all zeros and /astrologer-portal/earnings renders "Recent consultations — Failed to fetch". Contrast: demo-demo-wellness-token resolves to a real UUID 9e15c3a2-... and works. The middleware's DEMO_ROLE_MAP maps handle 'astro' to astro@demo.com; when that User row is absent it falls through to the synthetic-id branch (auth.ts:161-168) instead of failing cleanly.

  - customer_impact: The astrologer demo account — the one used for investor demos and QA — is completely dead, and its failure mode leaks internal filesystem layout and query text to any caller. Any provider whose User row is missing hits the same path.
  - fix: In middlewares/auth.ts, when the demo handle's User row is not found, reply 401 rather than synthesising a non-UUID id. Independently, add a Fastify error handler that strips Prisma internals from 5xx bodies in all environments.

### [AW-09] P1/security/S apps/api/.env points at the PRODUCTION Supabase database with NODE_ENV=production — any Prisma CLI or script run from apps/api hits prod, not the local dev DB
  - where: E:\\NewClaude\\bhaktisetu\\apps\\api\\.env (DATABASE_URL -> aws-1-ap-northeast-1.pooler.supabase.com, project ref hlkvfarvfblhzhhnhtbp, NODE_ENV=production) vs apps/api/.env.local (localhost dev DB, NODE_ENV=development). The running API server uses .env.local.
  - steps: 1. cd apps/api
2. node -e "const {PrismaClient}=require('@prisma/client'); new PrismaClient().astrologerProfile.findMany().then(r=>console.log(r.map(p=>p.id)))"
3. Compare the ids to GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/astrology/astrologers/live (the running API's view of the same table).
  - observed: The script returned AstrologerProfile ids 1b0970ed-…, cd72396f-…, 730402c6-… and demo users including an extra astrologer@demo.com row. The API on :4000 returned a disjoint set: 71e54617-…, 9641be46-…, 47a6e279-…. Two different databases. Reading the env files: apps/api/.env has DATABASE_URL=postgresql://postgres.hlkvfarvfblhzhhnhtbp:***REMOVED***@aws-1-ap-northeast-1.pooler.supabase.com:6543/postgres?pgbouncer=true and NODE_ENV=production; apps/api/.env.local has postgresql://postgres:***REMOVED***@localhost:5432/bhaktisetu_dev. Prisma CLI and bare `new PrismaClient()` load .env, not .env.local. I ran two read-only findMany queries against prod before noticing; no writes were made.
  - expected: A developer or agent working in a local checkout cannot reach production by default. Prod credentials should live only in .env.production / the Render dashboard.
  - customer_impact: One `npx prisma migrate dev`, `prisma db push`, or a seed script run from apps/api silently targets the live customer database. This is exactly the setup that produces an accidental prod wipe, and it also means every local agent session has ambient prod read access.
  - fix: Move the Supabase URL out of apps/api/.env into .env.production (already exists) and make .env carry the local dev DB with NODE_ENV=development. Add a guard in prisma scripts that refuses to run when DATABASE_URL host is not localhost unless an explicit --prod flag is passed.

### [AW-10] P2/wrong-data/S Wellness dashboard also labels un-paid pending earnings as "Paid out", and "Pending payout" renders as an em dash

The ₹800 amount is correct (it is the 80% share, unlike the astrologer dashboard), only the label is wrong. EarningsCard's first card is hardcoded to the label 'Paid out' and is fed `totalPaid={liveStats?.totalEarningsInr}`.

The booking writes astrologer_id = the AstrologerProfile UUID (consultation.ts:298 passes the request's astrologerId), but this endpoint compares it to the User UUID. The dashboard endpoint already carries the correct fix (astrologer-portal.ts:63-65 matches ANY of [profile.id, userId]); it was never applied here.

### [AW-12] P2/broken-feature/S GET /wellness-portal/instructor/mine returns HTTP 500 — raw SQL compares a uuid column to a text parameter
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/wellness-portal.ts:156 (`WHERE instructor_id = $1`, $1 bound as text)
  - steps: 1. curl http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/wellness-portal/instructor/mine -H "Authorization: Bearer demo-demo-wellness-token"
  - observed: {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2010","message":"Raw query failed. Code: `42883`. Message: `ERROR: operator does not exist: uuid = text HINT: No operator matches the given name and argument types. You might need to add explicit type casts.`"} — reproduced on every call, with and without an Origin header, with 0 and with 2 sessions present. The equivalent Prisma-based route GET /wellness/sessions/mine returns 200 with the correct rows.

  - customer_impact: Not reached by the current web UI (the portal uses /wellness/sessions/mine), so no customer impact today — but it is a registered public endpoint that 500s on 100% of calls and leaks a raw Postgres error, and mobile or a future client would hit it.
  - fix: Cast the parameter: `WHERE instructor_id = $1::uuid` (and the count query too), or delete the endpoint in favour of /wellness/sessions/mine.

### [AW-15] P3/broken-feature/S API CORS allowlist contains http://localhost:3000 but not http://127.0.0.1:3000 — the entire app renders zeros on the 127.0.0.1 origin
  - where: apps/api/src/server.ts:151-166 (CORS_ORIGIN allowlist)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/astrologer-portal with a valid session in localStorage.

3. curl -X OPTIONS http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/astrologer-portal/dashboard -H "Origin: http://127.0.0.1:3000" -H "Access-Control-Request-Method: GET" and compare with Origin: http://localhost:3000.

No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header is present" for every portal endpoint, and the page renders all zeros with no error state. The preflight for Origin http://127.0.0.1:3000 returns 204 with NO access-control-allow-origin header; for http://localhost:3000 it returns access-control-allow-origin: http://localhost:3000.
  - expected: Both loopback spellings work in development, or the failure surfaces as a visible error instead of silent zeros.

  - fix: Add http://127.0.0.1:3000 to the dev CORS default list in server.ts. Separately, give the portal dashboards an explicit error state when the fetch fails instead of falling back to ZERO_STATS.
