## AREA: store-temple-gaushala   score=15

### [S-01] P0/broken-feature/S Store checkout always fails with 400: saved addresses have line2:null but the API schema rejects null
  - where: http://localhost:3000/store/checkout — "Place Order - Rs 733" button. apps/api/src/routes/store-orders.ts:29 (line2: z.string().optional()) vs apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:211 (line2: addr.line2)
  - steps: 1. Log in as devotee@demo.com / demo123.
2. Go to /store, open any product, Add to Cart.
3. Go to /store/checkout.
4. Leave the default saved address selected (its line2 is null in the DB).
5. Pick Cash on Delivery, click "Place Order - Rs 733".
  - observed: HTTP 400 from POST /api/v1/store/orders with body {"error":"Invalid request body","details":{"formErrors":[],"fieldErrors":{"shippingAddress":["Expected string, received null"]}}}. On-screen text: 'Order failed (400) {"error":"Invalid request body","details":{"formErrors":[],"fieldErrors":{"shippingAddress":["Expected string, received'. Console: 'Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 400 (Bad Request)'. GET /store/orders count before = 0, after = 0 (delta 0). Isolation proof: identical payload with line2 omitted passes validation and reaches the next layer.
  - expected: Order is created, cart clears, and an "Order Placed!" confirmation with a real order number appears.
  - customer_impact: Every single store checkout fails for any customer whose saved address has no second address line — which is the default for addresses added without a line2. Zero store revenue is collectible. The customer is shown a raw JSON blob and has no way to recover.
  - fix: Change line2 to z.string().nullish() (or .nullable().optional()) in shippingAddressSchema; optionally also send `line2: addr.line2 ?? undefined` from the web client. Apply the same nullish treatment to phone.

### [S-02] P0/broken-feature/L No product in the shopper catalog can ever be ordered: catalog ids are not UUIDs, so the order query 500s
  - where: POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/store/orders — apps/api/src/routes/store-orders.ts:176 (prisma.storeProduct.findMany with id in productIds)
  - steps: 1. GET /api/v1/store/products?limit=1 — note the id is `prod_015` (a plain string, not a UUID).
2. POST /api/v1/store/orders with Authorization: Bearer demo-devotee-token and body {"items":[{"productId":"prod_015","qty":1,"priceAtPurchase":349}],"totalAmountInr":349,"shippingAddress":{"fullName":"QA","line1":"L1","city":"Delhi","state":"DL","pincode":"110001","country":"India"}}
  - observed: HTTP 500 {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2023","error":"Internal Server Error","message":"Invalid `prisma.storeProduct.findMany()` invocation ... Inconsistent column data: Error creating UUID, invalid character: expected an optional prefix of `urn:uuid:` followed by [0-9a-fA-F-], found `p` at 1"}. Corroborating: GET /store/products returns 24 products with ids prod_015, prod_004, prod_003, prod_016, prod_024; GET /store/products/live (the DB-backed owner catalog) returns {"products":[],"total":0,"source":"live"}.
  - expected: Either the order is created against a real product row, or a clean 400 "Unknown product" — never a 500.
  - customer_impact: Even after S-01 is fixed, checkout still fails. The shopper-facing store is 24 static fixtures that are not purchasable, and there are zero real products in the database, so the store has no working commerce at all. This is the second half of the founder's reported "order placed error".
  - fix: Unify the catalog: back GET /store/products with StoreProduct rows (as /products/live already does), or map the static handle to a StoreProduct before querying. Immediately, guard the query with a UUID-shape check so a bad id returns 400 rather than throwing P2023.

### [S-03] P1/security/S 500 responses leak the absolute server filesystem path and source code to the client
  - where: POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/store/orders (any 500) — response body
  - steps: 1. POST /api/v1/store/orders with a non-UUID productId as in S-02.
2. Read the raw response body.
  - observed: Response body contains the absolute path `E:\\NewClaude\\bhaktisetu\\apps\\api\\src\\routes\\store-orders.ts:176:48` together with verbatim source lines 173-176 including the code comment text ("// atomically. The client-sent price/total are ignored.").
  - expected: A generic error body such as {"error":"Internal Server Error"} with details logged server-side only.
  - customer_impact: Discloses deployment paths, internal file layout and implementation comments to anyone who can trigger an error. Useful reconnaissance for an attacker and unprofessional if a customer ever sees it.
  - fix: Register a Fastify setErrorHandler that logs the full error internally and returns a generic message plus a correlation id when NODE_ENV !== 'development'. Never pass Prisma error.message through to the response.

### [S-04] P1/dead-control/M Every filter, sort and search control on /store is decorative — the page is a server component with no handlers
  - where: http://localhost:3000/store — apps/web/app/store/page.tsx: search input line 139-143, Sort select line 178-184, price select line 189-195, category pills line 152-172
  - steps: 1. Open /store (no login needed).
2. Change the Sort dropdown from "Sort: Popular" to "Price: Low to High".
3. Change the price dropdown to "Under Rs 500".
4. Type "rudraksha" into the search box.
5. Click any category pill.
  - observed: apps/web/app/store/page.tsx has no 'use client' directive and does export const metadata (line 31), so it is a React Server Component. The <input> at line 139 has no value and no onChange; both <select> elements (lines 178, 189) have no value and no onChange; the category <button> elements (line 153) have no onClick and the active pill is hardcoded to `i === 0`. No state, no router push, no query params — these controls cannot affect the listing under any circumstance.
  - expected: Sort reorders the grid; the price filter removes out-of-range products; search narrows the list; category pills filter by collection.
  - customer_impact: This is precisely the defect class the founder called out. A shopper on the main store page cannot sort, filter or search 24 products. The UI silently pretends to work, which reads as broken rather than absent. Note /yatra implements the same pattern correctly (apps/web/app/yatra/page.tsx lines 145-177 have real onChange handlers), so this is an unfinished page, not a missing capability.
  - fix: Split the filter bar into a client component holding sort/price/query/category state (or drive it via searchParams so the server component can filter), then apply the predicate to PRODUCTS before rendering the grid.

### [S-05] P1/wrong-data/M A paid Rs 2,100 donation is invisible to the receiving gaushala — portal shows zero
  - where: GET /api/v1/gaushala-portal/donations and /gaushala-portal/dashboard vs POST /api/v1/donations/verify
  - steps: 1. POST /api/v1/donations/create-order as demo-devotee-token with {"amountInr":2100,"causeKind":"gaushala","gaushalaId":"gs_001","donorPan":"ABCDE1234F"}.
2. POST /api/v1/donations/verify with the returned donationId and razorpayOrderId.
3. Immediately GET /api/v1/gaushala-portal/donations and /gaushala-portal/dashboard as demo-gaushala-token.
  - observed: Step 2 returned {"ok":true,"donationId":"don_1786539550524_d02d4eda","receiptNumber":"DR-20260812-D287","amount":2100,"status":"paid"}. Step 3 returned {"donations":[]} and {"profile":null,"stats":{"totalCows":0,"totalDonors":0,"totalDonationsInr":0,"monthDonationsInr":0},"recentDonations":[],"recentCows":[]}.
  - expected: The gaushala portal shows the Rs 2,100 donation, the donor count increments, and totalDonationsInr reflects money actually received.
  - customer_impact: A provider cannot see money donated to them — the single most important number in their portal reads zero while the donor holds a valid 80G receipt for Rs 2,100. This destroys provider trust and makes reconciliation impossible. Two compounding causes: donations are recorded against the static directory id gs_001 rather than a GaushalaProfile UUID, and the portal filters by the manager's profile (which does not exist for this account).
  - fix: Make the public gaushala directory resolve to real GaushalaProfile rows so donations carry a FK the portal can filter on; backfill/alias the gs_* fixture ids to profile UUIDs. Add a reconciliation test asserting a verified donation appears in the recipient portal.

### [S-09] P2/wrong-data/S Order total silently drops GST and delivery: checkout promises Rs 733, server records and charges Rs 698
  - where: apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:206 (totalAmountInr: total) vs apps/api/src/routes/store-orders.ts:204-206,232 (serverTotal = sum(price*qty))
  - steps: 1. Put 2 x Bhagavad Gita (Rs 349) in the cart and open /store/checkout.
2. Read the Order Summary: Subtotal Rs 698, Delivery FREE, GST (est.) Rs 35, Total Rs 733; the button reads "Place Order - Rs 733".
3. Inspect the POST body (totalAmountInr: 733) and compare with the server's recompute path.
  - observed: The checkout UI displayed exactly: 'Subtotal (2 items) Rs 698 / Delivery FREE / GST (est.) Rs 35 / Total Rs 733 / Place Order - Rs 733'. The server explicitly ignores the client total and stores totalAmountInr = serverTotal, which is only sum(unitPrice * qty) = 698; the Razorpay order is then created from the StoreOrder total. GST and delivery are never added server-side.
  - expected: The persisted order total and the amount charged equal the total the customer agreed to, or the UI stops displaying a GST line the backend does not implement.
  - customer_impact: The business under-collects GST on every order and the order record disagrees with the checkout screen the customer saw — a reconciliation and tax-compliance problem. Currently latent because S-01/S-02 prevent any order from being created, so it will surface the moment checkout is fixed.
  - fix: Compute GST and delivery server-side and include them in serverTotal, then assert the client-sent total matches within tolerance and reject if not. Add a test pinning UI total == persisted total == Razorpay amount.

### [S-10] P2/ux/S Checkout failure shows the customer a raw JSON error blob
  - where: http://localhost:3000/store/checkout — error banner; apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:219-221 throws `Order failed (${res.status}) ${txt.slice(0,120)}`
  - steps: 1. Reproduce S-01 (log in as devotee@demo.com, add to cart, checkout, Place Order).
2. Read the error text rendered on the page.
  - observed: On-screen: 'Order failed (400) {"error":"Invalid request body","details":{"formErrors":[],"fieldErrors":{"shippingAddress":["Expected string, received' — the raw response body, truncated mid-word at 120 chars.
  - expected: A human message such as "We couldn't place your order. Please check your delivery address and try again." with a retry affordance.
  - customer_impact: At the highest-intent moment in the funnel the customer is shown a truncated developer error. It looks broken and unsafe, and gives no corrective action — guaranteeing abandonment even for otherwise recoverable failures.
  - fix: Map status codes to friendly copy via the existing lib/friendly-error.ts helper; log the raw body to the console/monitoring instead of rendering it.

### [S-11] P2/ux/S Order confirmation screen fabricates a fake order number when the API returns none
  - where: apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:149
  - steps: 1. Reach the order-placed state with json.data.orderNumber absent (e.g. an API shape change, or the COD path when serializeOrder omits orderNumber).
2. Read the "Order ID:" line on the confirmation card.
  - observed: Source reads: Order ID: {orderNumber ?? `ORD-${Date.now().toString().slice(-8)}`} — when orderNumber is null the screen renders an invented identifier derived from the browser clock. Reported from source inspection: I could not reach the confirmation screen at runtime because no order can be created (S-01/S-02).
  - expected: Show the server's real order number, or state that the number is pending — never synthesise one.
  - customer_impact: Directly matches the founder's "confirmation screen missing the reference number" concern, and is worse than missing: the customer records a reference that matches nothing in the system, then support cannot find their order. Silent data fiction.
  - fix: Remove the fallback. If orderNumber is null, render the order id or a 'confirmation on its way by email' message, and log the anomaly.

### [S-12] P2/security/S apps/api/.env in the working tree holds live production Supabase service-role credentials
  - where: E:\NewClaude\bhaktisetu\apps\api\.env
  - steps: 1. Open apps/api/.env (it is UTF-16/mixed-encoding, so plain grep reports it as binary).
2. Inspect DATABASE_URL, DIRECT_URL, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY, JWT_SECRET, NODE_ENV, CORS_ORIGIN.
  - observed: Without reproducing values: the file sets NODE_ENV=production, points DATABASE_URL/DIRECT_URL at the live Supabase pooler host (project ref hlkvfarvfblhzhhnhtbp, aws-1-ap-northeast-1), and contains a Supabase service-role key, a production JWT secret, and live Groq and Cohere API keys. CORS_ORIGIN includes https://darshya.online. The file is also malformed — duplicated COHERE_API_KEY fragments and interleaved UTF-16 text. Confirmed by direct read-only connection that this URL reaches a live Postgres 17.6 instance holding 20 temples and 9 @demo.com users. I verified the currently running local API is NOT on that project (the UUIDs it serves for store/temple/gaushala/devotee do not exist there) before performing any write, and I restricted my writes to the donations flow.
  - expected: Local development env files contain local/disposable credentials; production secrets live only in the deployment platform.
  - customer_impact: Anyone who starts the local API without an overriding DATABASE_URL points a full-privilege service-role connection at production while agents are instructed to "break things on purpose". The brief for this run stated the database was a disposable local Postgres; there is no Postgres on 5432 and no docker, so that premise did not hold and the safety margin here was accidental rather than designed.
  - fix: Remove production secrets from apps/api/.env, rotate the exposed Supabase service-role key, JWT secret and vendor keys, ensure the file is gitignored, and make local dev fail loudly if DATABASE_URL resolves to a non-local host while NODE_ENV!=production.

### [S-13] P3/parity/S API dev CORS allowlist omits 127.0.0.1, so every client-side call fails preflight when driven from that origin
  - where: apps/api/src/server.ts:156-166 (default corsOrigins = ['http://localhost:3000','http://localhost:3001'])
  - steps: 1. curl -i -X OPTIONS http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/store/products -H 'Origin: http://127.0.0.1:3000' -H 'Access-Control-Request-Method: GET'
2. Repeat with -H 'Origin: http://localhost:3000'.
  - observed: With Origin http://127.0.0.1:3000 the 204 response contains access-control-allow-credentials, -methods and -headers but NO access-control-allow-origin header. With Origin http://localhost:3000 the response includes 'access-control-allow-origin: http://localhost:3000'.
  - expected: Loopback origins behave identically in development, or the mismatch is documented.
  - customer_impact: No production impact (CORS_ORIGIN is set in deployment), but it silently breaks local QA: browsing the app at 127.0.0.1:3000 makes every authenticated fetch fail, so portals render permanently empty and a tester concludes the feature is broken. This run's own instructions specified 127.0.0.1:3000; all browser work had to be redirected to localhost:3000.
  - fix: Add http://127.0.0.1:3000 and http://127.0.0.1:3001 to the development default allowlist.

### [LC-01] P1/dead-control/M /store sort dropdown does nothing — all 5 options leave the grid identical
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/store — <select> labelled "Sort: Popular" (first control in the filters row); apps/web/app/store/page.tsx:178-184
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store
2. Note the first product tile is "Premium Brass Puja Thali Set" at ₹1,299
3. Open the "Sort: Popular" dropdown and choose "Price: Low to High"
4. Look at the grid
  - observed: The 24 product titles are byte-identical before and after (SAME=true), and the on-screen price sequence stays ₹1299, ₹349, ₹249, ₹199, ₹179, ₹599, ₹399, ₹799, ₹2499, ₹3299, ₹2899, ₹899, ₹649, ₹449, ₹349, ₹249, ₹199, ₹499, ₹699, ₹599, ₹299, ₹249, ₹3499, ₹549 — not ascending. Same for "Price: High to Low", "Rating: High to Low" and "Newest First". DOM inspection of the element's React props shows onChange is absent (wired=false); the markup is a bare <select className=…> with no value and no handler inside a server component. URL stays http://127.0.0.1:3000/store.
  - expected: "Price: Low to High" reorders the grid so prices are monotonically non-decreasing (₹179, ₹199, ₹199, ₹249, …). Each option changes the order.
  - customer_impact: This is the exact defect the founder reported. A shopper on a 24-SKU store cannot sort by price or rating — the single most-used control on any storefront. It also erodes trust in every other control on the page: once one dropdown is visibly fake, users stop trying the rest.
  - fix: The backend already does this correctly — GET /api/v1/store/products?sort=price_asc returns 179,199,199,249,249,249. Either (a) make the filter bar a client component holding sort state and sort the already-fetched array, or (b) follow the pattern /pujas already uses for search (comment S2C-12 in apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx:292): wrap the select in a GET <form action="/store"> with name="sort", read searchParams server-side, and pass sort through to listProducts(). Option (b) keeps the page a server component and makes sorted views shareable.

"bg-saffron-600 text-white" : …`), so the UI always claims "All Products" is selected no matter what you click.

  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/store — the "Add to Cart" button on every product tile; apps/web/app/store/page.tsx:314-317 (button nested inside the card's <a href={`/store/${product.handle}`}> which opens at line 240 and closes at line 319)

### [LC-10] P2/wrong-data/S Admin Users table can only ever show 20 of 23 users, and its own footer reports the wrong total
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/admin/users — the "Showing X-Y of Z users" footer and the pager; apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:123 (`getUsers(token)` called with no params) + apps/web/lib/admin-api.ts:117-126 (limit only sent when passed) + apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:94 (`const limit = filters.limit ?? 20`)
  - steps: 1. Sign in as admin@demo.com / demo123
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/admin/users
3. Read the "Showing … of N users" line at the bottom and page to the last page
  - observed: Direct API check: GET /api/v1/admin/users (no params, as the page calls it) returns 200 with users.length = 20 and total = 23. The page then paginates client-side over those 20 (PAGE_SIZE = 10, apps/web/app/admin/users/page.tsx:68) and its footer computes "of {filtered.length}" — i.e. it reports 20, not 23. Three seeded accounts are unreachable from the admin UI at any page number, and searching/filtering only searches the 20 that were fetched.
  - expected: All 23 users are listable and the footer says 23.
  - customer_impact: The founder's only view of who has signed up is silently truncated and mis-counted. As the user table grows past 20 the blind spot becomes total — new signups land at the end of a list that is never fetched. Client-side search over a truncated set will also report "no results" for users that exist.
  - fix: Either pass a real page/limit through to the API and paginate server-side (the endpoint already accepts page/limit and returns total), or at minimum call getUsers(token, { limit: 500 }). Also render the API's `total`, not `filtered.length`, in the footer when no filter is active.

### [LC-11] P2/wrong-data/M API GET /admin/users silently ignores the status filter, and applies the role filter after pagination
  - where: apps/api/src/services/admin.service.ts:91-145 — `where` is built only from `filters.search` (lines 97-105); `filters.status` is never referenced; `filters.role` is applied at line 141 to the already-paginated `enriched` array
  - steps: 1. Get an admin token: POST /api/v1/auth/login {"email":"admin@demo.com","password":"demo123"}
2. GET /api/v1/admin/users?status=blocked with that Bearer token
3. GET /api/v1/admin/users?role=PANDIT&limit=5
  - observed: The route accepts and zod-validates `status` (apps/api/src/routes/admin.ts:174) then hands it to a service that never uses it — `where` only ever receives an OR clause for `search`, so a status filter returns every user. Separately, `role` is applied with `enriched.filter(...)` on the page-sized slice, while the returned `total` comes from `prisma.user.count({ where })` which knows nothing about role — so a role-filtered call returns "some subset of page 1" alongside a total for all users. (I confirmed the unfiltered call returns n=20/total=23; the filtered probes could not be re-run because the API stopped responding under concurrent Playwright load — see LC-12 note.)
  - expected: `status` filters the query; `role` filters in the database (via the UserRoleAssignment relation) before skip/take, and `total` reflects the filtered count.
  - customer_impact: Any consumer of this endpoint — the admin UI once it starts passing params, or an ops script — gets wrong rows and a wrong count. A moderator filtering for blocked users would see the whole user base.
  - fix: Move both filters into the Prisma `where`: add the status condition, and use `{ roleAssignments: { some: { role: filters.role } } }` for role. Then `count({ where })` is automatically correct.

### [LC-12] P2/ux/M /temples and /astrology filter+sort state never reaches the URL — filtered views cannot be shared or un-done with Back
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples and /astrology — every filter/sort control; apps/web/components/temples/TemplesBrowser.tsx:93-102 and apps/web/app/astrology/AstrologyClient.tsx:281-289 (both call router.replace in an effect)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples
2. Click the "Lord Vishnu" chip — the list correctly narrows to 9 temples
3. Look at the address bar
4. Press Back
  - observed: location.href stays exactly http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples after every interaction I drove: deity chips (Shiva 15, Vishnu 9, Shakti 3, Ganesha 2, Sikh 1), state select, both sort modes, the Live Darshan toggle (33→27) and the search box (Tirupati → 2). Identical on /astrology: after choosing "Price: Low to High" (which correctly reorders to 20,25,40,45,50,55,60,80) the URL is still http://127.0.0.1:3000/astrology. The reverse direction does work — /temples?deity=Vishnu → 9, ?sort=alphabetical → A→Z order, ?state=Gujarat → 3, ?live=1 → 27 — so params are read but never written.
  - expected: Selecting a filter rewrites the URL (e.g. /temples?deity=Vishnu), so the view is linkable and Back removes the filter.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who filters to "Shiva temples in Gujarat" cannot send that link to family, cannot bookmark it, and Back exits the page instead of clearing the filter. It also blocks SEO landing pages for exactly the queries people search. The intent to support this is already in the code — the read path works — so only half the round trip is missing.
  - fix: Debug why the router.replace effect is not landing (log the qs it computes, and try window.history.replaceState as a fallback for pure filter state). Both components share the identical pattern, so one fix covers both.

### [LC-13] P2/wrong-data/S /astrology directory silently renders 8 hardcoded fallback astrologers instead of the 15 the API returns — so "Most Experienced" cannot surface the most experienced
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/astrology — the astrologer grid; apps/web/app/astrology/AstrologyClient.tsx:265-279 (fetch, falls back silently) and the fallbackAstrologers array in the same file
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/astrology
2. Count the astrologer cards and note their per-minute rates
3. Compare with GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/astrology/astrologers?limit=100
  - observed: The page renders 8 cards with rates ₹20, ₹25, ₹40, ₹45, ₹50, ₹55, ₹60, ₹80 — exactly the perMinuteRate values hardcoded in fallbackAstrologers (50,40,60,25,80,20,55,45). The API returns 15 astrologers including Pt. Harish Chandra (₹100/min, 35 yrs, 5.0★) and Acharya Bhaskar Rao (₹90/min, 28 yrs) — neither ever appears. Consequence: selecting "Most Experienced" puts a 30-year astrologer first while the actual 35-year, 5.0-rated astrologer is absent from the list entirely. No error, banner or empty state is shown; the substitution is invisible. Caveat: apps/web/.env.local sets NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:4000/api/v1 while the app is served from 127.0.0.1:3000, so a cross-origin/CORS mismatch is a plausible trigger for the fallback on this stack — but the defect being reported is the silent substitution itself, which would behave the same way during a real API outage.
  - expected: The directory shows the live astrologer list; if the fetch fails, the page says so rather than presenting fabricated practitioners as bookable.
  - customer_impact: Users browse, sort and try to book astrologers who are not the real roster. The top-rated and most-experienced practitioners are invisible, and every sort operates on the wrong universe — so a correctly-implemented sort still returns a wrong answer. Astrologers who edit their profile see no change on the public page.
  - fix: Surface the existing apiError state as a visible banner instead of rendering fallbacks as real data, and make the fallback empty (or clearly labelled sample data) rather than eight fake bookable profiles. Separately, align the local NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL host with the origin the app is served from so the local stack exercises the real path.

### [LC-14] P2/wrong-data/S /blog index renders zero posts
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/blog — the post list; apps/web/lib/blog-posts.ts:1-8 (fetches the post list from raw.githubusercontent.com at request time)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/blog
2. Count the post links
  - observed: Zero anchors matching /blog/ are rendered — the index is empty. The data source is a request-time fetch of docs/agent-reports/blog-drafts from GitHub raw, so an empty or unreachable directory produces an empty list.
  - expected: Either posts render, or the page shows an explicit "no posts yet" state rather than an empty shell.
  - customer_impact: The blog is a content/SEO surface linked from navigation; an empty index looks abandoned to a first-time visitor and wastes the crawl. TASKS.md already tracks this as #216 "blog stub", so this is a confirmation rather than a new discovery.
  - fix: Publish at least one post, and add an explicit empty state plus a logged warning when the GitHub fetch returns nothing so the failure is not silent.

  - fix: Confirm gurukul seed data exists; if the catalogue is genuinely empty, show a "coming soon" state rather than a bare filtered list.

### [LC-16] P3/wrong-data/S /store-admin/orders status-tab counts ignore the search box, so a tab labelled with a number can render fewer rows
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/store-admin/orders — the status tab pills; apps/web/app/store-admin/orders/page.tsx:293 (counts) vs :279-285 (filtered rows)
  - steps: 1. Sign in as store@demo.com / demo123
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store-admin/orders
3. Type a customer name into "Search by customer name or order number..."
4. Compare the number badge on each status tab with the rows now listed
  - observed: Tab counts are computed as `orders.filter((o) => o.status === s).length` — over the unsearched list — while the rows come from `filtered`, which applies both search and status. With a search active, a tab can read "5" and render 2 rows.
  - expected: Tab counts reflect the currently active search, or the counts are visually de-emphasised as totals.
  - customer_impact: Minor but it is exactly the "a number that disagrees with the same number on another screen" class — a store owner reconciling orders sees a badge that does not match the list underneath it.
  - fix: Compute the tab counts from the search-filtered array (apply matchSearch before the per-status tally).

### [LC-17] P3/ux/S /temples "Reset filters" does not reset the sort mode
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples — the "Reset filters" button; apps/web/components/temples/TemplesBrowser.tsx:136-142 (resetFilters omits setSort) and :133-134 (hasActiveFilters omits sort)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples
2. Set the sort dropdown to "A → Z" and pick any deity chip so the reset button appears
3. Click "Reset filters"
  - observed: resetFilters() clears search, deity, state and liveOnly but never calls setSort, so the list stays alphabetical while the UI presents itself as reset. hasActiveFilters also excludes sort, so a page with only a non-default sort applied shows no reset affordance at all.
  - expected: Either reset returns sort to "Most Popular" too, or the button is labelled precisely enough ("Clear filters") that leaving sort alone is obviously intentional.
  - customer_impact: Small confusion on an otherwise excellent browser — the temples page is the best-built list in the product and this is the only rough edge I found on it.
  - fix: Add setSort('popularity') to resetFilters, or keep the current behaviour and confirm the label reads "Clear filters" rather than "Reset".

## AREA: design-uniformity   score=41

### [DU-01] P1/design/S Three of the four brand font tokens are wired to names Tailwind v4 never reads, so 579 font-serif/font-mono call sites render in OS fallback faces
  - where: apps/web/app/globals.css:115-118 (--font-family-sans/display/serif/devanagari inside @theme) and :189-192 (--font-display/--font-body/--font-mono inside :root); rendered evidence on http://127.0.0.1:3000/ and http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login in Chrome DevTools console.
2. Run: getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--font-serif')
3. Run: getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--font-family-sans')
4. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/, inspect the Sanskrit festival name in the 'Next festival' strip (span.text-base.font-serif.text-surface-500.ml-1) and read its computed font-family.
5. On any page, inspect the keyboard-shortcut hint in the header search (kbd with class font-mono) and read its computed font-family.
  - observed: --font-serif resolves to "ui-serif, Georgia, Cambria, \"Times New Roman\", Times, serif" (Tailwind stock). --font-sans resolves to "ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif, ..." (Tailwind stock). --font-family-sans resolves to the EMPTY STRING. --font-mono resolves to "\"JetBrains Mono\", \"Fira Code\", monospace" — neither face is loaded anywhere (zero @font-face rules and no next/font entry in the whole app). The font-serif festival name on / computes to family 'ui-serif'; the font-mono kbd computes to 'JetBrains Mono' on all 10 screens. Repo counts: 442 uses of font-serif across 120 files, 137 uses of font-mono across 65 files.
  - expected: font-serif should resolve to the Crimson Pro face that apps/web/app/layout.tsx:71 already downloads via next/font, and font-mono to an actually-loaded mono face. Every element that asks for a brand typeface should get one.
  - customer_impact: Every Sanskrit shloka, verse and deity name styled with font-serif renders in Georgia/Times instead of the devotional serif the brand pays to load — the single most emotionally loaded text on a devotional product is in the OS default. Every monospaced value (donation receipt numbers in components/DonationForm.tsx:375, commission tables in components/CommissionsPanel.tsx) renders in a different face on Windows vs Mac vs Android, so the same receipt looks different to two devotees. Crimson Pro is downloaded on every page load and used only inside the book reader.
  - fix: In globals.css @theme, rename --font-family-sans -> --font-sans, --font-family-serif -> --font-serif, add --font-display, and move --font-mono into @theme. Point --font-serif at var(--font-reader-serif) (the Crimson Pro variable already declared at layout.tsx:74) and --font-sans at 'Satoshi', var(--font-noto-sans), system-ui. Delete the now-duplicate :root font declarations at globals.css:189-192.

### [DU-02] P1/design/S shadow-warm / shadow-warm-sm / shadow-warm-md are undefined utilities — 49 usages render box-shadow: none, including the sign-in card
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login, the sign-in card div with class="shadow-warm overflow-hidden rounded-xl border ... bg-[var(--color-surface)]" (apps/web/app/auth/login/page.tsx:303); token absent from apps/web/app/globals.css (only --shadow-sm/md/lg/float exist, at :203-206)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login.
2. Inspect the outer card that wraps the sign-in form (it carries the class shadow-warm).
3. Read its computed box-shadow.
4. In the console run: getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--shadow-warm')
  - observed: The card's computed box-shadow is exactly "none". --shadow-warm resolves to the EMPTY STRING. For contrast, --shadow-sm on the same page resolves to "0 1px 3px lab(12.9465% 3.69322 6.93114 / .06)", so the warm shadow tokens that DO exist are applying correctly. Grep across apps/web finds 49 occurrences of shadow-warm / shadow-warm-sm / shadow-warm-md in 22 files and zero matching --shadow-warm* declaration in any .css file.
  - expected: Either the tokens exist in @theme and the cards get the warm elevation they were designed with, or the classes are replaced with the shadow-sm/md/lg that do exist. A card marked shadow-warm should not be visually flat.
  - customer_impact: The sign-in card — the first authenticated surface a paying customer touches — sits completely flat on the page background with only a hairline border, while cards elsewhere on the same site (which use shadow-sm/md) float. Same for the temple-detail action cards and the dashboard stat tiles. It reads as an unfinished page rather than a deliberate flat style, because the intent to elevate is right there in the class name.
  - fix: Add --shadow-warm-sm/--shadow-warm/--shadow-warm-md to the @theme block in globals.css (they can simply alias the existing --shadow-sm/md/lg values), or sweep the 49 call sites to the existing shadow-sm/shadow-md/shadow-lg. Add a CI grep that fails on a shadow-*/text-*/bg-* utility with no matching @theme token.

### [DU-03] P1/design/L No shared Button primitive: the primary CTA ships in 11 pixel heights, 11 paddings, 4 radii and 5 type styles across the 10 measured screens
  - where: apps/web/components/ui/ contains only DateInput.tsx and CityAutocomplete.tsx — zero matches for 'function Button' or 'const Button' anywhere in apps/web/components. Measured on all 10 screens.
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/ and measure the height of 'Explore Temples' in the hero, then 'Get free panchang' lower on the page.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples and measure the 'All Deities' filter chip.
3. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store and measure 'Shop Sale' and the 'All Products' chip.
4. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login and measure the 'Sign In' submit button.
5. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/pricing and measure 'Get Started Free' and 'Start Free Trial'.
6. Compare against the header 'Sign In' pill, present on every page.
  - observed: 11 distinct heights for warm-filled primary buttons: 31.2, 33.2, 35.2, 36, 39.2, 40.8, 42, 42.4, 44, 49.6, 56px. 11 distinct padding sets: 0/12, 8/16, 10/20, 8/20, 0/20, 0/16, 6/12, 10/16, 12/24, 10/0, 10/24 (t/r/b/l). 4 radii: 8px (error page), 10px (header 'Ask Pandit'), 16px (most CTAs), full pill. 5 type styles: 13.4px/500 Satoshi, 13.4px/600 Satoshi, 16px/400 Satoshi, 16px/500 Satoshi, 14px/500 system-ui. 15 distinct full recipes in a single 8-page capture. Named outliers: /auth/login submit is 49.6px tall / 12px-24px padding / 16px-500 type — taller and larger-typed than any other CTA on the site; /pricing 'Start Free Trial' has 0px horizontal padding (10px/0px/10px/0px); the header 'Sign In' is the only gradient button on the site (linear-gradient(135deg, rgb(184,115,51), rgb(158,98,41))) with a shadow, and it appears on all 10 screens next to flat solid CTAs. Repo-wide: 297 elements carry bg-saffron-600, producing 85 distinct class recipes, 26 distinct padding combinations and 6 distinct radius utilities.
  - expected: One Button component with a small fixed set of sizes (e.g. sm 32px / md 40px / lg 48px), one radius, one fill per variant, one label type ramp. Every primary CTA on the site should be visually interchangeable.
  - customer_impact: A devotee moving from the homepage hero to a puja card to checkout to the sign-in form sees the same 'do the thing' button change height four times and change shape twice. On the temple detail page the five 'Book' buttons are 31.2px tall while the 'Book Puja' beneath them is 39.2px — buttons of the same intent, stacked, visibly different. This is the single loudest 'stitched together by different people' signal on the product and it sits directly on the conversion path.
  - fix: Create apps/web/components/ui/Button.tsx with variant (primary/secondary/ghost/danger) and size (sm/md/lg) props, all values read from @theme tokens. Migrate the 10 measured screens first (they cover the whole conversion funnel), then sweep the remaining 297 bg-saffron-600 sites. Add a lint rule banning bg-saffron-*/bg-amber-* directly on a button or anchor.

### [DU-04] P1/design/M Two different colours are both shipping as 'the primary' — #B87333 and #9E6229 — plus a gradient of the two, sometimes on the same screen
  - where: apps/web/app/globals.css:30-32 labels --color-saffron-500 #b87333 as --color-primary and --color-saffron-600 #9e6229 as --color-primary-hover; both render as resting fills. Seen on http://127.0.0.1:3000/, /store, /pricing, /temples.
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/ and read the computed background-color of the 'Explore Temples' CTA, then of the 'Get free panchang' button further down.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store and read the background-color of 'Shop Sale'.
3. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/pricing and read the background-color of 'Get Started Free'.
4. Read the background-image of the header 'Sign In' pill on any page.
  - observed: On / : 'Explore Temples' = #B87333 and 'Get free panchang' = #9E6229, on the same page. /store primary 'Shop Sale' = #9E6229. /pricing primary 'Get Started Free' = #B87333. Header 'Sign In' = linear-gradient(135deg, rgb(184,115,51), rgb(158,98,41)) — a blend of both. Repo-wide the token labelled 'hover' is the de-facto resting primary: bg-saffron-600 appears 339 times vs bg-saffron-500 53 times, and bg-saffron-700 (labelled 'active') appears 240 times.
  - expected: One resting primary fill across the product. If #9E6229 is the real primary, the token comments and --color-primary should be corrected to match, and #B87333 demoted to a lighter accent.
  - customer_impact: Two adjacent CTAs on the homepage are visibly different shades of copper. A devotee comparing the /store CTA against the /pricing CTA sees two different brand colours. It also means the hover state on a #9E6229 button moves to #7d4e1f (a much darker step) rather than the one-step change the ramp was designed for.
  - fix: Pick one (the usage data says #9E6229). Set --color-primary to it in globals.css, fix the misleading comments on lines 30-32, and sweep bg-saffron-500 -> bg-saffron-600 on interactive fills. Retire the gradient variant on the header CTA so it matches the in-page primary.

### [DU-05] P1/design/M The h1 is a different thing on every screen: 7 treatments, 18px to 70.4px, two typefaces — and two of the ten screens have no h1 at all
  - where: All 10 screens. Source recipes: apps/web/app/page.tsx (home hero), apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx:332, apps/web/components/temples/TemplesBrowser.tsx, apps/web/app/store/page.tsx, apps/web/app/temples/[slug]/page.tsx, apps/web/app/pricing/page.tsx:93, apps/web/app/priest/page.tsx, apps/web/app/admin/page.tsx:268, apps/web/app/auth/login/page.tsx:310
  - steps: 1. Visit each of /, /pujas, /temples, /store, /temples/tirumala-tirupati, /pricing, /priest, /admin, /auth/login.
2. On each, run in the console: const h=document.querySelector('h1'); h ? [document.querySelectorAll('h1').length, getComputedStyle(h).fontSize, getComputedStyle(h).fontWeight, getComputedStyle(h).fontFamily.split(',')[0], getComputedStyle(h).color] : 'NO H1'
  - observed: 7 distinct h1 treatments: / = Cabinet Grotesk 70.4px w600 lh77.44 ls-1.408 #2A2520; /pujas + /temples + /store = Satoshi 53.6px w700 lh59.56 ls-normal #2A2520; /temples/tirumala-tirupati = Satoshi 53.6px w600 #FFFFFF; /pricing = Cabinet Grotesk 43.2px w700 #F9F8F4; /priest = Satoshi 36px w600 ls-0.72 #2A2520; /admin = Satoshi 18px w600 #2A2520; the 404 page = Cabinet Grotesk 25.3px w600. /auth/login has ZERO h1 (its title is a span.text-surface-800.text-xl.font-semibold at page.tsx:310). The /pujas/[slug] error state has ZERO h1. /priest has TWO h1 in the DOM (h1_in_dom=2, h1_visible=1 at 1280px) because apps/web/app/priest/layout.tsx renders h1.text-sm.font-semibold.lg:hidden in addition to the page h1 — below the lg breakpoint both are visible. Repo-wide: 349 h1 tags in 45 distinct size+weight recipes, 4 weight/family combinations (font-bold 198, font-semibold 89, font-serif 52, font-serif+font-bold 6) and 7 colour tokens.
  - expected: One h1 recipe per page archetype (marketing / catalogue / detail / portal), exactly one h1 per page, and the display typeface used consistently for it.
  - customer_impact: The page title — the thing that tells a devotee where they are — is 3.9x bigger on the homepage than in the admin portal and switches typeface between /, /pricing and everything else. Two of ten screens have no h1, so screen-reader users landing on the sign-in page get no page title in the heading list, and the sign-in page has no h1 for search engines either.
  - fix: Define three h1 recipes in globals.css (marketing / catalogue / app-chrome), apply per archetype, promote the /auth/login title span to an h1, add an h1 to the /pujas/[slug] error boundary, and make the priest layout title an aria-labelled div rather than a second h1.

### [DU-06] P2/design/M The display typeface is effectively homepage-only — 6 of 10 screens render zero Cabinet Grotesk
  - where: Cabinet Grotesk is loaded on every page from api.fontshare.com (apps/web/app/layout.tsx:278) and declared as --font-display (globals.css:189), but is used almost nowhere.
  - steps: 1. Open each of the 10 screens.
2. Run: [...document.querySelectorAll('*')].filter(e=>!e.children.length && e.textContent.trim()).map(e=>getComputedStyle(e).fontFamily.split(',')[0].replace(/"/g,'')).reduce((a,f)=>(a[f]=(a[f]||0)+1,a),{})
  - observed: Cabinet Grotesk leaf-text counts: / = 57, /pricing = 10, 404 page = 1, and ZERO on /pujas, /pujas/[slug], /temples, /temples/tirumala-tirupati, /store, /auth/login, /priest, /admin. On those six screens every heading, including the h1, renders in Satoshi (the body face). The font is still downloaded from a third-party CDN on all of them.
  - expected: The display face carries the heading hierarchy across the product, or it is removed and the extra CDN round-trip with it.
  - customer_impact: The homepage looks like a different, more designed product than the rest of the site. A devotee arriving on / and clicking through to /pujas experiences a typeface change at the exact moment of highest intent. The site also pays a third-party font download on every page for a face that six of ten pages never paint.
  - fix: Decide the rule (recommended: display face for h1/h2 on marketing and catalogue pages, body face for app chrome), encode it as a .heading-display utility in globals.css, and apply it. If Cabinet Grotesk stays homepage-only, load it only on that route.

### [DU-07] P2/design/M Visual and semantic heading hierarchy contradict each other — an h3 renders at 36px on /pujas while an h2 on the same page renders at 16px
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/pujas ('Book a Pandit at Your Home' h3 vs 'Site footer' h2), http://127.0.0.1:3000/priest ('Upcoming Bookings' h2 vs 'Accept Bookings' h3), http://127.0.0.1:3000/admin ('Recent Signups' h2)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/pujas.
2. Run: [...document.querySelectorAll('h2,h3')].filter(e=>e.getBoundingClientRect().height>0).map(e=>e.tagName+' '+getComputedStyle(e).fontSize+' w'+getComputedStyle(e).fontWeight+' :: '+e.textContent.trim().slice(0,30))
3. Repeat on /priest and /admin.
  - observed: /pujas: h3 'Book a Pandit at Your Home' = 36px w700, h3 'Book a Pandit for Your Wedding' = 36px w700, while h2 'Site footer' = 16px w400 — the h3s are more than twice the size of the h2. /priest: h2 'Upcoming Bookings' = 16px w600 and h3 'Accept Bookings' = 16px w600, visually identical. /admin: h2 'Recent Signups' = 13.4px w600, smaller than the 16px body text. Across the 10 screens: 9 distinct visible h2 styles spanning 13.4px-43.2px and 10 distinct visible h3 styles spanning 12px-36px — the two ranges overlap almost completely, and the /admin h1 (18px) is smaller than the /pujas h3 (36px). Repo-wide: 811 h2 tags in 37 distinct size+weight recipes.
  - expected: h1 > h2 > h3 in rendered size on every page, and h2/h3 visually distinguishable from each other.
  - customer_impact: A devotee scanning /pujas cannot tell section headers from sub-items because size no longer tracks importance. On /priest the section title and the card titles under it are pixel-identical, so a pandit scanning for 'Upcoming Bookings' has no visual anchor. Assistive-technology users get a heading outline that does not match what sighted users see.
  - fix: Publish an h1/h2/h3 size-and-weight ramp derived from the existing --text-* scale and enforce it; forbid an h3 from exceeding the h2 size on the same page. Fix the three named pages first.

### [DU-08] P2/ux/S Heading level skips: /temples, /store and /priest jump straight from h1 to h3 with no h2 in between
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples, http://127.0.0.1:3000/store, http://127.0.0.1:3000/priest
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples.
2. Run: [...document.querySelectorAll('h1,h2,h3,h4,h5,h6')].map(e=>e.tagName[1]).join('')
3. Repeat on /store and /priest.
  - observed: /temples level sequence is 1333333333333333333333323333344442 — the h1 'Sacred Temples of India' is followed by 21 consecutive h3 temple-card titles with no h2 anywhere before them. /store is 1333333333333333333333333323333344442 — same pattern, 24 h3s. /priest is 11323323333344442 — two h1s, then an h3 ('Complete your Pandit profile') before the first h2. Only /, /pujas, /temples/[slug], /pricing and /admin have a clean sequence.
  - expected: No level skipped on the way down; each h3 group sits under an h2 that names it.
  - customer_impact: Screen-reader users navigating /temples or /store by heading get a flat list of 21-24 same-level items with no grouping, so there is no way to jump past the card grid. It also weakens the SEO outline on the two highest-traffic catalogue pages.
  - fix: Wrap the card grids in a section with a real h2 ('All temples' / 'All products') — visually hidden if the design does not want a visible label — and demote the card titles to h3 under it. On /priest, make the layout title a div and move the onboarding banner heading below the first h2.

### [DU-09] P2/design/M 16 distinct warm brand colours render across the 10 screens, including two Tailwind stock oranges that are not in the palette at all
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/ (worst offender), /temples, /priest, /admin; palette declared in apps/web/app/globals.css:25-34 (saffron ramp) and :76-85 (amber ramp)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/.
2. Run a census of computed colours: [...document.querySelectorAll('*')].filter(e=>e.getBoundingClientRect().width>0).map(e=>getComputedStyle(e).color).filter(c=>{const p=c.match(/\d+/g).map(Number);return p[0]>=90&&p[0]<=253&&p[0]-p[2]>=45&&p[1]<=p[0]}).reduce((a,c)=>(a[c]=(a[c]||0)+1,a),{})
3. Repeat on /temples, /priest, /admin.
  - observed: 16 distinct warm hexes across the 10 screens. Only 6 belong to the saffron ramp (#B87333, #9E6229, #7D4E1F, #F99A48, #FBBD7D, #FDD8AF). The rest are a different hue family: #78350F, #92400E, #B45309, #D97706, #F59E0B (amber), #D4A438 (temple gold), #C53030 (sacred red), #F2C4A8 (art peach), plus #E16318 (17 elements) and #9E3F0A (13 elements) on / which match no token declared anywhere in globals.css — they are Tailwind's stock orange-600/orange-700. So the homepage paints a pure orange on 30 elements right beside the brand's muted copper #B87333 (126 elements). /temples paints #F59E0B on 13 backgrounds. Repo-wide: 24 distinct warm background tokens over 1,680 uses (bg-saffron-50 494, bg-saffron-600 339, bg-saffron-700 240, bg-amber-50 196, bg-orange-* 26).
  - expected: One saffron ramp for brand, with amber/gold reserved for a named semantic role (time-sensitive, rating) and no stock Tailwind orange at all.
  - customer_impact: The homepage shows two visibly different oranges side by side — a pure saturated orange on the icons next to the muted copper of the brand. On a devotional product where saffron IS the brand, having three orange families on one screen is the difference between looking considered and looking assembled.
  - fix: Remove the orange-* palette from use entirely (26 sites), decide whether amber is a semantic role or dead weight, and add a lint rule allowing only saffron-*/temple-gold/semantic tokens for warm colour. Start with the two undeclared oranges on /.

### [DU-10] P2/design/M Reading size for body copy varies from 12px to 18px across the product — five different paragraph styles, and 12px/13.4px is below comfortable reading size
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/priest (12px), / and /auth/login and /admin and the puja error page (13.4px), /temples/tirumala-tirupati (16px), /pricing (17px), /pujas and /temples and /store (18px)
  - steps: 1. On each screen run: const p=[...document.querySelectorAll('p')].find(e=>e.textContent.trim().length>70 && e.getBoundingClientRect().height>0); [getComputedStyle(p).fontSize, getComputedStyle(p).lineHeight, getComputedStyle(p).color]
2. Compare /priest against /pujas.
  - observed: Five distinct body-paragraph styles across the 10 screens: 12px w400 lh16px #7A756E (/priest), 13.4px w400 lh21.504px #7A756E (/, /auth/login, /admin, puja error), 16px w400 lh25.6px #4A4540 (/temples/[slug]), 17px w400 lh27.136px #B5B0A8 (/pricing), 18px w400 lh28px #7A756E (/pujas, /temples, /store). That is a 50% spread in reading size for the same role. Note the line-height ratio also swings: 1.33 on /priest vs 1.60 on /pujas. Root cause visible in source: globals.css declares two competing 'base' sizes — --text-base: clamp(0.9375rem, ..., 1.0625rem) in @theme at :119 and --type-base: clamp(0.875rem, ..., 0.9375rem) in :root at :197. Repo-wide there are also 722 arbitrary text-[Npx] sizes bypassing the scale entirely, 102 of them text-[9px].
  - expected: One body reading size (16px+) with one line-height ratio, used for all prose; small sizes reserved for metadata.
  - customer_impact: A pandit reading his own dashboard gets 12px prose while a devotee browsing /pujas gets 18px — the people who use the product all day get the least readable text. 9px and 10px labels (722 arbitrary sizes site-wide) are below what most users over 40 can read on a phone, and this is a product whose core audience skews older.
  - fix: Delete one of the two base scales in globals.css (keep --text-*), set a single prose size and line-height, and sweep the 722 text-[Npx] arbitrary values onto the scale — starting with the 102 text-[9px].

### [DU-11] P2/design/S The body font stack falls back to a font that is never loaded, while the self-hosted fallback that IS loaded is referenced nowhere
  - where: apps/web/app/globals.css:190 (--font-body: 'Satoshi', 'Inter', sans-serif) and apps/web/app/layout.tsx:57-62 (Noto Sans loaded as --font-noto-sans) and :278 (Satoshi + Cabinet Grotesk loaded from api.fontshare.com)
  - steps: 1. Open any page and run: getComputedStyle(document.body).fontFamily
2. Grep the repo for --font-noto-sans and count the references.
3. Block api.fontshare.com in DevTools Network conditions, reload, and observe the rendered body face.
  - observed: Computed body font-family is "Satoshi, Inter, sans-serif" on all 10 screens. 'Inter' has no @font-face and no next/font entry anywhere in apps/web, so it can never resolve. Meanwhile Noto Sans IS downloaded on every page via next/font (layout.tsx:57) and --font-noto-sans has exactly ONE occurrence in the entire repo — its own declaration at layout.tsx:60 — so no rule ever uses it. Satoshi and Cabinet Grotesk both come from the third-party api.fontshare.com CDN.
  - expected: The fallback chain should be Satoshi -> the self-hosted Noto Sans -> system-ui, so a Fontshare outage degrades to a loaded webfont rather than to Arial.
  - customer_impact: If api.fontshare.com is slow, blocked by an ISP or corporate proxy, or blocked by an ad-blocker, the entire site drops to the OS default sans — not to the Noto Sans it already downloaded. Devanagari content is the specific risk: Satoshi and Cabinet Grotesk carry no Devanagari glyphs, so Hindi text already relies on the fallback path working. As shipped, the app pays for a Noto Sans download on every page and gets nothing for it.
  - fix: Change --font-body to 'Satoshi', var(--font-noto-sans), system-ui, sans-serif and drop the unreachable 'Inter'. Consider self-hosting Satoshi and Cabinet Grotesk to remove the third-party dependency from the critical render path.

### [DU-12] P2/design/L Cool-gray leak: 2,128 gray/slate/zinc/neutral/stone utilities on a product whose neutral ramp is warm cream
  - where: apps/web (122 files). Design system declares the warm surface ramp at apps/web/app/globals.css:11-22 (#f5f3ef through #1a1510).
  - steps: 1. Grep apps/web for (text|bg|border|from|to|divide|ring)-(gray|slate|zinc|neutral|stone)-[0-9]{2,3} and count.
2. Grep for <h1 className="..."> and tabulate the colour token used.
3. Open a page such as /jyotirlinga or /yoga-sutras next to / and compare the heading colour.
  - observed: 2,128 cool-gray utility occurrences across 122 files. In the h1 colour census, 86 of 349 h1 tags use text-gray-900 (a cool #101828-family gray) versus 213 using the warm text-surface-800/900. In the h2 census, 218 of 811 use text-gray-900 versus 362 warm. Card grounds show the same split: 1,345 uses of bg-white (pure #FFFFFF) versus 653 uses of bg-surface-50/100/200 (#f5f3ef/#faf9f6/#ede9e3), so two different card grounds coexist on a #F5F3EF page. Shadow temperature splits the same way: --shadow-sm/md/lg are warm lab()-tinted, but 25 uses of shadow-xl/shadow-2xl fall through to Tailwind's cool black shadows.
  - expected: One neutral ramp. Warm surface tokens everywhere, with cool grays removed.
  - customer_impact: Whole sections of the content library render in cool gray-on-cream, which reads as slightly dirty next to the warm sections — the kind of thing a user cannot name but registers as 'cheap'. Pure-white cards next to #faf9f6 cards on the same page produce a visible seam.
  - fix: Codemod text-gray-N -> text-surface-N and friends across the 122 files, standardise on one card ground, and add a lint rule banning gray/slate/zinc/neutral/stone utilities in apps/web.

### [DU-13] P3/design/S The puja error screen is rendered in the OS system font with an off-scale radius and no heading
  - where: apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/error.tsx:31 (fontFamily: 'system-ui, sans-serif'), :51 (borderRadius 0.5rem), :35 (page title as a <p>); observed at http://127.0.0.1:3000/pujas/rudrabhishek
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/pujas/rudrabhishek (it currently 500s because the API is down, which is what surfaces this boundary).
2. Inspect the 'Couldn't load this puja' text and the 'Try again' button and read their computed font-family and border-radius.
3. Run: document.querySelectorAll('h1').length
  - observed: 4 leaf elements render in family 'system-ui' and 1 in 'monospace' — the only screen of the 10 where either appears. The 'Try again' button computes to height 42.4px, border-radius 8px, font 14px w500 system-ui, background #B87333 — 8px is a radius that appears on no other button in the product (all others are 10px, 16px or pill) and 14px/500 system-ui matches no other button type style. document.querySelectorAll('h1').length is 0; the title is a <p>. Colour values are inlined as literals (#b87333, #ede9e3, #7a756e, #b5b0a8) rather than tokens. The file's own comment on line 5 describes it as 'a branded recovery UI'.
  - expected: The error boundary uses the same fonts, tokens, button component and h1 as the rest of the product.
  - customer_impact: The moment a booking page fails is the moment the customer most needs to trust the brand, and it is the one screen that visibly is not the brand — different typeface, different button shape, hardcoded colours. It also has no h1, so it is the second of two screens with no page title.
  - fix: Rewrite error.tsx with Tailwind classes and the shared Button, promote the title to an h1, and delete the inline style object. Apply the same treatment to the sibling boundaries under app/temples/[slug]/error.tsx.

### [DU-14] P3/design/M Page container width changes route to route — 11 distinct max-w values across 679 container declarations
  - where: apps/web/app (679 occurrences of a centred max-w-* container)
  - steps: 1. Grep apps/web/app for 'mx-auto max-w-[a-z0-9]+' and 'max-w-[a-z0-9]+ mx-auto' and tabulate.
2. Open / then /pujas then /pricing at 1440px and watch the left content edge move.
  - observed: 11 distinct container widths in use: max-w-4xl (183), max-w-6xl (137), max-w-5xl (125), max-w-3xl (88), max-w-2xl (46), max-w-7xl (45), max-w-xl (29), max-w-md (18), max-w-lg (5), max-w-xs (2), max-w-sm (1). Individual routes mix several within one page (app/yatra/[slug]/page.tsx alone uses 3xl, 4xl, 5xl, 6xl and lg).
  - expected: Two or three container widths — a wide one for grids, a narrow one for prose — applied consistently.
  - customer_impact: The content column jumps horizontally as a devotee navigates between routes, so the page never feels like one continuous product. On wide monitors the effect is obvious.
  - fix: Define .container-wide / .container-prose utilities in globals.css and migrate the 679 sites, starting with the top-level page shells of the 10 highest-traffic routes.

### [DU-15] P3/design/M Border radius sprawls to 13 utilities over 888 uses against a 6-token scale, including 41 uses of the untokenised bare `rounded`
  - where: apps/web (888 rounded-* occurrences); scale declared at apps/web/app/globals.css:141-147 (--radius-sm through --radius-3xl)
  - steps: 1. Grep apps/web for \brounded(-[a-z0-9]+)?\b and tabulate the variants.
2. Compare against the six --radius-* tokens declared in globals.css:142-147.
  - observed: 13 distinct radius utilities in 888 uses: rounded-lg (293), rounded-xl (240), rounded-full (192), rounded-md (64), rounded-2xl (44), bare rounded (41), rounded-sm (3), rounded-br (3), rounded-bl (3), rounded-t (2), rounded-none (1), rounded-tl (1), rounded-3xl (1). The design system declares six radius tokens; bare `rounded` maps to no declared token. On the button set alone the measured radii were 8px, 10px, 16px and pill.
  - expected: Buttons one radius, cards one radius, pills one radius — three values from the declared scale, nothing else.
  - customer_impact: Corner roundness is one of the strongest cues that two elements belong to the same system. With four radii on buttons and six on containers, adjacent components read as imported from different kits.
  - fix: Pick three radii (control / card / pill), replace the rest, delete the unused --radius tokens, and add a lint rule for bare `rounded`.

## AREA: responsive   score=42

### [RESP-01] P0/broken-feature/S Cookie consent card buries the entire /auth/login form at 390x844 — Sign In is invisible and un-tappable on first visit
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login at 390x844 — consent overlay apps/web/components/CookieBanner.tsx:95 (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-[80]`) vs the Sign In button apps/web/app/auth/login/page.tsx:563
  - steps: 1. Open a fresh browser profile (or delete localStorage key `darshya_cookie_consent_v1`).
2. Set the viewport to 390x844.
3. Navigate to http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login and wait for hydration.
4. Look for the email input, the password field and the Sign In button.
  - observed: Consent card rect: top=497 bottom=844, height=347px = 41.2% of the 844px viewport. Sign In button rect: top=649 bottom=699. `document.elementFromPoint(195, 674)` (the Sign In button's own centre) returns `<button>` whose text is "Reject all" — logged as `{"signIn":{"top":649,"bottom":699,"cx":195,"cy":674},"banner":{"top":497,"bottom":844},"hitTag":"button","hitText":"Reject all","intercepted":true}`. The 390x844 screenshot shows the page ending at the "Email Address" label with the input itself ~90% covered; the password field, "Forgot password?", Sign In, Terms of Service, Privacy Policy and "Sign Up" are all behind the opaque white card. Scrolling does not help — the overlay is position:fixed (verified: scrolled to document bottom, banner still at 497-844). After programmatically clicking "Reject all", the same hit test returns the Sign In button: `{"intercepted":false}`. Same occlusion at 1440x900: Sign In at [720,715] hit-tests to `div.mx-auto.max-w-3xl` (banner card, top=692); at 768x1024 the "Sign Up" link at [455,855] hit-tests to `h2#darshya-cookie-title`.
  - expected: Consent UI must never overlay the primary form. Either the page content is offset by the banner height while it is open, or the banner is a compact single-row bar that leaves the form reachable.
  - customer_impact: This is the door to every authenticated flow. A first-time visitor on the most common Indian phone size opens the login page and sees a cookie wall where the form should be — the email box is half-cut and Sign In does not exist on screen. It is recoverable with one tap on "Accept all", but the page reads as broken and the tap they will instinctively make (on the form) lands on "Reject all" instead. Straight signup/login conversion loss on mobile.
  - fix: In CookieBanner.tsx, while `open`, publish the banner height as a CSS var and reserve space: `useLayoutEffect` -> `document.documentElement.style.setProperty('--consent-h', rect.height+'px')` with `body { padding-bottom: var(--consent-h, 0) }`, cleared on dismiss. Cheaper alternative: make the <640px variant a single-line bar (title truncated to one line, `flex-row` buttons) so it is ~72px instead of 347px, and still reserve that height.

### [RESP-02] P1/ux/S Same consent overlay occludes the primary CTA on six more surfaces across all three viewports
  - where: apps/web/components/CookieBanner.tsx:95 — affects /, /pujas, /pujas/rudrabhishek, /pujas/rudrabhishek/book, /temples, /store, /admin, /admin/users
  - steps: 1. Fresh profile (no `darshya_cookie_consent_v1` in localStorage).
2. For each viewport 390x844 / 768x1024 / 1440x900, open each URL and hit-test the CTA nearest the bottom of the first screen.
  - observed: Banner height by viewport: 347px @390 (41.2% of viewport), 206px @768, 208px @1440.
• / @390: `a` "Live DarshanWatch live aarti from 200+ temple" at [195,559] -> hit `h2#darshya-cookie-title`; "Book a PujaSchedule a ritual at a temple" at [195,714] -> hit `button.inline-flex.h-11`.
• / @1440: "Live Darshan" [352,789], "Book a Puja" [720,789], "Ask a Pandit" [1088,789] all -> hit the banner card `div.mx-auto.max-w-3xl.rounded-xl`.
• /store @390: category filter buttons "All Products24" [82,587] and "Puja Essentials5" [229,587] -> hit the banner paragraph. /store @1440: "Shop Sale" [1242,715] -> hit `div.fixed.inset-x-0.bottom-0`, coverZ 80.
• /temples @390: "Live Darshan" toggle [78,584] -> hit the banner.
• puja detail @768: "Book This Puja" [384,898] -> hit the banner paragraph; "Save" [342,953] -> hit `button.inline-flex.h-11`.
• /pujas/rudrabhishek/book @390: "Retry" [195,555] and "Browse all pujas" [195,600] -> hit the banner.
• /admin and /admin/users @1440: sidebar links "Reports" [128,723], "Metrics" [128,767], "Agents" [128,811], "Settings" [128,855] all -> `div.fixed.inset-x-0.bottom-0`, coverPosition fixed, coverZ 80 (the banner is `inset-x-0` so it spans over the fixed left sidebar too).
  - expected: Consent UI sits below page content, not on top of navigation and conversion buttons.
  - customer_impact: Every entry point into the product — the three homepage category cards, the store's category filter row, the puja page's Book CTA, and the admin's lower nav — is dead on the first pageview until consent is dismissed. Users who ignore the banner (most do) click and nothing happens.
  - fix: Same single fix as RESP-01: reserve the banner height on <body>. For the admin sidebar case also constrain the banner to the content column (`lg:left-[var(--sidebar-w)]`) so it never paints over fixed navigation.

### [RESP-03] P1/dead-control/S Consent overlay (z-80) completely covers the mobile bottom tab bar (z-40) — the app's primary mobile navigation is unreachable on first visit
  - where: apps/web/components/MobileBottomTabs.tsx:53 (`md:hidden fixed bottom-0 left-0 right-0 z-40`) vs apps/web/components/CookieBanner.tsx:95 (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-[80]`) — every page at 390x844 except /auth/* and /checkout/*
  - steps: 1. Fresh profile, viewport 390x844.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/.
3. Try to tap Home / Book / Library / Profile in the bottom tab bar.
  - observed: Bottom nav rect top=787 bottom=844, z-index 40. Banner rect top=497 bottom=844, z-index 80 — the banner's box fully contains the tab bar's box. Hit test at the tab bar's centre: `{"top":787,"bottom":844,"z":"40","hitAtCenter":"div#.mx-auto.max-w-3xl","blocked":true}`. Scrolled to the very bottom of the document on three separate pages, individual tabs still report as covered: on / , /pujas and /store the sweep returned `{"el":"a","text":"Book","coveredBy":"button.inline-flex.h-11"}`, `{"el":"a","text":"Library","coveredBy":"div.mx-auto.max-w-3xl"}`, `{"el":"a","text":"Profile","coveredBy":"div.mx-auto.max-w-3xl"}`. The 390x844 screenshot shows the tab labels peeking out below the bottom edge of the consent card.
  - expected: Persistent navigation is never obscured; a consent bar should sit above the tab bar, not on top of it.
  - customer_impact: On a phone, the tab bar IS the app's navigation. On the first pageview all four destinations — including the saffron "Book" button that is the revenue action — swallow taps silently. The user has no way to know why nothing happens.
  - fix: Give the tab bar a higher stacking order than the consent card (e.g. tabs `z-[90]`, banner `z-[80]`) AND bottom-offset the banner by the tab-bar height on <md (`bottom-14 md:bottom-0`), so both are usable simultaneously.

### [RESP-04] P1/design/S Homepage "Next festival" strip collides at 390px — the CTA pill prints on top of the festival name
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/ at 390x844 — the "Next festival" card; the anchor wrapping "In 3 days / Next festival / Hartalika Teej हरतालिका तीज" inside `div.flex.items-center.justify-between`
  - steps: 1. Viewport 390x844, open http://127.0.0.1:3000/.
2. Dismiss the cookie banner.
3. Scroll to the "Next festival" strip (just below the Ask a Pandit card).
4. Try to read the festival name.
  - observed: The anchor computes to `{"w":21.9,"h":76.8,"display":"flex","minWidth":"0px","flex":"1 1 0%"}` with box left=37 right=59, while its first child div renders left=37 right=101 (64px wide) and overflows the collapsed box; the parent flex row spans left=37 right=353. In the 390x844 screenshot the brown "Book parvati puja →" pill is painted across the eyebrow label so only "NEXT" and a clipped "FESTIVAL" show, and the festival title "Hartalika Teej हरतालिका तीज" is entirely hidden behind the pill. Same anchor also flagged as a tap target at 21.9x76.8. At 1440x900 the strip lays out correctly, so this is purely a narrow-width failure.
  - expected: At 390px the festival name is readable and the CTA sits on its own row beneath it.
  - customer_impact: The one merchandising slot on the homepage that drives a seasonal booking is illegible on a phone — the visitor sees a smear of overlapping text and a button for a puja whose occasion they cannot read.
  - fix: On the text block use `min-w-0 flex-1` and on the container switch to `flex-col items-start gap-3 sm:flex-row sm:items-center sm:justify-between` so the CTA group wraps below at <640px. Add `truncate`/`overflow-hidden` on the title as a belt-and-braces guard.

### [RESP-05] P2/design/M Text below the 12px legibility floor is systemic — 190 elements under 12px on the mobile homepage, smallest 9px
  - where: apps/web/app/globals.css:121 (`--text-xs: clamp(0.6875rem, 0.66rem + 0.15vw, 0.75rem)`) plus hardcoded arbitrary sizes throughout apps/web; worst single instance apps/web/app/page.tsx:446 and :453
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/ at 390x844.
2. Scroll to the priest preview card in the hero area.
3. Read "Vedic • 15yr exp" / the "preview" label.
  - observed: Census of every visible element owning a text node — @390x844: `{"under12":190,"under10":2,"min":9,"sample":[{"fs":9,"t":"Vedic • 15yr exp"},{"fs":9,"t":"preview"}]}`; @768x1024: `{"under12":191,"under10":2,"min":9}`; @1440x900: `{"under12":78,"under10":2,"min":9}`. Root font-size measured 16px at all three viewports, so this is not user-agent scaling — `--text-xs` itself computes to 11.14px at 390px and 11.71px at 768px and only reaches its 12px cap at ~960px viewport width, so every one of the 2,743 `text-xs` usages is sub-12px on phones and tablets. On top of that: 102 occurrences of `text-[9px]`, 362 of `text-[10px]`, 208 of `text-[11px]` in apps/web/**/*.tsx. Concrete instances measured: bottom tab labels "Home"/"Book" at 10px, "LIVE" badge 10px, "Aadhaar authenticated" 10px, "Manager-signed, on-site audit" 11px, /store "Bestseller"/"-32%"/rating "4.7"/"(342)" all 11.14px, /auth/login "Forgot password?" and the Terms/Privacy consent line 11.14px, /admin table headers Name/Role/Actions 11.14px.
  - expected: No body or label text under 12px on any viewport; 14px+ for anything a user must read to make a decision.
  - customer_impact: Prices, ratings, review counts, delivery promises, verification badges and legal consent text are all rendered at 10-11px on a phone. This is the trust copy — "Aadhaar authenticated", "Manager-signed, on-site audit" — and it is the least readable text on the page. Older devotees, a core segment for this product, will not read it at all.
  - fix: Raise the clamp floor: `--text-xs: clamp(0.75rem, 0.72rem + 0.15vw, 0.8125rem)` in globals.css:121. Then codemod `text-[9px]`/`text-[10px]` -> `text-xs` and `text-[11px]` -> `text-xs`; keep 10px only for non-essential decorative badges if at all.

### [RESP-06] P2/ux/S Global header controls are 36x36 or smaller on every page at 390px
  - where: apps/web/components/GlobalSearch.tsx:145 (`h-9 w-9`), apps/web/components/NavCartButton.tsx:13 (`p-2` + 20px icon), the mobile menu toggle, and the logo link — present on all 9 audited pages
  - steps: 1. Viewport 390x844, open any page (e.g. http://127.0.0.1:3000/).
2. Measure the three icon buttons at the top right and the logo at the top left.
  - observed: Measured identically on /, /pujas, puja detail, booking, /temples, /store, /auth/login, /priest, /admin, /admin/users at 390px: `{"sel":"button.xl:hidden...","w":36,"h":36,"text":"Search"}`, `{"sel":"button.relative.p-2...","w":36,"h":36,"text":"Cart (0 items)"}`, `{"sel":"button.md:hidden.p-2...","w":36,"h":36,"text":"Open menu"}`, `{"sel":"a.flex.items-center.gap-2","w":28,"h":28,"text":"Darshya"}`. Three 36px targets sit adjacent in the same 65px-tall header row.
  - expected: 44x44 CSS px minimum per WCAG 2.5.5 / Apple HIG; adjacent targets need spacing so a thumb cannot hit two.
  - customer_impact: Search, cart and menu are the three most-used controls in the app and all three are 18% under the minimum, side by side. Mis-taps opening the cart when the user wanted the menu are the predictable outcome.
  - fix: Change `h-9 w-9` -> `h-11 w-11` on GlobalSearch.tsx:145 and `p-2` -> `p-3` on NavCartButton.tsx:13 and the menu toggle (keeps the 20px icon, grows the hit box to 44). Bump the logo link to `min-h-11 min-w-11` with `-m-2` negative margin so visual alignment is unchanged.

### [RESP-07] P2/ux/S /auth/login form controls under 44px at 390 — password show/hide toggle is 16x16
  - where: apps/web/app/auth/login/page.tsx:550-554 (unpadded absolutely-positioned eye button), :467 and :481 (Email/Mobile tabs), and the "Forgot password?" link
  - steps: 1. Viewport 390x844, open http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login.
2. Dismiss the cookie banner (see RESP-01).
3. Try to tap the eye icon inside the password field.
  - observed: `{"sel":"button.text-surface-400.hover:text-surface-600.absolute","w":16,"h":16,"text":""}` — 16x16px, 13% of the required 44x44 area, and it carries no accessible name. Also `{"sel":"button.flex.flex-1.items-center","w":150,"h":34,"text":"Email"}` (the Email/Mobile method tabs) and `{"sel":"a.text-saffron-700...text-xs","w":89.2,"h":14.8,"text":"Forgot password?"}` — a 14.8px-tall link. 24 sub-44px targets on this page at 390px.
  - expected: 44x44 minimum for every control in an auth form; the reveal toggle in particular is used on phones where typing errors are common.
  - customer_impact: Users who cannot see what they typed on a phone keyboard reach for the eye icon and repeatedly miss it, then mistype the password and get bounced. The 14.8px "Forgot password?" link is the recovery path and is almost impossible to hit accurately.
  - fix: Give the toggle padding and a label: `className="... absolute right-1 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2 p-3"` plus `aria-label={showPassword ? 'Hide password' : 'Show password'}`. Change the method tabs from `py-2` to `py-3` (44px) and give "Forgot password?" `py-2 -my-2 inline-block`.

### [RESP-08] P2/ux/S Catalogue filter controls sit at 32-41px tall on /temples and /store at 390px
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples (deity chips, State select, Sort select, Live Darshan toggle) and http://127.0.0.1:3000/store (category chip rail, Free Delivery filter) at 390x844
  - steps: 1. Viewport 390x844, open /temples, dismiss consent.
2. Measure the deity filter chips and the two dropdowns.
3. Repeat on /store for the horizontal category rail.
  - observed: /temples @390 (17 sub-44 targets): `"All Deities" 81.9x32`, `"Lord Shiva" 86.2x32`, `"Lord Vishnu" 92.9x32`, `"Devi / Shakti" 96.6x32`, `"Lord Ganesha" 105.7x32`, `"Sikh" 50.5x32`, State `select 177x37`, Sort `select 141x37`, `"Live Darshan" 124.1x36`. /store @390 (39 sub-44 targets): category chips `"All Products24" 131.5x40.8`, `"Puja Essentials5" 146.2x40.8`, `"Rudraksha & Malas3" 170x40.8`, `"Home Temple & Decor2" 186.5x40.8`, and `"Free Delivery" 132.8x40`. The store rail is a legitimate horizontal scroller (`div.flex.gap-2.overflow-x-auto`, scrollWidth 1459 vs clientWidth 358) — the issue is only the 40.8px height.
  - expected: 44px minimum height on filter chips and native selects, which are the highest-frequency interactions on a browse page.
  - customer_impact: Filtering is how anyone finds a temple or a product. 32px chips packed in a scrolling row on a phone mean the user zooms, mis-taps an adjacent deity, and loses their place in the rail.
  - fix: Bump the chip padding from `py-1.5` to `py-2.5` (or add `min-h-11`) and the selects from `py-2` to `py-2.5` with `min-h-11`. Nothing else about the rail needs to change.

### [RESP-09] P2/ux/S Standalone section CTAs and footer links render as 14.8-18px tall tap targets on mobile
  - where: apps/web/app/page.tsx:347 ("All streams") and :488 ("Talk to Astrologer"), plus the shared footer and puja-detail action row — measured on /, /pujas, puja detail, /auth/login, /admin at 390x844
  - steps: 1. Viewport 390x844, open http://127.0.0.1:3000/, dismiss consent.
2. Try to tap "All streams" at the right of the Live Darshan section heading.
3. Scroll to the footer and try to tap a language link.
  - observed: These are `inline-flex text-xs` links with zero vertical padding, so their box height equals the 11.14px line box: `"All streams" 71.1x14.8`, `"Browse Pujas" 86.1x14.8`, `"Talk to Astrologer" 103.5x14.8`, `/admin "View All" 56.8x14.8`, footer `summary "Discover Hindu Wisdom" 342x14.8`. Footer language switcher: `"English" 41.3x18`, `"हिन्दी" 25.6x18`, `"தமிழ்" 37.4x18`, `"తెలుగు" 40.2x18`, `"বাংলা" 28.9x18` — five links, all 18px tall, sitting on one row. Puja detail breadcrumb/actions: `"Home" 33.6x18`, `"Pujas" 31.5x18`, `"Save" 48.7x18`, `"Share" 53.6x18`. Social icons `32x32`. Primary buttons are also short: hero `"Explore Temples" 154.8x38` and `"Book a Puja" 107.8x38`, `"Book parvati puja" 157.6x34`, `"Learn more" 112.5x34`, /pujas `"Book Now" 89.3x34`, puja detail `"Book Now" 107.3x34`, wishlist heart `32x32`.

  - customer_impact: A 14.8px-tall link is roughly a third of a fingertip. "Save", "Share" and "Book Now" on the puja detail page are the actions the whole page exists for, and the multilingual footer — important for a Hindi/Tamil/Telugu/Bengali audience — is five 18px targets crammed onto one line.
  - fix: Add a shared utility (e.g. `.tap-safe { padding-block: .625rem; margin-block: -.625rem; display:inline-flex; }`) and apply it to these `inline-flex text-xs` CTA links, the footer link rows and the breadcrumb/action row. Bump `py-2` -> `py-3` on the hero and Book Now buttons.

Total permanent chrome 185px = 21.9% of the viewport. /admin @390: 0->65 plus `header.h-14` sticky 64->120 plus tab bar 787->844 = 177px. Add the consent card (497->844, RESP-01) and only 320px of the 844px viewport is left for portal content on a first visit. The consumer chrome also leaks into the portals: the header still renders `Cart (0 items)` and the bottom bar still renders the devotee "Book" tab while an admin is signed in. Same double header persists at 768x1024 (0->65 plus 64->128/120).
