## AREA: security   score=79

### [SEC-01] P2/security/M Web security headers (CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options/nosniff) are defined only in public/_headers — a Cloudflare Pages convention the current Render Next-standalone host does not honor; next.config.ts headers() sets Cache-Control only, so no CSP/anti-clickjacking header is applied
  - where: apps/web/next.config.ts headers() (lines ~113-190, sets only Cache-Control) vs apps/web/public/_headers (holds the real CSP/HSTS/X-Frame-Options/nosniff/Referrer-Policy/Permissions-Policy). Observed on http://127.0.0.1:3000/ , /auth/login
  - steps: 1. curl -s -D - -o /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:3000/auth/login | grep -iE 'content-security|x-frame|strict-transport|x-content-type|referrer-policy'  -> returns NOTHING. 2. curl -s -D - -o /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:3000/robots.txt | grep -i cache-control -> returns 'public, s-maxage=3600, stale-while-revalidate=86400', the exact value from next.config.ts, proving headers() IS active but simply defines no security headers. 3. Read apps/web/public/_headers: it contains a full CSP + HSTS + X-Frame-Options: DENY, but that file is only interpreted by Cloudflare Pages/Workers Static Assets, not by Next standalone on Render (the current prod host per CLAUDE.md) nor by next dev.
  - observed: On every web page including /auth/login and /, the only headers are 'Cache-Control' and 'X-Powered-By: Next.js'. No Content-Security-Policy, Strict-Transport-Security, X-Frame-Options, X-Content-Type-Options, Referrer-Policy, or Permissions-Policy. robots.txt Cache-Control matches next.config.ts exactly, confirming headers() runs and simply omits security headers.
  - expected: Every HTML response (especially auth pages) should carry Content-Security-Policy, X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Strict-Transport-Security, and Referrer-Policy — regardless of host — via a mechanism the running server actually applies.
  - customer_impact: With auth tokens stored in localStorage (see SEC-02), the absent CSP means any reflected/DOM/stored XSS can run inline script and exfiltrate the session token for full account takeover; the absent X-Frame-Options/frame-ancestors permits clickjacking of authenticated actions. This is the single biggest security exposure for the product and its users. Production darshya.online could not be checked here (out of scope) — an owner curl is needed to confirm whether a Cloudflare Transform Rule is injecting these; the code strongly indicates it is not.
  - fix: Move the security headers out of public/_headers and into a mechanism the Render deployment honors: add them to next.config.ts async headers() as a source:'/(.*)' block (CSP, HSTS, X-Frame-Options: DENY, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy, Permissions-Policy), OR add a Cloudflare Transform Rule / Worker that injects them at the edge. Then verify prod: curl -sI https://darshya.online/auth/login | grep -i content-security-policy.

### [SEC-02] P3/security/L Auth access token persisted in localStorage (poojly_session/poojly_user) — any XSS yields full account + API takeover as that user
  - where: Web client session storage: localStorage keys 'poojly_session' (contains session.accessToken) and 'poojly_user'; the API accepts that token as a Bearer header on every /api/v1/* route (apps/api/src/middlewares/auth.ts extractBearerToken)
  - steps: 1. Log in (the documented recipe writes localStorage.setItem('poojly_session', JSON.stringify(session)) where session.accessToken is a working Bearer). 2. In devtools console on the site: localStorage.getItem('poojly_session') exposes the raw access token in JS-reachable storage. 3. That token authenticates every API call (verified: Authorization: Bearer <token> to /api/v1/users/me returns the account).
  - observed: Access tokens live in JS-readable localStorage. An httpOnly cookie path (darshya_session) exists and the API accepts it, but the localStorage copy remains the primary Bearer source, so it is not JS-inaccessible.
  - expected: Session tokens should live only in an httpOnly, Secure, SameSite cookie so JavaScript (and thus any XSS) cannot read them; the Bearer-in-localStorage path should be retired on web now that the cookie path is wired.
  - customer_impact: Turns any single XSS anywhere on the site into complete session theft and account/API takeover (bookings, payouts for providers, PII). Combined with SEC-01 (no CSP to contain XSS) this is a compounding risk. On its own it is a defense-in-depth gap because React auto-escaping and the escaping markdown pipeline currently limit the XSS surface.
  - fix: Cut web fully over to the existing httpOnly darshya_session cookie for auth and stop writing the token to localStorage (remove the poojly_session accessToken persistence); keep localStorage only for non-sensitive UI state. Mobile can keep the Bearer/JSON path via secure-store.

### [SEC-03] P3/security/S X-Powered-By: Next.js exposed on every web response (poweredByHeader not disabled)
  - where: apps/web/next.config.ts (poweredByHeader is not set; defaults to true). Observed on all http://127.0.0.1:3000 responses
  - steps: 1. curl -s -D - -o /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:3000/ | grep -i x-powered-by -> 'X-Powered-By: Next.js'. 2. grep -n poweredByHeader apps/web/next.config.ts -> not present.
  - observed: Every web response includes 'X-Powered-By: Next.js', fingerprinting the framework to attackers.
  - expected: No framework/version disclosure header; poweredByHeader: false in next.config.ts.
  - customer_impact: Low direct impact — aids reconnaissance/targeted-CVE selection. Trivial one-line hardening.
  - fix: Add poweredByHeader: false to nextConfig in apps/web/next.config.ts.

### [SEC-04] P3/security/S Two API read routes echo raw error.message in 500 responses, bypassing the prod-safe global error sanitizer
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:723 (GET /bookings/my) and :783 (GET /bookings/availability) — 'return reply.status(500).send({ error: error.message || ... })'
  - steps: 1. Read apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:722-724 and 782-784: both catch blocks send error.message straight to the client. 2. The global server.setErrorHandler in server.ts intentionally suppresses err.message in production (safeToEcho=false for 5xx when isProd), but these handlers catch first and never reach it, so error.message is returned verbatim regardless of NODE_ENV.
  - observed: Two handlers return raw error.message on 500. In production a triggered exception here (e.g. a Prisma error) would echo internal error text (table/column/constraint names, query fragments) to the client, which the global handler is specifically designed to prevent.
  - expected: All 5xx responses in production should return a generic message; internal error detail goes to logs/Sentry only. These handlers should send a static string and let the error be logged.
  - customer_impact: Low — requires forcing an exception in these two read paths, and leaks schema/query detail rather than data. Still an information-disclosure inconsistency with the rest of the codebase's hardened error handling.
  - fix: In both catch blocks send a fixed message (e.g. { error: 'Failed to fetch bookings' } / 'Failed to check availability') and log the real error, matching the pattern used by the global setErrorHandler.

### [LEG-01] P0/legal/M Published refund ladder is 100/50/0; the code refunds 100/75/50/25 — a 24-48h cancellation is short-paid 25%
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/refunds section 2 table; http://127.0.0.1:3000/terms section 8; apps/api/src/services/cancellation.service.ts:47-58 (DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY); apps/api/src/services/refund.service.ts:410-425
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/refunds and read the 'Cancellation Window / Refund Amount' table.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/terms section 8.
3. Open apps/api/src/services/cancellation.service.ts and read DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY.tiers.
4. Note that processAutoRefunds (refund.service.ts:410) calls computeDevoteeRefund with that policy and passes the result straight to Razorpay as a partial refund.
  - observed: /refunds table, verbatim: "More than 24 hours before scheduled puja | 100% refund · 6 to 24 hours before scheduled puja | 50% refund · Less than 6 hours before scheduled puja | No refund (preparations underway)". /terms section 8, verbatim: "Puja bookings may be cancelled up to 24 hours before the scheduled time for a full refund." The code ladder is `{48h:1.0},{24h:0.75},{4h:0.5},{0h:0.25}` and refund.service.ts:419-424 sends `refundType:'partial', amountInr: quote.refundAmountInr` to Razorpay.
  - expected: The refund percentages published on /refunds and /terms are the percentages actually refunded.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who cancels 30 hours before a ₹5,000 puja reads '100% refund' on two legal pages and receives ₹3,750. ₹1,250 is withheld against the published policy, automatically, with no human in the loop. Under the Consumer Protection Act 2019 that is deficiency of service plus an unfair trade practice, and it is provable from the site's own text — the cheapest possible complaint for a consumer to file.
  - fix: Pick ONE ladder. If the 100/75/50/25 code ladder is the intended commercial policy, rewrite the /refunds table and Terms section 8 to state it exactly, including the 48h boundary. Better: render the table on /refunds from DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY.tiers via a public endpoint so the page cannot drift from the engine again.

### [LEG-02] P0/legal/S 80G receipt issued for a donee with no 80G registration and no PAN — receipt asserts Section 80G eligibility unconditionally
  - where: POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/donations/verify; apps/api/src/lib/pdf-receipt.ts:85 and :156; apps/web/lib/company.ts:95-102

3. POST /api/v1/donations/verify with the returned donationId + razorpayOrderId.
4. Read apps/api/src/lib/pdf-receipt.ts lines 85, 137-144 and 156.
  - observed: create-order = 200, verify = 200 :: {"ok":true,"donationId":"don_1786551965012_eb0d48b4","receiptNumber":"DR-20260812-7335","amount":501,"status":"paid"}. The generated PDF prints the header '80G Income Tax Exemption' (pdf-receipt.ts:85) and the line 'Eligible for deduction under Section 80G of the Income Tax Act, 1961.' (pdf-receipt.ts:156) with NO conditional. The '80G Registration:' and donee 'PAN:' lines are inside `if (data.gaushala80GNumber)` / `if (data.gaushalaPan)` and are simply omitted, so the document claims the exemption while silently dropping the two fields that would substantiate it. apps/web/lib/company.ts has HAS_80G_VERIFIED:false, pan:null, eightyGNumber:null.
  - expected: PROJECT_CONTEXT.md business rule 7: '80G receipt requires donee 80G regn + PAN.' A donee with neither should get a plain donation acknowledgement with no 80G wording.
  - customer_impact: Every donor gets a PDF telling them to claim a deduction that the Income Tax Department will disallow, because the donee has no 80G(5) registration on file and the receipt carries no registration number or donee PAN. Donors face disallowance and penalty on assessment; the platform faces a false-document problem it created at scale, automatically, from ₹101 upward. This is the single worst legal exposure I found.
  - fix: Gate the 80G wording on data. In generate80GReceipt, only emit the '80G Income Tax Exemption' header and the Section 80G eligibility line when BOTH gaushala80GNumber and gaushalaPan are present; otherwise title it 'DONATION RECEIPT' with 'This is an acknowledgement of your contribution. It is not an 80G tax receipt.' Then refuse to advertise 80G on /donations and /gaushala for donees that lack registration.

3. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/why-darshya and read the 'Cancellation up to 24h before' row.
4. Compare with cancellation.service.ts tiers `{minHoursBefore:4, refundFraction:0.5}` and `{minHoursBefore:0, refundFraction:0.25}`.
  - observed: /faqs verbatim: "Yes. You can cancel or reschedule up to 24 hours before the scheduled puja time for a full refund. Within 24 hours, cancellations are not permitted because materials (samagri) have already been procured and the pandit slot is locked." /nri-pujas verbatim: "Within 24 hours, the booking is non-refundable since the pandit has already prepared materials." /why-darshya verbatim: "After 24h: samagri procured + pandit slot locked, so no refund." The code refunds 50% for 4-24h out and 25% for under 4h, and the booking page exposes a live Cancel control for those bookings.
  - expected: If the engine pays 50%/25% inside 24h, the site must say so. Telling a customer they have no remedy when they do is the reverse of the usual mis-selling and is still actionable.

They forfeit the ₹4,000 the platform's own engine would have paid them. The money stays with the platform purely because the published copy was wrong — the worst possible optics if a single customer ever discovers it.
  - fix: Replace the 'not permitted / non-refundable' wording on /faqs, /nri-pujas, /why-darshya and Terms section 8 with the real inside-24h tiers, and surface the tier the devotee qualifies for on the booking page before they decide.

Searching the whole API for a plan-based commission lookup returns nothing: the real rates are a flat ASTROLOGER_PLATFORM_RATE=0.2, storeProfile.commission default 0.20, and 1 - Priest.commissionRate (schema default 0.80) = 20%. apps/web/lib/pricing.ts COMMISSION_RATES is referenced only by pricing/page.tsx:415 — nothing charges from it.

Not one of the five things /faqs promises appears in the plan that is sold.

  - customer_impact: '4 free pujas per year' is worth ₹2,000-₹14,000 at the site's own puja prices — more than the ₹5,000 subscription. Someone buys the membership on the strength of the FAQ, receives none of the five promised entitlements, and has a clean claim for a full refund plus the value of the promise.
  - fix: Delete the invented benefits from the /faqs answer and have both /faqs and /nri render nri_premium_yearly.features from the API.

### [LEG-09] P1/legal/S /nri-pujas advertises 7-currency checkout with 'no markup'; the product is INR-only via Razorpay and Terms says so
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/nri-pujas (benefits grid); http://127.0.0.1:3000/nri (hero CTA); http://127.0.0.1:3000/terms section 7
  - steps: 1. Open /nri-pujas and read the 'Dual currency' benefit tile.
2. Open /nri and read the hero CTA button.
3. Open /terms section 7, first bullet.
4. Compare /uk and /nri/ca, which handle this correctly.
  - observed: /nri-pujas verbatim: "Dual currency, no markup — USD/GBP/CAD/AUD/EUR/SGD/AED — full price shown upfront." /nri hero button verbatim: "Go Premium — $60 USD / year". /terms section 7 verbatim: "All payments, domestic and international, are processed through Razorpay and billed in Indian Rupees (INR)." The USD rail was removed with Stripe (apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:520, checkout/offering/page.tsx:126 both note the INR/USD toggle was removed 2026-08-10). By contrast /uk states "you are charged in INR" and /nri/ca states "A native CAD rail is on the roadmap... billed in INR" — those two pages are honest.
  - expected: Currency claims match the one rail that exists, as /uk and /nri/ca already do.
  - customer_impact: An NRI clicks a button that says '$60 USD / year' and their card is charged ₹5,000 plus their bank's FX spread and foreign-transaction fee — more than $60, in a currency they were not quoted. 'No markup' across seven currencies is a specific pricing representation with nothing behind it.
  - fix: Replace the /nri-pujas 'Dual currency, no markup' tile with the /uk wording ('reference pricing only; you are charged in INR'), and relabel the /nri CTA to '₹5,000/year (about $60)'.

### [LEG-10] P1/legal/M No legal entity, GSTIN or registered address anywhere — yet the platform issues a PDF titled 'DARSHYA TAX INVOICE' and adds 5% GST at checkout
  - where: apps/web/lib/company.ts:38-50 (IS_INCORPORATED_VERIFIED:false, legalEntity:null); apps/api/src/lib/pdf-invoice.ts:195,259; apps/web/components/CartContext.tsx:135; http://127.0.0.1:3000/terms section 6
  - steps: 1. Open /terms, /privacy, /refunds, /contact, /shipping and search each for a company name, CIN, GSTIN or postal address.
2. Read apps/web/lib/company.ts — postalContactLine() returns null whenever IS_INCORPORATED_VERIFIED is false, which it is.
3. Read apps/api/src/lib/pdf-invoice.ts:195 and :259.
4. Read apps/web/components/CartContext.tsx:135 and apps/web/app/store/checkout/page.tsx:542.
  - observed: No page renders a legal entity name, CIN, GSTIN or registered office — postalContactLine() returns null and every page's postal block is conditionally omitted. The only identity published is 'Bengaluru, India' in the footer. Meanwhile pdf-invoice.ts:195 draws the header 'DARSHYA TAX INVOICE' and :259 prints 'Amount inclusive of all applicable taxes where chargeable', with no GSTIN anywhere on the document; CartContext.tsx:135 computes `const tax = Math.round(subtotal * 0.05)` which the checkout renders as a 'GST (est.)' line ADDED to the subtotal — while /terms section 6 states "Prices include applicable taxes unless stated otherwise" and no store product card carries any tax disclosure.
  - expected: Either collect no GST and issue no 'TAX INVOICE', or publish the entity name, GSTIN and registered address and issue a compliant invoice; and make the checkout total consistent with the tax-inclusive claim in Terms.
  - customer_impact: A document headed 'TAX INVOICE' with no GSTIN is not a valid tax invoice, and collecting a line item labelled GST without a registration is the kind of thing that draws a notice on its own. For the customer it is drip pricing: a ₹1,299 thali advertised as tax-inclusive becomes ₹1,364 at checkout with an 'estimated' tax appearing only at the last step — a pattern the CCPA dark-pattern guidelines name explicitly. The Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules also require the seller's legal name and registered address to be displayed, and none is.
  - fix: Until incorporation and GST registration land: retitle the PDF 'RECEIPT' (not TAX INVOICE), remove the 5% line from CartContext or fold it into displayed prices so Terms section 6 stays true, and add a single 'Operated by <name>, <address>' block driven from company.ts to /terms, /privacy, /refunds and /contact.

### [LEG-11] P1/legal/S Grievance officer is unnamed and the site publishes two conflicting grievance SLAs
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/privacy section 9; http://127.0.0.1:3000/refunds section 10; apps/web/lib/company.ts:78-84
  - steps: 1. Open /privacy and read the Grievance Officer paragraph at the end of section 9.
2. Open /refunds section 10 'Disputes'.
3. Read the comment above specialtyEmails in apps/web/lib/company.ts.
  - observed: /privacy verbatim: "Grievance Officer: For DPDP-related complaints, email grievance@darshya.online. We aim to acknowledge within 48 hours and resolve within 30 days as required by the Act." /refunds verbatim: "...escalate the matter by emailing grievance@darshya.online. Our Grievance Officer will review and respond within 15 business days." No page anywhere states the officer's NAME or DESIGNATION. grievance@darshya.online is hardcoded into both pages and is not even one of the addresses declared in company.ts, whose own comment says of the specialty inboxes: "support@ is already in supportEmail; the others are aspirational."
  - expected: One named grievance officer with designation, email and phone, one SLA, published identically on both pages.
  - customer_impact: IT Rules 2021 Rule 3(2) and Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 Rule 4(5) both require the name, designation and contact details of a grievance officer to be published. Publishing only an unattributed alias, with two different response promises depending on which page the consumer lands on, is a straightforward compliance finding — and the aggrieved customer cannot tell whether to expect an answer in 15 days or 30.
  - fix: Add a `grievanceOfficer: { name, designation, email, phone }` block to company.ts, render it identically on /privacy, /refunds and /contact, and settle on one SLA (24h acknowledgement / 15 days resolution satisfies both rule sets).

### [LEG-12] P1/wrong-data/S /shipping publishes a ₹999 free-delivery threshold and weight-based fees; the cart uses ₹499 and a flat ₹49
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/shipping section 3; http://127.0.0.1:3000/store (badge); apps/web/components/CartContext.tsx:134
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/shipping and read section 3 'Shipping Charges', first bullet.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store and read the badge row under the hero.
3. Read apps/web/components/CartContext.tsx line 134.
  - observed: /shipping verbatim: "Free shipping on all domestic orders above ₹999. Below ₹999, a flat shipping fee of ₹49–₹99 applies based on weight and destination." /store badge verbatim: "Free Delivery above Rs 499". CartContext.tsx:134: `const deliveryFee = subtotal >= 499 ? 0 : 49;` — a single flat ₹49 with no weight or destination input at all.
  - expected: One threshold and one fee rule across the shipping policy, the store badge and the cart.
  - customer_impact: A ₹600 order: the legal shipping policy says a fee applies, the store badge and the cart say free. A ₹300 order to a remote pincode: the policy says up to ₹99 by weight and destination, the cart always charges ₹49. Whichever way it resolves, one of the two published numbers is wrong and the shipping policy is the one a consumer forum would read.
  - fix: Update /shipping section 3 to state the real rule — free above ₹499, otherwise a flat ₹49, no weight or destination variation — or change CartContext to implement the published rule.

### [LEG-13] P2/wrong-data/M /about publishes '200+ Temples partnered' and '100% Bookings with video proof' against 20 temples and completed bookings with no video
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/about (stat strip); GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/temples?limit=500; GET /api/v1/bookings/my
  - steps: 1. curl 'http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/temples?limit=500' and read the total.
2. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/about and read the four-stat strip under the hero.
3. Log in as devotee@demo.com and GET /api/v1/bookings/my; inspect videoProofUrl on rows in status 'completed'.
  - observed: API total = 20 temples. /about verbatim: "200+ Temples partnered · 8 Role portals live · 9–10× NRI multiplier · documented · 100% Bookings with video proof", and further down "Video proof, every time — Every puja booking gets a video recording from the priest before payout. No exceptions". GET /bookings/my returns BS-20260812-9HWMA, BS-20260812-JMHM4 and BS-20260812-A6UON all in status 'completed' with videoProofUrl:null, plus BS-20260812-QX6DC in 'video_uploaded' whose videoProofUrl is the two-character string '  ' and BS-20260812-683BK whose proof URL is the literal placeholder 'https://drive.google.com/CORRECT-LINK'. /nri-pujas separately claims "50+ verified pujas across 30+ temple cities" against 10 seeded puja types and 20 temples.
  - expected: Headline trust statistics that are true, or are labelled as targets.
  - customer_impact: The whole pitch is 'we tell the truth about whether the puja happened'. A prospective devotee or temple partner who can count 20 temples and see completed bookings with no video has caught the trust page itself overstating by 10x. It is also the easiest claim for a competitor or journalist to check.
  - fix: Drive the stat strip from live counts (a cached API call), and either enforce video proof on the transition into 'completed' the way it is already enforced for 'video_uploaded'/'delivered' (routes/bookings.ts checkVideoProofGate) or soften the 100% claim.

### [LEG-14] P2/legal/S /csr-match publicly claims 'Bhaktisetu is 80G certified + CSR-1 registered' while the platform's own config says neither is verified
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/csr-match; apps/web/app/csr-match/page.tsx:203; apps/web/lib/company.ts:95-102
  - steps: 1. curl -s http://127.0.0.1:3000/csr-match — returns 200.
2. Search the page body for '80G certified'.
3. Read apps/web/lib/company.ts donationLegal.
  - observed: Page returns 200 and renders, verbatim: "Bhaktisetu is 80G certified + CSR-1 registered. Religious heritage promotion qualifies under CSR Schedule VII Item III." company.ts has HAS_80G_VERIFIED:false, pan:null, eightyGNumber:null and IS_INCORPORATED_VERIFIED:false. The file's own header comment reads "Dev-server only. NOT deployed until founder approves 80G + CSR-1 compliance attestation" — but nothing in the route enforces that, and the page is reachable and indexable on any environment where it is built.
  - expected: A page making a statutory certification claim should not build at all until the certificate exists, or should be gated behind an env flag.
  - customer_impact: This is aimed at corporates deciding where to route CSR spend. A false CSR-1 / 80G certification claim to a corporate donor is materially worse than the consumer-side 80G issue: the corporate's own CSR compliance depends on it, and the claim is made in writing on a public URL. The 'dev-server only' comment is not a control.
  - fix: Gate the route behind `process.env.ENABLE_CSR_MATCH === 'true'` (defaulting off) or delete it until the 80G(5) certificate and CSR-1 filing are in hand, and drive the claim text from company.ts HAS_80G_VERIFIED so it cannot be rendered while false.

### [LEG-15] P2/legal/S No way to withdraw or change cookie consent; the policy tells users to hand-edit browser storage
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/legal/cookies ('Your choices'); cookie banner on any page
  - steps: 1. Clear cookies and open http://127.0.0.1:3000/ in a fresh context.
2. Click 'Reject all' on the banner.
3. Search the page (and the footer) for any 'Cookie settings' / 'Manage preferences' control.
4. Open /legal/cookies and enumerate its buttons.
  - observed: After clicking 'Reject all', a scan of every <a> and <button> for /cookie|consent|preferen|privacy setting/i returned []. /legal/cookies has no consent control either — its only buttons are the site nav. The policy's remedy, verbatim: "You can change your choices at any time by clearing the darshya_cookie_consent_v1 entry in your browser storage, or contact privacy@darshya.online for help." localStorage after rejection contains exactly that key. On the positive side, no analytics/marketing requests fired before consent, and the Reject/Accept buttons are equal weight as the page claims.
  - expected: A persistent 'Cookie settings' link (footer is standard) that reopens the preference panel. Withdrawal of consent must be as easy as giving it.
  - customer_impact: Under UK GDPR (this site publishes a UK privacy notice) and the DPDP Act, consent withdrawal must be as easy as consent. Instructing a consumer to locate and delete a localStorage key by hand is not that, and it is stated in the cookie policy itself — self-documenting non-compliance.
  - fix: Add a 'Cookie settings' link to the footer and to /legal/cookies that re-opens the existing preference panel, then replace the 'clear darshya_cookie_consent_v1' sentence with a link to it.

### [LEG-16] P2/legal/M DPDP rights are email-only: the built DSAR endpoint is unreachable from the web app and every rights inbox is 'aspirational'
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/privacy section 8; apps/api/src/routes/dpdp.ts:108,142,174,211; apps/web/lib/company.ts:78-84
  - steps: 1. Open /privacy section 8 and read how to exercise data rights.
2. grep the whole of apps/web for 'dpdp' — returns nothing.
3. Read apps/api/src/routes/dpdp.ts: POST /dsar, GET /dsar/mine, POST /consent, GET /consent/mine all exist under /api/v1/dpdp.
4. Read the comment above specialtyEmails in apps/web/lib/company.ts.
  - observed: /privacy verbatim: "To exercise any of these rights, email us at privacy@darshya.online with your registered email address" — the listed rights include "Request a copy of your data in a portable format" and "Request deletion of your account and data". A grep of apps/web for 'dpdp' returns zero hits, so the working POST /api/v1/dpdp/dsar endpoint is wired to no UI. /account, /account/privacy, /account/data and /settings/privacy all return 404. company.ts describes privacy@ (and legal@, press@, partners@, sales@) as "aspirational".
  - expected: A self-serve DSAR / delete-account screen that posts to the endpoint that already exists, or at minimum a rights inbox the operator confirms is monitored.
  - customer_impact: A user exercising a statutory right has exactly one channel, and the codebase's own single source of truth describes that channel as aspirational. Meanwhile the backend to do it properly is already written and shipped, just unreachable — so the compliance gap is pure wiring.
  - fix: Add an /account/privacy screen that calls POST /api/v1/dpdp/dsar and GET /dsar/mine, link it from /privacy section 8, and confirm privacy@ and grievance@ actually deliver before publishing them.

### [LEG-17] P2/legal/S Store runs a permanently-live 'Akshaya Tritiya Sale — offer valid till April 30' with struck-through MRPs, four months after that date
  - where: http://127.0.0.1:3000/store (promo banner and product cards)
  - steps: 1. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/store on 2026-08-12.
2. Read the promo banner above the product grid.
3. Read any product card's price pair and discount badge.
  - observed: Banner verbatim: "Akshaya Tritiya Sale — Up to 50% Off! Shop puja essentials, gold-plated idols, and rudraksha malas at unbeatable prices. Offer valid till April 30." Today is 2026-08-12. Product cards show permanent struck-through pairs with fixed discount badges: ₹1,299/₹1,899 (-32%), ₹349/₹549 (-36%), ₹599/₹999 (-39%), ₹249/₹399, ₹199/₹299. A search of the page for any tax-inclusivity disclosure returns nothing.
  - expected: Time-limited offers expire; a struck-through price must be a price the item was actually sold at.
  - customer_impact: An expired festival offer left running as permanent decoration, plus reference prices that are never the selling price, is textbook false urgency and misleading discounting under the CCPA dark-pattern guidelines. It also makes the whole store look unmaintained, which undercuts the trust the rest of the site is trying to buy.
  - fix: Give the promo an end date the component honours and hide it when past; either remove the struck-through MRPs or back them with a real prior-price history.

No control with that label exists — the real buttons are 'Cancel Booking' (page.tsx:1054) and 'Request cancellation & refund' (page.tsx:1100). Worse, the page itself did not load on two separate attempts with a fresh 200-login session: it rendered "We could not load this booking / Failed to fetch" (1,285 chars of chrome and footer, booking number absent, Cancel button count 0) for a valid booking id.

### [LEG-19] P3/wrong-data/S Refund settlement timelines differ across four pages, including 'UPI 1–3 business days' vs 'UPI refunds are usually instant'

The code's single refundDaysLabel is '5-7 business days'.

## AREA: app-web-parity   score=10

### [PAR-01] P0/broken-feature/M Mobile Upgrade grants a ₹5,000/yr ACTIVE subscription with no payment and says "Upgrade complete"
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/upgrade.tsx:56-68 -> packages/api-client/src/hooks/wave-5.ts:190 -> apps/api/src/routes/subscriptions.ts:37
  - steps: 1. curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/auth/login -d '{"email":"store@demo.com","password":"demo123"}' -H 'content-type: application/json' and keep session.accessToken.
2. POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/subscriptions/subscribe with that Bearer token and body {"planId":"nri_premium_yearly","role":"DEVOTEE"}.
3. GET /api/v1/subscriptions/is-premium with the same token.
4. Log in as admin@demo.com and GET /api/v1/admin/dashboard.
  - observed: Step 2 -> 200 {"message":"Subscription created","subscription":{"plan":"ENTERPRISE","role":"DEVOTEE","startDate":"2026-08-12T16:20:31.414Z","endDate":"2027-08-12T16:20:31.414Z","amount":5000,"status":"ACTIVE"}}. Step 3 -> {"isPremium":true}. Step 4 -> recentTransactions contains {"type":"SUBSCRIPTION","amount":5000,"status":"COMPLETED"}. No Razorpay order, no payment, no mandate. Because the response carries no shortUrl, upgrade.tsx:66 takes the else branch and shows Alert 'Upgrade complete' / 'Your Premium benefits are now active.'
  - expected: POST /subscribe should create a PENDING subscription plus a Razorpay order/mandate and return a payment URL; premium entitlement and a COMPLETED transaction should only exist after payment verification.
  - customer_impact: Anyone with a free account gets a year of Premium for ₹0 by tapping one button — on mobile and on the website (apps/web/app/upgrade/page.tsx:145 only redirects to Razorpay when planId === 'astro_monthly', otherwise it also just routes to a success page). The admin revenue ledger books ₹5,000 that was never collected, so every revenue number the founder looks at is inflated by phantom subscriptions.
  - fix: Gate subscriptionService.subscribe behind a created-and-verified payment for every paid plan. Return {subscription:{status:'PENDING', shortUrl}} and only flip to ACTIVE + write the COMPLETED transaction from the Razorpay webhook. Remove the 'no URL means the plan was granted server-side' else branch in upgrade.tsx:65-70 — treat a missing URL as a failure.

### [PAR-02] P0/wrong-data/S Mobile "My Bookings" is always empty and Profile shows 0 bookings / 0 addresses — envelope key mismatch
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/bookings.tsx:19 and apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/profile.tsx:197-198, via packages/api-client/src/hooks/index.ts:199 (useBookings) + :441 (useUserAddresses)
  - steps: 1. Log in as devotee@demo.com / demo123 and grab the accessToken.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/users/me/bookings with that Bearer token.
3. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/users/me/addresses.
4. Compare against the accessor the screens use: `bookingsQuery.data?.data` and `addressesQuery.data?.data`.
  - observed: API returns keys [success,bookings,pagination] with bookings.length=10 and pagination {"page":1,"limit":10,"total":80,"totalPages":8}, and [success,addresses] with addresses.length=2. Both screens read `.data`, which does not exist on either payload. Simulated render: bookings=0, addresses=0. api-client returns the raw body (client.ts:213 only unwraps when parseEnvelope is passed, and no caller passes it), so the PaginatedResponse/ApiResponse types in packages/shared/src/types/index.ts:436-455 describe an envelope the API never sends.
  - expected: The Bookings tab should list the devotee's 80 bookings and Profile should read '80 bookings, 2 addresses'.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who has paid for pujas opens the app and sees no bookings at all — no reference number, no date, no priest. This is the single screen a paying customer returns to after checkout, and it is blank for every user.
  - fix: Either make api-client normalise (`useBookings` -> `api.get<...>(...).then(r => r.bookings)`) or fix the accessors to `.bookings` / `.addresses`. Better: add a `schema` (the client already supports zod validation at client.ts:215) to every list hook so a shape drift throws RESPONSE_SHAPE_MISMATCH in CI instead of rendering an empty list in production.

### [PAR-03] P0/broken-feature/M Mobile Priest Dashboard crashes on render as soon as the real API response arrives
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(priest)/dashboard.tsx:52 (and :59), type declared at packages/api-client/src/hooks/providers.ts:217-224
  - steps: 1. Log in as pandit@demo.com / demo123.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/priest/dashboard with the Bearer token.
3. Evaluate the exact render expression from dashboard.tsx:52: `` `₹${data.earningsToday.toLocaleString('en-IN')}` ``.
  - observed: API returns {"priest":{...},"earnings":{"total":0,"thisMonth":0,"pending":"2310"},"bookings":{"total":51,"upcoming":35,"completed":13},"upcomingBookings":[...]}. There is no `earningsToday`, no `earningsThisMonth`, no `todayBookings`. Running the render expression against that payload: `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toLocaleString')`. The interface `PriestDashboard {todayBookings, earningsToday, earningsThisMonth, rating, ratingCount, pendingVideoUploads}` is a fabrication, so TypeScript passes.
  - expected: Dashboard renders 35 upcoming / 13 completed bookings, ₹0 this month, ₹2,310 pending, rating 4.61 (43).
  - customer_impact: Every pandit who opens the app lands on a red screen / error boundary on their home tab. Providers are the supply side — they cannot see today's jobs, earnings, or pending payouts. Until the fetch resolves they briefly see fabricated FALLBACK numbers (dashboard.tsx:13-20: 3 bookings, ₹4,500 today, ₹86,500 this month, 4.8 from 124 reviews) that bear no relation to reality.
  - fix: Rewrite the PriestDashboard interface to match the real payload ({priest,earnings:{total,thisMonth,pending},bookings:{total,upcoming,completed},upcomingBookings}), update the six StatCards, and delete the FALLBACK constant so a failed fetch shows an error state rather than invented money. Add a contract test that asserts the interface against a live response.

### [PAR-04] P0/broken-feature/S Mobile Admin Dashboard and Reports both crash on render against the real payload
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(admin)/dashboard.tsx:45 and apps/mobile/app/(admin)/reports.tsx:62, type at packages/api-client/src/hooks/providers.ts:300-306
  - steps: 1. Log in as admin@demo.com / demo123.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/admin/dashboard with the Bearer token.
3. Evaluate dashboard.tsx:45 `data.totalBookings.toLocaleString('en-IN')` against that payload.
  - observed: API returns {"totalUsers":23,"usersByRole":{...},"totalRevenue":25718,"pendingApprovals":0,"activeSubscriptions":7,"recentTransactions":[...]} — no `totalBookings`, no `activeTemples`. Render expression -> `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toLocaleString')`. Identical crash at reports.tsx:62. The `AdminDashboard` interface declares totalBookings and activeTemples, which the endpoint has never returned. Separately, the Revenue StatCard formats ₹25,718 as `₹${(25718/100000).toFixed(1)}L` = "₹0.3L".
  - expected: Admin home renders 23 users, ₹25,718 revenue, 0 pending approvals, 7 active subscriptions.
  - customer_impact: The founder's own admin app crashes on its landing screen. Before the crash it flashes FALLBACK values of 12,450 users / 3,240 bookings / ₹45.2L revenue / 128 temples (dashboard.tsx:13-19) — numbers that are 500x the truth and could easily be screenshotted into an investor deck.
  - fix: Align AdminDashboard with the real payload, drop the two non-existent StatCards (or add totalBookings/activeTemples server-side), remove the FALLBACK constant, and render ₹ amounts with toLocaleString('en-IN') rather than a lakh divisor that hides four-figure revenue.

### [PAR-05] P0/broken-feature/S Live Darshan player calls a route that does not exist — every stream is unwatchable in the app
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/darshan-player.tsx:79 -> packages/api-client/src/hooks/index.ts:396 -> packages/shared/src/constants/index.ts:313 (DARSHAN_STREAM)
  - steps: 1. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/darshan/live and take streams[0].id (60a66677-2dd0-487c-a418-6ad91da483d1).
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/darshan/60a66677-2dd0-487c-a418-6ad91da483d1 — the exact URL useDarshanStream builds.
3. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/darshan/60a66677-2dd0-487c-a418-6ad91da483d1/stream — the route that actually exists (apps/api/src/routes/darshan.ts:380).
  - observed: Step 2 -> 404 {"message":"Route GET:/api/v1/darshan/60a66677-2dd0-487c-a418-6ad91da483d1 not found","error":"Not Found","statusCode":404}. Step 3 -> 200 with playbackUrl. Since darshan.tsx:116 and temples.tsx:127 navigate with only {id,title} and no url param, `liveUrl` stays undefined and the player renders the fallback 'Stream player not available'; tapping 'Open in browser' fires Alert 'Stream unavailable — No public stream URL for this temple yet.' The hook sets refetchInterval: 15_000, so the app re-requests the 404 every 15 seconds for as long as the screen is open.
  - expected: DARSHAN_STREAM should be `/api/v1/darshan/${streamId}/stream` and the player should load the HLS playbackUrl.
  - customer_impact: Live Darshan is the headline consumer feature. In the shipped binary it is 100% dead — every temple tile leads to 'Stream player not available'. A published app cannot be hotfixed; this ships until the next store release. It also burns battery/data re-polling a 404 four times a minute.
  - fix: Change packages/shared/src/constants/index.ts:313 to append `/stream`, map the response fields the player reads (`playbackUrl` not `streamUrl`, `temple.deityPrimary` not `temple.deity`), and set `retry: false` on 404 so the poll stops.

### [PAR-06] P0/broken-feature/S Every donation from the mobile app fails with 400 — the field is named `amount`, the API requires `amountInr`
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/donations.tsx:41 -> packages/api-client/src/hooks/wave-1-4.ts:360-365 (useCreateDonationOrder, typed `input: unknown`)
  - steps: 1. Log in as any seeded user (store@demo.com / demo123 works).
2. POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/donations/create-order with the Bearer token and the exact body donations.tsx builds: {"amount":101,"currency":"INR","cause":"Gaushala","donorName":"QA"}.
  - observed: 400 {"error":"Invalid request body","details":{"formErrors":[],"fieldErrors":{"amountInr":["Required"]}}}. donations.tsx:57 catches and shows Alert('Error', 'Invalid request body'). The mutation hook types its input as `unknown`, so TypeScript never checked the field name.
  - expected: 200 with an orderId and a Razorpay payment URL, then Linking.openURL to complete the donation.
  - customer_impact: Temple and gaushala donations — a primary revenue line and the emotional core of the product — are impossible from the app. The user sees a bare 'Invalid request body' alert, which reads like the app is broken (it is) and destroys trust at the moment they were about to give money.
  - fix: Give useCreateDonationOrder a real typed input matching the zod schema in apps/api/src/routes/donations.ts (amountInr, not amount) and send `amountInr` from donations.tsx:41. Replace every `input: unknown` mutation in wave-1-4.ts with a concrete interface — this class of bug is invisible to the compiler today.

### [PAR-07] P0/wrong-data/M Mobile Temples / Pujas / Darshan tabs render hardcoded mock catalogues, and the prices contradict the website
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/temples.tsx:205, pujas.tsx:245, darshan.tsx:193, index.tsx:57 and :70
  - steps: 1. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/temples — note the top-level key.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/pujas/types — note the top-level key.
3. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/darshan/live — note the top-level key.
4. Compare with the accessor each screen uses (`.data?.data`).
5. Open http://127.0.0.1:3000/pujas/satyanarayan-katha in a browser and read the price.
  - observed: /temples -> keys [temples,total,page,limit,totalPages] (20 rows). /pujas/types -> keys [pujaTypes,total,page,limit,totalPages] (10 rows). /darshan/live -> keys [streams,total,upcoming] (1 live stream, Kashi Vishwanath). All three screens read `.data`, get undefined, and fall through to the hardcoded arrays: templesData (5 rows), pujasData (6 rows), liveStreams (4 rows) + scheduledStreams (4 rows). Concrete price divergence: the app's pujasData lists 'Satyanarayan Katha' at ₹2,100; the API returns basePrice 1501 and the web page renders '₹1,501'. 'Sunderkand Path' is ₹551 in the app, ₹1,101 in the API. 'Ganesh Atharvashirsha' (₹751 in the app) does not exist in the catalogue at all — the real puja is 'Ganesh Puja'. The Temples tab header reads '5 verified temples' (temples.tsx:244 uses sourceTemples.length) while the website says '22 temples' and the API says total=20. The comment at index.tsx:68 claims 'PujaType doesn't carry price' — it does: every row has basePrice and nriPrice.
  - expected: All three tabs show real seeded data, and a puja costs the same in the app as on the website.
  - customer_impact: The app is a brochure of invented inventory. A devotee taps 'Satyanarayan Katha ₹2,100', is sent to the web checkout, and is charged ₹1,501 — or the reverse on a different puja. Temples that do not exist in the catalogue are advertised as bookable, and 4 fake 'live' streams are shown while the one genuinely live temple is hidden.
  - fix: Fix the three accessors to `.temples` / `.pujaTypes` / `.streams`, bind price to `basePrice`/`nriPrice`, and delete templesData / pujasData / FALLBACK_PUJAS / FALLBACK_DARSHAN / liveStreams / scheduledStreams outright. Mock fallbacks that are indistinguishable from real data are worse than an empty state.

### [PAR-08] P1/wrong-data/S Mobile home screen shows a hardcoded panchang (wrong tithi, nakshatra, sunrise, rahu kaal) every single day
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/index.tsx:83-88
  - steps: 1. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/panchang/today.
2. Compare its keys with `panchang.data?.data?.tithi` used at index.tsx:83.
  - observed: The endpoint returns a FLAT object with keys [id,date,tithi,nakshatra,yoga,karana,sunrise,sunset,rahuKaal,shubhMuhurat,festivals,vrat,specialInfo,locationCity,computed] — there is no `data` wrapper. Every accessor resolves to undefined, so the home card permanently renders the literal fallback strings 'Dwadashi (Shukla Paksha)', 'Uttara Phalguni', '06:02 AM', '06:41 PM', '07:30 AM - 09:00 AM', 'Sarvartha Siddhi Yoga'. Note also index.tsx:87 reads `rahukaal` (lowercase k) while the API key is `rahuKaal` — a second, independent mismatch.
  - expected: Today's real tithi, nakshatra, sunrise/sunset and rahu kaal, matching http://127.0.0.1:3000/panchang.
  - customer_impact: Devotees plan fasts, muhurats and rituals off this card. It is confidently wrong 364 days out of 365, and it disagrees with the website's panchang page for the same date. For a devotional product, publishing a fabricated tithi is a credibility-ending error.
  - fix: Read the flat object (`panchang.data?.tithi`), fix `rahukaal` -> `rahuKaal`, and render an explicit 'Panchang unavailable' state instead of literal fallback strings.

### [PAR-09] P1/wrong-data/S Temple portal dashboard, pujas and streams screens are permanently empty for the temple manager
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(temple)/dashboard.tsx:19 (and :20), pujas.tsx:18, streams.tsx:12
  - steps: 1. Log in as temple@demo.com / demo123.
2. GET /api/v1/darshan/live, /api/v1/temples and /api/v1/pujas/types.
3. Compare against the `.data?.data` accessor used on all three screens.
  - observed: /darshan/live returns {streams:[1 live stream],total,upcoming}; /temples returns {temples:[20]}; /pujas/types returns {pujaTypes:[10]}. All three screens read `.data`, get undefined, and render 0 rows. The temple manager's own temple (Kashi Vishwanath) is live right now and the Streams screen shows nothing.
  - expected: Streams shows the temple's live stream; Pujas lists the 10 puja types; the dashboard stat cards show non-zero counts.
  - customer_impact: A temple partner who has been onboarded opens their portal and sees an empty product — no streams, no pujas, nothing to manage. That is the exact impression that makes a temple churn in week one.
  - fix: Change the three accessors to `.streams`, `.temples`, `.pujaTypes`. Also note these screens use the PUBLIC /darshan/live and /temples endpoints rather than /temple-portal scoped ones, so even when fixed they will show other temples' data — point them at /api/v1/temple-portal/*.

### [PAR-10] P1/wrong-data/S Wellness instructor sees zero sessions although the API returns their sessions
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(wellness)/dashboard.tsx:17, sessions.tsx:53, and apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/wellness.tsx:389 — all via packages/api-client/src/hooks/providers.ts:133
  - steps: 1. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/wellness/sessions.
2. Compare its top-level key with `sessionsQuery.data?.sessions`.
  - observed: Endpoint returns keys [data] with 2 rows. The hook types it as `{sessions: WellnessSession[]}` and all three screens read `.sessions` -> undefined -> 0 rows. The devotee Wellness tab then falls back to the 4 hardcoded `yogaSessions` entries (wellness.tsx:404), so devotees see fake classes while the instructor sees none of their real ones. Note /api/v1/wellness/sessions is registered twice in server.ts (line 356 wellnessRoutes, line 387 wellnessSessionRoutes) — the `{data}` shape is the one that wins.
  - expected: Both the instructor portal and the devotee tab list the 2 real seeded sessions.
  - customer_impact: An instructor cannot see or manage the classes they created; devotees are shown four classes that do not exist and cannot be booked.
  - fix: Change the accessor to `.data` in providers.ts:133 (and wave-1-4.ts:135 which already uses `{data}`), then delete the yogaSessions mock fallback.

### [PAR-11] P1/wrong-data/S Priest Profile screen is always blank and Priest Services is always empty
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(priest)/profile.tsx:30 and apps/mobile/app/(priest)/services.tsx:30
  - steps: 1. Log in as pandit@demo.com / demo123.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/priest/profile with the Bearer token.
3. Compare with `profileQuery.data?.profile` at profile.tsx:30.
4. For services, GET /api/v1/pujas/types and compare with the `.data` accessor at services.tsx:30.
  - observed: /priest/profile returns the profile FLAT: {id,userId,displayName:'Demo Pandit',bio,avatarUrl,specializations[3],languages[2],experienceYears,isVerified,ratingAvg:4.61,ratingCount:43,commissionRate,status,user,temples[1],stats,createdAt} — there is no `profile` wrapper. `data.profile` evaluates to undefined, so the screen renders null. services.tsx:30 reads `(pujasQuery.data)?.data` against {pujaTypes:[10]} -> undefined -> empty catalogue.
  - expected: Profile shows 'Demo Pandit', 4.61 (43 ratings), 3 specializations, 1 temple. Services lists the 10 puja types the pandit can offer.
  - customer_impact: A pandit cannot review or correct the profile devotees book them from, and cannot see or price their own service catalogue. Their listing is effectively unmanageable from the app.

### [PAR-12] P1/wrong-data/S Gaushala portal lists other gaushalas' cows as its own, and newly added cows never appear
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(gaushala)/cows.tsx:57 + :78 and dashboard.tsx:17, via packages/api-client/src/hooks/providers.ts:196 (useCows -> /api/v1/gaushala/cows)
  - steps: 1. Log in as gaushala@demo.com / demo123.
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/gaushala/cows (the PUBLIC endpoint the list uses).
3. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/gaushala-portal/cows with the Bearer token (the SCOPED endpoint the create button writes to, cows.tsx:78).
  - observed: Public /gaushala/cows -> {cows:[19],total:19} — every cow across all 5 seeded gaushalas. Scoped /gaushala-portal/cows -> {cows:[]} — this manager owns none. The list reads the public endpoint, so the manager sees 19 cows that are not theirs; the Add Cow button POSTs to the scoped endpoint, so a cow they create is written to a collection the screen never reads and will never show up in the list.
  - expected: The list reads GET /api/v1/gaushala-portal/cows so it shows exactly the cows this gaushala owns, and a newly created cow appears immediately.
  - customer_impact: A gaushala manager sees a herd of 19 animals they do not own (with adoption status and health records for other people's cows), and every cow they add silently vanishes. Adoption revenue is attributed against the wrong herd.
  - fix: Add a `useGaushalaPortalCows()` hook against /api/v1/gaushala-portal/cows and use it in cows.tsx:57 and dashboard.tsx:17, invalidating that query key after the create mutation.

### [PAR-13] P1/broken-feature/S Astrologer portal dashboard returns 500 and leaks a Prisma stack trace with absolute server paths
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/astrologer-portal.ts:53 — consumed by apps/mobile/app/(astrologer)/dashboard.tsx:18 and http://127.0.0.1:3000/astrologer-portal
  - steps: 1. POST /api/v1/auth/login with astro@demo.com / demo123 (returns user.id = "demo-astro", not a UUID).
2. GET http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/astrologer-portal/dashboard with that Bearer token.
  - observed: 500 {"statusCode":500,"code":"P2023","error":"Internal Server Error","message":"\nInvalid `prisma.astrologerProfile.findUnique()` invocation in\nE:\\NewClaude\\bhaktisetu\\apps\\api\\src\\routes\\astrologer-portal.ts:53:52\n...\nInconsistent column data: Error creating UUID, invalid character: expected an optional prefix of `urn:uuid:` followed by [0-9a-fA-F-], found `m` at 3"}. The response body contains the absolute filesystem path and four lines of server source. Every other portal (priest/temple/store/gaushala/wellness) handles the same non-UUID demo ids and returns 200.
  - expected: 200 with the astrologer's stats, or a clean 404/403 — never a 500 with source code in the body.
  - customer_impact: The astrologer portal home is a hard error on both app and web for this account. Separately, the leaked stack trace discloses the server's directory layout and exact source lines to any unauthenticated-adjacent caller, which is a real information-disclosure issue if the same handler ever 500s in production.
  - fix: Wrap the findUnique in a guard that validates the id is a UUID (or catch P2023 and return an empty profile), and register a Fastify error handler that strips `message`/stack from 5xx bodies in non-development environments.

### [PAR-14] P1/parity/S Website muhurat finder sends no Authorization header — it 401s for every visitor, while the mobile version works
  - where: apps/web/app/weddings/muhurat/page.tsx:68 — http://127.0.0.1:3000/weddings/muhurat
  - steps: 1. Open http://localhost:3000/weddings/muhurat.
2. In the page context, replay the exact request the submit handler makes: fetch('http://localhost:4000/api/v1/weddings/muhurat', {method:'POST', headers:{'content-type':'application/json'}, body:JSON.stringify({fromDate:'2026-09-01',toDate:'2026-09-30',city:'Delhi'})}).
  - observed: 401 {"error":"Unauthorized","message":"Missing or invalid Authorization header. Expected: Bearer <token>"}. The handler's catch at page.tsx:75 turns this into the on-screen string 'Request failed: 401'. apps/api/src/routes/weddings.ts:28 registers POST /muhurat with preHandler [authenticateUser], and the web fetch sends neither a Bearer token nor credentials. The mobile path (packages/api-client/src/hooks/wave-5.ts:152, useFindMuhurats) goes through the shared client which injects the token at client.ts:146, so the app succeeds where the site fails.
  - expected: Either the endpoint is public (it is a lead-generation tool) or the web page attaches the session token like every other authed web call.
  - customer_impact: A high-intent wedding lead fills in a 10-field form, hits Find, and gets 'Request failed: 401'. This is the top of the highest-value funnel in the product and it is broken for 100% of web visitors, signed in or not.
  - fix: Attach the session bearer token in page.tsx:68 (matching the pattern other authed web pages use), or drop authenticateUser from apps/api/src/routes/weddings.ts:28 and rate-limit it instead — the muhurat calculation needs no user context.

### [PAR-15] P1/parity/L Puja booking does not exist in the app — the Book button opens the production website, and the create-booking hook points at a 404
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/pujas.tsx:161-163 and :230; dead hook at packages/api-client/src/hooks/index.ts:229 -> packages/shared/src/constants/index.ts:291 (BOOKINGS)
  - steps: 1. Read pujas.tsx:161 — bookUrl is `https://darshya.online/pujas/book?slug=${puja.slug}&utm_source=mobile-app...` and both the card (line 163) and the Book Puja button (line 230) call Linking.openURL(bookUrl).
2. POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/bookings with any body to test the hook api-client actually exposes.
3. Note the real route is POST /api/v1/bookings/book (apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:652).
  - observed: POST /api/v1/bookings -> 404 {"message":"Route POST:/api/v1/bookings not found","error":"Not Found","statusCode":404}. So `useCreateBooking` is dead on arrival. In practice nothing calls it because there is no in-app booking flow at all: tapping Book Puja ejects the user into the mobile browser on darshya.online. Because the catalogue is served from the hardcoded pujasData (see PAR-07), the slug in that URL comes from the mock, and the price the user saw in the app does not match the page they land on. The same pattern applies to /api/v1/payments/booking/:id (PAYMENT_BY_BOOKING, constants:299) which also 404s — the real route is /api/v1/payments/:id.
  - expected: An in-app booking flow, or at minimum a hook that points at POST /api/v1/bookings/book and a deep link carrying the real slug and price.
  - customer_impact: The core revenue action requires leaving the app, re-authenticating in a mobile browser, and paying on a page that quotes a different price. Conversion through that hand-off will be a fraction of native checkout, and the app cannot claim any booking attribution. A shipped binary with a 404 create-booking hook cannot be corrected without a store release.
  - fix: Point API_ENDPOINTS.BOOKINGS at '/api/v1/bookings/book' and PAYMENT_BY_BOOKING at '/api/v1/payments/${id}', then build the booking + Razorpay flow in-app. Until then, at least deep-link with the real slug from the API rather than a mock.

### [PAR-16] P1/dead-control/M Store "Add to cart" does not add to cart — it opens a web search for the product name
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/store.tsx:188 (and :148, :253)
  - steps: 1. Read store.tsx:188 — the Add button's onPress is Linking.openURL(`https://darshya.online/store?utm_source=mobile-app&utm_medium=add-to-cart&q=${encodeURIComponent(product.name)}`).
2. Confirm the real cart API works: POST http://127.0.0.1:4000/api/v1/store/cart/items returns 401 (route exists) and GET /api/v1/store/cart returns 200 {cart:{...}} with a devotee token.
3. Confirm the hooks already exist: packages/api-client/src/hooks/store.ts:156 useAddToCart, :142 useCart.
  - observed: The Add-to-cart control performs zero API calls; it launches the external browser to a store search filtered by the product NAME (not even the product page). The cart icon at store.tsx:253 likewise opens darshya.online/store/cart. Meanwhile useAddToCart / useCart / useUpdateCartItem / useRemoveCartItem all exist in api-client and all resolve to live routes.
  - expected: Tapping Add posts to /api/v1/store/cart/items and the cart badge increments in-app.
  - customer_impact: Nothing can be purchased in the app. A user who taps Add three times ends up with three browser tabs and an empty cart. The store is decorative.
  - fix: Wire the existing useAddToCart / useCart hooks into store.tsx and build an in-app cart screen; the API surface is already complete and working.

### [PAR-17] P1/broken-feature/S Temple card deep link uses the temple id, but the web route expects a slug — every link 404s
  - where: apps/mobile/app/(devotee)/temples.tsx:124
  - steps: 1. Read temples.tsx:124 — detailUrl is `https://darshya.online/temples/${encodeURIComponent(temple.id)}?...`.
2. curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples/1  (the id from the mock fallback that is actually rendered).
3. curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples/6dd80402-b4eb-42be-b66c-c64b2054daa2  (a real temple UUID).
4. curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi  (the slug).
  - observed: /temples/1 -> 404. /temples/6dd80402-b4eb-42be-b66c-c64b2054daa2 -> 404. /temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi -> 200. The web route is apps/web/app/temples/[slug]/page.tsx, so neither the mock id nor the real UUID resolves.
  - expected: The link should use temple.slug and land on the temple page.
  - customer_impact: Every non-live temple card in the app opens a 404 page on the website. Combined with PAR-05 (live temples open a dead player), there is no path from the Temples tab to any working temple destination.
