There is no health or readiness endpoint anywhere that reports channel status.
  - customer_impact: Every notification in the product is currently a no-op. Because env-or-noop is applied uniformly and no counter distinguishes 'skipped' from 'sent', an operator, a smoke test, and the admin panel all see green while a devotee who paid hears nothing — which arrives as a refund request or a chargeback, not as an alert.
  - fix: Add `GET /api/v1/health/channels` returning {email, whatsapp, webPush, fcm} enabled booleans, and surface it on /admin. Make POST /notifications/test return `{ok:false, reason:'web-push not configured'}` when webPushConfig.enabled is false instead of ok:true. In production, fail the boot (or fire a startup alert) if RESEND_API_KEY and VAPID keys are both absent.

### [NOTIF-04] P0/comms/S notifyRefundProcessed has zero call sites — a refunded devotee without an email address is told nothing on any channel
  - where: apps/api/src/services/booking-notify.service.ts:269-312; apps/api/src/services/refund.service.ts:318-334
  - evidence: Grep for `notifyRefundProcessed` across apps/ returns exactly three files: its own definition (booking-notify.service.ts:285), its own docblock, and its own unit test (booking-notify.service.test.ts:167-206). No production caller exists. The only refund notification in the codebase is refund.service.ts:319-330, gated twice: `if (booking.user.email)` and then RESEND_API_KEY inside sendEmail. The docblock at booking-notify.service.ts:269-284 describes this function as the fix for exactly that gap ('a phone-only devotee was told nothing on any channel when their money went back... Worst on the cron auto-refund path') and says wiring it in 'is purely additive' — but the wiring was never done.
  - customer_impact: On the cron auto-refund path (processAutoRefunds, which runs an hour after cancellation with no user-initiated trigger), a phone-OTP devotee — the exact cohort the WhatsApp-first GTM targets — gets their money back with zero notice on push, WhatsApp or email. The predictable next step is a chargeback on a refund that was already paid, which costs the platform the disputed amount plus the fee.
  - fix: Call `notifyRefundProcessed({bookingId, amountInr: refundInr.toString()})` immediately after the refund transaction commits in refund.service.processRefund (after line 334), and again in processAutoRefunds. The function already refuses to send email, so it cannot duplicate the existing refundSuccessEmail.

### [NOTIF-05] P0/comms/M The video-proof delivery you promise in writing has no email and no WhatsApp implementation
  - where: apps/api/src/lib/email.ts:146; apps/api/src/services/booking.service.ts:258-262,777-786; apps/web/app/nri-pujas/page.tsx:66,131
  - evidence: bookingConfirmationEmail's body literally says: 'The priest will perform the puja and upload a video proof. You'll receive an email when the recording is ready.' Grep for `export function .*Email(` across apps/api/src returns 8 templates (magicLink, bookingConfirmation, providerApproved, providerRejected, supportTicketAlert, supportTicketReceived, supportTicketReply, donationReceipt) plus refundSuccessEmail defined locally in refund.service.ts — none is a video-ready email. The only thing that fires on video_uploaded is pushMessageFor -> notifyUser (web-push only, booking.service.ts:780-786). The BookingEvent 'video_ready' and its WhatsApp template 'puja_video_ready' are declared at booking-notify.service.ts:114,127 and never passed to notifyDevotee from anywhere. Meanwhile /nri-pujas tells the highest-value cohort 'You receive a WhatsApp link within 24 hours of completion' and 'Within 24 hours, a 3-7 minute video clip arrives on WhatsApp'.
  - customer_impact: Video proof is business rule #1 and the entire justification for 9-10x NRI pricing. An NRI who paid a premium is promised the video on WhatsApp and by email, and the system can only fire a browser push — which requires a desktop browser with granted permission, i.e. essentially nobody in that cohort. They will not know the puja happened until they log in and look.
  - fix: Add a videoReadyEmail template to lib/email.ts and call notifyDevotee('video_ready', ctx) from booking.service.updateBookingStatus on the video_uploaded transition (the template name puja_video_ready and push copy already exist). Until the WhatsApp templates are approved in Meta Manager, correct the nri-pujas and confirmation-email copy to say what actually ships.

### [NOTIF-06] P1/comms/M notifyPriest is dead code and 6 of 11 declared BookingEvents never fire
  - where: apps/api/src/services/booking-notify.service.ts:106-131,314-336
  - evidence: Grep for `notifyPriest` across apps/ finds only its definition at line 315 — no caller, not even a test. Of the 11 BookingEvents, the events actually passed to notifyDevotee anywhere in the repo are: 'confirmed' (payment.service.ts:320), 'assigned' (priest.service.ts:298), 'reassigned' (priest.service.ts:361, cancellation.service.ts:200, dispatch.service.ts:478) and 'refund' (only from the uncalled notifyRefundProcessed). Never fired from any call site: offer_timeout, in_progress, completed, video_ready, cancelled, eta. Their WhatsApp templates (pandit_offer_timeout, puja_started, puja_completed, puja_video_ready, booking_cancelled, pandit_eta) and their push copy are written and unreachable.
  - customer_impact: A pandit is never notified of anything after the initial dispatch offer — not that their offer expired, not that a booking was reassigned away from them. A devotee is never told the puja started, that it completed, or — on the cancellation path — anything beyond a browser push. The 11-template WhatsApp approval effort listed as founder-gated in TASKS.md is sized for a fan-out that only 4 templates are wired to use.
  - fix: Wire the missing transitions in booking.service.updateBookingStatus (in_progress, completed, cancelled) and in dispatch.service.sweepExpiredOffers (offer_timeout -> notifyPriest for each priest whose offer expired). Delete 'eta' or build the day-of trigger it implies; a declared event with no producer reads as shipped policy that was not shipped.

### [NOTIF-07] P1/data-integrity/M Aarti opt-in stores a web-push endpoint URL as an FCM device token — undeliverable, and the first send deletes the registration
  - where: apps/web/components/AartiOptIn.tsx:94-116; apps/api/src/lib/fcm.ts:112-128; apps/api/src/jobs/aarti-notify.ts:156-171
  - evidence: AartiOptIn.tsx:100-101 does `const subJson = sub.toJSON(); const deviceToken = subJson.endpoint || JSON.stringify(subJson)` and POSTs it to /notifications/register-token — i.e. a `https://fcm.googleapis.com/fcm/send/...` URL is written into fcm_device_tokens.device_token. aarti-notify.ts:157 then calls sendFcm(row.deviceToken, ...), which puts that URL in `message.token` of an FCM HTTP v1 send (fcm.ts:112-118). FCM v1 requires a registration token there, not a push endpoint, and returns INVALID_ARGUMENT — which fcm.ts:124-128 classifies as `expired:true`, so aarti-notify.ts:165-171 deleteMany's the row. Measured: fcm_device_tokens holds 1 row with platform 'web' (created by my /register-token probe). The comment at AartiOptIn.tsx:77-79 assumes 'the API treats both as opaque strings' — FCM does not.
  - customer_impact: Every web user who enables '15-minute aarti reminders' sees the toggle go on and will never receive one. Worse, the first aarti send after they opt in silently deletes their registration, so the toggle also cannot be diagnosed as stuck-on — the row is just gone.
  - fix: Either route web opt-ins through the VAPID path (push_subscriptions + sendPush, which already works and has a correct service worker) and reserve fcm_device_tokens for native iOS/Android FCM tokens, or add a platform check in sendFcm that refuses non-FCM tokens loudly instead of classifying them as expired. Also stop treating INVALID_ARGUMENT as 'expired' — a malformed token is a bug, not a dead device.

### [NOTIF-08] P1/scalability/M Aarti reminder window is 3 minutes wide but the documented cron cadence is 30 minutes — ≥90% of reminders are structurally missed
  - where: apps/api/src/jobs/aarti-notify.ts:6,108-111; apps/api/src/jobs/master-cron.ts:35
  - evidence: aarti-notify.ts:111 is `if (minutesUntil < 14 || minutesUntil > 16) continue;` — a 3-minute-wide match window. Its own header comment (line 6) says 'Runs every ~5 min from master-cron' and line 110 justifies the width as 'Master-cron tick is ~5 min so 3-min spread ensures we hit each aarti at least once'. But master-cron.ts:35, the only place the real cadence is stated, says 'Hit it from cron-job.org (free, every 30 min)'. At 30-minute ticks the hit rate per aarti is 3/30 = 10%. The two files contradict each other and the deployment doc loses.
  - customer_impact: Even after FCM is configured and NOTIF-07 is fixed, 9 out of 10 aarti reminders never fire, and the ones that do are unpredictable. A reminder feature that works 10% of the time is worse than none — users disable it after the first missed aarti.
  - fix: Make the window derive from the actual tick interval rather than a hardcoded 14-16: persist a `last_fired_at` per (templeId, slotKey, ist_date) row and fire on the first tick where minutesUntil is between 0 and TICK_INTERVAL+15. Or run aarti-notify on its own */5 schedule separate from the 18-tick daily chain.

### [NOTIF-09] P1/ux-mindset/S Aarti global opt-in ignores which temple the user follows — 20 pushes per device per day on the current seed
  - where: apps/api/src/jobs/aarti-notify.ts:133-146
  - evidence: After collecting tokens for users who follow the temple, lines 135-146 unconditionally append EVERY token with `aartiOptIn: true` regardless of temple, merging by deviceToken. Applying the repo's own parseSchedule/AARTI_HINTS logic (lib/darshan-schedule.ts:43-48) to the seeded temples where live_darshan_enabled=true AND status='active': Kashi Vishwanath 3, Tirumala 1, Siddhivinayak 2, Jagannath 3, ISKCON Bangalore 1, Govind Dev Ji 6, Shirdi Sai 4, Dagdusheth 0, Vaishno Devi 0 = 9 temples, 20 aarti slots/day. temple_follows currently holds 0 rows, so the targeted branch has no audience at all and 100% of the fan-out comes from the global branch.
  - customer_impact: One opt-in produces 20 notifications a day about temples the user never chose, starting at 02:45 IST (Tirumala suprabhatam) and running to 21:00 (Kashi shringar). That is a same-day uninstall or a permanent browser-level notification block, which also kills the booking notifications that actually matter.
  - fix: Drop the global-opt-in branch: send only to followers of that temple (the aartiOptIn flag then means 'aarti reminders for temples I follow'). If a global option is wanted, cap it — nearest 1-2 temples, or a single daily digest of that day's aartis rather than one push per slot.

### [NOTIF-10] P1/scalability/M Instant-tier dispatch promises a 60-second accept window but the sweep that acts on it runs on the cron tick
  - where: apps/api/src/services/dispatch.service.ts:50,400-488; apps/api/src/services/cancellation.service.ts:187; apps/api/src/jobs/master-cron.ts:35,477-489
  - evidence: TIER_CONFIG.instant is `{candidateCount: 6, acceptWindowSec: 60, maxRounds: 2}`. Nothing acts on an expired offer except sweepExpiredOffers, which is only called from tickDispatchSweep inside runDailyOps — documented cadence 30 minutes (master-cron.ts:35), and per NOTIF-01 currently scheduled by nothing. So round 2 of a 60-second instant offer lands up to 30 minutes late, and the escalation notification ('we're re-matching your pandit', dispatch.service.ts:478) up to 60 minutes late. cancellation.service.ts:187 sets `offerExpiresAt: new Date()` with the comment 'trigger immediate re-broadcast on next sweep' — the word 'immediate' is doing work the scheduler does not do.
  - customer_impact: The instant tier is the paid product differentiator against SmartPuja/Harivara. A devotee pays for instant dispatch, gets no acceptance in 60 seconds, and then sits in silence for up to half an hour before anyone else is even offered the booking — and up to an hour before they are told anything. Same for a pandit-initiated cancellation: 'immediate re-broadcast' means up to 30 minutes.
  - fix: Run sweepExpiredOffers on a tight independent loop (a 30-second setInterval in the API process, or a dedicated */1 cron hitting a token-gated sweep endpoint) rather than as tick 13 of an 18-tick daily chain. The sweep is already bounded (take: 50) and idempotent, so a fast cadence is safe.

### [NOTIF-11] P1/resilience/S The only scheduled cron in the repo runs three hardcoded stubs and reports ok:true every day
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/cron.ts:46-69,74-81; .github/workflows/daily-cron.yml
  - evidence: All three registered jobs are literal stubs: refreshPanchang returns `{daysRefreshed: 0}` ('Real implementation would call mhah-panchang...'), sendFestivalReminders returns `{sent: 0}` ('Stub for now — wire when push subs land in Prisma' — push subs landed in Prisma months ago, see the push_subscriptions table and notifications.ts:48), expireBookings returns `{expired: 0}`. POST /cron/daily runs all three, computes `ok = results.every(r => r.ok)` and returns 200. daily-cron.yml uses `curl -fsS`, which only fails on non-2xx — so the workflow goes green daily.
  - customer_impact: apps/web/app/panchang/page.tsx:602 sells 'daily panchang, shubh muhurat alerts, and festival reminders'. Festival reminders are `return { sent: 0 }`. Nobody has ever received one, and the daily green check mark is the reason nobody noticed.
  - fix: Delete the stubs or make them throw so the workflow goes red. Point daily-cron.yml at /api/v1/admin/cron/tick (the real runDailyOps entrypoint) and have the workflow assert on the returned counters, not just the HTTP status.

### [NOTIF-12] P1/comms/S Daily panchang push has no per-day idempotency — it fires once per cron tick inside the 5am IST hour
  - where: apps/api/src/jobs/master-cron.ts:132-139,219-261
  - evidence: tickPanchangBlast's only gate is `isFiveAmIstWindow(now)`, which returns true for the entire hour (`istHour === 5`, lines 132-139). Unlike the weekly digest — which persists WeeklyDigestRun and enforces a 6-day cooldown in maybeRunWeeklyDigest (weekly-digest.ts:378-399) — there is no record that today's panchang was already sent. At the documented 30-minute cadence the 5:00-5:59 IST hour contains exactly 2 ticks, so every subscriber gets the identical 'Today's Panchang' push twice each morning. A 10-minute cadence would make it 6.
  - customer_impact: Duplicate 5am notifications are the single most reliable way to get a devotional app's notifications turned off permanently — and turning them off also kills the booking-confirmed and video-ready pushes that carry the paid product.
  - fix: Add a PanchangBlastRun row (or reuse a generic job_runs table) keyed by IST date and skip when today is already stamped, mirroring WeeklyDigestRun. One INSERT ... ON CONFLICT before the fan-out.

### [NOTIF-13] P1/scalability/M Weekly digest is 1+2N queries with fabricated personalization and no retry past the Resend free-tier cap
  - where: apps/api/src/jobs/weekly-digest.ts:174-198,218-243,306-346,348-361
  - evidence: Per user the loop issues two extra queries: fetchUpcomingAartisForUser (a raw temple_follows join) and fetchRecommendedPuja. fetchRecommendedPuja (lines 218-243) takes the user as a parameter and never uses it — the gotra branch at 223-227 is an empty block with a comment, and the query is `pujaType.findFirst({where:{isActive:true}, orderBy:{sortOrder:'asc'}})`, identical for every recipient. So N identical queries return one identical row, and the email says 'Based on your <gotra> gotra, devotees often book: <the same puja for everyone>'. fetchUpcomingAartisForUser hardcodes `aarti: 'Sandhya Aarti', whenLocal: 'today 6:30 PM IST'` for every followed temple regardless of that temple's actual timings — the DB has real per-temple aarti windows (Govind Dev Ji has 6, Shirdi 4) that are ignored. At 10,000 users that is 20,001 queries plus 10,000 × 20ms of deliberate sleep (SEND_RATE_MS) inside one HTTP request. docs/free-tier-budgets.md caps Resend at 100/day: recipients 101+ come back `ok:false`, are counted as `failed`, and are never retried — yet line 350 stamps WeeklyDigestRun unconditionally, and maybeRunWeeklyDigest then skips the job for 6 days.
  - customer_impact: The digest is unsendable past 100 users on the current plan, and the 99% who fail are silently written off for a week with no dead-letter. The 1% who receive it get a fabricated aarti time and a 'personalized' recommendation identical to everyone else's — which is worse than no recommendation for a product whose differentiator is knowing the devotee's gotra and rashi.
  - fix: Hoist fetchRecommendedPuja out of the loop (or implement the gotra→deity mapping it pretends to do); join temple_follows once for the whole batch instead of per user; render real aarti times from Temple.timingInfo via the existing parseSchedule; and only stamp WeeklyDigestRun when failed === 0, persisting failures to a retry queue.

### [NOTIF-14] P1/resilience/M There is no in-app notification centre and no record that any notification was ever attempted
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/notifications.ts; apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma
  - evidence: Seven candidate read endpoints all 404 on the live API: GET /api/v1/notifications, /list, /inbox, /feed, /me, /unread, /history. There is no Notification model in schema.prisma — the notification-adjacent tables in the live DB are exactly WeeklyDigestOptOut, WeeklyDigestRun, email_sessions, fcm_device_tokens, outreach_logs and push_subscriptions, and outreach_logs belongs to the B2B lead-outreach pipeline (outreach.service.ts), not to user notifications. Every send path is fire-and-forget with the result discarded: notifyDevotee/notifyPriest wrap each leg in `.catch(() => undefined)`, sendEmail's `{ok:false}` return is ignored at almost every call site, and nothing persists a row.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who misses a push has no way to see it later — there is no bell, no history, nothing. Support cannot answer 'did the confirmation go out?' for any booking. Nothing can be retried, because nothing knows a send failed. Combined with NOTIF-03, the system is silent and also unable to prove it was silent.
  - fix: Add a NotificationLog table (userId, channel, event, bookingId, status enum sent|skipped|failed, providerId, error, createdAt) written by notifyUser/sendEmail/sendWhatsappTemplate, and expose GET /api/v1/notifications for the devotee (the same rows) — one table gives you the in-app centre, the support answer, and the retry source at once.

### [NOTIF-15] P2/ux-mindset/S "Set reminder" bell on the darshan schedule writes to localStorage and nothing else
  - where: apps/web/components/DarshanScheduleRemindButton.tsx:48-56; apps/web/app/darshan/page.tsx:375
  - evidence: toggle() reads and writes the localStorage key `poojly_darshan_reminders` and sets a toast reading `Reminder set for ${label}`. There is no fetch, no API_BASE_URL import, no network call of any kind in the component. Grep for `poojly_darshan_reminders` across apps/ returns exactly two files: this component and DarshanBrowser.tsx:50, which also only reads it for UI state. No server ever learns the reminder exists and no job could deliver it.
  - customer_impact: The user is explicitly told 'Reminder set for Sandhya Aarti' and will never be reminded. It also silently forgets on a different device or after a cache clear. This is the kind of thing users discover by missing the aarti they were counting on.
  - fix: Either POST the reminder to a real DarshanReminder table and have the aarti job read it, or replace the bell with the AartiOptIn control that at least registers with the server — but do not show a confirmation toast for a preference that never leaves the browser.

### [NOTIF-16] P2/comms/S Payout-sent notification is web-push only — providers learn their money moved from a channel they almost certainly do not have
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payout.service.ts:332-338,446-455
  - evidence: Both payout notifications are a bare notifyUser (web-push): 'Payout requested' at line 333 and 'Payout sent' / 'Payout update' at line 447. No sendEmail, no sendWhatsappTemplate. notifyUser resolves subscriptions from push_subscriptions, which the whole DB currently has 1 row in (created by my own probe). All 23 seeded users have both an email and a phone on file, so both alternative channels are available and unused. astrologer.service.ts:96-126 has the same shape — astrologerContact even selects the phone and then never uses it.
  - customer_impact: A pandit whose payout completes gets a browser push or nothing. Pandits are mobile-first and are the cohort least likely to have granted desktop web-push permission, so in practice the settlement is invisible until they open /payouts. Silence about money is what drives the support calls and the trust loss that a marketplace cannot afford while supply is thin.
  - fix: Route payout completion through the same fan-out as bookings (push + WhatsApp template + email with the transfer reference). The provider's phone and email are already on the User row and the WhatsApp/email clients already exist — this is a call-site change, not new infrastructure.

## AREA: resilience   score=45

### [RES-01] P0/resilience/M Only auth calls have a timeout; every other fetch hangs forever, leaving a permanent spinner
  - where: apps/web/lib/api-config.ts:47 (fetchWithTimeout, 9 call sites) vs 231 bare fetch sites; measured on apps/web/app/priest/page.tsx:131+174, app/profile/page.tsx:95+206, components/Inbox.tsx:66, app/dashboard/page.tsx:76, app/payouts/page.tsx:52
  - evidence: Static scan of apps/web/{app,components,lib}: 240 awaited fetch sites, 9 use fetchWithTimeout, 231 are bare fetch with no AbortController; 78 of those 231 are reached AFTER a loading/submitting flag has been set true. Live Playwright route interception holding every /api/v1/* request open (never answering): /priest showed "Loading dashboard..." with 1 spinner at T+10s AND still at T+30s (elapsed 44s); /profile "Loading profile..." spinner at T+10s and T+30s; /messages "Loading inbox..." spinner at T+10s and T+30s; /dashboard "Loading bookings..." with 1 spinner + 4 skeletons at T+10s and T+30s; /payouts "Available to withdraw …" placeholders + "Payout history Loading…" at T+10s and T+30s. No error text, no retry control, no timeout in any of them. fetch() never rejects on a stalled socket, so the try/catch/finally blocks these pages do have never run.
  - customer_impact: This is the same defect class as the reported production sign-in bug (TASKS.md LIVE P0), which was patched for auth only. On a flaky Indian mobile connection, a captive portal, or a cold Render dyno, a pandit opening his portal, a devotee opening her profile or bookings, and anyone opening Messages sees a spinner that never stops and never explains itself. There is no way to recover except guessing to reload.
  - fix: Export the existing fetchWithTimeout from lib/api-config.ts as the only sanctioned client fetch and route all 231 call sites through it (a codemod on `await fetch(` inside apps/web is mechanical). For the react-query surfaces, wrap queryFn with an AbortSignal.timeout(15000) and render `error` — QueryProvider already sets retry:1. Pair every timeout with the existing NETWORK_ERROR_MESSAGE plus a Retry button. Add an ESLint no-restricted-syntax rule banning bare `fetch(` in apps/web so this cannot regress.

### [RES-02] P1/ux-mindset/S 7 of 10 authenticated pages render fabricated zeros with no error when the API returns 500
  - where: apps/web/app/payouts/page.tsx:52-67, app/temple-portal/page.tsx:69-80, app/astrologer-portal/page.tsx:89-101, app/wellness-portal/page.tsx:72-98, app/gaushala-portal/page.tsx:70-80, app/dashboard/page.tsx:76-101, app/profile/page.tsx:95-120
  - evidence: Every /api/v1/* call fulfilled with HTTP 500 {"error":"Internal Server Error"}, then the page's <main> innerText captured at T+7s. NO error anywhere: /payouts → "Available to withdraw ₹0 Processing ₹0 Paid out (total) ₹0 … Payout history No payouts yet."; /temple-portal → "0 Total Bookings 0 Bookings This Month ₹0 Revenue This Month ₹0 Total Revenue … No bookings yet"; /gaushala-portal → "0 Total Cows 0 Active Donors ₹0 Monthly Donations ₹0 Total Donations … No donations yet"; /wellness-portal → "0 Upcoming Sessions 0 Total Students"; /astrologer-portal → "0 Today's Sessions 0 Total Consultations 0 Active Clients — Rating"; /messages → "Messages 0 conversations No conversations yet."; /dashboard → "Total Bookings 0 … Recent Bookings No Bookings Yet Begin your spiritual journey by booking your first puja." Only 3 of 10 were honest: /priest "Could not load dashboard (status 500). Showing baseline.", /store-admin "Couldn't load live dashboard data (Orders 500). Showing zeros — refresh to retry.", /bookings "Could not load your bookings: …". The code pattern is uniform: `if (!r.ok) return;` or `catch { /* keep mock */ }` with no error state. Same result with a malformed body (/payouts still "₹0" at T+7s).
  - customer_impact: During any backend incident every provider on the platform is told their business produced nothing: ₹0 withdrawable, ₹0 revenue this month, no bookings, no donors. Providers churn on exactly this signal, and support gets "where did my money go" tickets during an outage instead of "the site is down". For a devotee, "No Bookings Yet — begin your spiritual journey" is shown to someone who already has confirmed pujas.
  - fix: Adopt the /store-admin copy as the house pattern and apply it to all seven: keep the zeros but always render the banner "Couldn't load live data (HTTP <n>). Showing zeros — retry" plus a Retry button wired to the loader. Concretely, replace `if (!r.ok) return;` with `if (!r.ok) { setLoadError(...); return; }` in temple-portal:74, astrologer-portal:94, gaushala-portal, priest:158 (kyc), and surface the `error` object react-query already produces in wellness-portal:72 and payouts:52. Never render a currency figure or a "you have none yet" empty state from a failed load without the banner.

### [RES-03] P1/data-integrity/S /profile shows blank fields on a failed load, and saving then NULLs out gotra, nakshatra, rashi, city and email
  - where: apps/web/app/profile/page.tsx:91-120 (load: console.error only, no error state) and apps/web/app/profile/page.tsx:152-171 (save: explicit null for every empty field)
  - evidence: With /api/v1/users/me returning 500, /profile rendered at T+7s with every field empty and no error message: "Full Name * / Phone Number N/A / Email Address / City / Gotra / Nakshatra (Birth Star) Select Nakshatra …" (4 API calls fired, showsError=false). The loader at line 99 only populates state `if (res.ok)`; the catch at 115 does `console.error` and nothing user-visible. handleSave then builds the PATCH body as `if (form.gotra.trim()) payload.gotra = ...; else payload.gotra = null;` — repeated verbatim for email (152), gotra (155), nakshatra (158), rashi (161), locationCity (164), locationState (167), nriCountry (170). Only fullName is required-checked.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who opens Edit Profile during a backend blip sees a blank form, assumes their profile was never filled in, types their name, presses Save, and silently destroys their gotra, nakshatra, rashi, city and email. Gotra and nakshatra are load-bearing for sankalp during a puja, so the next booking is performed with the wrong ritual details and the devotee has no idea why.
  - fix: Two guards. (1) In loadProfile, add a `loadFailed` state on non-ok/catch; render an error banner and disable Save until a successful load. (2) Make the PATCH a true partial update: only include a key when the field is dirty (track a `touched` set), so an untouched field is never sent as null. This also removes the need for the null-vs-omitted asymmetry at 152-171.

### [RES-04] P1/resilience/S loadRazorpay() has no onerror and no timeout in the two puja payment flows — the order is already minted, then the Pay button locks forever
  - where: apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:341-353 and apps/web/app/checkout/offering/page.tsx:38-50
  - evidence: Both define `new Promise((resolve) => { … script.onload = () => resolve(window.Razorpay); document.body.appendChild(script); })` with NO `script.onerror` and no timer — if checkout.razorpay.com fails to load the promise never settles. In the wizard this `await loadRazorpay()` sits at page.tsx:848, AFTER `POST /bookings/create-order` has already succeeded at :801 (I verified the endpoint creates the PujaBooking row and the Razorpay order before responding: apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1703-1731). paymentLoading stays true and creatingOrderRef.current stays true, so the Pay button is permanently disabled with a spinner. The same file's sibling flows DO handle it — components/DonationForm.tsx:106 `script.onerror = () => resolve(null)`, app/astrology/[id]/book/page.tsx:44 and app/store/checkout/page.tsx:54 both reject — so this is an omission, not a design choice.
  - customer_impact: Any devotee behind an ad-blocker, a corporate/ISP filter, or during a Razorpay CDN blip completes the entire multi-step booking form, presses Pay, and gets a spinner that never resolves and never errors — while a pending_payment booking and a live Razorpay order already exist server-side holding slot capacity. The user cannot retry, cannot pay, and gets no support reference. The code even has a correct message ready for the sibling branch ("Could not load Razorpay checkout. Please retry.") that this path can never reach.
  - fix: Add `script.onerror = () => resolve(undefined)` and a `setTimeout(() => resolve(undefined), 10000)` to both loaders (mirroring DonationForm.tsx:106). The existing `if (!Razorpay) { setPaymentError('Could not load Razorpay checkout. Please retry.'); … }` branch then fires correctly. Also surface the already-created bookingNumber in that message so support can reconcile the orphan pending_payment row.

### [RES-05] P1/data-integrity/M POST /bookings/create-order has no idempotency: repeated identical requests mint extra bookings and extra payable Razorpay orders
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1649-1731 (create-order: unconditional createBooking + createRazorpayOrder, no idempotency key, no reuse of an existing pending_payment booking)
  - evidence: Three byte-identical POSTs fired in the same tick with one devotee token (offering ee690cd7…, 2027-02-28, slot 07:00) returned statuses [201,201,201] with three distinct bookings BS-20260812-VXVHO / RQRUX / PP6S4 and three distinct Razorpay order ids order_demo_1786547225603 / …224641 / …225565, each amount 155200 paise (₹1,552). A sequential repeat on slot 08:00 did the same: BS-20260812-U2NH5 / WWQCW / 89GE6. Confirmed in Postgres: three separate pending_payment rows for the same temple_puja_offering_id + scheduled_date + scheduled_time_slot. (Capacity is correctly protected — max_bookings_per_slot=5 with an advisory lock inside the transaction — so this is duplicate intent, not oversell.) Client-side the wizard's useRef guard does hold (I measured 3 parallel clicks and a native dblclick on a useState-guarded form producing exactly 1 POST each), so the exposure is server-side: a retried request after a dropped response, a second tab, or a refresh-and-resubmit.
  - customer_impact: A devotee whose response is lost to a mobile handover retries and now owns two bookings and two live Razorpay orders for one puja — either two charges to reverse, or one paid booking plus a phantom pending_payment row that holds slot inventory. Each duplicate also consumes 1 of the 5 slot places, so three retries on a popular muhurat slot lock out real devotees for the 30-minute hold window.
  - fix: Accept an `Idempotency-Key` header (or derive a natural key from userId+templeOfferingId+scheduledDate+scheduledTime) and, inside the existing slot transaction, return the existing pending_payment booking and its Razorpay order instead of creating a second one when the same key/tuple arrives within the UNPAID_HOLD_MINUTES window. The lock at booking.service.ts:551 is already the right place to do the lookup.

### [RES-06] P1/comms/S During an outage every provider portal tells an approved, KYC-verified provider that they are unapproved and unverified
  - where: apps/web/app/priest/page.tsx:148-168 (fetchKyc silently returns on !r.ok, leaving kycState='unsubmitted'), app/astrologer-portal/page.tsx:103-123, app/wellness-portal/page.tsx:86-110 (useQuery, kyc undefined ⇒ 'unsubmitted'), plus MyProviderProfileCard on all 6 portals
  - evidence: With every /api/v1/* returning 500, all six provider portals rendered the onboarding-nag state: /priest "KYC required for payout … Complete your Pandit profile — Submit your details so an admin can review and approve your listing. Complete onboarding"; identical strings on /temple-portal, /gaushala-portal, /wellness-portal, /astrologer-portal and /store-admin. The seeded pandit is verified in Postgres (priests.is_verified = true for all 3 seeded priests) and GET /payouts/balance returns {kycVerified:true, kycOnFile:true} when the API is healthy. The default is baked in: priest/page.tsx:105 `useState<'verified'|'pending'|'unsubmitted'|'rejected'>('unsubmitted')` and fetchKyc's `if (!r.ok) return;` at :158 never corrects it.
  - customer_impact: The single most alarming message you can show a provider — "your KYC is missing, you cannot be paid, an admin still has to approve you" — is what they see whenever the backend hiccups. Providers who already went through onboarding will re-submit KYC documents, open support tickets, or conclude the platform lost their verification. This is the opposite of reassuring during an incident.
  - fix: Make the KYC/approval badge tri-state: 'unknown' until a successful fetch, and render nothing (or a neutral "checking…") for 'unknown' instead of defaulting to 'unsubmitted'. Same for the profile-completion card — do not render the onboarding CTA on a failed load.

### [RES-07] P2/comms/S The booking wizard throws away the API's error body, so an unrecoverable 409 is shown as "Please try again"
  - where: apps/web/app/pujas/book/page.tsx:820-831 (builds the message from res.status only; never parses the JSON body)
  - evidence: The API returns a fully localized, actionable failure: HTTP 409 {"error":"The selected time slot is no longer available.","code":"SLOT_UNAVAILABLE","message":"…"} (apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1741-1750, classifier at :578-590, strings in src/lib/error-i18n.ts:81). I reproduced the 409 repeatedly against the live API. The web handler discards all of it: `const message = res ? \`Could not create your booking (${res.status}). Please try again or contact support at support@darshya.online.\` : 'Network error…'`. The sibling flow does it correctly — app/checkout/offering/page.tsx:241-247 reads `body?.error` first.
  - customer_impact: A devotee who loses a slot race is told to "try again", which can never succeed, and is shown a raw HTTP number. The same unhelpful string covers 400 (invalid slot), 404 (offering gone), 409 (slot full / temple unverified / puja inactive) and 500. Users retry in a loop, then email support.
  - fix: Parse the body before composing the message: `const body = await res.json().catch(()=>null); setPaymentError(body?.error ?? body?.message ?? fallback)`, and for code==='SLOT_UNAVAILABLE' send the user back to the slot picker with the date preserved rather than telling them to retry. Copy the pattern already in checkout/offering/page.tsx:241.

### [RES-08] P2/resilience/S Inbox polls every 3s with no in-flight guard, no 5xx backoff and no timeout — it storms a failing API and stacks stalled sockets
  - where: apps/web/components/Inbox.tsx:53 (POLL_INTERVAL_MS=3000), :66-84 (fetchConvs: `if (!res.ok) return true` = keep polling), :95-101 and :289 (two independent setInterval loops)
  - evidence: With every request held open and never answered, /messages fired 12 API calls in 37 seconds while the UI sat on "Loading inbox..." — a new request every 3s with the previous ones still pending, and no request is ever aborted. The 401/403 case IS guarded (authFailCount stops after 3, added for issue #220) but 5xx explicitly returns true to keep polling, and there is no exponential backoff. Opening a thread starts a second 3s interval (:289), so an active conversation is 40 req/min per tab.
  - customer_impact: During a backend incident every open Messages tab adds 20-40 requests/minute of pure load to an already failing API, and Chrome's 6-connections-per-host limit means the tab eventually cannot issue any request at all. The user sees a spinner and the platform's own client amplifies the outage.
  - fix: Add an in-flight ref so a new poll is skipped while one is pending; wrap the fetch in AbortSignal.timeout(10000); and on repeated 5xx apply exponential backoff (3s → 6s → 12s → 60s cap) with a visible "Reconnecting…" chip, resetting on the first success. Same treatment for both intervals.

### [RES-09] P2/comms/S Raw developer error text is rendered to users; the friendly-error helper exists but is wired into only 2 surfaces
  - where: apps/web/app/bookings/page.tsx:196-205 and :251-255; apps/web/lib/friendly-error.ts (imported only by app/admin/ratings/page.tsx and app/admin/leads/page.tsx)
  - evidence: /bookings with a 500 rendered: "Could not load your bookings: API 500: {\"error\":\"Internal Server Error\"}". /bookings with a malformed body (HTML served as application/json) rendered: "Could not load your bookings: Unexpected token '<', \"<html>502 \"... is not valid JSON" — a JavaScript parser message shown to a devotee — and simultaneously displayed "All 0 / No bookings yet / Explore our pujas to book your first sacred ritual", contradicting its own error line. friendlyApiError() in lib/friendly-error.ts already maps 5xx to "Something went wrong on our side. Please try again in a moment." and network failures to "Could not reach the server…", and a grep across apps/web shows exactly 2 consumers, both under /admin.
  - customer_impact: Devotees see JSON blobs and JS syntax errors, which reads as broken software rather than a temporary outage, and the contradictory "no bookings yet" empty state next to the error makes them think their bookings were deleted. Neither state offers a retry.
  - fix: Route every user-visible error string through friendlyApiError() (it already exists and is tested) and suppress the empty-state block whenever an error is present — show the error plus a Retry button instead. Start with /bookings, /bookings/view, /dashboard, and the six portals.

### [RES-10] P2/resilience/S Sending a message and starting a call fail completely silently
  - where: apps/web/components/Inbox.tsx:315-336 (send) and :338-348 (startCall)
  - evidence: send() is `setSending(true); try { const res = await fetch(POST /conversations/:id/messages); if (res.ok) { setDraft(''); … } } finally { setSending(false); }` — there is no `else` and no `catch`. On a 500 the draft stays in the box, no error renders, the button re-enables, and the message is never delivered; on a network failure the fetch rejects and, because send() is called straight from onClick with no .catch, it becomes an unhandled promise rejection. startCall() is `if (!res.ok) return;` — pressing the call button during an outage does literally nothing visible.
  - customer_impact: A devotee messaging her pandit about tomorrow's puja presses Send, the text stays in the box looking like it did not submit, she presses Send again, and nothing is ever delivered. There is no failure indicator and no queued retry, so the conversation silently dies at the worst moment.
  - fix: Add an else/catch that sets an error state and renders a per-message "Not sent — tap to retry" affordance keeping the draft; do the same for startCall. Wrap both in the timeout helper from RES-01.

### [RES-11] P2/resilience/S "Go on duty" / "Go online" toggles fail silently, so a provider believes they are accepting work when they are not
  - where: apps/web/app/priest/page.tsx:113-125 (toggleDuty) and apps/web/app/astrologer-portal/page.tsx:69-81 (toggleOnline)
  - evidence: Both are `setSaving(true); try { const res = await fetch(PATCH/POST …); if (res.ok) setOnDuty(next); } catch { /* keep prior state */ } setSaving(false);`. On any non-2xx or network failure the switch reverts to its previous position with no message. Under my 500 injection both portals rendered the prompt "Go on duty for instant bookings — Turn on to receive instant puja requests near you (Uber-style dispatch)" and "Go online to take consultations" with the toggle off and no error.
  - customer_impact: The T1 instant-dispatch pool and the astrologer live directory are driven by this flag. A pandit flips the switch, sees it snap back or assumes a rendering lag, walks away believing he is on duty, and receives no instant bookings all day — lost revenue for him and unfilled T1 demand for the platform, with nothing in the UI to diagnose.
  - fix: On failure, set an inline error next to the toggle ("Couldn't go on duty — tap to retry") and keep the switch visibly in the off position with the error attached, rather than silently reverting. Add the request timeout from RES-01 so a stalled PATCH also lands in that branch.

### [RES-12] P2/resilience/S A negative page parameter returns HTTP 500 on three public list endpoints
  - where: apps/api list handlers for GET /api/v1/temples, /api/v1/pujas/types, /api/v1/content (pagination skip computed from an unvalidated page)
  - evidence: Direct API probes: GET /api/v1/temples?page=-1 → 500 {"error":"Failed to fetch temples"}; GET /api/v1/pujas/types?page=-1 → 500 {"error":"Failed to fetch puja types"}; GET /api/v1/content?page=-1 → 500 {"error":"Failed to fetch content"}. Neighbouring inputs are handled correctly, which isolates the cause to a negative skip: ?page=0 → 200, ?page=abc → 200, ?limit=-5 → 200, ?limit=0 → 200, and /providers/pandit?page=-1 → 200 (returns an empty list). Every other hardening probe passed: empty body → 400 FST_ERR_CTP_EMPTY_JSON_BODY, invalid JSON → 400 FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_JSON_BODY, null templeOfferingId → 400 zod Validation Error, no auth → 401, bad token → 401.
  - customer_impact: Any crawler, scanner, or hand-edited URL with page=-1 gets a 500 on three of the most-indexed public endpoints. It pollutes error monitoring during real incidents (a 500 that is actually a client input problem) and, if Google or a partner integration hits it, signals server instability on catalogue pages that are meant to be the SEO surface.
  - fix: Coerce and clamp pagination at the zod boundary the same way limit already is: `page: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).default(1)`, `limit: z.coerce.number().int().min(1).max(100).default(20)`, and return 400 rather than 500 on anything that still fails. Apply to all three handlers; providers/pandit already tolerates it and can adopt the same clamp for consistency.

### [RES-13] P3/resilience/S Offline fallback is inconsistent across routes, and nothing recovers when the connection returns
  - where: apps/web/public/sw.js:47-68 (navigate-only fetch handler, cache contains only /offline); no online-event listener anywhere in apps/web
  - evidence: The service worker does register (1 registration, scope http://127.0.0.1:3000/) and the offline page works on two of three paths: a hard navigation to a never-visited /pujas/home-puja while offline rendered "You are offline — Darshya needs an internet connection…", and an in-app link click to /ask-pandit while offline also rendered it. But a subsequent offline navigation to /temples produced a completely blank page (main innerText = "", offline page not shown). Cache inspection shows caches = {darshya-offline-v1: ['/offline']} — only the offline shell is ever cached, so sw.js's `cache.match(req)` branch ("serve cached page if any") can never hit and no previously-visited page works offline. After restoring connectivity and waiting 10s the page was still blank: a grep for addEventListener('online'), navigator.onLine and window.ononline across apps/web returns zero matches, so nothing re-fetches until the user manually reloads.
  - customer_impact: On a metro or lift with intermittent 4G, some navigations give the reassuring offline page and some give a white screen; and when signal returns the white screen stays white until the user thinks to pull-to-refresh. Users read a blank page as "the app is broken", not "I have no signal".
  - fix: Add a window 'online' listener in the root layout (or in offline/refresh-button.tsx) that calls router.refresh() / location.reload() when connectivity returns, plus a persistent "You're offline" banner driven by navigator.onLine. In sw.js, either start populating the runtime cache on successful navigations (so the cached-page branch is real) or delete that dead branch, and make sure the offline fallback returns for every navigate request including ones initiated from an already-SW-served page.

## AREA: data-integrity   score=55

### [DI-01] P0/data-integrity/S Full refund via POST /payments/:id/refund never reverses the commission or temple share — the money stays withdrawable
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:1254-1295 (processRefund transaction) vs the correct sibling at apps/api/src/services/refund.service.ts:284-315
  - evidence: Drove it end to end on booking BS-20260812-J3M76 (priest-direct path, paid ₹1,151). Ledger after payment: commission ₹770.00 PENDING (payout_request_id NULL), temple_payout ₹55.00 pending. Then POST /api/v1/payments/8add2b95-.../refund {fullRefund:true} -> HTTP 200, refundId rfnd_demo_1786549590433, payments.status='refunded', payments.refund_amount=1151.00, puja_bookings.status='refunded'. Ledger AFTER refund, by direct SQL: commission STILL ₹770.00 PENDING, temple_payout STILL ₹55.00 pending — byte-identical. GET /payouts/balance then returned {"availableInr":770}. POST /payouts/request returned 201 with PayoutRequest e5505442-c652-4a98-a387-db0ad456bc69 for ₹770, and the linked commission query confirms its only row is booking_number BS-20260812-J3M76 with booking status 'refunded'. The sibling rail refund.service.ts:284-315 performs exactly the reversal that is missing here (commission.updateMany -> REVERSED, templePayout.updateMany -> reversed), so the correct code already exists in the repo — it is simply not called on this route.
  - customer_impact: Every admin refund taken through the payments route pays out twice: the devotee is made whole and the pandit keeps 100% of an earning for a puja that generated zero revenue. On this ₹1,151 booking that is ₹1,151 refunded + ₹770 withdrawable + ₹55 temple share = ₹1,976 lost per refund. It is silent — no warning, no reconciliation flag — and it scales with refund volume, which is highest exactly when something has gone wrong operationally.
  - fix: Extract the RV-02 reversal block from refund.service.ts:284-315 into a shared helper (e.g. reverseBookingLedger(tx, bookingId)) and call it from inside the payment.service.ts:1254 transaction on the isFullRefund branch. Both rails must unwind identically; the reason two rails diverged is that the reversal was added to one file and never backported.

### [DI-02] P1/data-integrity/M Partial refunds reverse nothing on either rail, and the cancellation ladder makes partial the normal case
  - where: apps/api/src/services/refund.service.ts:280-315 (guarded by `if (refundType === 'full')`) and apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:1272-1294 (no reversal in either branch)
  - evidence: Both reversal paths are gated on a full refund. refund.service.ts:280-283 documents the deferral explicitly: partial refunds leave the ledger untouched by design. Meanwhile the cancellation engine at apps/api/src/services/cancellation.service.ts:95-126 (computeDevoteeRefund) picks a tier fraction from the policy ladder, so a devotee cancelling inside the window is refunded a FRACTION — that is the designed common path, not an edge case. payment.service.ts:1230 computes isFullRefund = refundAmount.plus(alreadyRefunded).gte(paid), so any ladder tier below 100% takes the else branch at :1281 which touches only cancellationReason.
  - customer_impact: On a 75%-tier cancellation of a ₹1,100 puja the devotee gets ₹825 back while the pandit keeps the full ₹770 commission and the temple its ₹55 — the platform collects ₹275 and pays out ₹825. Every mid-ladder cancellation is loss-making, and the ladder tiers are the marketing promise (100/75/50/25/0), so this is the expected flow rather than a rare one.
  - fix: Reverse proportionally: scale the commission and temple payout by the same fraction actually refunded (refundAmount / paid), writing a REVERSED row for the clawed-back portion rather than mutating the original. If proportional unwind needs an owner policy decision, at minimum flag the booking for manual finance review the way the alreadySettled branch at refund.service.ts:292-301 already does, so the loss is visible instead of silent.

### [DI-03] P1/data-integrity/S Admin settlement screen overstates what a pandit is owed by 7.6% using a legacy 0.75 multiplier on gross price
  - where: apps/api/src/routes/admin-bookings.ts:66-71 (payoutMultiplier) feeding GET /admin/bookings/payouts/pending
  - evidence: Direct SQL over priest 6a56eba6 across the 42 bookings in payable statuses: sum(price_amount)=₹46,404.00, authoritative sum(priest_payout)=₹32,340.00, screen formula price*0.75=₹34,803.00 — an overstatement of ₹2,463.00 (+7.6%). Two independent errors compound: (a) the multiplier is 0.75 (a legacy 25% take) while the product doctrine and the priest's own commission_rate produce 0.70-0.80, and (b) it multiplies price_amount, which includes the ₹51 platform fee and ₹150 prasad-delivery fee that apps/api/src/routes/bookings.ts:1955-1957 explicitly documents as platform revenue and pass-through, NOT part of the split. The booking row already carries the correct answer in the priest_payout column.
  - customer_impact: Settlement is a manual bank/UPI transfer driven off this screen, so the inflated figure is what an admin actually pays. At the measured rate the platform overpays 7.6% on every priest settlement made from this list, and the number an admin quotes a pandit will not match what the pandit's own portal shows, generating disputes.
  - fix: Sum the priest_payout column instead of recomputing from price_amount — the split is already resolved per-booking at creation time honouring each pandit's negotiated commission_rate. Delete payoutMultiplier entirely rather than correcting the constant; any recomputation here will drift from booking.service again.

### [DI-04] P1/data-integrity/M The same pandit appears twice on the admin payout screen under two different provider ids
  - where: GET /api/v1/admin/bookings/payouts/pending — apps/api/src/routes/admin-bookings.ts (group assembly)
  - evidence: Live response groups include BOTH: {providerId: 6a56eba6-e340-40bc-b236-97440ada4001, providerName: 'Demo Pandit', role: 'PRIEST', bookingsCount: 13, totalPayout: 10763.25} and {providerId: b61f02c9-b795-4e2e-a1e8-1f10660520b9, providerName: 'Demo Pandit', role: 'PANDIT', bookingsCount: 1, totalPayout: 770}. SQL confirms these are one human: 6a56eba6 is priests.id and b61f02c9 is the users.id it joins to — both resolve to pandit@demo.com / 'Demo Pandit'. The first row is keyed on the PriestPayout sidecar rail, the second on the Commission ledger rail. I did verify the rails are disjoint per booking (join of priest_payouts to commissions on booking_id returned 0 rows), so this is not an automatic double-count — but nothing on the screen says so.
  - customer_impact: Settlement is manual. An admin working this queue sees two 'Demo Pandit' rows with different totals and no indication they are the same person or that the two rails are mutually exclusive; paying both is a natural mistake and transfers the same provider twice. The embedded schemaNote explaining the two rails is a free-text field on the response, not on the row.
  - fix: Group by the canonical User.id and resolve Priest.id to its userId before aggregating, emitting one row per human with a per-rail breakdown. If the rails must stay visually separate, label the row with the rail and show the shared identity so an admin can see it is one payee.

### [DI-05] P1/comms/M A pandit paid through the temple booking path sees ₹0 in their own payout portal while being genuinely owed money
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:232-249 (writePujaCommissionOnConfirm returns early when booking.priestId is null) vs GET /payouts/balance and /payouts/commissions in apps/api/src/routes/payout-requests.ts
  - evidence: Ran the full temple-path lifecycle: POST /bookings/create-order -> POST /bookings/verify-payment (HTTP 200, payment captured, booking 'confirmed') -> PATCH priest_assigned -> in_progress -> completed, all 200. At every step direct SQL showed commissions=0 for that booking while puja_bookings.priest_payout read ₹770.00 and a TemplePayout of ₹55.00 WAS written. The pandit's own surfaces then returned GET /payouts/balance {availableInr: 0, pendingInr: 0, paidOutInr: 0} and GET /payouts/commissions {data: [], total: 0}. Cause is structural: no priest is assigned at payment time on the temple path, so the commission writer returns early, and nothing re-runs it when the priest is later attached. The priest-direct path behaves correctly — the same test via POST /bookings/from-offering minted a ₹770.00 PENDING commission on every one of 3 bookings. Four surfaces report four different numbers for this one pandit: /payouts/balance ₹770 available, /payouts/pandit/mine ₹2,310 pending (3 PriestPayout rows), booking columns ₹10,010 owed across completed/delivered/video_uploaded, and the admin screen ₹10,763.25.
  - customer_impact: A pandit who completed pujas booked through a temple opens the payout page the product tells them to use and sees zero. The money is recoverable only through the legacy admin-only PriestPayout rail, which they cannot see or trigger. This is the single most trust-destroying screen in a marketplace, and it is the default experience for temple-anchored bookings.
  - fix: Either mint the Commission when the priest is attached (call writePujaCommissionOnConfirm from the priestId-assignment paths, which is safe because it is already idempotent on bookingId), or surface the PriestPayout rail inside /payouts/balance so one endpoint answers 'what am I owed' across both rails. Do not leave two provider-facing rails where only one is discoverable.

### [DI-06] P2/data-integrity/S Zero UNIQUE constraints on any money table — every idempotency guard is an application-level TOCTOU
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:252-256 and apps/api/src/services/temple-payout.service.ts:74-79 (findFirst-then-create); schema has no backing index
  - evidence: Queried pg_constraint for contype in ('u','x') across puja_bookings, commissions, temple_payouts, payout_requests, payments and store_orders: the result set is EMPTY — not one unique or exclusion constraint. Both ledger writers guard with findFirst-then-create; temple-payout.service.ts:74 even carries a comment acknowledging the missing index. Importantly, I could NOT force a duplicate: 6 concurrent verify-payment calls, 3 sequential replays, and 6 rounds of a deliberate verify-payment-vs-PATCH-confirmed race all yielded exactly 1 commission and 1 temple payout, and a global check found 0 bookings with duplicate commissions and 0 with duplicate temple payouts. The reason it holds is incidental: each transaction's FIRST statement takes a row lock (the Payment row in verifyPayment, the PujaBooking row in updateBookingStatus), so racers serialize and the loser re-reads the committed row under READ COMMITTED.
  - customer_impact: No customer impact observed today — this is a latent hazard, reported as such. The protection is an emergent property of statement ordering, not a declared invariant. Any future writer that reaches the ledger without first locking the same Payment or Booking row (a webhook handler, a settlement cron, a bulk backfill) reopens the window and mints duplicate earnings with nothing at the database layer to stop it.
  - fix: CREATE UNIQUE INDEX CONCURRENTLY on commissions(booking_id) WHERE booking_id IS NOT NULL, and on temple_payouts(booking_id). Note the repo's documented Prisma hazard: migrate deploy wraps each migration in a transaction and CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY fails with 25001 inside one — apply via $executeRawUnsafe and migrate resolve, per docs/. Then let the create fail on conflict instead of pre-checking.

### [DI-07] P2/data-integrity/M commissions.booking_id is text pointing at a uuid primary key, with no index, no foreign key, and 2 existing orphans
  - where: commissions.booking_id column definition; hot lookup at apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:252
  - evidence: information_schema: commissions.booking_id is data_type 'text' while puja_bookings.id is 'uuid' — every join needs an explicit cast (my queries failed with 'operator does not exist: uuid = text' until cast). pg_indexes on commissions lists payout_request_id, provider_id, status, store_order_id and wellness_enrollment_id — there is NO index on booking_id, the column the idempotency guard queries on every single payment confirm. EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) of that exact lookup returns 'Seq Scan on commissions  Filter: (booking_id = '...'::text)'. pg_constraint on commissions shows a foreign key only on payout_request_id, so booking_id has no referential integrity; a query for commissions whose booking_id matches no puja_booking already returns 2 rows (they point at consultation bookings — the column is overloaded across booking domains).
  - customer_impact: Two compounding problems. Performance: the confirm path does a full table scan of the commission ledger on every payment, which is O(ledger size) and degrades as revenue grows — trivial at 14 rows, material at 100k. Integrity: with no FK and a type mismatch, a deleted or mistyped booking leaves an orphan earning that still counts toward a provider's withdrawable balance, and the column silently means different things for different booking kinds.
  - fix: Migrate commissions.booking_id to uuid with a real FK to puja_bookings(id), or — if it must stay polymorphic across puja/consultation bookings — add an explicit booking_kind discriminator and a partial index per kind. Either way add the index on booking_id; that is a one-line win independent of the type decision.

### [DI-08] P2/comms/S /admin/revenue and /admin/commissions report figures 5.8x apart, both labelled commissions
  - where: GET /api/v1/admin/revenue (apps/api/src/routes/admin.ts:257) vs GET /api/v1/admin/commissions
  - evidence: Same instant, same admin token: /admin/revenue returned totalCommissions 1142.91 while /admin/commissions returned total=8 rows summing to 6580, which matches SQL sum(commissions.amount)=6580.00 exactly. The two measure different quantities — platform take rate versus provider earning, per the documented convention that Commission.amount is the PROVIDER's earning and Commission.percentage is the PLATFORM's rate — but both are surfaced to an admin under the word 'commissions' with no unit label. The neighbouring figure is correct and verifiable: /admin/revenue pendingPayouts 4270 matches SQL sum of PENDING commissions 4270.00 to the paisa.
  - customer_impact: An admin reconciling the revenue dashboard against the commission ledger sees ₹1,142.91 versus ₹6,580 and cannot tell whether the platform is under-collecting, the ledger is corrupt, or the labels differ. It undermines confidence in every other number on the page and makes real discrepancies (like DI-01) harder to spot against the noise.
  - fix: Rename the fields to what they measure — platformTakeInr versus providerEarningsInr — and show both on each screen so the relationship is visible rather than inferred. The underlying maths is already correct.

### [DI-09] P3/data-integrity/S Replayed verify-payment silently overwrites gatewayPaymentId, repointing any future refund
  - where: apps/api/src/services/payment.service.ts:596-685 (verifyPayment unconditionally writes gatewayPaymentId)
  - evidence: Sent two verify-payment calls for the same booking with DIFFERENT razorpayPaymentIds, each correctly signed. Both returned HTTP 200. Direct SQL then showed a single Payment row whose gateway_payment_id had been overwritten from pay_probe_alt_1_... to pay_probe_alt_2_... — the first id is gone. The update at payment.service.ts:652-657 sets gatewayPaymentId with no guard on whether the payment is already captured.
  - customer_impact: Low likelihood because forging the second call requires the Razorpay secret, but the blast radius is real: refund.service.ts:185 issues refunds against captured.gatewayPaymentId, so after an overwrite a refund targets whichever id was seen last rather than the one that actually captured. A legitimate gateway retry carrying a new payment id is enough to trigger it, and the audit trail of which payment settled the booking is destroyed.
  - fix: Only set gatewayPaymentId when the payment is not already captured; if a captured payment presents a different id, reject and log for reconciliation rather than overwriting.

### [DI-10] P3/ux-mindset/S Repeat payout completion returns a success indistinguishable from the first, on a manual-transfer flow
  - where: POST /api/v1/admin/bookings/payout-requests/:id/process -> apps/api/src/services/payout.service.ts:349-457
  - evidence: Fired 5 concurrent completes on PayoutRequest c0a1b627 (₹2,310): all 5 returned HTTP 200 with identical bodies. A subsequent sequential re-complete also returned 200. The ledger is correct — exactly ₹2,310 flipped to PAID once, /payouts/balance shows paidOutInr 2310, and no double-pay occurred; the already-completed short-circuit at payout.service.ts works and the reference field kept its original value rather than being overwritten.
  - customer_impact: The ledger is safe but the human is not informed. Because the actual bank/UPI transfer happens outside the system, an admin who clicks Complete twice gets two identical success responses and has no signal that the first click already recorded the transfer — inviting a second real-world payment that the software will never know about.
  - fix: Return a distinguishable response for the no-op case (e.g. 200 with {alreadyCompleted: true, processedAt, reference}) so the UI can say 'already settled on <date> ref <x>' instead of 'Payout completed'. The `changed` flag is already computed in the transaction; just surface it.

## AREA: seo-perf-budget   score=18

### [SEO-01] P0/seo/S 349 indexable pages canonicalise to the homepage (inherited root canonical)
  - where: apps/web/app/layout.tsx:113 — `alternates: { canonical: "https://darshya.online", ... }`
  - evidence: Next.js inherits parent `alternates.canonical` into any child route that does not set its own. Scanning all 1,785 prerendered HTML files in apps/web/.next/server/app (Aug-7 prod build, BUILD_ID 6LOMT7MOslGKj3JHXa64e): 363 emit `<link rel="canonical" href="https://darshya.online">`; excluding the 14 noindex pages and index.html itself, 349 are indexable and self-declare as the homepage. Confirmed live: `/pujas` returns title "Book Online Pujas at Verified Indian Temples | Darshya" + a unique 220-char description but `<link rel="canonical" href="https://darshya.online">`. Same for /temples (prod temples.html line-level match). Source-side: 56 page.tsx files export metadata with no `canonical` key; mapping the sitemap onto route templates, 49 sitemap-advertised URLs are affected, including both priority-1.0 commercial hubs (/pujas, /temples), plus /darshan, /astrology, /wellness, /panchang, /donations, /gurukuls, /yatra and 9 /yatra/[slug] pages, and every supply-acquisition page (/become-priest, /list-temple, /become-astrologer, /list-gaushala, /become-gurukul). The full prod set also includes 30+ /content/* pages (hanuman-chalisa, gayatri-mantra, shiv-tandav-stotram, ...).
  - customer_impact: Google treats a self-referencing-to-homepage canonical as an explicit instruction to drop the URL and consolidate its signals into the homepage. Every one of these pages is a de-indexation candidate. /pujas and /temples are the two pages the entire acquisition funnel depends on: 'book puja online', 'online puja booking temple' etc. can never rank because the page hands its equity to a homepage that does not target those queries. The supply-side pages (/become-priest, /list-temple) are how pandits and temples find the platform — also invisible.
  - fix: Delete `alternates.canonical` from the root metadata in apps/web/app/layout.tsx (keep `languages`). A missing canonical is far safer than a wrong one — Google self-canonicalises by default. Then add an explicit `alternates: { canonical: ... }` to each of the 56 page.tsx files that lack one; the 183 files that already set it are the template to copy. Add a build-time assertion over .next/server/app/**/*.html that fails CI if any indexable page's canonical !== its own URL.

### [SEO-02] P0/seo/S robots.txt blocks /_next/ — Googlebot cannot fetch any CSS, JS or font on the site
  - where: apps/web/app/robots.ts:64 — `'/_next/'` in the disallow array
  - evidence: `curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/robots.txt` returns `Disallow: /_next/`. Every stylesheet, script and font the site serves lives under that prefix — the production homepage head references `/_next/static/css/5c7e0bfe58e74166.css`, `/_next/static/css/8aaad888183048fd.css`, 14 `/_next/static/chunks/*.js` files, and all 28 next/font woff2 files under `/_next/static/media/`. Grepping the served homepage HTML for asset prefixes returns exactly two: `/_next/static/chunks/` and `/_next/static/media/`. Nothing is served from any other path.
  - customer_impact: Googlebot renders every page as unstyled, unhydrated HTML. Mobile-friendly evaluation fails (no viewport CSS applied), layout-dependent signals are wrong, and any content that only appears after client hydration is invisible. This compounds SEO-16: Next 15 streaming metadata puts <title> at byte offset 206,042 of the homepage body, and Google is the one crawler deliberately given the streamed (not blocking) variant — so Google is being asked to render a page whose renderer it has been forbidden from loading.
  - fix: Remove '/_next/' from the disallow list in apps/web/app/robots.ts. If the intent was to keep RSC/data payloads out of the index, disallow the narrow paths only (`/_next/data/`, `/_next/image?`) and never `/_next/static/`. Google's own guidance is explicit that blocking CSS/JS causes suboptimal rendering and indexing.

### [PERF-01] P0/seo/M next/image optimization disabled + full-resolution Wikimedia originals hotlinked: 20.85 MB of images on /pujas at 375px
  - where: apps/web/next.config.ts:26 `unoptimized: true`; image URLs from apps/web/lib/images.ts
  - evidence: Measured via CDP Network.loadingFinished encodedDataLength (real transferred bytes; image bytes are identical in dev and prod because they are absolute third-party URLs). Desktop 1280x720: / = 54 requests / 20.01 MB of which Image 15 req / 14,953 KB. /pujas = 48 req / 30.77 MB of which Image 11 req / 25,799 KB. /temples = 68 req / 11.47 MB, Image 30 req / 6,056 KB. /temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi = 45 req / 8.30 MB, Image 6 req / 2,645 KB. Single largest asset anywhere: https://upload.wikimedia.org/.../Pinda_Daan_-_Jagannath_Ghat_-_Kolkata_2012-10-15_0701.JPG = 14,915 KB, natural 6016x4000, displayed 387x242. Homepage runner-up: Rudraksha_mala.jpg = 5,594 KB, natural 4000x2250, displayed 256x256. Mobile 375x812 run is essentially identical because there is no srcset and no `sizes`: / = 12.72 MB across 15 images with 14/15 wider than 2x their display slot; /pujas = 20.85 MB across 11 images, 6/10 oversized; /temples = 4.76 MB across 30 images, 32/33 oversized. Aggregate oversize waste measured per page: / 14,317 KB, /pujas 24,853 KB, /temples 6,397 KB, /temples/[slug] 3,459 KB. All 1,785 prerendered prod pages reference upload.wikimedia.org.
  - customer_impact: LCP cannot pass on any image-bearing page. A user in Gurugram on a typical 4G connection (~2 Mbps effective) waits ~80 seconds for /pujas to finish loading images, and pays roughly 20 MB of a metered data plan to look at a puja catalogue. This is the page the entire paid-acquisition plan lands on. It also silently outsources availability and cost to Wikimedia — a host that rate-limits and can block hotlinkers, and whose licence terms generally require visible attribution that is not present.
  - fix: Three steps, in order. (1) Set `unoptimized: false` in next.config.ts and give every next/image a `sizes` prop so Next emits a srcset — the config comment claims remote images 'are already served optimized from Supabase/CloudFront', which the measurements disprove: 100% of them are raw Wikimedia originals. (2) Stop hotlinking: run a one-time script that pulls each distinct Wikimedia image, resizes to the largest slot actually used (256, 400, 800, 1600), converts to AVIF+WebP, and uploads to Supabase Storage; rewrite lib/images.ts to point at those. (3) Until (2) lands, cheapest possible mitigation with zero infra: every Wikimedia URL supports a `/thumb/.../NNNpx-` form — the codebase already uses it for some images (960px on /temples) and not others. Rewriting the ~12 raw-original URLs to `/thumb/.../800px-` cuts /pujas from 25.8 MB to under 1.5 MB in a single find-and-replace.

### [PERF-02] P1/seo/S Every page head preloads 1,718 KB of third-party images, two of the three below the fold and one on pages with zero images
  - where: apps/web/app/layout.tsx:281-287 (global `<link rel="preload" as="image" fetchPriority="high">` on getTempleHero('kashi-vishwanath')); plus 2 page-level preloads on /
  - evidence: Production index.html head contains three `<link rel="preload" as="image">`: Golden Temple 1920px (measured 810 KB), Mahakaleshwar 1920px (638 KB), and Kashi Vishwanath 1920px at fetchPriority="high" (270 KB) = 1,718 KB preloaded before CSS. Measured positions on the live homepage: the Mahakaleshwar image sits at topAbs=1784 with a 720px fold — 1,064 px below the fold. The high-priority Kashi image is used only at topAbs=4614 in a 258x144 thumbnail. The layout-level preload fires on every route: on /pujas/rudrabhishek the page renders 0 `<img>` tags yet still transfers exactly one image — the 270 KB Kashi preload — which is never painted. The one genuinely above-fold image (Golden Temple, topAbs=201) is 1920x1280 natural displayed at 526x160.
  - customer_impact: fetchPriority=high on a below-fold/unused image directly steals bandwidth and connection slots from the real LCP element during the most contended moment of page load, making LCP worse than doing nothing. On every non-homepage route it is 270 KB of pure waste per uncached visit.
  - fix: Remove the global preload from layout.tsx entirely — a layout cannot know what the LCP element of an arbitrary route is. Move the preload into app/page.tsx only, pointing at the one image that is actually above the fold, sized to its 526x160 slot. Delete the Mahakaleshwar preload (below fold). Add `priority` to the single above-fold next/image instead of hand-rolling link tags.

### [PERF-03] P1/resilience/M Brand webfonts have been dead for some time: Fontshare API returns 'temporarily restricted', 5,545 of 5,604 measured glyphs paint in Arial while 201.9 KB of unused Google woff2 still downloads
  - where: apps/web/app/layout.tsx:276-279 (Fontshare stylesheet link); apps/web/app/globals.css:189-190 (`--font-display`, `--font-body`); layout.tsx:57-77 (three next/font/google families)
  - evidence: `curl 'https://api.fontshare.com/v2/css?f[]=cabinet-grotesk@500,700&f[]=satoshi@400,500,700&display=swap'` returns HTTP 200 with a 162-byte body whose entire content is the comment 'Access to the Fontshare API has been temporarily restricted.' No @font-face rules are served. globals.css sets `--font-body: 'Satoshi','Inter',sans-serif` and `--font-display: 'Cabinet Grotesk','Helvetica Neue',sans-serif`; neither Satoshi nor Inter is loaded by any other means. Measured with CDP CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode across 352 text-bearing nodes on the homepage: 5,545 glyphs Arial (system), 58 glyphs Nirmala UI (system), 1 glyph Georgia (system), 0 glyphs from any webfont. Body computed font-family resolves to Arial (ArialMT), h1 to Arial-BoldMT. Separately, next/font/google loads Noto Sans (5 weights), Noto Sans Devanagari (4) and Crimson Pro (4) generating 28 woff2 files, of which 3 transfer on the homepage (47.3 + 35.3 + 118.6 = 201.9 KB) — but their CSS variables (--font-noto-sans etc.) are never referenced by --font-body/--font-display, and document.fonts reports 0 of 71 registered faces in 'loaded' state. Production head contains zero `<link rel="preload" as="font">`.
  - customer_impact: The product does not look like the brand — every page renders in Arial, on a devotional app whose entire differentiation is warmth and craft. The team almost certainly does not know, because it degrades silently. Meanwhile users pay 201.9 KB of font bytes that render nothing, plus a render-blocking cross-origin round trip to api.fontshare.com in <head> that delays first paint on every single page load for a response containing one comment.
  - fix: Self-host. Download Satoshi and Cabinet Grotesk woff2 once, drop them in public/fonts, declare them via next/font/local, and delete both the api.fontshare.com stylesheet link and the api/cdn.fontshare.com preconnects. Either wire --font-body/--font-display to the next/font variables (`var(--font-noto-sans)`) or drop the three Google families entirely — as configured they are 201.9 KB that paints nothing. Add a Playwright assertion that CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode on body reports isCustomFont:true, so this can never regress silently again.

### [SEO-03] P1/seo/M 1,043 production pages render a doubled site name: 'Vishnu Aarti ... — Darshya | Darshya'
  - where: apps/web/app/layout.tsx:88 `template: "%s | Darshya"` colliding with 61 route templates that already hard-code the site name in `title`
  - evidence: Scanning all 1,785 prerendered prod HTML files: 1,043 titles contain 'Darshya | Darshya'. Production example, .next/server/app/aarti/vishnu-aarti.html: `<title>Vishnu Aarti (Jai Jagdish Hare Vishnu) Lyrics — Hindi + English + Meaning — Darshya | Darshya</title>` (93 chars). Confirmed live on 12 of 15 randomly sampled sitemap URLs. Source: 61 page.tsx files write a `title` string ending in 'Darshya' — e.g. aarti/[slug]/page.tsx:24 `` `${a.name} Lyrics — Hindi + English + Meaning — Darshya` ``, chalisa/[slug]/page.tsx:24, chants/[slug]/page.tsx:25. Mapping to the sitemap, 1,108 of 1,584 advertised URLs (70%) sit on such a route. Related: 1,308 of 1,785 titles exceed 60 characters, and 1,159 of 1,785 descriptions exceed 160 characters.
  - customer_impact: Every SERP listing wastes ~10 characters on a repeated brand name inside an already-truncated title, so the differentiating tail of the title ('Meaning', 'Benefits', 'Vidhi') is cut off in Google's ~60-char display. Lower CTR on the 1,100 long-tail content pages that are supposed to be the organic acquisition engine.
  - fix: Strip the trailing ' — Darshya' / ' | Darshya' from the 61 page.tsx title strings and let the root template append it once. Then rewrite the templates so the informative part fits in 60 chars before the suffix. Add a CI check over .next/server/app/**/*.html asserting no title matches /Darshya.*Darshya/ and that title length <= 60.

### [SEO-04] P1/seo/M 1,652 pages emit the homepage's Twitter card; 158 sitemap URLs emit the homepage's Open Graph card including og:url
  - where: apps/web/app/layout.tsx:120-137 (root openGraph/twitter blocks, inherited); 158 sitemap URLs on routes that set no openGraph, 1,456 on routes that set no twitter
  - evidence: Next.js inherits the parent `twitter` block whenever a page does not set one — it does not derive it from the page's own openGraph. Production .next/server/app/aarti/vishnu-aarti.html has correct `og:title=Vishnu Aarti (Jai Jagdish Hare Vishnu) — जय जगदीश हरे विष्णु` and `og:url=https://darshya.online/aarti/vishnu-aarti`, but `twitter:title=Darshya — India's Devotional Super App`, `twitter:description=Live darshan, puja booking, panchang...`, `twitter:image=.../golden-temple-og.jpg`. 1,652 of 1,785 prod pages have twitter:title equal to the homepage title. Worse for routes with no openGraph at all: prod temples.html and aarti.html emit `og:url=https://darshya.online` and `og:title=Darshya — India's Devotional Super App` — the OG card for /temples IS the homepage card. Source scan: 158 sitemap URLs on routes with no openGraph (incl. /temples, /pujas, /darshan, /astrology, /wellness, /panchang, /yatra/[slug] x9, /tithi/[slug] x30, /ekadashi/[slug] x24). Related: Organization JSON-LD on every page sets `"logo": "https://darshya.online/logo.png"` and /logo.png returns 404.
  - customer_impact: Devotional content spreads on WhatsApp and X, not Google. Someone sharing the Vishnu Aarti page to a family group gets a generic 'India's Devotional Super App' card with a Golden Temple photo — no indication of what was actually shared, so the share does not convert. Every X share of 1,652 pages is indistinguishable from every other. The broken /logo.png also denies Google a valid Organization logo for knowledge-panel eligibility.
  - fix: In layout.tsx, delete the root-level `twitter` block and set only `twitter: { card: 'summary_large_image' }` so pages inherit the card type but not the content; then add per-page twitter or, simpler, add a shared `buildMetadata()` helper that derives twitter from the page's own openGraph. Add openGraph to the 158 URLs' routes (start with /pujas and /temples). Ship a real /logo.png (or point the JSON-LD at the existing /favicon.svg rendered to PNG).

### [SEO-05] P1/data-integrity/S Product JSON-LD advertises a highPrice that is literally basePrice x 5 — a price no offer, page or checkout can honour
  - where: apps/web/app/pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:232 — `highPrice: Number(puja.basePrice) * 5`
  - evidence: Rendered JSON-LD on /pujas/rudrabhishek: `offers: { '@type':'AggregateOffer', priceCurrency:'INR', lowPrice:1100, highPrice:5500, offerCount:2, offers:[{price:1100,...Kashi Vishwanath},{price:1100,...Somnath}] }`. Both enumerated Offers are 1100. DB puja_types.base_price for rudrabhishek = 1100.00 and there is no 5500 anywhere. Scraping every rupee amount rendered in the /pujas/rudrabhishek HTML returns exactly one distinct value: ₹1,100. So the AggregateOffer range ₹1,100–₹5,500 has an upper bound that appears in no offer, on no page, and in no database row — it is `basePrice * 5`. Separately, each Offer `url` (https://darshya.online/pujas/rudrabhishek/book?temple=...) returns HTTP 307 to /pujas/book?slug=... rather than resolving directly. A second fabrication: apps/web/app/temples/[slug]/page.tsx:257 builds `aggregateRating` from an internal ops metric — `ratingValue: Math.min(5, ldTrustScore / 20), ratingCount: 1` — presenting a trust score as if it were a user review average.
