  - customer_impact: Google's structured-data policy requires prices in markup to match the price shown on the page. A rich result quoting 'from ₹1,100' up to ₹5,500 that the checkout cannot produce is both a manual-action risk (spammy structured markup) and a trust break for the first user who clicks a ₹5,500 expectation and lands on ₹1,100 — or vice versa if they were price-anchored. The synthetic aggregateRating carries the same manual-action risk and would be a plain misrepresentation to a devotee choosing a temple.
  - fix: Set `highPrice` to the real maximum of the enumerated offers: `Math.max(...puja.temples.map(t => Number(t.priceOverride || puja.basePrice)))`. When lowPrice === highPrice, emit a single `Offer` rather than an `AggregateOffer`. Point each Offer `url` at the final /pujas/book?slug=... target so it does not 307. Delete the trustScore-derived aggregateRating from temples/[slug]/page.tsx — only emit aggregateRating when it is computed from real rows in the `reviews` table, with the true ratingCount.

### [SEO-06] P1/seo/S Temple pages are reachable at two self-canonicalising URLs — short slug and long slug both return 200
  - where: apps/web/app/temples/[slug]/page.tsx; apps/web/app/sitemap.ts:44-52 (toCanonicalTempleSlug)
  - evidence: `/temples/kashi-vishwanath` → 200, 737,485 B, canonical=https://darshya.online/temples/kashi-vishwanath. `/temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi` → 200, 741,087 B, canonical=https://darshya.online/temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi. Both render the same temple; each declares itself canonical. The sitemap advertises only the short form (19 temple URLs, all short slugs) while the DB carries the long form (temples.slug = 'kashi-vishwanath-varanasi'), so internal links and any external link built from API data point at the un-advertised twin. Additional contradiction: on /temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi the PlaceOfWorship JSON-LD sets `"url": "https://darshya.online/temples/kashi-vishwanath"` while the page's own canonical says .../kashi-vishwanath-varanasi. Same pattern for /temples/tirupati-balaji (737 KB) vs /temples/tirumala-tirupati (360 KB).
  - customer_impact: Duplicate content across ~20 temple pages: link equity splits between two URLs, Google picks one arbitrarily and may pick the one not in the sitemap, and the structured data actively contradicts the canonical on the same page. Temple detail pages are the highest-intent local-search surface ('kashi vishwanath puja booking'); halving their authority for a slug bug is expensive.
  - fix: Pick the DB slug as the single source of truth. In temples/[slug]/page.tsx, if the incoming slug is an alias, `permanentRedirect()` (308) to the canonical slug rather than rendering. Derive the JSON-LD `url` from the same helper that builds the canonical so the two can never disagree. Then make sitemap.ts emit that same canonical slug and drop toCanonicalTempleSlug.

### [SEO-07] P2/seo/S Sitemap: 49 duplicate <loc> entries and a single build-time lastmod on all 1,633 URLs
  - where: apps/web/app/sitemap.ts (multiple generator arrays overlapping); /sitemap.xml
  - evidence: /sitemap.xml = 284,033 bytes, 1,633 <loc> elements, 1,584 unique — 49 URLs appear exactly twice (e.g. /deity/krishna, /deity/rama, /aarti/sai-baba-aarti, /chalisa/durga-chalisa, /vrat/chhath-vrat, /muhurat/guru-pushya-yoga, /tirth/somnath-jyotirlinga, /shlokas/gita-18-66 — full list captured). All 1,633 entries share ONE lastmod value: 2026-08-12T15:30:37.573Z (sitemap.ts:85 `const now = new Date()`), i.e. the render timestamp, not the content's modification date. All 1,633 carry changefreq and priority. No robots-disallowed path appears in the sitemap (that part is clean), and 15/15 sampled URLs returned HTTP 200 locally.
  - customer_impact: An always-now lastmod is a signal Google learns to ignore, and once ignored it cannot be used to prioritise recrawl of the pages that genuinely changed — so genuinely-updated panchang and festival pages recrawl no faster than static scripture pages. Duplicate <loc> entries are a validity warning in Search Console and waste crawl budget on a 1,600-URL site that has more URLs than it has authority to support.
  - fix: Dedupe with a `new Map(entries.map(e => [e.url, e]))` before returning from sitemap.ts. Set lastmod per entry from real data — content_items.updated_at, temples.updated_at, puja_types.updated_at — and omit lastmod entirely for hard-coded static pages rather than stamping them with build time. changefreq and priority can be dropped; Google ignores both.

### [SEO-08] P2/seo/S /feed.xml is advertised on every page and in every head but contains zero items
  - where: apps/web/app/feed.xml (route); apps/web/app/layout.tsx:301 `<link rel="alternate" type="application/rss+xml" ...>`
  - evidence: `curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/feed.xml` returns 576 bytes: a valid <rss><channel> with title, link, description, language, lastBuildDate and atom:link — and not a single <item> element. The `<link rel="alternate" type="application/rss+xml" title="Darshya RSS" href="https://darshya.online/feed.xml">` tag is in the root layout, so all 1,785 pages advertise it.
  - customer_impact: Feed readers, content aggregators and the Indian devotional-blog syndication network that would otherwise pick up daily panchang and festival content find an empty channel and stop polling. A zero-item feed is worse than no feed — it advertises a distribution channel and then delivers nothing, and aggregators that see an empty feed twice typically drop the source.
  - fix: Populate it from the same data the sitemap already reads: the 20 most recent content_items plus today's panchang and the next festival, as <item> entries with pubDate, guid and description. If the feed is not going to be maintained, remove the <link rel="alternate"> from layout.tsx rather than advertising an empty one.

### [PERF-04] P2/seo/S Images ship without width/height and the 404 page weighs 200-260 KB
  - where: apps/web/app/page.tsx (product/temple strips), apps/web/app/temples/[slug]/, apps/web/app/pujas/page.tsx; apps/web/app/not-found.tsx
  - evidence: Measured per page (attrW/attrH read off the live DOM): homepage 11 of 15 <img> have no width and no height attribute; /pujas 10 of 10; /temples/kashi-vishwanath-varanasi 10 of 10. /temples is the good case — 0 of 33 missing. Lazy-loading is mostly right: only 1 below-fold image on the homepage loads eagerly (Mahakaleshwar at topAbs=1784, and that one is additionally head-preloaded, see PERF-02). Separately, 404 responses are correct semantically (HTTP 404 + `<meta name="robots" content="noindex">` + canonical, verified on /temples/definitely-not-a-temple-xyz, /pujas/not-a-puja-xyz, /aarti/not-an-aarti-xyz, /city/notacity-xyz) but each one transfers 199,758-259,962 bytes of HTML. The largest prod pages are also heavy: aarti.html 588 KB raw HTML with 326 inline <svg>, chants.html 396 KB, calendar.html 386 KB, index.html 355 KB; 37 pages exceed 250 KB of raw HTML.
  - customer_impact: Missing intrinsic dimensions means the browser cannot reserve space, so every image that resolves shoves the content below it down — CLS on exactly the pages where a user is trying to tap a 'Book' button. The 260 KB 404 means a crawler hitting a stale link, or a user mistyping a temple slug, pays a quarter-megabyte to be told the page does not exist.
  - fix: Add explicit `width`/`height` (or `fill` with a sized parent) to the 31 next/image instances that lack them — /temples already does this correctly and is the pattern to copy. For the 404, replace not-found.tsx's full-layout render with a minimal standalone page (it does not need the mega-nav, footer, or 100+ inline SVG icons).

### [PERF-05] P2/seo/S Open Graph images are oversized and wrongly proportioned: 368 KB at 1280x853 site-wide, 261 KB PNG on 647 pages
  - where: apps/web/public/images/golden-temple-og.jpg (376,900 B, 1280x853); apps/web/public/og/default.png (261,150 B); referenced from layout.tsx:127 and 647 prod pages respectively
  - evidence: `/images/golden-temple-og.jpg` → 200, 376,900 bytes, JPEG SOF dimensions 1280x853 (aspect 1.50:1). Facebook and X both specify 1.91:1 (1200x630) for summary_large_image. `/og/default.png` → 200, 261,150 bytes; 647 of 1,785 prerendered pages use it as og:image. Every temple detail page instead uses a hotlinked Wikimedia original as its og:image (e.g. og:image=https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/ff/Kashi_Vishwanath.jpg with og:image:width=1200 og:image:height=630 declared — the declared dimensions do not match the actual file).
  - customer_impact: At 1.50:1 the card gets centre-cropped by the scrapers, so whatever is at the top and bottom of the Golden Temple photo is cut. WhatsApp — the dominant sharing channel for this audience — is aggressive about skipping large preview images, so a 368 KB JPEG risks the card rendering with no image at all. Declaring 1200x630 for a Wikimedia file that is a different shape means the crop is wrong on temple shares too.
  - fix: Re-export golden-temple-og.jpg at exactly 1200x630 and quality 80 (target under 150 KB). Convert og/default.png to JPEG at 1200x630 (a photographic OG card should never be PNG) — that alone takes 261 KB to roughly 90 KB across 647 pages. For temple pages, either drop the og:image:width/height declarations or generate a correctly-cropped 1200x630 derivative when the images are migrated off Wikimedia in PERF-01.

### [SEO-09] P2/seo/M 128 production pages share one title and 129 share one description — the root defaults
  - where: 12 sitemap routes export no metadata at all (/astrology/horoscope, /astrology/kundli, /yatra, /yatra/plan, /weddings/muhurat, /weddings/inquiry, /contact, /ask-pandit, /upgrade, /support/faq, /refer, /shloka-card) plus 116 non-sitemap prerendered pages
  - evidence: Across the 1,785 prerendered prod pages: 1,634 unique titles, 19 duplicate-title groups covering 170 pages. Largest group is 128 pages all titled "Darshya — India's Devotional Super App"; correspondingly 129 pages share the description "Experience live darshan, book pujas, explore panchang, listen to bhajans, and shop for sac...". Next largest: 8 pages titled "Cow Details — Gau Seva | Darshya" with an identical description, and 7 pairs of "Live Darshan — <temple>" pages duplicated exactly twice each. Source scan confirms 135 of 369 page.tsx files export no metadata whatsoever; 9 sitemap URLs inherit the root description verbatim.
  - customer_impact: Duplicate titles and descriptions across 170 pages means Google picks one and treats the rest as near-duplicates, and Search Console flags them. /contact, /ask-pandit and /refer are conversion pages that will never surface for their own branded queries. The 7 duplicated Live Darshan pairs also suggest the same stream is prerendered at two URLs — another duplicate-URL problem in the same family as SEO-06.
  - fix: Add `export const metadata` to the 12 sitemap-listed routes first (they are advertised to Google and currently indistinguishable from each other). For the 8 gaushala cow pages, generate the title from the cow's name. Investigate the 7 duplicated Live Darshan URLs — they are the same content at two paths, same root cause as the temple slug aliasing.

### [CWV-01] P0/seo/L Which pages fail Core Web Vitals in production, and why — structurally
  - where: /, /pujas, /temples, /temples/[slug] — every image-bearing route
  - evidence: Stated structurally, not from dev-mode wall-clock. LCP: the LCP candidate on / is a 1920x1280 hotlinked JPEG (810 KB measured) painted into a 526x160 slot, with 1,718 KB of head-preloaded competing image bytes ahead of it and a render-blocking cross-origin stylesheet (api.fontshare.com) in front of everything. On /pujas the largest above-the-first-scroll image is a 14,915 KB original. There is no srcset, no `sizes`, no AVIF/WebP, and `unoptimized: true`, so byte counts are identical at 375px and 1280px (measured: / = 12.72 MB mobile vs 14.95 MB desktop of images; /pujas = 20.85 MB mobile). No amount of server speed makes a 14.9 MB LCP resource pass a 2.5 s budget on 4G. CLS: 11/15, 10/10 and 10/10 images on /, /pujas and /temples/[slug] carry no width/height, so each late-arriving image reflows everything below it — and because the images are megabytes, they arrive very late. INP/TBT: the homepage ships 1,827 DOM nodes with 132 inline <svg> in a 363 KB document that is 56% RSC flight payload (204,454 of 363,319 bytes), plus 14 async chunks, all of which Googlebot is forbidden from fetching (SEO-02). Production CSS is 245 KB raw / 33 KB gzip across three files — that part is fine. The only routes likely to pass are text-only ones with no images (e.g. /pujas/rudrabhishek transfers 0 images once the stray layout preload is removed).
  - customer_impact: Core Web Vitals is a ranking signal and, more importantly, a real user-experience floor. Every commercial page — the homepage, the puja catalogue, the temple directory, the temple detail pages — fails LCP and CLS today for structural reasons that no CDN or server upgrade addresses. The failure is worst for the target audience: Indian mobile users on metered 4G, and NRI users on mobile abroad.
  - fix: Priority order, all measured above: (1) PERF-01 image pipeline — this alone takes /pujas from 20.85 MB to roughly 1.5 MB and is the single highest-leverage change in this entire audit; (2) PERF-02 remove the three head image preloads and preload only the true LCP element, correctly sized; (3) PERF-03 self-host fonts and delete the render-blocking Fontshare link; (4) PERF-04 add width/height for CLS; (5) SEO-02 unblock /_next/ so Google can actually measure any of it. Re-measure with Lighthouse against a production build (`next build && next start`) after each step — dev-mode timings in this report were deliberately not used as evidence.

### [HOME-01] P2/seo/M Scroll-to-load on the homepage: ranked deferral list with measured bytes
  - where: apps/web/app/page.tsx — the 23 below-fold sections; components/home/{ShopStrip,YatraStrip,WorshipStrip,WisdomStrip,ConsultStrip,TrustStrip}.tsx
  - evidence: Measured on the live homepage at 1280x720 (fold = 720 px, document height 8,224 px). Only 2 of 25 top-level sections are above the fold: the hero (topAbs=64, 6,381 B HTML, 1 image 812 KB) and IntentTrio 'Choose where to begin' (topAbs=591, 5,069 B, 0 images). Above-fold total: 11,450 B HTML + 812 KB images. Below-fold total: 23 sections, 103,965 B of DOM HTML, 850 DOM nodes, 97 inline <svg>, and 13,886 KB of images. In the production document the RSC flight payload is 204,454 B against 158,865 B of rendered HTML (ratio 1.29), so each byte of deferred section markup removes ~2.29 B from the 363,319 B document; deferring all below-fold sections would remove ~237,765 B = 65% of the homepage document. Ranked by measured saveable bytes: 1) BHAKTI STORE 'Everything for daily worship' topAbs=3417 — 9,079 KB images + 4,569 B HTML (4 images: 2000x1333, 4000x2250, 600x600, 3000x4500 — every one rendered in a 256x256 box); 2) YatraStrip 'Pilgrimage with peace of mind' topAbs=5845 — 2,460 KB + 6,517 B; 3) WorshipStrip 'Watch the divine, schedule a ritual, give to a cause' topAbs=4493 — 1,488 KB + 12,255 B; 4) LIVE DARSHAN 'See the divine, from anywhere' topAbs=1784 — 638 KB + 2,763 B (also head-preloaded, see PERF-02); 5) PUJA+ASTROLOGY 'Authentic rituals, verified priests' topAbs=2549 — 222 KB + 9,628 B; 6) WisdomStrip topAbs=4891 — 7,498 B (17,148 B of document); 7) ShopStrip topAbs=5583 — 7,472 B (17,088 B); 8) ConsultStrip topAbs=5281 — 6,868 B (15,707 B); 9) WHAT WE OFFER topAbs=1415 — 6,642 B (15,190 B); 10) Quick actions topAbs=1160 — 5,147 B (11,771 B).
  - customer_impact: Deferring in this order gets a first-contentful homepage that costs the hero image plus ~11 KB of markup, instead of 8,224 px of document and 14.7 MB of images. But the honest framing matters: 99.3% of the deferrable weight is images, not markup. Shipping scroll-to-load without fixing PERF-01 first just moves 13.9 MB from page-load to scroll — the user still pays it, now with a stall every time they scroll.
  - fix: Do PERF-01 first — it removes ~13 MB of the 13.9 MB regardless of any deferral. Then defer in the ranked order above using next/dynamic with `ssr: false` inside an IntersectionObserver wrapper (rootMargin ~600px so content is ready before it enters view), keeping sections 1-5 as the first tranche since they carry all the image weight. Do NOT defer the hero or IntentTrio (above fold), and do NOT defer the H1 or any text a crawler needs — with ssr:false that content leaves the HTML entirely, and Googlebot currently cannot run the JS that would restore it (SEO-02) so unblocking /_next/ is a hard prerequisite for any ssr:false deferral. A safer variant for the 6-10 text-only sections is `content-visibility: auto` with `contain-intrinsic-size`, which skips rendering work without removing the markup from the HTML.

### [SEO-10] P3/seo/S Metadata is streamed into the body at byte 206,042 rather than served in <head> for Googlebot
  - where: Next.js 15.5.18 streaming metadata (default); apps/web routes with async generateMetadata
  - evidence: Homepage HTML: `</head>` ends at byte 5,303 while `<title>` first appears at byte 206,042 — the title, description and canonical are emitted mid-body after a $RC hydration script. Same on /temples (title at 216,858, head ends at 5,137) and /pujas/rudrabhishek (120,696 vs 5,186). Testing user agents against /temples: Googlebot gets the streamed variant (title at 216,858, metaInHead=false), while facebookexternalhit, Twitterbot, WhatsApp, Slackbot and bingbot all get the blocking variant with metadata correctly inside <head> at byte 3,491. This is Next's intended htmlLimitedBots behaviour — the JS-executing crawlers get streaming.
  - customer_impact: Low risk for Google and Bing, which handle this. The exposure is crawlers not on Next's htmlLimitedBots list that also do not execute JS — AI/LLM crawlers, smaller search engines, SEO tooling, and link-preview services beyond the big five — which will see no title or description at all. It becomes a real problem only in combination with SEO-02: Google is given the streaming variant precisely because it renders, and robots.txt currently forbids it from fetching the renderer.
  - fix: No change needed once SEO-02 is fixed. If broader crawler coverage is wanted, add the extra agents to `htmlLimitedBots` in next.config.ts, or make the affected routes' generateMetadata synchronous/cached so Next emits metadata in <head> for everyone.

## NARRATIVE
# Darshya Pre-Launch Readiness: Executive Brief

**Eight measurement passes across scalability, conversion psychology, accessibility, resilience, localisation, notifications, money integrity, and SEO/performance.** Weighted average score: **33/100**. The codebase is better than that number suggests — the query layer, the concurrency locks, and the content depth are genuinely good work. What is broken is almost entirely *configuration, wiring, and last-mile delivery*: things that are cheap to fix and catastrophic to ship.

**The single most important sentence in this document:** a devotee can pay you ₹1,301 today, receive no confirmation on any channel, and if you refund them you will pay the pandit anyway.

---

## 1. THE FIVE THINGS THAT MATTER MOST

### #1 — A refund pays out twice. You lose 172% of the booking value.
**What it is.** `POST /payments/:id/refund` returns the devotee's money and leaves the pandit's commission and the temple's share fully `PENDING` and withdrawable. This was driven end-to-end: booking BS-20260812-J3M76, ₹1,151 refunded, then a **₹770 payout request successfully raised against that same refunded booking**. The correct reversal code already exists twenty files away in `refund.service.ts:284-315` — it was written once and never backported to the second rail.

Worse, *partial* refunds reverse nothing on **either** rail — and your published cancellation ladder (100/75/50/25/0) makes partial the *normal* case. A 75%-tier cancellation on a ₹1,100 puja: you keep ₹275, you owe ₹825. **Net −₹550 per mid-ladder cancellation, by design.**

**Cost if ignored.** Every refund is a cash transfer out of the company. Refunds cluster exactly when operations go wrong, so the loss rate spikes precisely when you can least afford it. There is no alert, no reconciliation flag, nothing on any dashboard. You would find out from your bank balance.

**Fix.** Extract the reversal block from `refund.service.ts:284-315` into `reverseBookingLedger(tx, bookingId)`; call it from inside the `payment.service.ts:1254` transaction. Then make it proportional for partials (scale by `refundAmount / paid`, write a REVERSED row rather than mutating). Add a test that asserts `availableInr === 0` after a full refund.

**Effort.** S — half a day for full refunds, one day for proportional partials. **Do this before the next paying customer.**

---

### #2 — Nobody gets told anything. Every notification channel is a silent no-op.
**What it is.** Four independent failures stacked:
- **All four channels are unconfigured** and report success anyway. `vapid-public-key` → `enabled:false`. WhatsApp → `whatsappEnabled:false`. Resend → `"RESEND_API_KEY not set"`. FCM → `"FCM not configured"`. And `POST /notifications/test` still answers `{"ok":true}`.
- **No scheduler in the repo invokes `runDailyOps`.** A repo-wide grep for `daily-ops`, `cron/tick`, `x-cron-token` returns *zero callers*. That function owns panchang blasts, aarti reminders, weekly digests, the dispatch sweep, auto-refunds and weekly settlement. The one scheduled workflow calls a *different* endpoint whose three jobs are literal `return { sent: 0 }` stubs — and goes green daily.
- **The refund notification function has zero call sites.** A phone-only devotee gets their money back with no notice on any channel.
- **The video-proof email you promise in the confirmation email does not exist as a template.** Video proof is the entire justification for 9-10x NRI pricing.

**Cost if ignored.** A devotee pays ₹1,301 for a puja and receives nothing. No receipt, no confirmation, no video-ready notice. In a prepay-a-stranger category the receipt *is* the proof of purchase — its absence reads as fraud. This is your #1 generator of chargebacks and one-star reviews, and none of it is visible in monitoring.

**Fix.** (a) Set `RESEND_API_KEY` and VAPID keys, and fail boot in production if they are absent. (b) Add `GET /health/channels` returning per-channel enabled state, surfaced on /admin. (c) Add a GitHub Actions workflow hitting `/api/v1/admin/cron/tick` every 10 minutes and assert on the returned counters, not the HTTP status. (d) Wire `notifyRefundProcessed` into both refund paths. (e) Add a `NotificationLog` table — one table buys you the in-app centre, the support answer, and the retry source.

Also: **the digest unsubscribe throws Postgres 42804 on every call, writes nothing, and returns 200 "You're unsubscribed."** One-character fix (`$1::uuid`), but it is a live DPDP/CAN-SPAM consent-withdrawal failure.

**Effort.** M — two days end to end. The env vars and the cron workflow are one hour.

---

### #3 — The entire API is one global 100 requests/minute bucket.
**What it is.** Measured, not inferred. After burning the bucket as one devotee, a *different authenticated user* got 6/6 HTTP 429, and anonymous requests with distinct `X-Forwarded-For` headers also got 6/6 429. Two compounding defects: the rate limiter's `keyGenerator` reads `req.currentUser`, which is set by a *preHandler* that runs after the limiter's onRequest hook — so the per-user branch is unreachable dead code; and Fastify is constructed without `trustProxy`, so `req.ip` is the socket peer. And because every page is server-rendered, **all traffic originates from one web container with one IP**.

Effective ceiling: **~1.7 requests/second for the whole platform**, roughly 15-30 concurrent readers. `/health` is allowlisted so Render stays green and nothing pages you. `gift-puja` is capped at 2 per *hour*, globally — the second person on the planet to gift a puja in an hour is refused.

**Cost if ignored.** Navratri, a temple's WhatsApp blast, or one press mention and the site refuses everyone simultaneously, silently, with your monitoring showing green.

**Fix.** Three lines in `server.ts`: `trustProxy: true`; register rate-limit with `hook: 'preHandler'` (or decode the JWT `sub` inside `keyGenerator`); raise `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` to 600/min per IP and allowlist the web container's server-to-server SSR calls. Add a test asserting two users do not share a bucket.

**Effort.** S — under two hours. **This is the highest value-per-minute fix in the document.**

---

### #4 — The funnel shows "★ 0.2", "Trust score: 4.9/100", and changes the price at step 3 of 4.
**What it is.** Four trust breaks a real buyer hits between the homepage and the pay button:

| Break | Where | What the buyer sees |
|---|---|---|
| `trustScore / 20` on a 0-5 scale | `pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:501` | Kashi Vishwanath rendered as **★ 0.2** |
| Same bug, printed as text | `pujas/[slug]/temples/page.tsx:163` | **"Trust score: 4.9/100"** directly under "Higher = safer" |
| ₹51 platform fee + ₹150 prasad disclosed at step 3 of 4 | `pujas/book/page.tsx:141` | ₹1,100 advertised → **₹1,301 charged**, on a homepage that says "**₹0 hidden fees**" |
| Three contradictory refund policies live at once | badge / `/refunds` / `cancellation.service.ts:47` | Badge promises "100% refund if not satisfied"; code pays 75% at 12h; policy page says 50% |

And there is **zero social proof anywhere** — no star, review, testimonial, booking count or sample proof video on home, listing or detail. Meanwhile 39 real bookings and 6 uploaded proof videos sit unused in the database.

**Cost if ignored.** This is the whole business. A first-time buyer comparing two ₹1,100 temples sees a near-zero rating on one of Hinduism's most revered shrines and concludes the site is fake. The price surprise at the last gate is the best-documented cause of checkout abandonment in e-commerce, and here it is compounded by an explicit contradicting promise. The refund-badge mismatch is also a Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 exposure once you take real money.

**Fix.** Delete `/20`, render `{trustScore.toFixed(1)}/5` (or stop putting a star glyph on an internal ops score — a star means *customer reviews* to every shopper alive). Fold ₹51 into base price; move the prasad toggle to step 1. Make `DEFAULT_CANCEL_POLICY` the single source of truth and render the badge and /refunds table from it. Add `bookingCount` to the pujas contract and render "39 devotees booked this puja" — it's a `COUNT`. Embed one real proof video.

**Effort.** S for the scale bug and pricing (one day). M for social proof plumbing (two days).

---

### #5 — Google is told every page is the homepage, and forbidden from loading your CSS.
**What it is.** Two two-line bugs that individually nullify all organic acquisition.

1. `layout.tsx:113` sets `alternates.canonical = "https://darshya.online"` in the **root** layout. Next inherits it. **349 indexable pages declare themselves the homepage** — including /pujas, /temples, /darshan, /astrology, /panchang, all supply-acquisition pages (/become-priest, /list-temple) and 30+ /content pages. That is an explicit instruction to Google to de-index them.
2. `robots.ts:64` disallows `/_next/` — where **100% of your CSS, JS and fonts live**. Googlebot renders every page unstyled and unhydrated. It is also the one crawler deliberately served Next's *streaming* metadata variant, so Google is being asked to render a page whose renderer you have forbidden it to fetch.

**Cost if ignored.** You have built ~1,600 pages of genuinely good long-tail devotional content and hreflang for six locales. None of it can rank. Every rupee of content investment is stranded, and the pandit/temple acquisition pages are invisible to supply too.

**Fix.** Delete `alternates.canonical` from the root layout (a *missing* canonical is far safer — Google self-canonicalises). Remove `'/_next/'` from the disallow array; if you wanted to hide RSC payloads, disallow `/_next/data/` only. Then add explicit canonicals to the 56 page files that lack one, and add a CI assertion over `.next/server/app/**/*.html` that fails if any indexable page's canonical ≠ its own URL.

**Effort.** XS — thirty minutes for both deletions. S for the per-page canonicals and CI guard.

---

## 2. SCALE & COST REALITY

### What breaks, in order, as traffic grows

| Volume | What breaks | Why | Cost to fix / absorb |
|---|---|---|---|
| **~15-30 concurrent readers** | Everything. Global 429s. | One 100 req/min bucket, keyed on the SSR container's IP | Free — 3 lines (`#3` above) |
| **~33 signups/day** | Welcome emails, booking receipts, 80G receipts, password resets all stop | Resend free = 100/day, ~3 emails per signup. Fails silently with a 429. Supabase's built-in mailer is tighter still at **2 emails/hour project-wide** until SMTP points at Resend | **$20/mo** Resend paid (50k). Point Supabase Auth SMTP at Resend today |
| **Any cold start, at any volume** | 40-90 second first page load | Render free spins down at 15 min idle (30-60s) **plus** 80 sequential DDL statements in `ensure-schema.ts` at ~95ms each = **7.6s of pure network wait** before a single route registers. Cloudflare's origin timeout is 100s — you are one slow boot from a 524 on your own homepage | Free: gate `ensure-schema` behind `RUN_SCHEMA_INVARIANTS=true` (it exists because a migration step was once bypassed; that condition no longer holds). **$7/mo** Render Starter to stop sleeping |
| **Two services, always** | You cannot keep the site warm | `render.yaml` declares **two** free web services = ~1,460 instance-hours against a 750-hour allowance. Your own `docs/free-tier-budgets.md` rule #2 says "Don't add a second free service" | Move web to Vercel Hobby/CF Pages (free, doesn't sleep) and keep the single Render free service for the API; or **$7/mo** |
| **~180 admin/cron page loads per month** | Supabase egress cap | `/admin/metrics/funnel` serialises **28 MB** at 200k users (measured with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, SERIALIZE)`). 5 GB/month free. A daily funnel cron alone is ~840 MB/mo at that scale | Free — replace `findMany`-then-bucket-in-JS with `groupBy`/`aggregate`. **111 of 205 `findMany` sites have no `take`/`cursor`** |
| **A few concurrent admin loads** | 512 MB container OOMs, taking every devotee request with it | Same unbounded reads pulled into Node. `userRoleAssignment.findMany()` with *no where clause* reads the whole table (33 MB) to count roles in a JS loop | Free — `groupBy({ by: ['role'], _count: true })` |
| **~200k rows in any growth table** | Admin panel takes 1-2s of DB time *before* the 95ms network hop | **No `created_at` index on any table that grows.** Measured on 200k-row shadow tables: user list 155ms warm / 2,113ms cold → **0.109ms with an index**. Transactions top-10: 1,418ms → 0.146ms | Free — index build measured at **3.7 seconds** for five 200k tables. Do it now, not later |
| **~230,000 completed orders** | Supabase 500 MB storage | ~1.08 KB per completed order fully indexed. That is ~₹80 crore of lifetime GMV | **Ignore this.** Storage is the last thing to break |

### The two structural costs

**Geography.** The API runs in Render **Oregon**; the database is Supabase **ap-northeast-1 Tokyo**. Every query crosses the Pacific at ~95ms. `/admin/reports` issues 25 sequential statements = **~2.4 seconds of pure network** before Postgres does any work; the pandit dashboard 12 statements = ~1.1s. Your users are in India, so the real path is India → Cloudflare → Oregon → Tokyo → Oregon → India. **You cannot optimise your way out of this in code.**

> Fix: recreate the Supabase project in **ap-south-1 (Mumbai)** and set both Render services to `region: singapore` — ~50ms from Indian users, ~5ms from the DB. If migrating Supabase is too disruptive now, move Render to Singapore alone (Singapore↔Tokyo ~70ms, and Singapore↔India is far better than Oregon↔India). Separately, `Promise.all` the sequential awaits in `getReports` and the priest dashboard so round trips overlap. **Effort: M, but this is the single largest latency win available.**

**Auth.** Every authenticated request in production makes a live HTTPS call to Supabase GoTrue in Tokyo (`auth.ts:243`), then 1-3 Prisma user lookups, then a role `findMany`. There is no local JWT verification and no cache. Your throughput ceiling is Supabase Auth's rate limit, not your own capacity — and if Supabase Auth has a slow minute every logged-in user is locked out with a 500. Verify the JWT offline with `jose` and cache `(userId → {roles, blocked})` for 60s. **Effort: S. Removes ~100-300ms from every authenticated page.**

**Redis is not configured.** `temple.service` wraps its hot paths in `cached()` with 300s/600s/6h TTLs — and `getClient()` returns null because `REDIS_URL` is unset, so every cache call is a no-op. The three most-hit public endpoints hit Tokyo on every request. Either provision Upstash free (10k commands/day covers it) or add a 20-line in-process LRU fallback so `cached()` degrades to memory instead of nothing. **The code reads as if caching is on — all future perf work is being measured against a false baseline.**

### Connection pooling
`DATABASE_URL` correctly uses the Supabase pooler with `pgbouncer=true`. No pooling defects found. The write path survived 20 parallel POSTs on one slot cleanly.

### Total monthly cost to remove every infrastructure constraint above
**~$52/mo**: Render Starter ×1 ($7) + Supabase Pro ($25) + Resend paid ($20) + Upstash free. Migrate the web front end off Render to a non-sleeping free tier and you stay at that number.

---

## 3. THE CONVERSION CASE

Ordered by expected lift per unit of effort. A buyer must clear four gates: *does this look real → do I want this specific thing → do I trust these people with ₹1,100 → is the price the price.* You currently fail at least one gate on every path.

**1. Fix the rating scale. (S — one hour)**
"★ 0.2" and "Trust score: 4.9/100" appear on the two screens whose only job is picking a temple to pay. There is no conversion work worth doing above this line. Four call sites disagree about whether the scale is 0-5 or 0-100; the database says 0-5.

**2. Make the advertised price the charged price. (S — half a day)**
₹1,100 → ₹1,301 at step 3 of 4, next to a homepage promise of "₹0 hidden fees". Fold the ₹51 into base price. Surface the ₹150 prasad toggle at step 1, right next to the trust pillar that has been promising prasad for four screens. Remove the "₹0 hidden fees" claim until it's true.

**3. Ship social proof from data you already have. (M — two days)**
Zero stars, reviews, testimonials, booking counts or proof videos anywhere in the funnel — while the DB holds 34 bookings (39 on Rudrabhishek) and **6 rows with a populated `video_proof_url`**. The site asserts trustworthiness eleven times ("verified" ×7, "video proof" ×4) and demonstrates it zero times. Cheapest first: add `bookingCount` to `/pujas/types`, render "39 devotees booked this puja"; embed one real proof video under "See what you receive"; trigger a review request on the `delivered` transition (the `reviews` table and `POST /ratings` already exist and nothing writes to them).

**4. Fix product cards. (S — one day)**
The only clickable region is an **89×34px** button — the image, title and price are inert `<div>` content. It's a bare `<a>`, not a `Link`, so every card click is a full document reload. And "Book Now" navigates to an *article*, not a booking form. Wrap the card in `next/link`, raise the button to 44px minimum, and rename it "View details" or deep-link to `/pujas/{slug}/book`.

**5. Rewrite card and hero copy. (S — one day + copywriting)**
Card descriptions average **1,144 characters** of scripture citation under a `line-clamp-2` (9.6% visible). The detail hero has *no* clamp — 1,144 characters ≈ 900px of prose between the H1 and the price on a 390px phone. And raw enum values leak to customers: "special_occasion", "planetary", "ancestral". Add a 100-140 char benefit-first `shortDescription` ("90-minute Shiva abhishek at Kashi Vishwanath. Samagri included, live video, prasad to your door."), drop `description` from the list response entirely (removes 72.5% of the payload), and map the category enum to display labels.

**6. Fix the homepage. (M — two days)**
H1 is a brand metaphor, not the job. Two co-equal CTAs split intent at the highest-stakes decision. Cookie banner covers **29% of the fold** on first paint. Page is 8,222px desktop / 12,151px mobile (14.4 screens) with Live Darshan merchandised four separate times. "How it works" appears **zero times**; "video proof" is asserted 4× with 0 examples. → Rewrite H1 to state the job. Demote "Explore Temples" to a text link. Insert a 3-step How-It-Works with a real 20-second proof clip under the hero. Lift "Free cancellation up to 48h" next to the CTA. Delay the cookie banner to first scroll. Cut to 5-6 sections.

**7. Re-point "Explore Temples". (S)**
**15 of 20 temples have zero bookable offerings** — including Vaishno Devi, Shirdi, Badrinath, Meenakshi Amman, Akshardham. Half your hero traffic goes to a directory where 3 in 4 destinations cannot take money. Badge them "Darshan info only — pujas coming soon" with a "Notify me" capture so the demand is measured rather than lost, and point the second CTA at "Browse pujas" until coverage is real.

**8. Make search find things you can buy. (M)**
`/search` is titled "Search knowledge" and searches only scripture articles — but the header placeholder rotates through "Search puja, temple, kundli, saint…". Someone who already knows they want Rudrabhishek types it in and gets nothing. Zero-result state is a hard dead end with no route back into the catalogue.

**9. Unblock NRI. (M — two days)**
The homepage promises "INR or USD, GBP, AUD, CAD" and "Book from 40+ countries". The booking form has **no country field**, hard-rejects any non-6-digit postcode and any non-`[6-9]\d{9}` phone — at both the web layer *and* the Zod schema — and the gateway currency is `const currency = 'INR'` unconditionally. A US ZIP, a UK postcode and a US mobile each fail both layers. This is the cohort your 9-10x pricing doctrine is built on. Meanwhile `/gift`, the NRI conversion page, has **no prices at all** and a 4-hour quote SLA. Add a country select and branch validation; show indicative prices on /gift; until the multi-currency rail ships, replace the currency list with the sentence already on /gift: "Book from anywhere — billed in INR, international cards accepted."

**Also worth an hour each:** 404 page is a single "Back to Home" link (add search + popular pujas); `/temples` and `/astrology` render an **empty full-viewport div** as their loading state (add a skeleton — users read blank as broken); Save and Share on the price card have **no onClick handlers at all** (wire Share to `navigator.share`, or remove both — a missing feature costs less trust than a broken one); `/pricing` advertises **30% commission** against your shipped 20%, throwing away your supply-side pitch against AstroTalk's 50%.

---

## 4. INTEGRITY RISKS

**The concurrency half is excellent and I could not break it.** Reported honestly because it changes where you spend effort: 12-way concurrent bookings against a cap-5 slot → exactly 5 rows. 10-way against a cap-1 pandit slot → exactly 1, including the cross-offering "one body, one puja" case. 8 simultaneous withdraw clicks → exactly 1 `PayoutRequest`, 0 hollow rows. 5 concurrent payout completions → ledger flipped once. Zero duplicate commission or temple-payout rows across 6-way verify-payment races, sequential replays, and 6 rounds of a deliberate cross-writer race. **You do not have a double-booking or oversell problem.** Keep the concurrent-slot test as a permanent integration test.

The problems are all in **ledger unwind and reconciliation**:

| ID | Severity | Finding | Money at risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| DI-01 | **P0** | Full refund via `/payments/:id/refund` never reverses commission or temple share. Drove a real ₹770 payout request against a booking whose status is `refunded` | **₹1,976 out on ₹0 revenue**, per refund |
| DI-02 | **P1** | Partial refunds reverse nothing on *either* rail — and the cancellation ladder makes partial the normal path | **−₹550** per 75%-tier cancellation |
| DI-03 | P1 | Admin settlement screen uses a legacy `×0.75` multiplier on **gross price** (which includes the ₹51 platform fee and ₹150 prasad pass-through). Measured: ₹34,803 shown vs ₹32,340 owed | **+7.6% overpay** on every manual settlement |
| DI-04 | P1 | The same pandit appears **twice** on the payout screen under two different IDs (`priests.id` and `users.id`), from two rails, with no indication they are one human | Duplicate real-world transfer |
| DI-05 | P1 | A pandit paid via the *temple* booking path sees **₹0** in their own payout portal while genuinely owed money. `writePujaCommissionOnConfirm` returns early when `priestId` is null at payment time, and nothing re-runs it when the priest is later assigned. **Four surfaces report four different numbers** for one pandit: ₹770 / ₹2,310 / ₹10,010 / ₹10,763.25 | Trust, not cash — but this is the most trust-destroying screen in a marketplace |
| DI-06 | P2 | **Zero UNIQUE constraints on any money table.** `pg_constraint` for `contype in ('u','x')` across bookings, commissions, temple_payouts, payout_requests, payments, store_orders returns an **empty set**. Every idempotency guard is an application-level find-then-create. It held during testing only because each transaction's first statement happens to take a row lock — an emergent property, not a declared invariant | Latent. Any future writer that reaches the ledger without locking the same row mints duplicate earnings |
| DI-07 | P2 | `commissions.booking_id` is **text** pointing at a **uuid** PK, with **no index** and **no FK** — and 2 orphan rows already exist. The idempotency guard does a `Seq Scan` on every payment confirm | O(ledger size) on the hot path; orphan earnings count toward withdrawable balance |
| DI-08 | P2 | `/admin/revenue` reports ₹1,142.91 and `/admin/commissions` reports ₹6,580 — both labelled "commissions". They measure platform take vs provider earning; neither is labelled | Undermines confidence in every other number, hides real discrepancies |
| DI-09 | P3 | Replayed `verify-payment` silently overwrites `gatewayPaymentId`, repointing any future refund at whichever ID was seen last | Low likelihood (needs the Razorpay secret), destroys audit trail |
| DI-10 | P3 | Repeat payout completion returns a response identical to the first, on a **manual bank-transfer** flow | Ledger safe; invites a second real-world payment |

**Order of work:** DI-01 → DI-02 → DI-03 → DI-05 → DI-04 → DI-06/07 (indexes and constraints). Note the repo's documented Prisma hazard for DI-06: `migrate deploy` wraps each migration in a transaction and `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` fails with 25001 inside one — apply via `$executeRawUnsafe` + `migrate resolve`.

---

## 5. THE PROMISE GAP

Everywhere the UI makes a commitment the system cannot keep. Each of these is a refund request or a support ticket waiting to happen.

| The promise | Where it's made | What actually happens |
|---|---|---|
| "100% refund if not satisfied" | Puja detail trust pillar (hardcoded literal) | Code pays 75% at 12h, 25% at 3h. "Not satisfied" is not a refund ground *anywhere* in the written policy |
| "₹0 hidden fees" | Homepage trust strip | ₹51 + ₹150 appear at step 3 of 4 |
| "You'll receive an email when the recording is ready" | Booking confirmation email body | **No video-ready email template exists.** Only a browser web-push fires |
| "Within 24 hours, a 3-7 minute video clip arrives on WhatsApp" | `/nri-pujas`, to your highest-value cohort | WhatsApp is unconfigured; the `puja_video_ready` template is declared and never sent from anywhere |
| "You're unsubscribed" | Digest unsubscribe page | Postgres 42804, nothing written, digest keeps sending |
| "Reminder set for Sandhya Aarti" | Darshan schedule bell | Writes to `localStorage`. No network call exists in the component. No server ever learns it |
| "15-minute aarti reminders" toggle | AartiOptIn | Stores a **web-push endpoint URL** in the FCM token column. FCM returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, the code classifies it as "expired", and **deletes the registration** on the first send |
| "200+ verified temples", "500+ vetted pandits" | Homepage, ×4 components | Hardcoded strings. Reality: 20 temples, 3 priests. A sceptical buyer clicks through to verify and finds an order of magnitude less — ASCI / Consumer Protection Act exposure |
| Instant tier: 60-second pandit accept window | Paid product differentiator | The only thing that acts on an expired offer runs on a documented **30-minute** cron — which nothing currently triggers. "Immediate re-broadcast" means up to 30 minutes, and the "we're re-matching" notice up to 60 |
| "Daily panchang, shubh muhurat alerts, festival reminders" | `/panchang` | `sendFestivalReminders` is `return { sent: 0 }`. Nobody has ever received one, and the daily CI check goes green |
| 6 languages in the footer | Every page | **One** locale file exists (`en.json`). `lib/t.ts` hard-imports it and takes no locale argument. /hi is 16.4% translated; /pujas is **0.7%**; all 7 localized product routes (`/hi/pujas`, `/ta/pujas`, …) **404** |
| Multi-currency for NRI | Homepage | `const currency = 'INR'`, unconditional |
| "From ₹1,100" up to **₹5,500** in Product JSON-LD | `pujas/[slug]/page.tsx:232` | `highPrice: basePrice * 5`. The ₹5,500 exists in **no offer, no page, and no database row**. Google's structured-data policy requires markup prices to match the page — this is a manual-action risk |
| `aggregateRating` on temple pages | `temples/[slug]/page.tsx:257` | Built from `trustScore / 20` with `ratingCount: 1`. An internal ops metric presented as a customer review average. Same manual-action risk, plus a plain misrepresentation to a devotee |
| "Multilingual help centre: 50 articles × 6 languages = 300" | `help-articles.ts:5` | **One** article (`how-to-book-a-puja`) in six languages. Not linked from `/help` at all, not in the sitemap |
| "Money in your account" | Payout completion | Web-push only. The whole DB has **1** push subscription. All 23 users have both email and phone on file, both unused |
| KYC verified | Provider portals | On **any** backend hiccup, all six portals tell an approved, KYC-verified provider they are unverified and must complete onboarding — because the default state is `'unsubmitted'` and `if (!r.ok) return;` never corrects it |

**Two rules to adopt:** (1) Nothing ships a success toast for a state change the server did not confirm. (2) Marketing numbers come from a `COUNT`, or they are phrased as a roadmap ("200 temples by March"), never as a present-tense fact.

---

## 6. ACCESSIBILITY & LEGAL EXPOSURE

Measured on 7 pages with a pixel-truth contrast engine (glyphs set transparent, element rasterised, real background pixels read) and real Tab-key traversal. **No page tested is WCAG AA clean.** Two Level-A defects.

**The fundamentals are unusually strong** and worth protecting: working skip link, first in tab order on all 7 pages; `lang="en"` everywhere; **0 missing alt across 49 images** with genuinely descriptive text; 0 positive tabindex; 0 duplicate IDs; 0 keyboard traps; `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured; a global `:focus-visible` ring visible on 66/66 measured tab stops.

### The two tokens that fail everything
`--color-surface-500: #7a756e` (**1,236 uses / 236 files**) — 4.12:1 on the body background, 3.78:1 on section bands, 3.32:1 in the footer. Needs 4.5:1. This is the default muted-text token: every pricing subtext, form hint, dashboard caption and footer line in the product.
`--color-surface-400: #b5b0a8` (**613 uses**) — and it is the **global `::placeholder` colour at 2.05:1**. Every form field's placeholder in the app is effectively unreadable, including fields whose *only* label is the placeholder.
Plus the footer copyright at **1.60:1** on every page.

> **Fix (one CSS file, ~30 minutes):** `--color-surface-500` → `#6b6660` (4.90:1 on the body bg). Change `::placeholder` to use `--color-text-muted` instead of `--color-text-faint`. Give the dark footer its own token pair (`#b5b0a8` reaches 7.04:1 on `#2a2520`). Everything consumes the tokens — **no component changes needed.** This single edit clears the largest category of failures site-wide.

### Level A — keyboard
- **Four of the five desktop mega-menu panels are unreachable by keyboard** (`CategoryNav.tsx`). Each trigger opens its own panel on focus, but the single shared panel renders *after* all five triggers in the DOM — so forward Tab only ever enters the last one. ArrowDown declares the APG menubar contract and never moves focus. **~70 of 90 primary navigation destinations closed to keyboard and switch users.** Fix: focus the first `[role="menuitem"]` on open; add roving ArrowUp/Down. Escape already works.
- **The mobile nav overlay is not a modal.** `role=null`, `aria-modal=null`, no `inert` on the page behind it. **176 background controls stay focusable**; 15/15 measured Tab presses landed *outside* the sheet, drawing a focus ring on elements hidden behind an opaque overlay. Focus is never moved in and never restored on close. Mobile navigation is unusable by assistive technology — on your majority surface. Fix: `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`, focus the Close button on open, `inert` the page wrapper, restore focus on close.

### Level AA — the revenue path
The booking wizard is **silent to assistive technology**: 0 live regions, 0 fieldsets, 0 radiogroups, 0 progressbars, no `aria-current` on the 4-step indicator. Pressing Continue announces nothing. The **date field has no label at all** — placeholder "DD/MM/YYYY" only, at 2.05:1 — and it determines the muhurat. Login inputs have no `autocomplete` and no `name` (SC 1.3.5), so password managers can't fill them. The password-reveal button has **no accessible name** (the only unnamed control on all 7 pages).

**Every `Book Now` is 89×34px** and both hero CTAs are 38px tall — 62 sub-44px targets on the homepage, 54 on /pujas. WCAG 2.5.8 (AA) floor is 24px, 2.5.5 (AAA) and both Apple HIG and Material say 44-48. On a mobile-first Indian audience skewing older, a missed tap on /pujas lands on dead card space and does nothing.

`/store` nests two buttons inside each product anchor — **49 invalid nested-interactive controls and 48 dead tab stops**, and neither button has an onClick, so "Add to Cart" just navigates. Users believe they added an item when they did not.

### Legal exposure summary
| Exposure | Basis |
|---|---|
| Accessibility complaint | Two WCAG 2.1 Level A failures on primary navigation; no page AA-clean |
| Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 | Displayed refund/cancellation terms do not match terms applied (three live variants) |
| ASCI / Consumer Protection Act | "200+ verified temples", "500+ vetted pandits" against 20 and 3 |
| DPDP / CAN-SPAM | Unsubscribe confirms success and records nothing |
| Google manual action | `highPrice = basePrice × 5`; `aggregateRating` synthesised from an ops metric |

**Total remediation for the AA contrast + Level A keyboard set: ~2 days.**

*Note for your QA harness:* the local API CORS allowlist omits `127.0.0.1`, so any audit run against `http://127.0.0.1:3000` silently gets error-state pages returning HTTP 200 that look plausible. Add `127.0.0.1:3000` to the dev allowlist or standardise all tooling on `localhost`.

---

## 7. HOMEPAGE SCROLL-TO-LOAD — IMPLEMENTATION PLAN

**Measured at 1280×720. Fold = 720px. Document height = 8,224px.**

**Above the fold: 2 sections, 11,450 B of HTML + 812 KB of images.**
**Below the fold: 23 sections, 103,965 B of DOM HTML, 850 nodes, 97 inline SVG, and 13,886 KB of images.**

In the production document the RSC flight payload is 204,454 B against 158,865 B of rendered HTML (ratio 1.29), so each byte of deferred section markup removes **~2.29 B** from the 363,319 B document. Deferring everything below the fold removes **~237,765 B = 65% of the homepage document**.

### Read this before implementing
**99.3% of the deferrable weight is images, not markup.** Shipping scroll-to-load without fixing the image pipeline first just *moves* 13.9 MB from page load to scroll — the user still pays it, now with a stall every time they scroll. **PERF-01 is a hard prerequisite**, and it removes ~13 MB of the 13.9 MB on its own.

**Second hard prerequisite:** `ssr: false` removes content from the HTML entirely, and Googlebot currently cannot run the JS that would restore it because robots.txt blocks `/_next/`. **Unblock `/_next/` before any `ssr:false` deferral**, or you will de-index the homepage's content on top of everything else.

### Order of work
**Step 0 — PERF-01 (images).** Rewrite the ~12 raw Wikimedia originals to their `/thumb/.../800px-` form. Wikimedia supports this and your codebase already uses it for *some* images (960px on /temples) and not others. **/pujas goes from 25.8 MB to under 1.5 MB in a single find-and-replace.** Then set `unoptimized: false`, add `sizes` props, and migrate the assets to Supabase Storage as AVIF/WebP.
**Step 0b — SEO-02.** Remove `'/_next/'` from robots.ts.
**Step 1 — PERF-02.** Delete the three `<link rel="preload" as="image">` tags. They total **1,718 KB**, two are below the fold, one is `fetchPriority="high"` on an image used in a 258×144 thumbnail 4,614px down — and because it lives in the *root layout* it fires on every route. `/pujas/rudrabhishek` renders **zero `<img>` tags** and still transfers 270 KB of never-painted Kashi Vishwanath.
**Step 2 — deferral, in this order.**

| # | Section | topAbs | Images saved | HTML saved | Doc bytes (×2.29) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | BHAKTI STORE "Everything for daily worship" | 3417 | **9,079 KB** | 4,569 B | 10,463 B |
| 2 | YatraStrip "Pilgrimage with peace of mind" | 5845 | **2,460 KB** | 6,517 B | 14,924 B |
| 3 | WorshipStrip "Watch the divine…" | 4493 | **1,488 KB** | 12,255 B | 28,064 B |
| 4 | LIVE DARSHAN "See the divine, from anywhere" | 1784 | 638 KB | 2,763 B | 6,327 B |
| 5 | PUJA + ASTROLOGY "Authentic rituals, verified priests" | 2549 | 222 KB | 9,628 B | 22,048 B |
| 6 | WisdomStrip | 4891 | — | 7,498 B | 17,148 B |
| 7 | ShopStrip | 5583 | — | 7,472 B | 17,088 B |
| 8 | ConsultStrip | 5281 | — | 6,868 B | 15,707 B |
| 9 | WHAT WE OFFER | 1415 | — | 6,642 B | 15,190 B |
| 10 | Quick actions | 1160 | — | 5,147 B | 11,771 B |

**Sections 1-5 are the first tranche** — they carry all the image weight. Use `next/dynamic` with `ssr: false` inside an `IntersectionObserver` wrapper at `rootMargin: 600px` so content is ready before it enters view.

**Sections 6-10 are text-only.** Prefer `content-visibility: auto` with `contain-intrinsic-size` — it skips rendering work without removing the markup from the HTML, so there is no crawler risk at all.

**Do NOT defer:** the hero (topAbs 64) or IntentTrio (topAbs 591) — both above the fold — or the H1, or any text a crawler needs.

**Result:** first-contentful homepage costs one correctly-sized hero image plus ~11 KB of markup, instead of 8,224px of document and 14.7 MB of images.

**While you are in there (BHAKTI STORE, section 1):** its four images are 2000×1333, 4000×2250, 600×600 and 3000×4500 — **every one rendered in a 256×256 box.**

### One more thing on performance
**Your brand webfonts have been dead for some time and nobody noticed.** `api.fontshare.com` returns HTTP 200 with a 162-byte body: *"Access to the Fontshare API has been temporarily restricted."* No `@font-face` rules are served. Measured across 352 text nodes on the homepage: **5,545 glyphs in Arial, 58 in Nirmala UI, 1 in Georgia, 0 from any webfont.** Meanwhile next/font/google downloads **201.9 KB of woff2** whose CSS variables are never referenced by `--font-body`/`--font-display`, and a render-blocking cross-origin request to Fontshare sits in `<head>` on every page load. Self-host Satoshi and Cabinet Grotesk via `next/font/local`, delete the Fontshare link and preconnects, and add a Playwright assertion that `CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode` on body reports `isCustomFont: true` so it can never regress silently again.

### If you only do one day of work
Items 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16 — about six hours total. That is: the platform stops refusing all users at 1.7 req/s, emails start sending, crons start running, unsubscribe stops lying, Google can index and render your site, Kashi Vishwanath stops showing ★ 0.2, /pujas drops from 20.85 MB to under 1.5 MB, and cold starts lose 7.6 seconds. Then spend day two on item 1 so refunds stop costing you 172% of the booking.
